# alifshop.uz — REST POST, multi-seller

Reference implementation: [`fill_alifshop_prices.py`](../../fill_alifshop_prices.py)

Multi-seller marketplace: one variant is listed by many partners at different
prices, so the output is a **median**, not a single price. Highest raw coverage of
any source (63/66).

---

## 1. Endpoint

```
POST https://gw.alifshop.uz/web/client/search/full-text
```

No auth. The one non-obvious header is `service-token`, which is a **fixed public
string**, not a credential:

```http
Accept: application/json
Accept-Language: ru
Content-Type: application/json
service-token: service-token-alifshop
Origin: https://alifshop.uz
Referer: https://alifshop.uz/
User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0 (Windows NT 10.0; Win64; x64) AppleWebKit/537.36 (KHTML, like Gecko) Chrome/150.0.0.0 Safari/537.36
```

Body:

```json
{"query": "Honor X9d 12/256", "page": 0, "size": 20}
```

- `page` is **0-based** (unlike beemarket/texnomart, which are 1-based)
- `size` is **capped at 20** server-side — asking for more is silently ignored
- 3 pages (60 listings) is plenty for a stable median

## 2. Response shape

```jsonc
{
  "items": [
    {
      "id": "4ab27e23-1f44-4c49-8a0d-213b82ab40a1", // per-listing UUID, distinct per partner offer
      "price": 259900000,                       // TIYIN — divide by 100
      "slug": "honor-x9d-12-256-gb-midnight-black-abc123",
      "quantity": 7,
      "partner_id": 1378,
      "product": {"name": "Honor X9d 12/256 GB Midnight Black"},
      "partner": {"id": 1378, "slug": "some-seller"}
    }
  ],
  "n_pages": 4
}
```

> **Corrected 2026-07-31, live crawler client.** This section previously omitted
> `items[].id`. It is the field the crawler client (`narxnoma/sources/alifshop.py`)
> uses as `source_key` — a UUID assigned per listing by alifshop itself, distinct
> per partner offer even when `slug`, `product.name`, and `price` are identical
> across sellers. `slug` alone is not documented as guaranteed unique across
> partners and must not be relied on for dedup; `id` is. Also present but unused
> by the crawler client: `quantity` (stock count, used for availability) and
> `partner_id` (redundant with `partner.id`).

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `items[].id` | **per-listing UUID** — use this as the dedupe/upsert key, not `slug` |
| `items[].price` | **TIYIN** — divide by **100** to get SUM |
| `items[].product.name` | the string to match against |
| `items[].slug` | listing slug, used for the URL |
| `items[].quantity` | stock count; `0` means out of stock |
| `items[].partner.slug` | seller identity — useful for dedupe/diagnostics |
| `n_pages` | total pages for this query |

No per-item category or product-type field exists anywhere in this response.
`aggregations.category` is a dataset-wide histogram (10 buckets with counts) over
the *whole* result set for the query, not a per-item field — it cannot be used to
tag an individual item as phone vs. non-phone.

**But the search request accepts a `category` filter, and it genuinely narrows
server-side.** The valid values are exactly the display strings in that same
response's `aggregations.category.terms[].key` — there is no numeric id, and the
value is brand-specific ("Смартфоны Samsung", "Смартфоны Xiaomi", ...), so it has
to be read off each query's own aggregation rather than hardcoded. Measured live
on 2026-07-31:

| Request body | `n_items` | `n_pages` |
|---|---|---|
| `{"query": "Samsung", "page": 0, "size": 20}` | 1,034 | 52 |
| `{"query": "Samsung", "page": 0, "size": 20, "category": "Смартфоны Samsung"}` | 690 | 35 |
| `{"query": "Samsung", "page": 0, "size": 20, "category": "Nonexistent Category XYZ"}` | 0 | — |

The third row is what proves this is a real server-side filter and not an ignored
unknown key: a made-up category value returns nothing, and the unfiltered "Samsung"
sweep is one third non-phones — microwaves, TVs, washing machines, vacuums,
fridges, smartwatches, tablets, headphones, and phone cases — that a downstream
matcher-health table would otherwise have to absorb.

The crawler client (`narxnoma/sources/alifshop.py`) picks the phones bucket with
`phone_category_from_aggregations`: `key.casefold().startswith("смартфон")`.
**This must be a prefix test, not a substring test** — the observed bucket
"Чехлы для смартфонов" (phone cases) *contains* "смартфон" and a naive `in` check
would select it, which is exactly the non-phone noise this filter exists to
remove. "Смарт-часы" (smartwatches) starts with "смарт" but not "смартфон", so the
prefix rule correctly excludes it too. `sweep()` fetches page 0 unfiltered once per
brand to read the aggregation, re-requests page 0 with the discovered `category`,
and paginates that filtered set; if no phone-like bucket is found it logs a
warning and falls back to the unfiltered sweep for that brand.

> **Units trap:** `price` is in tiyin. `259900000 / 100 = 2,599,000 SUM`. Forgetting
> the divisor produces prices 100× too large, which is obvious — but a *partial*
> divisor bug is not, so assert the result lands in a plausible 500 K–50 M range.

## 3. Product URL — verified, and not what you'd guess

```
https://alifshop.uz/moderated-offer/<slug>
```

The intuitive `https://alifshop.uz/product/<slug>` **404s** ("Biz buni topa olmadik").
The slug returned by the API is correct; only the path prefix differs. This was
caught only by opening a generated link in a browser — do the same for any new site.

## 4. Query building — two-shot, colour-stripped first

> **Corrected 2026-07-26.** This section used to say "alifshop's search is forgiving
> enough that broad stripping isn't needed." **Measured false.** A target named with a
> marketing colour misses the listing alifshop stores under the plain Russian word:
> querying `Redmi Note 15 8/256 Glacier Blue` never returns
> `Смартфон Xiaomi Redmi Note 15 8/256 ГБ, Синий` at 2,723,000 — the cheapest genuine
> blue listing. Same for `Midnight Black` vs `Black` (2,609,000 missed).
> This is a **recall** failure, and it is exactly what the global rule
> *broad query, strict match* exists to prevent.

Run the colour-stripped query first, then the verbatim one, and dedupe by `slug`:

```python
def build_queries(name_ru):
    q = re.sub(r"^\s*Смартфон\s+", "", name_ru).strip()
    q = re.sub(r"\bGB\b", "", q, flags=re.I)
    verbatim = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", q).strip()
    broad = " ".join(t for t in verbatim.split()
                     if t.lower().strip(",()") not in set(COLOR_MAP))
    broad = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", broad).strip()
    return [broad, verbatim] if broad and broad != verbatim else [verbatim]
```

The colour is enforced in the matcher, never in the query. Keep the `12/256` form —
unlike beemarket/texnomart, alifshop does not need it collapsed.

## 5. Turning many sellers into one number

```python
listings = search_alifshop(session, build_query(name))
matched  = [l for l in listings if score_match(target, l["name"]) >= 30]
median, kept = robust_median([l["price_sum"] for l in matched])
# link = the listing whose price is closest to the median
near = min(matched, key=lambda l: abs(l["price_sum"] - median))
```

IQR stripping matters here: marketplaces carry both mis-listed accessories priced
like phones and phones priced like accessories.

## 6. Gotchas

- **`+` normalisation.** `"(nanoSim+eSim)"` appears in seller names. Converting every
  `+` to `plus` injects a phantom model token that rejects every candidate. Only
  treat `+` as `plus` when it *trails* a word (`Pro+`):
  ```python
  t = re.sub(r"\+(?=\s|$)", " plus ", t)
  t = t.replace("+", " ")
  ```
- **Colour qualifiers.** Do not map `velvet`/`light`/`golden` to colours —
  `"Velvet Grey"` → `{black, gray}` would pool Black sellers into a Grey median. The
  base colour word is always present anyway.
- Sellers frequently omit RAM. The matcher must treat a missing RAM on the candidate
  side as "unknown, don't reject", while a *mismatched* RAM is a hard reject.

## 7. Result

**63/66 (95 %)** matched — the highest count, though as a median across resellers it
is a softer number than the single-card retailers.



================================================
FILE: docs/parsers/asaxiy.md
================================================
# asaxiy.uz — server-rendered HTML, no JSON API at all

Client: [`narxnoma/sources/asaxiy.py`](../../narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/sources/asaxiy.py)

The eighth source, added 2026-08-10, and the one that breaks this directory's
opening rule.

> [README.md](README.md): *"None of these sites can be scraped from HTML — every
> one is a JavaScript SPA that renders an empty shell. The durable path is
> always the private JSON API the SPA itself calls."*

For asaxiy the durable path is the inverse, and it is not a preference. **There
is no catalogue JSON endpoint.** Probed live: the only `/ajax/*` routes the
site's own frontend calls are `check-phone`, `login-validate`,
`send-verification-sms` and `new-filter-url` — account and filter plumbing.
The category page returns a complete, fully-priced product grid in the initial
HTML document, and the "show more" button re-requests that same URL.

Broad-range retailer (books, toys, groceries) with a real electronics range.
Second-cheapest source to crawl after olcha: 34 requests for the whole
seven-category sweep.

---

## 1. Endpoint

```
GET https://asaxiy.uz/ru/product/<category-path>            # page 1
GET https://asaxiy.uz/ru/product/<category-path>/page=<N>   # page N ≥ 2
```

No auth, no cookies, no token.

```http
Accept: text/html,application/xhtml+xml,application/xml;q=0.9,*/*;q=0.8
Accept-Language: ru-RU,ru;q=0.9
Origin: https://asaxiy.uz
Referer: https://asaxiy.uz/
User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0 (Windows NT 10.0; Win64; x64) AppleWebKit/537.36 (KHTML, like Gecko) Chrome/150.0.0.0 Safari/537.36
```

**The page number is a PATH segment, not a query parameter.** `?page=2` is
ignored; `/page=2` is what asaxiy's own pagination links and its "load more"
button use.

## 2. Language: `/ru/`, not a cookie and not a header

asaxiy defaults to Uzbek (`<html lang="uz">`). Three ways to ask for Russian
were tried:

| Attempt | Result |
|---|---|
| `Accept-Language: ru-RU,ru;q=0.9` | ignored — titles stay Uzbek |
| `_language=ru` cookie, set by hand | ignored (the cookie is Yii-signed) |
| `_language` cookie obtained from the server | sets the chrome; **titles stay Uzbek** |
| `?language=ru` | works — by **301-redirecting to `/ru/product/...`** |
| `GET /ru/product/...` directly | works, no redirect, stateless ✅ |

So the client requests the `/ru/` path. Note the trap in the fourth row:
`?language=ru` combined with `X-Requested-With: XMLHttpRequest` returns a bare
301 that `requests` does not follow, and you get an empty body.

**Do not depend on the path alone anyway** — see §3, every card states its
Russian name in an attribute regardless of which language the page rendered.

## 3. The card, and what to read from it

Product cards start at `<div class="product__item ">`. Do not parse the
rendered price text (`6 719 000 so'm`) or the title span. Every card carries
the hidden block asaxiy's own add-to-cart reads:

```html
<span id="product_cart_data_71120"
      data-id="71120"
      data-name="Стиральная машина 7TECH 7WM4S1060GSS1 6 Кг. ХИТ ПРОДАЖ!"
      data-name-ru="Стиральная машина 7TECH 7WM4S1060GSS1 6 Кг. ХИТ ПРОДАЖ!"
      data-price="2739000"
      data-old-price="0"
      data-img="https://assets.asaxiy.uz/product/main_image/mobile/68d7ac8f1d42b.png.webp"
      data-cart-id="84417387bc832ab2a76535d990ce2675"
      data-brand-name="7TECH"
      hidden>
```

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `data-id` | **the source key.** Also appears as `data-product-id` on the card's buttons |
| `data-price` | **use this** — plain SUM, no separators, already discounted |
| `data-old-price` | struck-through pre-discount price; `0` when there is no discount |
| `data-name-ru` | **use this for the product name** — present even on an Uzbek page |
| `data-name` | the name in the page's own language |
| `data-img` | absolute image URL |
| `data-brand-name` | the retailer's own brand string, e.g. `"Samsung"` |

`data-price` equals the outer div's `data-actual-price` on every card
observed, discounted or not.

### 3a. The URL must be tied to the product id

The card's anchor is:

```html
<a href="/ru/product/stiralnaya-mashina-7tech-7wm4s1060gss1-6-kg"
   onclick="sendSelectItemEvent(71120, this)">
```

Splitting an HTML document on a class name is only safe if nothing downstream
trusts the split alone. **Require `sendSelectItemEvent(<id>` to name the same
id as the cart block**, and drop the card otherwise. Without that check, a
change to asaxiy's card markup hands a product its neighbour's URL — a wrong
link that no price check, no key check and no aggregate metric can notice.

The href is localised: `/product/<slug>` on the Uzbek page, `/ru/product/<slug>`
on the Russian one. Store it as given, so the stored link opens in the language
the sweep read.

### 3b. Availability: read the CSS class, not the href and not the text

An in-stock card renders the buy control; an out-of-stock one renders
`<div class="h6">● Нет в наличии</div>` instead.

Measured on the whole washers category (144 cards): 132 carried the control,
12 did not, and those 12 were exactly the 12 carrying the out-of-stock text.
No card had both; none had neither.

Use **`js-buy-guest`**, the class asaxiy's own JavaScript binds to. Both
alternatives are localised and both will silently lie:

- the button's href is `/cart/simple?slug=` on the Uzbek page and
  `/ru/cart/simple?slug=` on the Russian one — reading it scored **every**
  Russian card out-of-stock, while looking entirely reasonable;
- `Нет в наличии` is Russian-only.

## 4. Pagination: an out-of-range page serves page 1

**This is the trap on this site.** `stiralnye-mashiny/page=9` of a 6-page
category does not 404 and does not return an empty grid. It
**301-redirects to page 1 and serves 24 real products again.** A client that
stopped on "an empty page" would loop until `MAX_PAGES` re-ingesting page 1
and record a triumphant harvest of duplicates.

The end of the catalogue is stated by each page in its own inline script:

```js
let totalPages = parseInt('6', 10) || 1;
```

The client stops on that **and**, independently, on the first page that
contributes no product id it has not already seen.

### 4a. Do not use the lighter XHR fragment

Sending `X-Requested-With: XMLHttpRequest` returns the bare product grid,
~310 KB instead of ~800 KB. It also drops the `totalPages` script — the only
end-of-catalogue signal there is, against the paginator described above.
Trading the stop condition for bandwidth is not a trade. The largest swept
category is 10 pages.

## 5. Category leaves

Verified live 2026-08-10 by walking the site's own category tree.

| Our category | asaxiy leaf path | Items | Pages |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| `smartphones` | `telefony-i-gadzhety/telefony/smartfony` | 233 | 10 |
| `televisions` | `televizory-video-i-audio/televizory` | 57 | 3 |
| `laptops` | `kompyutery-i-orgtehnika/noutbuki/noutbuki-2` | 90 | 4 |
| `acs` | `klimaticheskaya-tehnika/kondicionery` | 113 | 5 |
| `fridges` | `bytovaya-tehnika/krupnaya-tehnika-dlya-kuhni/holodilniki` | 107 | 5 |
| `washers` | `bytovaya-tehnika/tehnika-dlya-doma-2/stiralnye-mashiny` | 132 | 6 |
| `ovens` | `bytovaya-tehnika/krupnaya-tehnika-dlya-kuhni/gazovye-plity` | 18 | 1 |

Leaves deliberately **not** swept, each checked rather than assumed:

- **`kuhnya/mikrovolnovye-i-duhovye-pechi`** reads like the oven category and
  is not one. All 120 of its listings were read: 91 microwaves, 29 mini-ovens,
  **zero built-in ovens**. All 540 `ovens` offers the other six sources
  contribute contain no microwave at all, so these could never price-compare
  and every one would render under the "Духовой шкаф" label. asaxiy does not
  sell built-in ovens.
- **`kuhnya/nastolnye-plity`** — 3 items, tabletop hotplates (Sonifer SF-3050,
  BQ HP124SS). A countertop hob is not the freestanding cooker the other
  sources file under `ovens`.
- **`kompyutery-i-orgtehnika/noutbuki`** (the parent) carries laptop bags,
  stands, docks and chargers as siblings of the machines. `noutbuki-2` is the
  leaf that holds only laptops.

## 6. A category leaf is broader than the category

asaxiy's leaves mix in things that are not the product, and unlike a stray
search result these **form keys**: "Сушильная машина Bosch WTN86211ME" has a
brand and a catalogue code, clusters perfectly, and produces a variant the
site then renders as *"Стиральная машина Bosch WTN86211ME"*. A tumble dryer
labelled a washing machine is not a coverage gap, it is a wrong page.

`sweep()` therefore applies a denylist (`is_wanted`), earned by reading all
769 live titles across the seven leaves. It refuses 76 (9.9%) — every one of
them genuinely not the category's product, and nothing real:

| Leaf | Refused | What they are |
|---|---:|---|
| washers | 50 | drying racks, tumble dryers, stacking kits, laundry gel |
| televisions | 13 | wall-mounting services, mobile brackets |
| laptops | 11 | Windows / Bitdefender / ESET licence keys |
| acs | 2 | installation services |
| smartphones, fridges, ovens | 0 | — |

A **denylist**, not an allowlist, for the reason
[not_a_phone.py](../../narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/sources/not_a_phone.py)
gives: asaxiy's titles are template-generated and noun-led today
("Смартфон Xiaomi …", 233 of 233), and an allowlist would silently drop a
whole category the day that template changes. The count is logged per sweep,
so a template change shows up as a moving number rather than as silence.

One entry is anchored to the start of the title on purpose:
**"Сушильная машина"** leading is a tumble dryer, but
"Стирально-сушильная машина" and "Встраиваемая стирально-сушильная машина"
are washer-dryer combos — washing machines, which olcha and uzum already list
under `washers`.

The `PRICE_MIN` fence turns out to be a second, independent line of defence
against the same noise: laundry gel at 99,000 and anti-vibration feet at
59,000 SUM are dropped before the denylist ever sees them.

## 7. Measured result

693 offers kept from a 34-request sweep, **96.8% of them attached** to a
canonical product — the highest attachment rate of the eight sources.

| Category | Offers | Attached |
|---|---:|---:|
| smartphones | 233 | 93.6% |
| acs | 118 | 98.3% |
| fridges | 107 | 100% |
| washers | 94 | 100% |
| laptops | 79 | 96.2% |
| televisions | 44 | 95.5% |
| ovens | 18 | 100% |

329 of those products are carried by at least one other store, so they became
real price comparisons rather than new single-store pages.

FILE: docs/parsers/beemarket.md

# market.beeline.uz (BeeMarket) — REST GET, single card

Reference implementation: [`fill_beemarket_prices.py`](../../fill_beemarket_prices.py)

Beeline's own retail storefront. Single canonical card per variant — no reseller
spread — so the best exact match's price is the answer directly.

---

## 1. Endpoint

```
GET https://market.beeline.uz/api/web/search?q=<text>&is_bshop=1&page=<1-based>
```

No auth.

```http
Accept: */*
Accept-Language: ru
Referer: https://market.beeline.uz/
User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0 (Windows NT 10.0; Win64; x64) AppleWebKit/537.36 (KHTML, like Gecko) Chrome/150.0.0.0 Safari/537.36
```

- `is_bshop=1` restricts to the BeeMarket shop catalogue — keep it
- `page` is **1-based**
- Read `data.last_page` and stop; 3 pages is ample

### 1a. Category-scoped sweep endpoint (crawler-discovered, 2026-07-31)

The endpoint above is a per-query search over BeeMarket's **entire**
catalogue (phones, headphones, air fryers, keyboards, network gear — 4,306
items across 270 pages with an empty `q`), which is the right shape for
`fill_beemarket_prices.py`'s per-product lookup but far too expensive for a
full-catalogue sweep. For sweeping, use the category-scoped endpoint
instead — the same request BeeMarket's own category page
(`https://market.beeline.uz/smartfony`) issues, found by watching that
page's network requests:

```
GET https://market.beeline.uz/api/web/categories/smartfony/products?page=<1-based>&is_bshop=1
```

Same headers, same doubly-nested `data.data` / `data.last_page` envelope and
item shape as the generic search endpoint. Confirmed live: 436 items across
28 pages, every item `category.slug == "smartfony"` and `is_smartphone:
true`. Extra params tried on the *generic* search endpoint to narrow it
in-place (`category=smartfony`, `category_id=1`, `category_ids[]=1`,
`category_slug=smartfony`, `is_smartphone=1`) were all silently ignored —
only this separate endpoint actually scopes server-side. `q=` and
`is_bshop=` appear to have no effect on this endpoint either (identical
results with and without), so it always returns the full smartfony
catalogue regardless.

`/api/web/categories/list` confirms `smartfony` (id 1) is a top-level
category with no `active_children` — the sole phone category, so this
endpoint alone covers V1's smartphones-only scope.

## 2. Response shape

Note the **doubly-nested `data`** — a common source of `KeyError`:

```jsonc
{
  "code": 200,
  "data": {
    "data": [
      {
        "name": "Honor X9d, Midnight Black, 12/256 GB",
        "slug": "honor-x9d-point-midnight-black-point-12256-gb",
        "category": {"slug": "smartfony"},
        "actual_price": 5290000,     // current price, PLAIN SUM
        "price": 5990000             // pre-discount list price
      }
    ],
    "last_page": 3
  }
}
```

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `actual_price` | **use this** — current displayed price, plain SUM, discount applied |
| `price` | struck-through pre-discount price — do not use |
| `name` | carries RAM + storage + colour, comma-separated |
| `slug` | URL slug |
| `category.slug` | needed for the URL; default `smartfony` if absent |

Fallback chain used in practice: `actual_price` → `discount_price` → `price`.

## 3. Product URL

```
https://market.beeline.uz/<category.slug>/<slug>
```

e.g. `https://market.beeline.uz/smartfony/honor-x9d-point-midnight-black-point-12256-gb`

Note BeeMarket encodes a literal `.` in names as the token `point` inside the slug —
another reason to use the API's `slug` verbatim rather than generating one.

## 4. Query building — broad, because the search is strict AND

BeeMarket's search ANDs every term, so an over-specific query returns **zero**
results. Collapse `12/256` to just the storage and drop colour/marketing tokens:

```python
_DROP_FROM_QUERY = set(COLOR_MAP.keys()) | {
    "awesome", "5g", "4g", "xiaomi", "point", "sim", "esim", "nanosim",
    "nano", "gb", "гб", "tb", "тб",
}

def build_query(name_ru):
    n = re.sub(r"^\s*Смартфон\s+", "", name_ru).strip()
    n = re.sub(r"\b(\d{1,2})[/ ](\d{2,4})\b", r"\2", n)   # "12/256" → "256"
    toks = [t for t in n.split() if t.lower() not in _DROP_FROM_QUERY]
    return re.sub(r"\s+", " ", " ".join(toks)).strip()
```

Precision is restored entirely by the matcher. `xiaomi` is dropped because BeeMarket
lists those phones as plain `Redmi …`.

## 5. Gotchas

- **The `SIM+eSIM` phantom token.** BeeMarket names contain `SIM+eSIM`. Blindly
  mapping `+` → `plus` created a spurious `plus` model token that rejected **every
  iPhone**. Only trailing `+` is a real modifier:
  ```python
  t = re.sub(r"\+(?=\s|$)", " plus ", t)   # "Pro+" → "pro plus"
  t = t.replace("+", " ")                  # "SIM+eSIM" → "sim esim"
  ```
- Names put colour in the middle (`"Honor X9d, Midnight Black, 12/256 GB"`), so
  parse by tokens, not position.
- Accessories carry no storage — the matcher's "target has storage, candidate has
  none → reject" rule removes them for free.

## 6. Result

**57/66 (86 %)**.



================================================
FILE: docs/parsers/glotr.md
================================================
# glotr.uz — server-rendered Yii catalogue (classifieds board)

> **Access.** glotr's `robots.txt` disallows the browse surface (`/search`,
> `/*?catid=`, `/*?page=`, `Crawl-delay: 10`) to every user agent, and names
> `ClaudeBot`/`GPTBot`/`CCBot` with `Disallow: /`. **We sweep it under an
> explicit grant from glotr.** That permission is specific to this operator and
> is what makes this client legitimate; the file itself cannot be changed on
> their side for unrelated security reasons. Do not copy this approach to
> another site whose robots.txt says the same thing.

Like [asaxiy](asaxiy.md), glotr has no JSON API. Pages are rendered server-side
(Yii: `yii.js`, `yii.activeForm.js`, `/assets/…`) and a category page carries a
complete, fully-priced product grid in the initial HTML document.

Unlike every other source here, **glotr is a classifieds board, not a shop.**
Sellers post their own listings into a category of their choosing. That single
fact drives most of what follows.

---

## 1. Request

```
GET https://glotr.uz/<category-path>/            # page 1
GET https://glotr.uz/<category-path>/?page=N     # page N
Accept-Language: ru-RU,ru;q=0.9
```

UTF-8, declared in both the `Content-Type` header and a `<meta charset>`. No
auth, no cookies, no token.

| our category | path | pages (2026-08-11) |
|---|---|---|
| smartphones | `telefoni-i-plansheti` | 38 |
| televisions | `televizori` | 20 |
| laptops | `noutbuki` | 36 |
| acs | `konditsioneri` | 58 |
| fridges | `xolodilniki` | 33 |
| washers | `stiralnie-mashini` | 23 |
| ovens | `duxovie-shkafi` | 5 |

56 cards per page, except the last.

---

## 2. The card, in source order

```html
<div class='product-card '>
  <div class='product-card__header'>
    <a href="/vstraivaemiy-…-p-881208/" class='product-card__swiper swiper-container'>
      <div class='swiper-slide'>
        <img class='lazyload-img'
             src="data:image/png;base64,…"                 <!-- 1x1 placeholder -->
             data-src='https://files.glotr.uz/company/…/….webp?_=ozauc'
             alt='Встраиваемый духовой шкаф Artel Art-Classico I6701 Серебристый'>
      …one more .swiper-slide per extra photo…
  <div class='product-card__content'>
    <a href='/vstraivaemiy-…-p-881208/' class='product-card__link …'>Встраиваемый …</a>
    <span class='text-stock text-success'>В наличии</span>
    <div class="price-retail proposal-price"> 2 610 800 <span>сум</span></div>
```

| field | where |
|---|---|
| product id | the `-p-<id>` tail of **either** anchor's href |
| name | the `product-card__link` anchor's text |
| price | `price-retail` block; space thousands separators + the word `сум` |
| availability | the `text-stock` modifier class |
| image | the **first** `.lazyload-img`'s `data-src` (`src` is a 1×1 placeholder) |

---

## 3. TRAP — the photos come before the name

**This is the one that cost us every picture on the site.**

The obvious card boundary is the `product-card__link` anchor, because it is the
only element carrying both the name and the id. Cutting cards there gives each
listing a region that *starts after its own photos and ends inside the next
card's*, so `image_url` is the neighbour's picture. Measured on page 1 of
`/duxovie-shkafi/`: **55 of 55 wrong**, shifted by exactly one card.

Nothing downstream can see it. The URL is well-formed, on the right CDN,
present on every row, and distinct per row. Row counts, attachment rates and
the coverage report are all identical whether the pictures are right or wrong.

**Cut at `<div class='product-card '` instead** — it encloses both halves — and
then read the id **twice**, from the photo anchor and from the name anchor, and
require the two to agree. A card whose ids disagree is dropped, not published:
a markup shift must cost a listing, never mint one pairing a real name with
another product's photograph.

Independent confirmation is available and cheap: each `<img>` carries an `alt`
equal to the product name (glotr blanks `"` and `,` in it). Comparing the two is
evidence that position agreeing with position is not.

---

## 4. TRAP — past its last page, glotr serves other people's goods

`?page=N` beyond the end does **not** return an empty grid, a 404, or a
redirect. It returns **21 unrelated products** under the category's own
heading (`<h1>Духовые шкафы - Страница №6</h1>`) — fridges, water coolers,
sewing machines, a face-recognition controller, an industrial chemical — and a
**different set on every page**, indefinitely.

So "stop when a page adds no product id already seen" never fires. The first
version of this client walked filler until `MAX_PAGES` and filed all of it
under whichever category it had been asked for:

| category | real size | first sweep ingested |
|---|---|---|
| ovens | 240 | **2,739** |
| smartphones | ~2,100 | 2,264 |
| acs | ~3,200 | 2,759 |

**The end signal is glotr's own pager:**

```html
<ul class="pagination-list">
  …
  <li class="next"><a href="/duxovie-shkafi/?page=5" data-page="4">&raquo;</a></li>   <!-- more -->
  <li class="next disabled"><span>&raquo;</span></li>                                 <!-- last page -->
</ul>
<!-- past the end: no <ul class="pagination-list"> at all -->
```

Follow the `next` link's `?page=` and stop when there is not one. Note the
class on the last page is `next disabled`, so matching bare `next` is not
enough — it has to be the one carrying an `<a>`.

The pager is also **windowed**: page 1 shows links to pages 2–5 only. Reading
"5 pages" off it is how this source was first sized at ~1,900 products when it
actually holds ~8,100.

Keep the no-new-ids rule underneath as a backstop, so a change in the pager
markup costs one wasted page rather than a catalogue of someone else's goods.

---

## 5. Availability — glotr's whole vocabulary

Counted live across six category pages:

| badge | text | maps to |
|---|---|---|
| `text-success` | В наличии | `IN_STOCK` |
| `text-warning` | Под заказ | `UNKNOWN` |
| `text-secondary` | Уточнить наличие | `UNKNOWN` |
| *(absent)* | — | `UNKNOWN` |

There is no fourth badge, and **no `text-danger`** — an earlier test asserted
one against a hand-written fixture that invented it.

**Nothing maps to `OUT_OF_STOCK`, and that is the finding rather than an
omission.** A seller who runs out delists rather than marking the card. "Под
заказ" means made to order and "Уточнить наличие" means ask; neither asserts
the item is gone. Reading them as `OUT_OF_STOCK` hid those offers from the
product page entirely, on the strength of a claim glotr never made. `UNKNOWN`
keeps the offer visible while still barring it from setting a headline price,
which only `IN_STOCK` may do.

---

## 6. Category filters — positive keeps, never denylists

A category page holds whatever sellers filed there. On the laptop page: a
perfume, a table, a microscope, a wireless mouse. On the oven page: furniture
repair services. This is not noise around the edges — it is the shape of the
source.

**Every category is judged by a positive keep plus an accessory drop.** A
denylist enumerates what to reject, and on a classifieds board the things to
reject are not enumerable.

Phones were the last category still on the shared `is_phone_listing` denylist,
on the reasoning that the page mixes in tablets and the phone detector already
drops those. Across all 442 names glotr serves under `/telefoni-i-plansheti/`,
that denylist kept **245**, among them:

> a SanDisk microSD card · an HP stylus · a thermal-imaging monocular · a Testo
> pyrometer · an Epson MFP · a GNSS receiver · prayer-bead rings · a pocket hand
> warmer · a patch panel · a kidney supplement · a toy kitchen · bicycle racks ·
> made-to-order shower cabins

The replacement keeps **124** of the same 442, in two measured parts:

1. **the noun rule** — the name announces a handset (`смартфон`, `iphone`,
   `мобильный телефон`, `айфон`) — keeps 120; the 4 it then loses to the
   accessory drop are a 20 W charger, a Borofone holder, a MagSafe station and
   a power bank, all of which name a phone without being one;
2. **the brand-first rule** — a leading phone brand *and* a memory pair
   (`8/256`, `8GB+256GB`, `3+64`) — adds exactly 4 more, every one a real
   handset, and no false positive.

Tablets are named exactly like handsets here — brand, model, memory pair,
colour, no "планшет" anywhere. `Redmi Pad 2 Pro 8/256 Grey` and
`Samsung X216 8/128GB Gray (Tab A9+)` were the only two wrong rows in 828 kept
phones after the first fixed sweep, and `is_phone_listing` catches neither;
`\bpad\b|\btab\b` in the accessory drop does.

Laptops keep their own rule (`_is_laptop`): the word *ноутбук/нетбук/ультрабук/
макбук* **or** a leading laptop brand, minus a long non-laptop drop list —
glotr writes laptop names both as "Ноутбук Acer…" and as bare "ASUS ROG Strix
G16", and a startswith test alone loses 142 real machines.

---

## 7. Measured result (2026-08-11, after the fixes)

- **8,138 offers**, every one with an image, across 7 categories.
- Refused as not-the-category: 917 phones, 333 ACs, 128 laptops, 122 fridges,
  124 washers, 98 TVs, 23 ovens.
- **Zero cross-category leakage**: every kept row in acs/fridges/ovens/
  televisions/washers carries its own category noun. The laptops (142) and
  phones (55) kept without one are the brand-first residues, read individually
  and confirmed.
- 5,436 rows the broken sweep had ingested were deleted from `offer`
  (`raw_offer` left intact — it is the append-only log, and those rows are a
  true record of what was seen). 2,038 products had been taking their headline
  price from one of them.

---

## 8. Prices

Rendered as `7 579 000 <span>сум</span>`. Strip spaces, then run through
`coerce_price` — never trust the raw string. Some cards carry no price at all
("цена по запросу"); they are dropped with a warning, not defaulted.



================================================
FILE: docs/parsers/idea.md
================================================
# idea.uz — REST GET, single card

Reference implementation: [`fill_idea_prices.py`](../../fill_idea_prices.py)

Electronics chain with broad model coverage. The simplest API of the seven — a
single GET, no auth, and it hands you the product URL so you never build one.

---

## 1. Endpoint

```
GET https://api.idea.uz/api/v2/search?search=<text>&quantity=100&type[]=shops&type[]=products
```

No auth.

```http
Accept: application/json
Accept-Language: uz
Origin: https://idea.uz
Referer: https://idea.uz/
User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0 (Windows NT 10.0; Win64; x64) AppleWebKit/537.36 (KHTML, like Gecko) Chrome/150.0.0.0 Safari/537.36
```

`type[]` is a **repeated** parameter, not a comma list. With `requests`:

```python
r = session.get(IDEA_API, params={
    "search": query,
    "quantity": 100,
    "type[]": ["shops", "products"],     # requests expands the list correctly
}, timeout=(15, 60))
```

`quantity` controls result count and is honoured up to about 100 (a live
call for "Samsung" on 2026-07-31 returned 74 products). The single-card
matcher this file originally documented only needed one match per name, so
it used `quantity=20`; the crawler's `sweep()` needs full-catalogue coverage
per brand query and uses `quantity=100` instead. There is no pagination
either way.

## 2. Response shape

```jsonc
{
  "products": [
    {
      // real item from tests/fixtures/sources/idea.json, trimmed
      "id": 10506,
      "name": "Galaxy S25 Ultra 12/256 Titanium Blue",                    // NO BRAND
      "title_name": "Smartfon Samsung Galaxy S25 Ultra 12/256 Titanium Blue",  // brand included
      "slug": "smartfon-samsung-galaxy-s25-ultra-12-256-titanium-blue",
      "url": "https://idea.uz/product/10506-smartfon-samsung-galaxy-s25-ultra-12-256-titanium-blue",
      "current_price": 16199000       // plain SUM
    }
  ],
  "shops": [], "brands": [], "categories": []
}
```

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `current_price` | **use this** — plain SUM |
| `name` | RAM + storage + colour, but **no brand** |
| `title_name` | **use this for the product name** — same text with the brand in front |
| `url` | **absolute product URL, ready to use** |

### 2a. `name` omits the brand; `title_name` does not

This is the single most important field choice on idea. `name` holds the model
half only, with the manufacturer stripped out:

| `name` | `title_name` |
|---|---|
| `VCC 5252 Black` | `Samsung VCC 5252 Black changyutgichi` |
| `Galaxy S25 Ultra 12/256 Titanium Blue` | `Smartfon Samsung Galaxy S25 Ultra 12/256 Titanium Blue` |
| `RT42CB662022WT` | `Sovutgich SAMSUNG RT42CB662022WT` |

Brand is a **hard identity field** downstream: `matching/keys.py::variant_key`
returns `None` without it, so a brandless name is not a slightly worse name —
it is an offer that can never be clustered and disappears from the catalogue
with no error anywhere. Measured on the recorded fixture
(`tests/fixtures/sources/idea.json`), reading `name` gave **0 of 20** keyable
listings; reading `title_name` gives 6 of 20 (14 of 20 recover a brand at all).

`narxnoma/sources/idea.py` therefore uses **`title_name`**, falling back to
`name` only when `title_name` is absent or empty — a brandless name still
beats no listing. The full raw item, `name` included, is kept in
`Listing.raw`.

Caveat, observed on the same recording: for a small number of products idea
stores `title_name` byte-identical to the brandless `name` (ids `10516` and
`10517`, both Galaxy S25 variants), so those stay brandless. Their `slug`
does carry `samsung`, which is a possible future fallback but changes the
client's contract (the slug is derived text, not a stated name).

## 3. Product URL

Take `products[].url` **verbatim**. idea is the only source that returns a complete
absolute URL, so there is nothing to construct and nothing to get wrong.

## 4. Query building

Minimal — strip only the leading `Смартфон`:

```python
def build_query(name_ru):
    return re.sub(r"^\s*Смартфон\s+", "", name_ru).strip()
```

## 4a. Responses mix categories -- `category_id` is the only usable signal

A brand-query search (e.g. `search=Samsung`) does not return only phones. In
a 20-item recording it returned 9 smartphones alongside 7 vacuum cleaners, 3
fridges and a TV, and none of the appliances fall below the price fence, so
nothing filters them out on price alone.

The response's human-readable category fields -- `category`, `category_name`,
`category_slug` -- are `null` or `""` on every observed item, on both phones
and appliances alike. The only field that actually distinguishes them is the
undocumented `category_id`. Observed mappings (from the recorded fixture and
a live "Samsung" sweep, both 2026-07-31):

| `category_id` | Category |
|---|---|
| `55` | Smartphones (e.g. Galaxy S25 Ultra, Galaxy A36) |
| `26` | Vacuum cleaners |
| `24` | Fridges |
| `9` | TVs |

**These ids are undocumented by idea** -- there is no field, header, or docs
page that names them; they were derived purely by observing which products
carried which id. The crawler (`narxnoma/sources/idea.py`) filters `sweep()`
output to `PHONE_CATEGORY_IDS = frozenset({55})` and logs the full category
distribution seen on every sweep, specifically so that a renumbering or a
second phone category being added shows up in the logs instead of silently
emptying or polluting the catalogue.

## 5. Gotchas

- **SKU codes read as model tokens.** idea appends manufacturer part numbers to
  names, e.g. `SM-A376EZADSOZ`. Parsed as a model token, it rejects an otherwise
  perfect match. Skip alphanumeric tokens of length ≥ 5 that mix letters and digits —
  genuine model designators (`a07`, `a17`, `x9d`) are ≤ 4 characters. This was the
  first site where the rule became necessary; it was later back-ported to all others.
- **Clear the cell on a miss.** idea is where the stale-value bug surfaced: an early
  loose pass wrote ~60 values, a later strict pass matched only 54, and the ~11
  orphans silently kept prices from the wrong products. Every filler now writes
  `None` to both the price and the link cell when nothing matches:
  ```python
  if res is None:
      prices_ws.cell(row=row_idx, column=p_col).value = None
      links_ws.cell(row=row_idx, column=l_col).value = None
  ```
- `Accept-Language: uz` is what the site sends; `ru` also works but returns Russian
  names, which changes what the matcher sees. Pick one and keep it stable.
- **A search with no results answers `404`, not an empty list.** Observed live
  2026-08-10 on `search=lg` and `search=hp` — idea sells neither as phones. This
  matters because the category frontier deliberately contains terms only some
  categories match, so a barren query is normal rather than exceptional. `sweep()`
  therefore tolerates a 404 **per query** and continues.

  The tolerance is deliberately narrow: only `404`, and only for one query. A `500`
  or a connection error still raises and still fails the run, because *"every query
  returned nothing"* and *"the source is down"* must not look the same — that is the
  silently-empty-catalogue failure `EXPECTED_MIN_OFFERS` exists to catch. Before this,
  one 404 aborted `sweep()` and cost idea its **entire** category harvest; source
  isolation kept the other six sources alive, which is exactly how it was noticed.

## 4b. The sweep frontier is per category

idea has **no pagination and no catalogue endpoint**, so the query list is not an
optimisation — it is the source's entire reach. Sweeping non-phone categories with
the phone brand list left idea holding 238 phones against **18 non-phone listings in
total**. Non-phone categories now sweep by product noun; see
`narxnoma/sources/brand_queries.py`.

## 6. Result

**54/66 (82 %)**.



================================================
FILE: docs/parsers/mediapark.md
================================================
# mediapark.uz — REST GET, Diginetica autocomplete

Reference implementation: [`fill_mediapark_prices.py`](../../fill_mediapark_prices.py)

Electronics chain. The only source here where the search is an **autocomplete**
endpoint — which shapes everything about how to query it.

---

## 1. Endpoint

```
GET https://api.v2.mediapark.uz/v1/diginetica/autocomplete?st=<text>&lang=ru
```

No auth. `x-device-id` / `x-session-id` are required to be *present* but **any value
works** — they are not credentials:

```http
Accept: */*
Content-Type: application/json
Accept-Language: ru
Origin: https://mediapark.uz
Referer: https://mediapark.uz/
x-device-id: Ywh1FNq17HzyCBJ15BDZl
x-session-id: vzNu2usamNvHs6plQvNO4
User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0 (Windows NT 10.0; Win64; x64) AppleWebKit/537.36 (KHTML, like Gecko) Chrome/150.0.0.0 Safari/537.36
```

Note the query parameter is `st`, not `q` or `search`.

## 2. Response shape

```jsonc
{
  "products": [
    {
      "name": "Смартфон HONOR X9d (8/256) Midnight Black",
      "price": "5299000",                              // STRING, plain SUM
      "linkUrl": "/ru/products/view/honor-x9d-8-128-gb-32417"
    }
  ],
  "suggestions": [...],
  "categories": [...]
}
```

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `price` | plain SUM but delivered as a **string** — cast it |
| `name` | trustworthy: carries RAM, storage and colour |
| `linkUrl` | **relative** path — prefix with `https://mediapark.uz` |

## 3. TRAP — the slug lies, the id doesn't

```
name:    "Смартфон HONOR X9d (8/256) Midnight Black"     ← correct
linkUrl: "/ru/products/view/honor-x9d-8-128-gb-32417"    ← says 8/128 (!)
```

A 256 GB Silver phone can carry a slug reading `...8-128-gb...`. The route resolves
by the **trailing numeric id**, so the link still opens the right product — but the
slug must never be parsed for specs, and never reconstructed.

**Rule: match on `name`, use `linkUrl` verbatim.**

## 4. Query building — the OPPOSITE of beemarket/texnomart

This is the important asymmetry. Because it is an autocomplete ranking engine rather
than a strict AND filter, dropping the RAM half **measurably worsens** recall for
Samsung and Redmi. Keep the name nearly verbatim, including the `6/128` form and the
colour word, so the exact variant ranks into the short list:

```python
def build_query(name_ru):
    q = re.sub(r"^\s*Смартфон\s+", "", name_ru).strip()
    q = re.sub(r"\bGB\b", "", q, flags=re.I)
    return re.sub(r"\s+", " ", q).strip()
```

Do not copy the aggressive `_DROP_FROM_QUERY` approach here — it makes things worse.

## 5. No pagination

Autocomplete returns the top ~5–8 hits in a single call. There is no page parameter.
This is the main reason coverage is the lowest of the seven: if the exact variant
doesn't rank into that short list, it is simply unreachable. It is also why the query
must stay specific.

## 6. Gotchas

- **SKU codes read as model tokens.** mediapark lists the Samsung A07 as
  `"SAMSUNG SM-A075F A07"`. `A075F` parsed as a model token and rejected the match.
  Skip alphanumeric tokens of length ≥ 5 that mix letters and digits — real model
  designators (`a07`, `a17`, `x9d`, `y31d`) are ≤ 4 characters.
- **Don't require `"galaxy"`.** That same listing has no "Galaxy" in it. Model
  identity must come from the `a07`/`a17`/`s26` token, never the series word.
- Headless-browser scraping of this site fails on bot detection — the JSON API is
  the only viable route.

## 7. Result

**46/66 (70 %)** — lowest coverage, entirely due to the no-pagination short list.



================================================
FILE: docs/parsers/olcha.md
================================================
# olcha.uz — REST GET, full-catalogue pull

Reference implementation: [`fill_olcha_prices.py`](../../fill_olcha_prices.py)

The only source fetched by **pulling the whole category once and matching locally**,
rather than one search per phone. That choice is what makes it efficient and gives it
the best coverage of the non-uzum sources.

---

## 1. Why catalogue, not search

olcha's search endpoint caps results per query, so 66 phones would mean 66 ranked,
truncated calls. The catalogue endpoint returns the *entire* smartphone category in
**~23 paged calls** (indicative, not fixed -- the catalogue grows) with no ranking
cap — fewer requests, complete data, and matching happens offline against the full
set.

Use this pattern whenever a site exposes a category listing and the category is small
enough (hundreds, not thousands). It is **not** viable on uzum or alifshop, where the
smartphone category runs to thousands of multi-seller listings.

## 2. Endpoint

```
GET https://mobile.olcha.uz/api/v2/products?page=<n>&per_page=20&category=telefony
```

No auth. Note the `mobile.` host — this is the mobile-app API.

```http
Accept: */*
Accept-Language: ru
clientmodel: web
Origin: https://olcha.uz
Referer: https://olcha.uz/
User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0 (Windows NT 10.0; Win64; x64) AppleWebKit/537.36 (KHTML, like Gecko) Chrome/150.0.0.0 Safari/537.36
```

`clientmodel: web` is required.

> **`per_page` is capped at 20 server-side.** Requesting 100 silently returns 20, so
> the page count is fixed by the category size (~460 phones → ~23 pages as observed
> in a live sweep on 2026-07-31; indicative, not fixed -- it drifts as the catalogue
> changes). Don't try to shortcut it.

## 3. Response shape

```jsonc
{
  "data": {
    "products": [
      {
        "name_ru": "Смартфон Samsung Galaxy A27 5G 8/256GB Pink",
        "discount_price": 3290000,   // current price, PLAIN SUM, arrives as an int
        "total_price": "3590000",    // pre-discount, arrives as a STRING
        "alias": "smartfon-samsung-galaxy-a27-5g-8-256gb-pink",
        "quantity": 12
      }
    ],
    "paginator": {"last_page": 23}
  }
}
```

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `discount_price` | **use this**; fall back to `total_price` when absent/0. Arrives as an **int**. |
| `total_price` | fallback only. Arrives as a **string**, not an int -- do not assume either field's type. |
| `name_ru` | carries RAM + storage + colour |
| `alias` | URL slug |
| `quantity` | stock |
| `paginator.last_page` | loop bound |

> **Both price fields must go through numeric coercion, never used raw.**
> `discount_price` and `total_price` are typed differently by the same
> endpoint on the same item -- a retailer changing a field's JSON type is
> invisible to a status-code check, and code that assumes an int is how a
> whole source silently yields nothing. This is why every price in this
> client passes through `coerce_price()` (`narxnoma/sources/base.py`)
> before a `Listing` is built.

## 4. Product URL — verified

```
https://olcha.uz/ru/product/view/<alias>
```

The intuitive `https://olcha.uz/ru/product/<alias>` **404s**. The `/view/` segment is
required. Discovered from a live category page and confirmed in a browser.

## 5. Fetch-and-cache

The catalogue changes slowly; a 24-hour cache makes reruns instant.

```python
CACHE_PATH = os.path.join("database", "cache", "olcha_telefony.json")
CACHE_TTL  = 24 * 3600

def fetch_catalog(session, refresh=False):
    if not refresh and os.path.exists(CACHE_PATH) \
            and time.time() - os.path.getmtime(CACHE_PATH) < CACHE_TTL:
        return json.load(open(CACHE_PATH, encoding="utf-8"))

    items, page, last_page = [], 1, 1
    while page <= last_page:
        r = session.get(OLCHA_API,
                        params={"page": page, "per_page": 20, "category": "telefony"},
                        timeout=(15, 90))
        data = (r.json() or {}).get("data") or {}
        for p in data.get("products", []):
            price = to_num(p.get("discount_price")) or to_num(p.get("total_price"))
            if not (price and p.get("name_ru") and p.get("alias")):
                continue
            items.append({"price": int(price), "name": p["name_ru"],
                          "url": PRODUCT_BASE.format(alias=p["alias"])})
        last_page = (data.get("paginator") or {}).get("last_page", 1)
        page += 1
        time.sleep(0.3)

    json.dump(items, open(CACHE_PATH, "w", encoding="utf-8"), ensure_ascii=False)
    return items
```

## 6. Matching against a full catalogue

Because you score every phone against ~460 items (indicative, not fixed) rather than
a ranked top-20, ties are common — the catalogue contains both parent rows and
per-variant rows. **Break ties toward the cheapest**, since variation rows carry the
full spec and win on model tokens anyway:

```python
if sc > best_score or (sc == best_score and best and item["price"] < best["price"]):
    best_score, best = sc, item
```

## 7. Gotchas

- `category=telefony` is the slug for smartphones. Other categories use their own
  slugs — check a category page URL.
- Some rows are accessories that slipped into the category; the storage rule filters
  them out.
- Watch out for false alarms when auditing: a match that looks wrong (`A37 Gray`
  matching a `Green` listing) may be correct — the ecom name really is
  `"Samsung Galaxy A37 5G 8 128 GB Awesome Gray Green"`. Always check the raw name
  before "fixing" a matcher.

## 8. Result

**52/66 (79 %)**. Misses are genuine absence (Vivo Y31d isn't stocked; some colours
like Reddish Brown / Ocean Cyan and configs like Honor X5d Plus aren't carried) —
zero matcher failures.

# openshop.uz — REST POST, full-catalogue sweep

Client: [`narxnoma/sources/openshop.py`](../../narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/sources/openshop.py)
Fixture: `tests/fixtures/sources/openshop.json` · Tests: `tests/sources/test_openshop.py`

Single-seller shop (OpenShop, stores in Tashkent). Next.js front end over a Laravel
JSON API. **The whole catalogue — 5,723 products on 2026-10-14 — comes down in ~120
requests (~2.5 min)**, so it is swept and matched locally, never searched per product.

Verified live 2026-10-14 and 2026-10-22: full sweep, 191 matched links each re-read
from the product API and the public page, six opened in a browser; `cli fetch` for
all seven categories; live contract test.

---

## 1. Transport

```
Base: https://web.openshop.uz/api/v1
Every call is POST. Filters go in the QUERY STRING, not the body.
```

```http
Accept: application/json, text/plain, */*
Accept-Language: ru
language: ru            # custom header; without it names come back in Uzbek
Origin: https://openshop.uz
Referer: https://openshop.uz/
```

No auth, no token, no cookies needed. The API advertises `x-ratelimit-limit: 120`
(per minute); the client enforces ≥ 0.55 s between requests on its own
(`MIN_INTERVAL`), independent of `NARXNOMA_MIN_DELAY`.

## 2. Endpoints

| Endpoint | What it is | Use |
|---|---|---|
| `POST /shop/product/get_products?category_id=&page=&per_page=48&sort_by=latest` | the catalogue browse the category pages call | **the sweep** |
| `POST /shop/category/categories_tree`, body `{}` | 3-level category tree with `products_count` | sweep plan, product type |
| `POST /shop/product/get_product?slug=<slug>`, body `{"with_variants": true}` | one product + its variant group | verification |
| `POST /shop/product/search_product?q=<text>` | header search box | humans only — §4 |

`get_products` also accepts `subcategory_id`, `subsubcategory_id`, `brand_id`,
`search`, `price_min`, `price_max`, and `*_slug` forms. They were read out of the
front-end bundle (`buildShopFilterQueryParams` → `/shop/product/get_products?…`),
which is only loaded on a category page — the homepage bundles do not contain it.

Response:

```json
{"data": [ {"id": 133328, "name": "Смартфон Samsung Galaxy A27 5G 8/256GB Синий",
            "slug": "133328-samsung-galaxy-a27-5g-8-256gb-moviy-smartfoni",
            "brand_name": "Samsung", "category_id": 11, "subcategory_id": 4,
            "subsubcategory_id": 20, "is_stock": true,
            "price": {"total": 4268000, "old_price": 4268000, "discount": 0, "currency": "UZS"},
            "link": "https://web.openshop.uz/shop/product/133328-…", "group": {"id": 98, "variants_count": 6},
            "tags": "…", "installment": {…}, … } ],
 "meta": {"current_page": 1, "last_page": 36, "per_page": 12, "total": 432}}
```

> **`/shop/category/<slug>` in the bundle is a front-end ROUTE, not an API path.**
> POSTing it to the API host returns HTTP 200 with an empty `data` — a "success"
> that is really a miss.

## 3. TRAPS in the sweep

1. **`per_page` is capped at 48.** Asking for 100 or 500 silently returns 48 and
   `meta.per_page` says 48. Smaller values are honoured.
2. **Deep paging stops at ~2,400 results per query.** An unfiltered walk reported
   `meta.total = 5731` but repeated earlier products from page 51 on. Walk each of
   the 11 **top-level categories** separately (largest ~1,577) and every product
   comes back. `iter_catalogue()` does this; `iter_raw()` logs a warning whenever a
   query's `total` exceeds the cap.
3. **Only in-stock goods are browsable** (`is_stock: true` on all 5,723). "Not found"
   can mean "sold out".
4. Sweep categories map to `subcategory_id` plus a third-level filter
   (`CATEGORY_SUBSUBCATEGORY_IDS`): TVs 30, ACs 31, fridges 33+34, washers 44+1411,
   ovens 32+1215+1403. **Countertop mini-ovens** sit in 1215 ("Духовые шкафы") and
   are dropped by title — no other source files them under `ovens`.

## 4. Why not the search endpoint

`search_product?q=` is fuzzy over the SEO `tags` field and capped at **60**
results: `q=Honor X9d` returns every Honor phone, tablet, watch and earbud.
`get_products?search=` is paginated but equally fuzzy (the full name of one phone
matches ~500 products). `search()` uses the latter and returns raw listings — pick
the variant by name.

## 5. How openshop tags goods

- **Every colour × memory combination is its own product** (own `id`, `slug`,
  `price`), tied together by `group.id`.
- `get_product` → `variants.axes` lists the attribute chips (`Оперативная память`,
  `Встроенная память`, `Цвет`); `variants.matrix` lists every sibling with its own
  price and stock.
- The category tree has 3 levels; **the third level is the product type**
  (`Беспроводные наушники`, `Утюги`, `Микроволновые печи`), and for phones and
  tablets it is the brand (`1213 Honor`). Restricting candidates to the right
  third-level ids is what keeps "Galaxy Buds 2" off a *case for* the Galaxy Buds 2.
- iPhones come in three SIM variants at different prices — `(SIM + eSIM)`,
  `(eSIM + eSIM)`, `(SIM + SIM)` — plus `Apple Certified Refurbished` listings.
- `badge.supplier` and `badge.edited_at` are **internal** fields (`"public": false`)
  the API leaks: the supplier is sometimes a person's name, `edited_at` a
  days-since-edit counter. Not catalogue data — never surface, store or match on them.

## 6. Price

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `price.total` | **what the customer pays now**, after any discount — the listing price |
| `price.old_price` | struck-through price; equals `total` when not discounted |

Integer UZS. No tiyin, no pre-discount trap (unlike mediapark and radius): confirmed
in a browser on a discounted listing (2 963 000 shown, 3 282 000 struck through).

## 7. Product URL

```
https://openshop.uz/shop/product/<slug>?lang=ru
```

- The item's `link` field points at `web.openshop.uz` — the **API host**, not the shop.
- Without `?lang=ru` the page renders in Uzbek (`Narx: … so'm`).
- The page is server-rendered and its `<title>` states price and memory:
  `Смартфон Honor X9d 5G 8/256GB Midnight Black | Цвет: Чёрный | Память: 8 ГБ / 256 ГБ | Цена: 4 899 000 сум`.
  `page_title(slug)` returns it with the parsed price — an independent second copy
  of the price for verification.
- The page server answers the occasional **502**; retry before calling a link dead.

## 8. Matcher gaps its names exposed

Found by matching a 2,619-name, 118-type assortment against the openshop catalogue
and reading every pair. These are gaps in how `matching/` handles names it was not
measured on; they are **not fixed in `matching/`** (the golden corpus pins phone
behaviour) and were worked around in the caller by rewriting names before
`extract_features`:

| Name | What went wrong | Caller workaround |
|---|---|---|
| `iPhone 17 Pro 256GB … (SIM + eSIM)` | `normalize()` turns `+` followed by a space into a `plus` model token → **every iPhone rejected** | collapse to `SIM+eSIM` |
| `Redmi Note 17 Pro Max 5Г` | Cyrillic `Г` survives homoglyph folding → token `5г` | `5Г` → `5G` |
| `Galaxy Watch 4` vs `Watch 8` | non-phone path drops 1-char tokens, the model digit included → **Watch 4 matched Watch 8** | glue the digit to the word: `watch4` |
| `PRM-20MW/LA1` vs `/LA2` | `head_for_model` cuts at `/` → **LA1/LA3/LA4 all matched LA2** | split `/` into a space instead of cutting |
| `Galaxy Buds 2 Оқ` | Uzbek `қ ў ғ ҳ` escape the `[а-я]+` Cyrillic-word filter → `оқ` became identity | drop words containing any Cyrillic letter (non-phones) |
| `Honor X7e … Lite Blue` | colour "Lite Blue" read as the model word `lite` | `Lite <colour>` → `light` |
| `Galaxy Tab A11+ (WiFi)` vs `(SIM)` | same name, different good, ~22 % apart | treat tablet connectivity as identity |
| `Samsung Buds 3 Pro` | openshop drops `Galaxy` on accessories | `galaxy` is not identity off-phone |

Each is a candidate fix for `matching/`, to be measured against the golden corpus
before it lands.

# radius.uz — Saleor GraphQL, public and introspectable

Client: [`narxnoma/sources/radius.py`](../../narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/sources/radius.py)

The ninth source, added 2026-08-11. Technically the cleanest of the nine and
the smallest by volume — and those two facts are unrelated to its value, which
is that it puts a **second price on 212 products**.

radius.uz runs [Saleor](https://saleor.io/). Its own front end talks to
`https://new.api.radius.uz/graphql/` anonymously: no token, no signing, no
per-brand search, and **introspection is enabled**, so the schema documents
itself. Every field we need is a typed column rather than a string to be
parsed.

---

## 1. Endpoint

```
POST https://new.api.radius.uz/graphql/
```

No auth.

```http
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Accept-Language: ru
Origin: https://radius.uz
Referer: https://radius.uz/
User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0 (Windows NT 10.0; Win64; x64) AppleWebKit/537.36 (KHTML, like Gecko) Chrome/150.0.0.0 Safari/537.36
```

Found by reading their own JS bundles (`/_next/static/chunks/*.js`) for a
`radius.uz` host — the front end is server-rendered, so the call never appears
in the browser's network tab. `api.radius.uz` does not resolve; `new.api.` is
the live one. `GET` on the endpoint serves a GraphQL Playground.

The schema is Saleor's, so the [Saleor API
reference](https://docs.saleor.io/api-reference) applies to anything not
covered here.

## 2. The query

```graphql
query CategoryProducts($slug: String!, $first: Int!, $after: String) {
  category(slug: $slug) {
    slug
    products(first: $first, after: $after) {
      totalCount
      pageInfo { hasNextPage endCursor }
      edges { node {
        id name slug isAvailable
        brand { name }
        category { slug }
        defaultVariant { id }
        variants { id name sku priceAmount currency
                   isAvailable quantityAvailable thumbnail(size: 512) { url } }
        pricing { priceRange { start { amount currency } } }
        thumbnail(size: 512) { url }
      } }
    }
  }
}
```

`Category.products` **already includes descendant categories** — verified:
`konditsionery` reports `totalCount: 17`, which is exactly the sum of its five
brand children. So the sweep asks the parent and gets the brands for free.

Pagination is Saleor's cursor connection: pass `pageInfo.endCursor` back as
`after` while `hasNextPage` is true. There is no page number.

**`Money` here is flat.** `{ amount currency }`, not Saleor's usual
`{ gross { amount } }`. Querying `gross` fails the whole request with
`Cannot query field "gross" on type "Money"`.

## 3. The variant is the offer, not the product

**This is the one decision that matters on this source, and getting it wrong
costs almost the whole phone catalogue.**

radius keeps the product name generic and the identity on the variant:

| | |
|---|---|
| `Product.name` | `Samsung Galaxy A26` |
| `Variant.name` | `Смартфон Samsung Galaxy A26 5G 8/256GB Black (Черный)` |

A phone key needs the memory pair and the colour, and the product name has
neither. Measured: emitting one listing per `Product` attached **1 of 80
phones (1.3%)**. Emitting one per `ProductVariant` attached **168 of 169
(99.4%)**.

Non-phones attached at 100% either way — which is exactly why this had to be
read per category rather than trusted as an average. A single headline
attachment rate would have hidden it.

Each variant also carries its own `priceAmount`, its own stock and its own
working URL, so this is not a modelling preference — a per-product parser
publishes one of "HUAWEI Nova Y61"'s three prices (1,397,000 … 1,959,000) as
the price of all three.

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `variant.id` | **the source key** (decoded, see §4) |
| `variant.name` | **use this for the product name** — carries memory and colour |
| `variant.priceAmount` | **use this** — plain UZS as a float |
| `variant.isAvailable` | stock; falls back to `product.isAvailable` |
| `variant.sku` | the manufacturer code, e.g. `ARTSID2CW12BE` |
| `product.slug` | the URL slug (see §5) |

## 4. Ids are base64 global ids

Saleor returns `id` as base64 `"<Type>:<number>"`:

```
UHJvZHVjdDo1MTk1              -> Product:5195
UHJvZHVjdFZhcmlhbnQ6ODA0Ng==  -> ProductVariant:8046
```

Decode to the number. A malformed id must cost one listing, not the sweep, so
the decoder returns `None` rather than raising.

## 5. The product URL uses the DEFAULT VARIANT id — not the product id

```
https://radius.uz/ru/product/<variant-id>-<product-slug>
```

This is the trap [README rule 1](README.md) exists for. Both ids sit next to
the slug in the payload and both are plausible integers, but:

```
/ru/product/5195-konditsioner-artel-artsid2cw12be-iceberg-inv   404   (product id)
/ru/product/8046-konditsioner-artel-artsid2cw12be-iceberg-inv   200   (variant id)
```

Checked on six products: **every product-id URL 404s.** Verified further that
a *non-default* variant's id also resolves and renders that specific colour,
which is what makes the per-variant URL in §3 legitimate rather than a guess.

## 6. Category slugs — and the Apple trap

Verified live 2026-08-11 by walking the whole tree through
`categories(first: 100, level: 0)` and reading every `totalCount`.

| Our category | radius slugs | Products |
|---|---|---:|
| `smartphones` | `smartfony`, `iphone-17-17-pro-17-pro-max-air`, `iphone-air-3` | 84 |
| `fridges` | `kholodilniki` | 22 |
| `televisions` | `televizory` | 18 |
| `acs` | `konditsionery` | 17 |
| `washers` | `stiralnye-mashiny` | 17 |
| `laptops` | `noutbuki`, `noutbuki-apple` | 16 |
| `ovens` | `gazovye-plity`, `dukhovoi-shkaf` | 13 |

**Not every category hangs where its name suggests.** `noutbuki` holds 9
laptops and not one is an Apple: the 7 MacBooks sit under a top-level **Apple**
category, as do the iPhones, which are absent from `smartfony` entirely.
Sweeping the obvious slug alone silently loses every Apple product — including
the iPhones, the most-compared phones in the catalogue — and the loss looks
exactly like a retailer that stopped stocking them.

The Apple entries are the fragile ones: they are marketing buckets
(`🆕 iPhone 17 | 17 Pro | 17 Pro Max`), renamed as lines launch. There is no
stable "Смартфоны Apple" node. `test_radius_categories.py` pins this map.

Deliberately not swept: `mikrovolnovye-pechi` (9) and `mini-pechi` (8) — the
same decision as [asaxiy](asaxiy.md#5-category-leaves), for the same measured
reason: all 540 `ovens` offers the other sources contribute contain no
microwave, so these could never price-compare.

## 7. GraphQL reports failure with HTTP 200

Same trap as [uzum](uzum.md): an `errors` key beside a `null` `data`, under a
200 status. The client raises on `errors` and on a null `category`, because
swallowing either would record a half-swept category as a successful harvest
of nothing — the one failure mode `EXPECTED_MIN_OFFERS` and `harvest_run.ok`
exist to catch.

## 8. Measured result

274 offers, **98.9% of them attached** to a canonical product.

| Category | Offers | Attached |
|---|---:|---:|
| smartphones | 169 | 99.4% |
| fridges | 22 | 100% |
| televisions | 18 | 100% |
| washers | 17 | 100% |
| acs | 17 | 100% |
| laptops | 17 | 88.2% |
| ovens | 14 | 100% |

**212 of its products are carried by at least one other store, and 54 of them
had only one price before radius arrived.** That is the number a small source
is worth judging on: radius adds 2.2% of the catalogue's offers and 4.8% of
its comparisons.

Its prices sit consistently at the top of the range (it sells on instalment
terms), which is itself useful — a comparison needs both ends.

All 212 multi-store variants were read offer by offer. Every merge is correct,
matched on catalogue codes: `ARTSIQ3BW12BE`, `ARTSID3AW12BE`, `AVLSIQ1AW12BE`.

---

## 9. TRAP — a thumbnail URL for a thumbnail that was never rendered

Saleor returns a `thumbnail(size: N) { url }` for every image, and the field is
non-null and well-formed regardless of whether the file exists. radius's
thumbnailer does not render `.avif` sources: the URL 404s with
`application/xml`.

Sampled across all 274 live radius offers:

| extension | count | thumbnail status |
|---|---|---|
| `.webp` | 218 | 200 |
| `.jpg` | 31 | 200 |
| `.png` | 23 | 200 |
| `.avif` | 2 | **404** |

The original **does** exist — the same path with `/thumbnails/<N>/` replaced by
`/media/` returns `200 image/avif` — so that is what we publish for those two.
Only for avif: `/thumbnails/` is the right thing to hotlink into a grid
everywhere it works, and swapping wholesale would serve full-size originals for
272 products to fix 2.

Worth stating plainly because no upstream check can catch it: "the field is
present" and "the file exists" are different questions, and only the second one
matters to a page that renders the URL.
