# texnomart

## Product-type denylist (sweep-time crawl policy)

texnomart items carry **no category field** — verified against the recorded
fixture, unlike idea (`category_id`) and beemarket (a category endpoint).
The only usable signal is the product-type noun in the name, which is weaker
evidence, so the filter is a **denylist of things that are not phones** (see
`narxnoma/sources/not_a_phone.py`), never an allowlist of phone words: an
allowlist would silently drop any phone whose name lacks the expected noun,
and losing a real phone is worse than admitting an accessory the price fence
and the matcher still get a chance to reject.

Measured on 467 live offers (2026-08-02): **142 (30.4%) are not phones**, and
15 of them had already become canonical products — a tablet carries a brand,
a memory pair and a colour, so it forms a full key exactly as a phone does.
Two false-positive classes were caught and fixed before this rule shipped:

- `Черное стекло` — "стекло" is a screen protector AND a colour; only the
  qualified protector sense counts.
- `USB-адаптер` in a phone's marketing title — "Samsung Galaxy A37 5G: …
  USB-адаптер 8/128+ …" is a real phone with the charger in the box.
  "adapter" is therefore NOT in the denylist: a standalone charger carries
  no memory pair, cannot form a variant key, and can only ever land in
  unresolved_offer. (The previous doc's mitigation belonged to the
  target-driven matcher and does not apply at harvest time.)



================================================
FILE: docs/parsers/uzum.md
================================================
# uzum.uz — GraphQL, multi-seller

Reference implementation: [`fill_uzum_prices.py`](../../fill_uzum_prices.py)

The largest marketplace in Uzbekistan and the richest data source here — it is the
only one that exposes **per-variant (SKU) price, stock and characteristics**, rather
than a single price attached to a name string.

It is also the only one with auth, and the only one where a naive implementation
returns **plausible but wrong numbers**. Read the two trap sections before writing code.

---

## 1. Auth — anonymous, self-minting

`graphql.uzum.uz` returns a bare **401 with an empty body** without a Bearer token.
The token requires no account:

```
POST https://id.uzum.uz/api/auth/token
Accept-Language: ru-RU
Referer: https://uzum.uz/
Body: {}
```

Response is **`204 No Content`** — the token is **not in the body**, it arrives as a
`Set-Cookie: access_token=<JWT>` header (a `refresh_token` is also set, on
`.id.uzum.uz`). This is why an early probe looked like it returned nothing.

TTL **6 hours** (`exp` in the JWT payload), no login, no captcha. Mint a fresh one
per run and refresh ~5 min before `exp`.

### Exactly two headers are required (measured by ablation)

| Header | Required | Omitting it gives |
|---|---|---|
| `Accept-Language` | **yes** (any value: `ru-RU`, `ru`, `en-US` all work) | `400 {"baseError":{"code":"insufficient_headers"}}` |
| `Referer` | **yes**, must be on the `uzum.uz` origin (any path) | `400 {"baseError":{"code":"unallowed_app"}}` |
| `Origin` | no — mints fine without it | — |
| `Accept`, `Content-Type`, `User-Agent`, `x-iid` | no | — |

A foreign `Referer` (e.g. `https://example.com/`) is rejected with `unallowed_app`,
so this is a soft origin check keyed on Referer rather than Origin. Send both plus a
real UA anyway to stay indistinguishable from the site's own traffic.

```python
import uuid, json, base64, requests

UA = ("Mozilla/5.0 (Windows NT 10.0; Win64; x64) AppleWebKit/537.36 "
      "(KHTML, like Gecko) Chrome/150.0.0.0 Safari/537.36")

def mint():
    iid = str(uuid.uuid4())
    s = requests.Session()
    s.post("https://id.uzum.uz/api/auth/token",
           headers={"Accept": "*/*", "Accept-Language": "ru-RU",   # required
                    "Content-Type": "application/json",
                    "Origin": "https://uzum.uz",
                    "Referer": "https://uzum.uz/",                 # required
                    "User-Agent": UA, "x-iid": iid},
           json={}, timeout=25)
    token = next(c.value for c in s.cookies if c.name == "access_token")
    p = token.split(".")[1]; p += "=" * (-len(p) % 4)
    exp = json.loads(base64.urlsafe_b64decode(p))["exp"]
    return s, token, iid, exp
```

## 2. The endpoint

```
POST https://graphql.uzum.uz/
```

Required set, measured by ablation — **four headers**, and two of them are
non-obvious:

| Header | Value | Required |
|---|---|---|
| `Authorization` | `Bearer <token>` | **yes** — bare `401` without |
| `Content-Type` | `application/json` | **yes** |
| `apollographql-client-name` | `web-customers` | **yes** — see below |
| `x-iid` | any UUID (install id) | **yes** — see below |
| `apollographql-client-version` | `1.63.2` | no |
| `city-id` | `1` (Tashkent) | no, but affects stock/delivery |
| `city-latitude` / `city-longitude` | `41.379112` / `69.29944` | no |
| `Origin` / `Referer` / `Accept` / `Accept-Language` / `User-Agent` | — | no |

> **Failure mode you must handle: it does not fail with an HTTP error.**
> Drop `apollographql-client-name` or `x-iid` and the request still returns
> **`200 OK`**, with `data: null` and:
> ```json
> {"errors": [{"message": "HTTP fetch failed from 'search-gateway': 4.."}]}
> ```
> Checking `response.status_code` alone will report success on a request that
> returned nothing. **Always inspect the `errors` key.** This is also true of the
> valid-query-but-null case:
> `{"extensions":{"valueCompletion":[{"message":"Cannot return null for non-nullable field Query.makeSearch"}]}}`
> — which is what a stale variables shape looks like.

Minimal working request: `Authorization` + `Content-Type` +
`apollographql-client-name` + `x-iid`. Send the full browser-like set anyway.

### Persisted queries are NOT enforced

The browser sends an Apollo **persisted query** — a `sha256Hash` instead of query
text, which is why the captured request body is only ~322 bytes. That normally
implies a server-side safelist. **It is not enforced here**: sending full query text
works fine. You do not need to extract hashes.

### Introspection is ON — use it, don't copy old scrapers

```graphql
{ __schema { queryType { name fields { name } } } }
```

works and returns 49 root queries / 428 types. **The query shape used by every
public uzum scraper is stale** — `SortType`, `SearchFilterInput`, and the old
`MakeSearchQueryInput` fields no longer exist. If the schema changes again,
re-introspect rather than guessing:

```graphql
{ __type(name: "MakeSearchQueryInput") {
    inputFields { name type { kind name ofType { kind name } } } } }
```

Note: this server rejects `__type(name: $var)` with a **variable** — inline the
string literal.

## 3. The search query (current, verified)

```graphql
query getMakeSearch($q: MakeSearchQueryInput!) {
  makeSearch(query: $q) {
    total
    items {
      catalogCard {
        __typename
        productId
        title
        category { id title }
        ... on SkuGroupCard {
          skuList {
            id
            sellPrice
            fullPrice
            availableAmount
            characteristicValues { title characteristic { title } }
          }
        }
      }
    }
  }
}
```

Variables:

```json
{"q": {
  "text": "Samsung Galaxy A17 6 128",
  "showAdultContent": "TRUE",
  "filters": [],
  "sort": "BY_RELEVANCE_DESC",
  "pagination": {"offset": 0, "limit": 72}
}}
```

`MakeSearchQueryInput` — required: `showAdultContent`, `filters`, `sort`,
`pagination`. Optional and useful: `categoryId`, `correctQuery`, `getFastCategories`,
`getFastFacets`, `showOnlyOriginalSKUs`.

- `Sort` enum: `BY_RELEVANCE_DESC`, `BY_PRICE_ASC`, `BY_PRICE_DESC`,
  `BY_RATING_*`, `BY_DATE_ADDED_*`, `BY_ORDERS_NUMBER_*`
- `ShowAdultContent` enum: `NONE`, `FALSE`, `TRUE`
- `CatalogCard` is an **interface** implemented by `ProductCard`, `SkuGroupCard`,
  `SkuCard` — `skuList` only exists on the concrete types, hence the inline fragment.

**Pagination caps at `offset` ≈ 1000** (beyond that `items` comes back empty).
`limit` is honoured generously — 72 is a good value, giving ~100 phone SKUs per call.
Per-query search is the right approach; a full-catalogue pull like olcha's is
infeasible because the Android smartphone category runs to thousands of multi-seller
listings.

### Category filter

Relevance search mixes in cases, chargers and (genuinely) vacuum cleaners. Filter
client-side on the card's `category.id`:

| id | title | our category |
|---|---|---|
| `15271` | Смартфоны Android | smartphones |
| `15272` | Смартфоны Apple iPhone (iOS) | smartphones |
| `12601` | Телевизоры | televisions |
| `12520` | Ноутбуки | laptops |
| `14373` | Сплит-системы | acs |
| `82` | Холодильники | fridges |
| `83` | Морозильники | fridges |
| `15291` | Полуавтоматические стиральные машины | washers |
| `15296` | Автоматические стиральные машины | washers |
| `11` | Духовые шкафы | ovens |
| `13` | Газовые плиты | ovens |

`showOnlyOriginalSKUs: "TRUE"` narrows 262 → 60 results but does **not** cleanly
strip grey-market resellers (the "IMEI зарегистрированы" cards survive it). Not a
useful lever — skip it.

### The query frontier is per CATEGORY, not per brand

**Verified live 2026-08-10.** Phones sweep by brand. Nothing else can, and using
the brand list for a category sweep is why uzum held 2,381 phones and **zero**
air conditioners, fridges, washers and ovens:

- the brands that sell white goods here — Artel, Premier, Shivaki, LG, Hisense —
  sell no phones, so they were never in the brand list to be queried;
- and for Samsung, which sells both, relevance ranks phones first while
  pagination caps at `offset` ≈ 1000, so a fridge never surfaces.

Measured, one page of 72 each:

| query | in-category | what else came back |
|---|---|---|
| `televizor` | 72/72 | — |
| `noutbuk` | 72/72 | — |
| `konditsioner` | 72/72 | — |
| `kir yuvish mashinasi` | 72/72 | — |
| `duxovka` | 45/45 | — |
| `холодильник` | 72/72 | — |
| `artel` | **0/72** | remote controls, vacuum filters, electric kettles |

So the noun does the work and brand queries are a supplement, not the frontier.
See `narxnoma/sources/brand_queries.py`.

**Two traps this measurement caught, both of which had silently cost a whole
product type:**

1. `газовая плита` is category **13**, not 11. Gas cookers were being fetched
   and then filtered out, because the config listed only `11` for ovens.
2. `muzlatgich` — the Uzbek for "refrigerator" — resolves to **83
   Морозильники** (freezers) on uzum, not to 82. `холодильник` and `sovutgich`
   are the words that reach 82. Other retailers do use "Muzlatgichi" for a
   fridge, so the word stays in the frontier; it simply must not lead it.

## 4. Response shape

```jsonc
{"data": {"makeSearch": {
  "total": 262,
  "items": [
    {"catalogCard": {
      "__typename": "SkuGroupCard",
      "productId": 1983388,
      "title": "IPhone 17 Pro/Pro Max, 256, 512 , 1 ТБ , 2 ТБ / Оба IMEI зарегистрированы",
      "category": {"id": 15272, "title": "Смартфоны Apple iPhone (iOS)"},
      "skuList": [
        {"id": 7028608, "sellPrice": 20789010, "fullPrice": 31000000,
         "availableAmount": 27,
         "characteristicValues": [
           {"title": "17 Pro Max 256 GB", "characteristic": {"title": "Модель"}},
           {"title": "Серебряный",        "characteristic": {"title": "Цвет"}}]}
      ]}}
  ]}}}
```

- **Price field: `skuList[].sellPrice`** — plain SUM integer, discount already applied.
  `fullPrice` is the struck-through pre-discount price and is often fantasy
  (`31,000,000` above) — never use it.
- `availableAmount` — filter to `> 0`; zero-stock SKUs are phantom listings.
- Card-level `minSellPrice` / `minFullPrice` also exist. **Do not use them** — see below.

> **V1 crawler note (`narxnoma/sources/uzum.py`).** The `availableAmount > 0`
> filter above is matcher-era guidance: it was written for a target-driven
> matcher hunting one specific phone, where a zero-stock SKU is noise to
> screen out. The V1 aggregator crawler is cataloguing every real offer, so
> it deliberately keeps zero-stock SKUs rather than dropping them — recorded
> with `availability="OUT_OF_STOCK"` (derived from `availableAmount`).
> Hiding an out-of-stock offer is a presentation decision that belongs
> downstream, not a reason to lose the observation. See the module
> docstring in `uzum.py` and `test_zero_stock_sku_is_kept_as_out_of_stock`
> in `tests/sources/test_uzum.py`.

## 5. TRAP 1 — card price ≠ product price

Uzum resellers bundle **several models and every storage tier under one card**:

```
"IPhone 17 Pro/Pro Max, 256, 512, 1 ТБ, 2 ТБ"    minSellPrice = 16,985,000
    ├ 17 Pro 256 GB          19,106,010
    ├ 17 Pro Max 256 GB      20,789,010   ← the one we actually sell
    ├ 17 Pro Max 512 GB      24,483,000
    └ 17 Pro Max 1 TB        39,999,000
```

`minSellPrice` — the obvious field, and the one every naive scraper reads — is the
cheapest variant *on the card*. For a 17 Pro Max 256 it understates by **~3.8 M**.

**Match at SKU level.** Build the candidate string from the SKU's own
`characteristicValues`, and use the card title only for brand context.

### 5a. Card titles are marketing blurbs

`"Смартфон Samsung Galaxy A17, 6.7\" Super AMOLED 90Гц экран, камера 50Мп, 5000 мАч"`
— those stray numbers become model tokens and reject every real match. Take the card
title only **up to its first comma**, and let the SKU's `Модель` characteristic
override it entirely when present.

### 5b. The same trap for memory — subtle and costly

```
card title:  "…Samsung Galaxy A17 4G 8/256GB"   ← the card's overall range
sku Память:  "6/128GB"                          ← what THIS sku actually is
```

Concatenating both lets the title's `8/256` satisfy an 8/256 target while the SKU is
really a 6/128 — it reported **2,672,010** for a phone whose true price was
**3,068,010**. When the SKU states its own memory, strip memory figures from the
title before concatenating:

```python
if mem_v:  # sku stated its own memory → it wins outright
    spec = re.sub(r"(?<![a-zа-я])\d{1,4}\s*(?:gb|гб|tb|тб)?\s*[/\\+|]\s*"
                  r"\d{1,4}\s*(?:gb|гб|tb|тб)?", " ", spec, flags=re.I)
    spec = re.sub(r"(?<![a-zа-я\d])\d{1,4}\s*(?:gb|гб|tb|тб)\b", " ", spec, flags=re.I)
```

> **V1 crawler note (`narxnoma/sources/uzum.py`).** The strip above applies
> to the **card title only**. Never run it over the SKU's own `Модель` or
> memory value: those are the trustworthy figures this section exists to
> protect, and stripping them turns `"A16 8/256"` into `"A16"` — a name
> with no storage tier, which the clustering stage cannot key at all.
> `_strip_memory` in the crawler is documented as card-derived-text-only for
> exactly this reason.

> **Measured 2026-08-02 — the `if mem_v:` gate above is not enough.** The
> sample code asks "did the SKU state its own memory?" by testing whether a
> memory *pair* or a *unit-bearing* figure is present. uzum's most common
> shape is neither: `Память: "128"`, `Модель: "17 Pro 256"` — a **bare
> figure**. Both read as *no memory stated*, so the protection in this
> section never engaged and the card's range won anyway, on 130 of 2,376
> live offers. Two separate symptoms, one blind spot:
>
> ```
> card 'Смартфон "Apple iPhone" 12, 64/128/256 ГБ' + Память "128"
>   -> 'Смартфон "Apple iPhone" 12 128 64/128 Алый'   ← 64 becomes a MODEL token
> card 'IPhone 17 Pro / Max 256/512 GB' + Модель "17 Pro 256"
>   -> 'IPhone / Max 256/512 GB 17 Pro 256 Оранжевый' ← keys as 512 GB
> ```
>
> The second merged a 17 Pro 256, a 17 Pro Max 256 and a 17 Pro 512 into one
> variant with one headline price of 18,349,000 against a median of
> 31,000,000 — the incumbent's own defect, different products behind one
> price, reproduced in our data. `_states_memory` now answers the question in
> every shape, and the last-resort card-title recovery is switched off
> entirely whenever the SKU stated anything. **A failure to recognise a
> stated figure is not a licence to substitute the card's range for it.**

### 5c. A RANGE is not a spec

A pair of figures means two different things depending on the figures:

| shape | reading | example |
|---|---|---|
| small + capacity | this SKU's RAM and storage | `4/128`, `12/256` |
| capacity + capacity | the **card's range** across bundled SKUs | `128/256`, `256/512` |

The rule that separates them is whether the smaller figure is a plausible
**RAM** size. A range identifies nothing, so recovering one puts a coin-toss
capacity on a real product; the crawler refuses it and lets the clustering
stage park the offer instead. The single-figure fallback is suppressed in the
same case, or it simply takes one half of the range it just declined.

Two further shapes were leaking figures into identity, both now handled:

- **chained ranges.** `12/256/512` had exactly two figures consumed, leaving
  `512` standing in the head — where the bare-pair scan read the card's 512 as
  the capacity and demoted the SKU's own 256 to a model token. The strip is
  greedy across the whole chain.
- **hyphen separators.** Sellers also write the RAM-ROM pair with a hyphen:
  `POCO C81 Pro (4-64/4-128)` matched no memory pattern at all and was chewed
  into `POCO C81 Pro (4- -128) 4/64`. Catalogue codes stay safe — the `0` of
  `SM-A075F` is preceded by a letter, so no chain can begin inside one.

### 5d. A card lends a SKU the model it is NOT

uzum bundles siblings on one card. `IPhone 17 Pro / Max 256/512 GB` carries
both the Pro and the Pro Max, and the head must supply only the **brand** the
bare `Модель` value lacks. Filtering out the head words the `Модель` repeats
is not sufficient: for the plain `17 Pro 256` SKU the word `Max` was not
repeated, so it survived into the prefix and the offer keyed as a Pro Max.

The head is kept only **up to** the first word the `Модель` value already
states. Once a head word belongs to the model, everything after it is model
territory and the per-SKU `Модель` field owns it — the head is the shared one.

## 6. TRAP 2 — eSIM-only variants

> **V1 crawler note (`narxnoma/sources/uzum.py`).** This whole section is
> matcher-era guidance, written for a target-driven matcher where an
> eSIM-only variant was the wrong answer to a specific query and had to be
> screened out. The V1 aggregator crawler keeps eSIM-only variants — they
> are a legitimate, separate product at a genuinely different price, and
> dropping them loses real offers. Whatever SIM characteristic exists (any
> of the three encodings below) survives verbatim into
> `Listing.raw["characteristic_values"]` for a later clustering stage to
> classify; `is_esim_only`/`sim_kind` (`narxnoma.matching.sim`) are not
> called from the crawler at all. See `test_esim_only_variant_is_kept` in
> `tests/sources/test_uzum.py`.

Our iPhones are **nanoSIM + eSIM**. Uzum lists eSIM-only variants side by side at a
materially lower price:

| variant | price |
|---|---|
| 17 Pro Max 256 GB (dual) | 20,789,010 |
| 17 Pro Max 256 (e-Sim) | 19,453,000 |
| 17 Pro Max 256GB (eSIM) | 18,099,000 |

Encoded **three different ways** — all must be screened:

1. `Модель: "17 Pro Max 256 (e-Sim)"` or `"iPhone 17 Pro Max 256GB (eSIM)"`
2. a SIM characteristic (`Сим-карта`, `SIM карта`, ` SIM карта`) with value
   `e-SIM` / `ESIM` — the physical variant reads `SIM`
3. the **card title** itself, e.g. `"Apple смартфон iPhone 17 Air 256GB, 512GB, 1TB, eSIM"`

Explicit dual markers (`SIM+eSIM`, `nanoSIM+eSIM`, `2 SIM`, `dual`) must **not** be
rejected — check them first. The dual marker appears with `+`, `/` and `-`
separators (`SIM+eSIM` in the raw characteristic value, `nano-SIM/eSIM` on the
rendered spec sheet), so all of them — plus whitespace, `_` and `.` — are
stripped before matching, collapsing every spelling onto one canonical form:

```python
_SEPARATORS = re.compile(r"[\s_.\-/+]")
_DUAL = re.compile(r"simesim|dual|2sim|двесим")
_ESIM = re.compile(r"esim")

def is_esim_only(text):
    low = _SEPARATORS.sub("", str(text).lower())
    if _DUAL.search(low):
        return False
    return bool(_ESIM.search(low))
```

`_DUAL` matches a **bare `dual`**, not just `dualsim`. The marker list above names
`dual` on its own, but the pattern used to require the compound spelling, so
`sim_kind("dual")` returned `"unknown"` — a phone explicitly documented as dual came
back unclassified. The bare alternative subsumes `dualsim`, so that spelling no longer
needs its own branch.

Verify on the rendered page: the product spec sheet shows
`Тип SIM карты : nano-SIM/eSIM` for the dual variant.

### An unmarked phone is dual, not unknown

Because eSIM-only variants are **always** explicitly marked — in all three encodings
above — silence carries information: an unmarked phone is the ordinary dual-SIM one.
`variant_key` therefore normalises `sim_kind="unknown"` to `"dual"` before building the
key.

This is also what keeps `slug()` injective. `slug()` only appends a suffix for
`esim_only`, so `"unknown"` and `"dual"` built two *different* `VariantKey`s that
rendered to the *identical* slug — two canonical products claiming one URL, with
whichever wrote last silently winning. Normalising at key construction collapses them
into the one product they always were. `esim_only` keeps its distinguishing `-esim`
suffix.

`SIM_KINDS` is enforced, not merely declared: `variant_key` raises `ValueError` on
anything outside `("dual", "esim_only", "unknown")`. Without the check a typo such as
`"Dual"` or `"dual_sim"` became part of a canonical identity and minted a second
product for a phone that already had one.

Note the normalisation is deliberately **not** applied to `partial_key`: there,
`"unknown"` still means "not yet known" and is what lets `resolve_partial` match a
partial offer against either SIM variant before refusing on ambiguity.

## 7. Characteristic keys are a typo minefield

Never match characteristic keys exactly. Observed across 66 queries:

`Цвет` · `Память` · `память` · `ПАМЯТЬ` · `Памят:` · `Память:` · `Памяьт:` ·
`памяять` · `Память ` · `Объем памяти` · `Xotirasi` (Uzbek) · `RAM/ROM` · `Модель` ·
`модел` · `Сим-карта` · `SIM карта` · ` SIM карта` (leading space) · `Тип` ·
`16 PRO MAX` (a value used as a key)

Added from the 2026-08-01 live "Samsung" recording: `Xotira` · `Xotira:`
(trailing colon) · `XOTIRA` · **`xotra`** (a typo missing the final `i`) ·
`Rang` (Uzbek for colour).

Classify loosely by prefix:

```python
def char_kind(key):
    k = normalize(key)
    if k.startswith("памя") or k.startswith("xotira") or "объем памяти" in k:
        return "memory"
    if k.startswith("модел"):           return "model"
    if "sim" in k or "сим" in k:        return "sim"
    if k.startswith("цвет") or k.startswith("rang"): return "color"
    return "other"
```

> **Corrected 2026-08-01 (V1 crawler, live "Samsung" recording).** Add
> `Xotira:` (trailing colon — already handled fine by `normalize`, which
> strips punctuation) and, more importantly, **`xotra`** — a typo missing
> the final `i` — to the observed-keys list above. `xotra` does **not**
> match this section's own `startswith("xotira")` sample: `"xotra"` is
> shorter than `"xotira"` and Python's `str.startswith` requires the full
> prefix to be present, so a key that reads `xotra` falls through to
> `"other"` and its memory value is silently lost. `narxnoma/sources/uzum.py`
> uses the shorter `k.startswith("xot")` instead, which catches `Xotirasi`,
> `Xotira`, `Xotira:` and `xotra` alike. If you copy this section's sample
> code elsewhere, use `"xot"`, not `"xotira"`.
>
> **`RAM/ROM` has the same defect, and it is in this section's own list
> above.** `normalize` folds the slash to a space, so the key arrives as
> `ram rom`, which none of the prefix tests in the sample recognise — it
> falls through to `"other"` and the SKU's memory value is silently
> dropped. `narxnoma/sources/uzum.py` additionally treats a bare `ram` or
> `rom` *token* as memory. Match on tokens, not as a substring: a plain
> `"ram" in k` also fires on the Uzbek colour key `Rang`.
> `test_char_kind_classifies_every_observed_key` in
> `tests/sources/test_uzum.py` runs every key named in this section through
> the classifier, so a key added here without a matching rule fails a test
> rather than losing data in production.

## 8. Colours are Russian — and, in card/model strings, Uzbek

> **Corrected 2026-07-26.** The heading used to read "Russian only". That holds for the
> **`Цвет` characteristic**, but *not* for card titles and `Модель` values, which are
> frequently Uzbek: `Smartfon HONOR X7d 6/128GB Tilla`, `Honor X7d 6/128GB Kumush rang`,
> `HONOR X6c smartfoni 6/128 GB Muz`. Because §5 builds the candidate string from the
> card title when the SKU states no `Цвет`, those Uzbek words reach the matcher — and an
> unresolvable colour **rejects** (see below), so every one of them was a silent miss.
> Adding the Uzbek vocabulary took a 20-target benchmark from **5/20 to 15/20 matched**.

```python
UZ_COLORS = {
    "qora": "black", "oq": "white", "kumush": "silver", "kulrang": "gray",
    "tilla": "gold", "oltin": "gold", "sariq": "yellow", "yashil": "green",
    "kok": "blue", "ko'k": "blue", "moviy": "blue", "muz": "blue",
    "binafsha": "purple", "pushti": "pink", "qizil": "red",
    "jigarrang": "brown", "bej": "beige",
}
```

`rang` means "colour", not a colour — put it in `NON_MODEL_TOKENS` or `Kumush rang`
leaves a stray `rang` model token. Same for the listing filler `smartfoni`, `telefon`,
`zarbaga`, `chidamli`. Uzbek qualifiers `to'q` (dark) and `och` (light) must stay
**unmapped**, exactly like `тёмно` / `светло`.

### The `Цвет` characteristic itself

`Цвет` values are always Russian, so an English colour map is useless. All 33 values
observed: Черный, Серый, Тёмно-серый, Серый металлик, Серый меланж, Угольный,
Темно-синий, Синий, Синий меланж, Светло-синий, Голубой, Небесно-голубой, Ледяной,
Бирюзовый, Фиолетовый, Лавандовый, Сиреневый, Серебряный, Зеленый, Тёмно-зелёный,
Мятный, Оранжевый, Манго, Золотой, Песочный, Бежевый, Белый, Молочный, Кремовый,
Бордовый, Коричневый, Алый, Черное стекло.

Map at **token** level after normalising `ё → е` and stripping punctuation, so
`Тёмно-серый` → `{темно, серый}` → gray. Leave qualifier tokens (`тёмно`, `светло`,
`небесно`, `металлик`, `меланж`) unmapped — the base colour word is always present.

**An unmapped colour must reject, not pass.** If a candidate has a `Цвет` value you
cannot resolve, drop it for any colour-bearing target. Treating it as a wildcard let
`Конфетный` (candy) and `Хром` match *both* the Awesome Gray and Awesome Navy targets
simultaneously.

## 9. Product URL

```
https://uzum.uz/ru/product/<productId>?skuId=<skuId>
```

`302`-redirects to the canonical slug URL, so **no slug construction is needed** —
`/ru/product/2996442` lands on `/ru/product/smartfon-samsung-galaxy-2996442`.
`?skuId=` pre-selects the exact variant, which is what makes the link verifiable.

## 10. Query building

Uzum's search is strict AND. Drop colour words **and `5G`/`4G`** from the query —
many seller titles omit the band (`"Samsung Galaxy A37 8/256GB Gray Green"`), so
keeping it silently excludes real listings. Restore precision in the matcher, which
treats `5g`/`4g` as reserved and never scores on them.

Two-shot strategy: broad query first (no colour, no band), then the verbatim name as
a fallback; dedupe candidates by `skuId`.

## 11. Metrics produced

Multi-seller, so a single number hides the spread. Two columns:

- **`uzum min`** — cheapest matching in-stock SKU
- **`uzum median (IQR)`** — median of matching SKUs after IQR stripping

Links sheet gets both URLs (median link = the SKU whose price is nearest the median).
A large min↔median gap is genuine grey-market spread, not an error — Redmi A5 3/64
had sellers at 1.45 M and 3.0 M.

## 12. Result

**61/66 (92 %)** — best of all seven sources. The 5 misses were each verified as
genuine absence: Honor 400 12/256 (uzum stocks only 8/256 and 12/512) and Vivo Y31d
×4 (not listed at all).

---

## 10. Product photos — the field nobody asked for

uzum published **no image at all** for 5,461 offers — the largest source in the
catalogue, every row imageless — for the simple reason that `SEARCH_QUERY` never
requested `photos`. The site's placeholder-heavy look traced back to this one
missing selection.

```graphql
catalogCard {
  ...
  photos { link(trans: PRODUCT_540) { high } }
}
```

Two things about the field are not guessable and were read off the live schema:

1. **`photos.link` requires a `trans:` argument** of type `Transformation!`.
   A bare `link { high }` is a *validation error* — HTTP 400 — not a null.
   Valid values: `ORIGINAL`, `PRODUCT_60`, `PRODUCT_80`, `PRODUCT_120`,
   `PRODUCT_240`, `PRODUCT_24034`, `PRODUCT_480`, `PRODUCT_540`, `PRODUCT_720`,
   `PRODUCT_800`. `PRODUCT_540` is a card-sized render; `ORIGINAL` is a
   full-resolution original and the wrong thing to hotlink into a grid.
2. **`PhotoLink.high` / `.low` are plain URL strings**, not objects.

Yielding e.g.
`https://images.uzum.uz/d9kvjoa1146g73i19b0g/t_product_540_high.jpg`.

**makeSearch returns one card per COLOUR.** A single `productId` comes back as
several cards (16 of 72 items in the recorded fixture) whose `skuList`s differ
only in storage tier. So the photo is accurate to the *colour*, not merely to
the model — which matters here, because colour is part of a phone's identity in
this catalogue and showing the black handset under the green variant would be a
substitution. The first test written for this asserted the opposite ("all SKUs
of one productId share one photo") and failed, which is how the structure was
found.

FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/__init__.py
================================================
"""Narxnoma crawler — electronics price aggregation for Uzbekistan."""



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/cli.py
================================================
"""Command line entry point:
    python -m narxnoma.cli fetch --source SLUG [--category C | --query Q]
    python -m narxnoma.cli harvest [--source SLUG]
    python -m narxnoma.cli report
    python -m narxnoma.cli research run|workbook|check ...   (see narxnoma/research/cli.py)
"""
import argparse
import dataclasses
import json
import logging
import sys

from narxnoma.db import repository
from narxnoma.db.connection import connect
from narxnoma.matching.keys import CATEGORIES
from narxnoma.db.migrate import migrate
from narxnoma.pipeline.aggregate import recompute_variant_prices
from narxnoma.pipeline.backfill import DEFAULT_MAX_QUERIES, backfill_search_only_sources
from narxnoma.pipeline import cluster
from narxnoma.pipeline.cluster import cluster_offers
from narxnoma.pipeline.extract import identify
from narxnoma.pipeline.quality import quality_report
from narxnoma.pipeline.quality import render as render_quality
from narxnoma.pipeline.harvest import HarvestResult, harvest_all
from narxnoma.pipeline.report import SourceCoverage, coverage_report
from narxnoma.sources import SOURCES

# A cycle makes 400+ requests at up to a 90-second read timeout; two of them
# overlapping double our request rate at every retailer at once. The advisory
# lock is session-scoped, so Postgres releases it automatically if the
# process dies however it dies -- no cleanup logic to get wrong.
CYCLE_LOCK_KEY = 20260802


def render_report(rows: list[SourceCoverage]) -> str:
    """Readable coverage table. Suspicious rows get a `*** SUSPICIOUS ***`
    marker so they cannot be skimmed past -- a source that quietly lost most
    of its catalogue is the one failure mode nothing else in this system
    would otherwise surface.

    The marker NAMES the rules that fired, because the three of them call for
    three different responses: "never fetched" means the source has never
    stored anything, "stale" means it stored rows once and stopped refreshing
    them (the upsert-only trap -- the count still looks healthy), and
    "last run failed" means the harvest itself errored. A bare SUSPICIOUS
    would leave an operator to guess which.
    """
    header = (f"{'slug':<10} {'offers':>7} {'floor':>7} {'median':>10} "
              f"{'oldest_seen':<20} {'newest_seen':<20} {'age_h':>7} "
              f"{'last_run_ok':<12} flag")
    lines = [header, "-" * len(header)]
    for r in rows:
        oldest = r.oldest_seen.isoformat(timespec="seconds") if r.oldest_seen else "-"
        newest = r.newest_seen.isoformat(timespec="seconds") if r.newest_seen else "-"
        median = str(r.median_price) if r.median_price is not None else "-"
        age = f"{r.age_hours:.1f}" if r.age_hours is not None else "-"
        last_run_ok = "-" if r.last_run_ok is None else str(r.last_run_ok)
        flag = f"*** SUSPICIOUS: {', '.join(r.reasons)} ***" if r.is_suspicious else ""
        lines.append(f"{r.slug:<10} {r.offers:>7} {r.expected_min:>7} {median:>10} "
                     f"{oldest:<20} {newest:<20} {age:>7} "
                     f"{last_run_ok:<12} {flag}")
    return "\n".join(lines)


def report_exit_code(rows: list[SourceCoverage]) -> int:
    """Non-zero the moment any source is suspicious -- a crawl that
    "succeeded" while losing most of a catalogue is not a success."""
    return 1 if any(r.is_suspicious for r in rows) else 0


def render_status(result: HarvestResult) -> str:
    """Render one of three visibly distinct states for a harvest result.

    `skipped` is checked before `ok` on purpose: a skipped source currently
    also carries ok=True (it isn't a failure), so checking `ok` first would
    render it as plain "ok" -- indistinguishable from a source that
    genuinely swept its whole catalogue. That was the bug: mediapark's
    skip looked identical to a real, successful, zero-listing run.
    """
    if result.skipped:
        return f"SKIPPED ({result.error})"
    if not result.ok:
        return f"FAILED: {result.error}"
    return "ok"


def run_clustering(conn) -> tuple[int, int, int]:
    """reconcile -> extract -> cluster -> aggregate. Returns (attached, created,
    unresolved).

    The reconcile step runs first so that an offer whose name has changed since
    it was attached is back in the unattached pool before clustering loads it.
    Without it a source fix can correct `name_raw` and still leave every
    affected offer sitting on the variant the old, wrong name produced.
    """
    detached = cluster.detach_drifted_offers(conn)
    if detached:
        logging.info("reconciled: detached %d offers whose name no longer matches "
                     "their variant", detached)
    renamed = repository.refresh_display_names(conn)
    if renamed:
        logging.info("reconciled: regenerated %d display names", renamed)
    rows = repository.load_unattached_offers(conn)
    result = cluster_offers(conn, (identify(row) for row in rows))
    # After re-attachment, not before: a variant emptied by the reconcile above
    # is often refilled by the clustering that follows, and deleting it first
    # would churn its id (and its URL) for no reason.
    orphaned = repository.delete_orphan_variants(conn)
    if orphaned:
        logging.info("removed %d variants no offer references any more", orphaned)
    recompute_variant_prices(conn)
    return result.attached, result.created_variants, result.unresolved


def run_backfill(conn) -> int:
    """Query every search-only source for variants the catalogue already has."""
    ids = repository.store_ids(conn)
    written = 0
    with conn.cursor() as cur:
        cur.execute("""
            SELECT v.id, v.display_name
              FROM variant v JOIN variant_price vp ON vp.variant_id = v.id
             ORDER BY vp.store_count DESC, vp.computed_at
             LIMIT %s
        """, (DEFAULT_MAX_QUERIES,))
        variants = cur.fetchall()

    for source in SOURCES.values():
        if source.can_sweep or source.slug not in ids:
            continue
        result = backfill_search_only_sources(conn, source, ids[source.slug], variants)
        logging.info("backfill %-10s queried=%d matched=%d offers=%d",
                     source.slug, result.queried, result.variants_matched,
                     result.offers_written)
        written += result.offers_written
    return written


def _stage(name: str, fn, outcomes: dict) -> None:
    """Run one stage, recording its outcome without letting it kill the rest.

    A clustering failure must not lose the harvest that preceded it: the
    offers are already durably written, and re-running the cycle would just
    re-fetch several thousand listings from retailers for nothing.
    """
    try:
        fn()
        outcomes[name] = True
    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - stage isolation is the point
        logging.exception("stage %s failed", name)
        outcomes[name] = False
        logging.error("%-10s FAILED: %s", name, exc)


def run_fetch(source, category: str, query: str | None, limit: int | None,
              raw: bool, out) -> int:
    """Stream one source's listings as JSON lines, one Listing per line.

    This is the whole engine with the database removed: no Postgres, no
    matcher, no clustering -- exactly what the retailer said, in the shape
    `Listing` guarantees. A search-only source asked to sweep is refused
    here, before any request is made, for the same reason `Source.sweep`
    raises: an empty stream that exits 0 is indistinguishable from an
    empty shop.
    """
    if query is None and not source.can_sweep:
        logging.error("%s cannot be swept (search-only); pass --query", source.slug)
        return 1
    listings = source.search(query) if query is not None else source.sweep(category)
    written = 0
    for listing in listings:
        row = dataclasses.asdict(listing)
        if not raw:
            row.pop("raw")
        out.write(json.dumps(row, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
        written += 1
        if limit is not None and written >= limit:
            break
    logging.info("%-10s wrote %d listings", source.slug, written)
    return 0


def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
    argv = sys.argv[1:] if argv is None else argv
    if argv and argv[0] == "research":
        # database-free, and kept out of the parser below so its options stay in one place
        from narxnoma.research.cli import main as research_main
        return research_main(argv[1:])
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="narxnoma")
    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", required=True)
    fetch = sub.add_parser("fetch", help="print one source's listings as JSON lines; "
                                        "needs no database")
    fetch.add_argument("--source", required=True, choices=sorted(SOURCES))
    fetch.add_argument("--category", default="smartphones", choices=sorted(CATEGORIES),
                       help="catalogue sweep to run (default: smartphones)")
    fetch.add_argument("--query", help="search instead of sweeping; the only way "
                                       "into a search-only source such as mediapark")
    fetch.add_argument("--limit", type=int, help="stop after this many listings")
    fetch.add_argument("--raw", action="store_true",
                       help="include the retailer's untouched payload per row")
    harvest = sub.add_parser("harvest", help="sweep retailers into the database")
    harvest.add_argument("--source", action="append", choices=sorted(SOURCES),
                         help="limit to these sources (repeatable)")
    # One category at a time is how a stalled or newly-configured category gets
    # re-run without re-fetching the other six -- a full sweep is 400+ requests
    # and 8-13 minutes, so re-running everything to fix ovens is wasteful and
    # discourages fixing ovens.
    harvest.add_argument("--category", action="append", choices=sorted(CATEGORIES),
                         help="limit to these categories (repeatable; "
                              "default: every category)")
    sub.add_parser("report", help="print per-source coverage and freshness; "
                                  "exits non-zero if any source is suspicious")
    sub.add_parser("cluster", help="identify offers and attach them to canonical "
                                   "products, refusing anything ambiguous")
    sub.add_parser("aggregate", help="recompute each variant's price rollup")
    sub.add_parser("quality", help="matcher health; exits non-zero if the "
                                   "unresolved rate is above the threshold")
    sub.add_parser("cycle", help="harvest, then cluster, aggregate and report "
                                 "quality, isolating each stage")
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)

    logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO, format="%(asctime)s %(levelname)s %(message)s")

    if args.command == "fetch":
        # JSON lines are meant to be piped into a file; a Windows console
        # defaulting to cp1251 would otherwise raise on the first Uzbek name.
        if hasattr(sys.stdout, "reconfigure"):
            sys.stdout.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8")
        return run_fetch(SOURCES[args.source], args.category, args.query,
                         args.limit, args.raw, sys.stdout)

    if args.command == "report":
        with connect() as conn:
            migrate(conn)
            rows = coverage_report(conn)
        print(render_report(rows))
        return report_exit_code(rows)

    if args.command == "cluster":
        with connect() as conn:
            migrate(conn)
            attached, created, unresolved = run_clustering(conn)
        logging.info("attached=%d new_variants=%d unresolved=%d",
                     attached, created, unresolved)
        return 0

    if args.command == "aggregate":
        with connect() as conn:
            migrate(conn)
            written = recompute_variant_prices(conn)
        logging.info("variant_price rows written: %d", written)
        return 0

    if args.command == "quality":
        with connect() as conn:
            migrate(conn)
            report = quality_report(conn)
        print(render_quality(report))
        return 1 if report.is_alerting else 0

    if args.command == "cycle":
        outcomes: dict[str, bool] = {}
        with connect() as conn:
            migrate(conn)
            if not repository.try_advisory_lock(conn, CYCLE_LOCK_KEY):
                logging.error(
                    "another cycle is already running; refusing to start a "
                    "second one. Two concurrent cycles double our request rate "
                    "at every retailer at once.")
                return 1
            results: list = []
            _stage("harvest", lambda: results.extend(
                harvest_all(conn, [SOURCES[s] for s in sorted(SOURCES)])), outcomes)
            _stage("cluster", lambda: run_clustering(conn), outcomes)
            # Pass B: the sources that cannot be swept are reached by asking
            # about products the catalogue now knows exist. It runs after
            # clustering for that reason, and its results are clustered on the
            # NEXT cycle -- a search hit is a suggestion, not evidence.
            _stage("backfill", lambda: run_backfill(conn), outcomes)
            rows = coverage_report(conn)
            report = quality_report(conn)

        for r in results:
            logging.info("%-10s seen=%-6d kept=%-6d %s",
                         r.slug, r.seen, r.kept, render_status(r))
        print(render_report(rows))
        print()
        print(render_quality(report))

        # The exit code reflects the worst outcome across the whole cycle.
        failed_stage = not all(outcomes.values())
        failed_source = any(not r.ok and not r.skipped for r in results)
        return 1 if (failed_stage or failed_source
                     or report_exit_code(rows) or report.is_alerting) else 0

    # harvest
    chosen = [SOURCES[s] for s in (args.source or sorted(SOURCES))]

    with connect() as conn:
        migrate(conn)
        results = harvest_all(conn, chosen, categories=args.category)
        rows = coverage_report(conn)

    for r in results:
        logging.info("%-10s seen=%-6d kept=%-6d %s", r.slug, r.seen, r.kept, render_status(r))
    print(render_report(rows))
    return 0 if all(r.ok for r in results) and report_exit_code(rows) == 0 else 1


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/config.py
================================================
"""Environment-driven settings. Import this, never os.environ directly."""
import os
from dataclasses import dataclass

USER_AGENT = (
    "Mozilla/5.0 (Windows NT 10.0; Win64; x64) AppleWebKit/537.36 "
    "(KHTML, like Gecko) Chrome/150.0.0.0 Safari/537.36"
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Settings:
    dsn: str
    user_agent: str = USER_AGENT
    min_delay: float = 0.3
    max_delay: float = 0.6
    connect_timeout: int = 15
    read_timeout: int = 90


def load_settings() -> Settings:
    return Settings(
        dsn=os.environ.get("NARXNOMA_DSN", "postgresql://narxnoma:narxnoma@localhost:5432/narxnoma"),
        min_delay=float(os.environ.get("NARXNOMA_MIN_DELAY", "0.3")),
        max_delay=float(os.environ.get("NARXNOMA_MAX_DELAY", "0.6")),
    )



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/daily.py
================================================
"""Unattended daily jobs: nightly price refresh, morning market report to Telegram.

Runs on the owner's own machine from the OS scheduler, with no Claude session involved.

    python -m narxnoma.daily refresh            03:00  re-sweep every store into the cache
    python -m narxnoma.daily report             09:00  catalogue -> research workbook -> Telegram
    python -m narxnoma.daily probe              show the shape of the catalogue response (no values)
    python -m narxnoma.daily report --no-send   build the file, do not send

Everything private lives in an env file OUTSIDE the repo (default `~/.narxnoma-daily.env`,
override with `--env-file` or `NARXNOMA_DAILY_ENV`); see docs/daily-report.md. Nothing about
the catalogue API (URL, token, field names) is hard-coded here.
"""

import argparse
import csv
import json
import logging
import os
import shutil
import sys
import tempfile
import time
from datetime import date
from pathlib import Path

import requests

logger = logging.getLogger("narxnoma.daily")

DEFAULT_ENV = Path.home() / ".narxnoma-daily.env"
LIST_KEYS = ("items", "results", "data", "products", "rows")
NAME_FIELDS = ("name", "title", "product_name")
PRICE_FIELDS = ("price", "sale_price", "retail_price", "current_price")
CATEGORY_FIELDS = ("category", "category_name", "category.name", "category_title")
ACTIVE_FIELDS = ("is_active", "active", "published", "is_published")
MAX_PAGES = 500


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- config


def load_env(path: str | Path | None) -> dict:
    """KEY=VALUE lines (quotes and `#` comments allowed) layered under the process env."""
    cfg: dict = {}
    p = Path(path) if path else DEFAULT_ENV
    if p.exists():
        for line in p.read_text(encoding="utf-8-sig").splitlines():
            line = line.strip()
            if not line or line.startswith("#") or "=" not in line:
                continue
            k, v = line.split("=", 1)
            cfg[k.strip()] = _env_value(v)
    for k, v in os.environ.items():
        if k.startswith(("ECOM_", "TG_", "NARXNOMA_")):
            cfg[k] = v
    return cfg


def _env_value(v: str) -> str:
    """A quoted value is taken as is; an unquoted one ends at ` #` (an inline comment), so
    `TG_SESSION=C:\\x\\s   # note` is the path, not the path plus the note."""
    v = v.strip()
    if v[:1] in ("'", '"') and v.count(v[0]) >= 2:
        return v[1 : v.index(v[0], 1)]
    for sep in (" #", "\t#"):
        if sep in v:
            v = v[: v.index(sep)]
    return v.strip()


def telegram_problem(cfg: dict) -> str | None:
    """Why a send cannot work, or None. Checked before the report does any work: a bad session
    path also breaks the failure notice, so without this check nothing would arrive and the
    only trace would be report.log."""
    missing = [k for k in ("TG_SESSION", "TG_API_ID", "TG_API_HASH") if not cfg.get(k)]
    if missing:
        return f"missing in the env file: {', '.join(missing)}"
    if not str(cfg["TG_API_ID"]).isdigit():
        return "TG_API_ID must be a number"
    if not _session_path(cfg).exists():
        return f"Telegram session file not found: {_session_path(cfg)}"
    try:
        import telethon  # noqa: F401
    except ImportError:
        return "telethon is not installed (pip install telethon)"
    return None


def _session_path(cfg: dict) -> Path:
    s = Path(cfg["TG_SESSION"])  # Telethon takes it with or without ".session"
    return s if s.suffix == ".session" else s.with_suffix(".session")


def read_token(cfg: dict) -> str:
    """The bearer token is rotated by another job, so it is read fresh at every run.
    `ECOM_TOKEN_FILE` may be plain text, a JSON object, or a KEY=VALUE env file."""
    if cfg.get("ECOM_TOKEN_FILE"):
        raw = Path(cfg["ECOM_TOKEN_FILE"]).read_text(encoding="utf-8-sig").strip()
        if raw.startswith("{"):
            obj = json.loads(raw)
            for k in ("access_token", "token", "bearer", "ECOM_TOKEN"):
                if obj.get(k):
                    return str(obj[k]).strip()
            raise ValueError("token file is JSON but has no access_token/token key")
        if "=" in raw.splitlines()[0]:
            for line in raw.splitlines():
                k, _, v = line.partition("=")
                if k.strip() in ("ECOM_TOKEN", "TOKEN", "ACCESS_TOKEN"):
                    return v.strip().strip("'\"")
            raise ValueError("token file has no ECOM_TOKEN= line")
        return raw.removeprefix("Bearer ").strip()
    if cfg.get("ECOM_TOKEN"):
        return cfg["ECOM_TOKEN"].removeprefix("Bearer ").strip()
    raise ValueError("set ECOM_TOKEN_FILE (preferred) or ECOM_TOKEN")


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- catalogue


def dig(obj, path: str):
    """`a.b.c` lookup through dicts; None when any step is missing."""
    for part in path.split("."):
        if not isinstance(obj, dict) or part not in obj:
            return None
        obj = obj[part]
    return obj


def first_field(obj: dict, candidates) -> object:
    for c in candidates:
        v = dig(obj, c)
        if v not in (None, ""):
            return v
    return None


def extract_items(payload) -> list:
    """The product list inside one page, whatever envelope it comes in."""
    if isinstance(payload, list):
        return payload
    if isinstance(payload, dict):
        for k in LIST_KEYS:
            if isinstance(payload.get(k), list):
                return payload[k]
        for v in payload.values():  # {"data": {"items": [...]}}
            if isinstance(v, dict):
                got = extract_items(v)
                if got:
                    return got
    return []


def fetch_catalogue(cfg: dict, session: requests.Session | None = None) -> list[dict]:
    """Walk the paginated admin endpoint and return every raw product dict."""
    url = cfg.get("ECOM_PRODUCTS_URL")
    if not url:
        raise ValueError("set ECOM_PRODUCTS_URL")
    size = int(cfg.get("ECOM_PAGE_SIZE", 100))
    params = dict(p.split("=", 1) for p in cfg.get("ECOM_EXTRA_PARAMS", "").split("&") if "=" in p)
    sess = session or requests.Session()
    headers = {"Authorization": f"Bearer {read_token(cfg)}", "Accept": "application/json"}
    pause = float(cfg.get("ECOM_PAUSE_S", 0.3))
    out: list[dict] = []
    seen_first = None
    for page in range(1, MAX_PAGES + 1):
        r = sess.get(
            url, params={**params, "page": page, "page_size": size}, headers=headers, timeout=60
        )
        if r.status_code in (401, 403):
            raise PermissionError(
                f"catalogue API said {r.status_code}: the bearer token is expired or wrong"
            )
        r.raise_for_status()
        items = extract_items(r.json())
        if not items:
            break
        marker = json.dumps(items[0], sort_keys=True, default=str)
        if marker == seen_first:  # a server that ignores `page` would loop forever
            break
        seen_first = seen_first or marker
        out.extend(items)
        if len(items) < size:
            break
        time.sleep(pause)
    else:
        raise RuntimeError(f"more than {MAX_PAGES} pages; refusing to continue")
    return out


def to_targets(items: list[dict], cfg: dict) -> tuple[list[dict], dict]:
    """-> (rows for the research input file, stats). Rows without a name or a price are dropped
    and counted; inactive ones too when ECOM_ONLY_ACTIVE (default on)."""
    f_name = [cfg["ECOM_FIELD_NAME"]] if cfg.get("ECOM_FIELD_NAME") else NAME_FIELDS
    f_price = [cfg["ECOM_FIELD_PRICE"]] if cfg.get("ECOM_FIELD_PRICE") else PRICE_FIELDS
    f_cat = [cfg["ECOM_FIELD_CATEGORY"]] if cfg.get("ECOM_FIELD_CATEGORY") else CATEGORY_FIELDS
    f_act = [cfg["ECOM_FIELD_ACTIVE"]] if cfg.get("ECOM_FIELD_ACTIVE") else ACTIVE_FIELDS
    only_active = cfg.get("ECOM_ONLY_ACTIVE", "1") not in ("0", "false", "no")
    stats = {"seen": len(items), "inactive": 0, "no_name": 0, "no_price": 0, "no_category": 0}
    rows = []
    for it in items:
        if only_active:
            flag = first_field(it, f_act)
            if flag is not None and not _truthy(flag):
                stats["inactive"] += 1
                continue
        name = first_field(it, f_name)
        if not name:
            stats["no_name"] += 1
            continue
        price = _num(first_field(it, f_price))
        if not price:
            stats["no_price"] += 1
            continue
        cat = first_field(it, f_cat)
        cat = cat.get("name") if isinstance(cat, dict) else cat
        if not cat:
            stats["no_category"] += 1
        rows.append({"name": str(name).strip(), "our price": price, "category": cat or ""})
    stats["kept"] = len(rows)
    return rows, stats


def _truthy(v) -> bool:
    if isinstance(v, str):
        return v.strip().lower() not in ("0", "false", "no", "inactive", "")
    return bool(v)


def _num(v) -> float | None:
    if v is None or isinstance(v, bool):
        return None
    try:
        return float(str(v).replace(" ", "").replace(",", "."))
    except ValueError:
        return None


def write_targets(rows: list[dict], path: Path) -> None:
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    with path.open("w", newline="", encoding="utf-8-sig") as fh:
        w = csv.DictWriter(fh, fieldnames=["name", "our price", "category"])
        w.writeheader()
        w.writerows(rows)


def shape(items: list[dict]) -> dict:
    """Field names and value types of the first product, never the values (safe to paste)."""
    if not items:
        return {}

    def walk(o, prefix=""):
        out = {}
        for k, v in o.items():
            if isinstance(v, dict):
                out.update(walk(v, f"{prefix}{k}."))
            else:
                out[f"{prefix}{k}"] = type(v).__name__
        return out

    return walk(items[0])


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- locking


class Lock:
    """One job at a time: `refresh` (03:00) and `report` (09:00) never run on the same cache
    together. A lock older than `stale_h` is taken over (a crashed run must not block forever)."""

    def __init__(self, path: Path, stale_h: float = 6):
        self.path, self.stale_h = path, stale_h

    def acquire(self, wait_s: float = 0) -> bool:
        deadline = time.time() + wait_s
        while True:
            try:
                self.path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
                fd = os.open(self.path, os.O_CREAT | os.O_EXCL | os.O_WRONLY)
                os.write(fd, str(os.getpid()).encode())
                os.close(fd)
                return True
            except FileExistsError:
                if time.time() - self.path.stat().st_mtime > self.stale_h * 3600:
                    logger.warning("taking over a stale lock %s", self.path)
                    self.path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
                    continue
                if time.time() >= deadline:
                    return False
                time.sleep(15)

    def release(self) -> None:
        self.path.unlink(missing_ok=True)


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- telegram


def send_telegram(cfg: dict, path: Path, caption: str) -> None:
    """Send a file to Saved Messages through the owner's existing Telegram user session.

    The listener that owns the session keeps it open, so the send works on a temporary COPY of
    the session file, deletes it afterwards, and never logs in (a login would invalidate the
    listener's authorization). API id/hash come from the env file only at send time."""
    try:
        from telethon.sync import TelegramClient
    except ImportError as exc:  # pragma: no cover - environment dependent
        raise RuntimeError("pip install telethon to send to Telegram") from exc
    src = _session_path(cfg)
    if not src.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError(f"Telegram session file not found: {src}")
    tmpdir = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="narxnoma_tg_"))
    try:
        copy = tmpdir / "send.session"
        shutil.copyfile(src, copy)
        client = TelegramClient(
            str(copy.with_suffix("")), int(cfg["TG_API_ID"]), cfg["TG_API_HASH"]
        )
        client.connect()
        try:
            if not client.is_user_authorized():
                raise RuntimeError("Telegram session is not authorised; log it in once by hand")
            client.send_file("me", str(path), caption=caption)
        finally:
            client.disconnect()
    finally:
        shutil.rmtree(tmpdir, ignore_errors=True)


def send_text(cfg: dict, text: str) -> None:
    """Failure notices go to the same place; a silent failure is worse than a noisy one."""
    try:
        from telethon.sync import TelegramClient
    except ImportError:  # pragma: no cover
        return
    tmpdir = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="narxnoma_tg_"))
    try:
        shutil.copyfile(_session_path(cfg), tmpdir / "send.session")
        client = TelegramClient(str(tmpdir / "send"), int(cfg["TG_API_ID"]), cfg["TG_API_HASH"])
        client.connect()
        try:
            if client.is_user_authorized():
                client.send_message("me", text)
        finally:
            client.disconnect()
    except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 -- a failed notice must not mask the original error
        logger.exception("could not send the failure notice")
    finally:
        shutil.rmtree(tmpdir, ignore_errors=True)


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- jobs


def work_dir(cfg: dict) -> Path:
    return Path(cfg.get("NARXNOMA_DAILY_DIR", Path.home() / "narxnoma-daily"))


def cache_dir(cfg: dict) -> Path:
    return work_dir(cfg) / "research_cache"


def job_refresh(cfg: dict) -> int:
    from narxnoma.matching.keys import CATEGORIES
    from narxnoma.research.collect import default_markets, refresh_cache

    lock = Lock(work_dir(cfg) / "daily.lock")
    if not lock.acquire(wait_s=0):
        logger.error("another daily job holds the lock; refresh skipped")
        return 2
    try:
        res = refresh_cache(default_markets(), sorted(CATEGORIES), cache_dir(cfg))
    finally:
        lock.release()
    logger.info("refresh: %d sweeps ok, %d failed", len(res["ok"]), len(res["failed"]))
    if res["failed"] and cfg.get("TG_SESSION"):
        lines = [f"{s}/{c}: {why[:80]}" for s, c, why in res["failed"][:12]]
        send_text(cfg, "narxnoma 03:00 refresh: failures\n" + "\n".join(lines))
    return 1 if res["failed"] and not res["ok"] else 0


def job_report(cfg: dict, send: bool = True, wait_lock_min: float = 90) -> int:
    from narxnoma.research.build import make_workbook
    from narxnoma.research.run import run_research
    from narxnoma.research.targets import load_targets

    out_dir = work_dir(cfg) / "reports"
    stamp = date.today().isoformat()
    if send and (why := telegram_problem(cfg)):
        logger.error("report not started, Telegram cannot send: %s", why)
        return 1
    lock = Lock(work_dir(cfg) / "daily.lock")
    # the nightly refresh may still be running on a slow night: wait for it, do not race it
    if not lock.acquire(wait_s=wait_lock_min * 60):
        logger.error("the refresh is still running after %s min; report skipped", wait_lock_min)
        if send and cfg.get("TG_SESSION"):
            send_text(
                cfg, f"narxnoma {stamp}: the 03:00 refresh was still running, no report today"
            )
        return 2
    try:
        items = fetch_catalogue(cfg)
        rows, stats = to_targets(items, cfg)
        logger.info("catalogue: %s", stats)
        if not rows:
            raise RuntimeError(f"no usable products in the catalogue response ({stats})")
        targets_path = out_dir / f"products_{stamp}.csv"
        write_targets(rows, targets_path)
        targets = load_targets(targets_path)
        research = run_research(
            targets,
            None,
            cache_dir=cache_dir(cfg),
            max_age_h=float(cfg.get("NARXNOMA_MAX_AGE_H", 30)),
            force=False,
            title=f"Market analysis {stamp}",
        )
        xlsx = out_dir / f"market_analysis_{stamp}.xlsx"
        research.to_json(xlsx.with_suffix(".json"))
        info = make_workbook(research, xlsx, pivots="auto", model="auto", verify=False)
    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 -- always tell the owner, then fail loudly
        logger.exception("report failed")
        if send and cfg.get("TG_SESSION"):
            send_text(
                cfg, f"narxnoma {stamp}: report FAILED - {type(exc).__name__}: {str(exc)[:300]}"
            )
        return 1
    finally:
        lock.release()

    caption = (
        f"Market analysis {stamp}: {stats['kept']} products, "
        f"{info['layout']['n_offers']} offers"
        + (
            f"; {stats['no_category']} without a category were not matched"
            if stats["no_category"]
            else ""
        )
    )
    logger.info("wrote %s", xlsx)
    if send:
        # the cube-status job also sends around 09:00 from a copy of the same session; if the
        # two collide Telegram may refuse one of them, so wait and try again instead of failing
        for attempt in range(1, 5):
            try:
                send_telegram(cfg, xlsx, caption)
                break
            except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001
                logger.exception("Telegram send failed (attempt %d/4)", attempt)
                if attempt == 4:
                    return 1
                time.sleep(90)
        logger.info("sent to Telegram Saved Messages")
    return 0


def job_probe(cfg: dict) -> int:
    items = fetch_catalogue({**cfg, "ECOM_PAGE_SIZE": "5"})
    print(json.dumps(shape(items), indent=2, ensure_ascii=False))
    rows, stats = to_targets(items[:5], cfg)
    print("with the current field mapping:", stats)
    return 0


def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        prog="narxnoma.daily",
        description=__doc__,
        formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter,
    )
    p.add_argument("job", choices=["refresh", "report", "probe"])
    p.add_argument("--env-file", default=os.environ.get("NARXNOMA_DAILY_ENV"))
    p.add_argument("--no-send", action="store_true", help="report: build the file, skip Telegram")
    args = p.parse_args(argv)
    cfg = load_env(args.env_file)
    wd = work_dir(cfg)
    wd.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    logging.basicConfig(
        level=logging.INFO,
        format="%(asctime)s %(levelname)s %(message)s",
        handlers=[
            logging.StreamHandler(),
            logging.FileHandler(wd / f"{args.job}.log", encoding="utf-8"),
        ],
    )
    if hasattr(sys.stdout, "reconfigure"):
        sys.stdout.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8")
    if args.job == "refresh":
        return job_refresh(cfg)
    if args.job == "probe":
        return job_probe(cfg)
    return job_report(cfg, send=not args.no_send)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
