"""PostgreSQL access. The only module that knows SQL."""



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/db/connection.py
================================================
import contextlib
from typing import Iterator

import psycopg

from narxnoma.config import load_settings


@contextlib.contextmanager
def connect(dsn: str | None = None) -> Iterator[psycopg.Connection]:
    """Open a connection that COMMITS on clean exit and rolls back on error.

    The commit is not optional and its absence was a real, silent bug. psycopg
    opens an implicit transaction on the first statement -- including a plain
    read, such as the catalogue load every pipeline stage begins with. Once
    that transaction is open, the repository's own `with conn.transaction()`
    blocks nest inside it as savepoints, which release without committing. So
    a `close()` with no commit rolled back everything the process had done.

    The symptom was as quiet as it gets: `recompute_variant_prices` reported
    "recomputed prices for 1380 variants", every log line looked healthy, the
    exit code was whatever the reports said -- and the database was untouched.
    """
    target = dsn or load_settings().dsn
    conn = psycopg.connect(target)
    try:
        yield conn
        conn.commit()
    except BaseException:
        conn.rollback()
        raise
    finally:
        conn.close()



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/db/migrate.py
================================================
import pathlib

import psycopg

SCHEMA = pathlib.Path(__file__).with_name("schema.sql")


def migrate(conn: psycopg.Connection) -> None:
    """Apply schema.sql. Idempotent by construction."""
    with conn.cursor() as cur:
        cur.execute(SCHEMA.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    conn.commit()



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/db/repository.py
================================================
"""The only place that writes offers. Keeps SQL out of the pipeline.

Commit boundary: each function below commits its own transaction
independently. start_run, the data writes (record_raw_offers /
upsert_offers) and finish_run are deliberately *not* one unit of work.
For a long-running crawl that is the right choice -- if a source dies half
way through a sweep, the listings already written should stay written
rather than being rolled back along with everything else, and start_run,
finish_run and the upsert are all idempotent enough that re-running a
failed step is safe.
"""
import functools
import json
from typing import Any, Iterable
from uuid import UUID

import psycopg
from psycopg.types.json import Jsonb

from narxnoma.matching import VariantKey, display_name, slug
from narxnoma.sources.base import Listing


def _scrub_nuls(value: Any) -> Any:
    """Recursively strip NUL (\\u0000) characters from strings nested in
    dicts, lists and scalars. \\u0000 is valid JSON but PostgreSQL's jsonb
    type rejects it outright with error 22P05, and retailer payloads --
    parsed straight from their API JSON -- occasionally contain it."""
    if isinstance(value, str):
        return value.replace("\x00", "")
    if isinstance(value, dict):
        return {k: _scrub_nuls(v) for k, v in value.items()}
    if isinstance(value, list):
        return [_scrub_nuls(v) for v in value]
    return value


# default=str: a retailer payload can contain a value json.dumps cannot
# serialise on its own (e.g. something that survived parsing as a non-JSON
# type). Rather than raising a TypeError mid-batch and aborting every row,
# such a value is degraded to its str() form.
_dump_json = functools.partial(json.dumps, ensure_ascii=False, default=str)


def store_ids(conn: psycopg.Connection) -> dict[str, UUID]:
    # Every function in this module wraps its body in conn.transaction(),
    # which commits on success and rolls back on error. Without this, a
    # failed statement leaves the connection in psycopg's "aborted
    # transaction" state, and every subsequent call on that connection
    # raises InFailedSqlTransaction -- including the finish_run(ok=False,
    # ...) call a caller makes to record the very failure that just
    # happened. The rollback here is what keeps the connection usable for
    # that call.
    with conn.transaction():
        with conn.cursor() as cur:
            cur.execute("SELECT slug, id FROM store WHERE is_active")
            return {slug: sid for slug, sid in cur.fetchall()}


def record_raw_offers(conn: psycopg.Connection, store_id: UUID,
                      listings: Iterable[Listing]) -> int:
    """Append one row per observation. Never updates -- this is the permanent
    log price history is later derived from, not a cache of current state."""
    rows = [
        (store_id, listing.source_key, listing.name_raw, listing.price, listing.url,
         listing.availability, listing.image_url, listing.category,
         Jsonb(_scrub_nuls(listing.raw), dumps=_dump_json))
        for listing in listings
    ]
    if not rows:
        return 0
    with conn.transaction():
        with conn.cursor() as cur:
            cur.executemany(
                "INSERT INTO raw_offer"
                " (store_id, source_key, name_raw, price, url, availability, image_url, category, payload)"
                " VALUES (%s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s)", rows)
    return len(rows)


def upsert_offers(conn: psycopg.Connection, store_id: UUID,
                  listings: Iterable[Listing]) -> tuple[int, int]:
    """Insert new listings, refresh existing ones. first_seen never moves.

    RETURNING always yields the *new* row version, whether the INSERT or the
    ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE branch fired. Its xmax is 0 (true) when INSERT
    fired -- a brand-new row has never been locked by anyone. It is
    non-zero (false) when DO UPDATE fired instead: Postgres locks the
    conflicting row first while evaluating ON CONFLICT, and that locker's
    transaction id is carried forward onto the new row version's xmax. This
    is an undocumented detail of how ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE is implemented,
    not a guarantee made by the PostgreSQL documentation -- re-verify it
    against the release notes before relying on it across a major version
    upgrade. first_seen is omitted from the DO UPDATE SET list entirely, so
    on conflict it keeps its original DEFAULT now() value from the first
    insert.

    Deliberately not batched into a single multi-row VALUES statement: with
    ON CONFLICT, one statement touching the same conflict key twice raises
    "cannot affect row a second time", which a buggy client sweep could
    trigger. The per-row loop tolerates that; a batched statement would not.
    """
    inserted = updated = 0
    with conn.transaction():
        with conn.cursor() as cur:
            for listing in listings:
                cur.execute(
                                    "INSERT INTO offer"
                                    " (store_id, source_key, name_raw, price, url, availability, image_url, category)"
                                    " VALUES (%s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s)"
                                    " ON CONFLICT (store_id, source_key) DO UPDATE SET"
                                    "   name_raw = EXCLUDED.name_raw,"
                                    "   price = EXCLUDED.price,"
                                    "   url = EXCLUDED.url,"
                                    "   availability = EXCLUDED.availability,"
                                    "   image_url = EXCLUDED.image_url,"
                                    "   category = EXCLUDED.category,"
                                    "   last_seen = now()"
                                    " RETURNING (xmax = 0) AS was_inserted",
                                    (store_id, listing.source_key, listing.name_raw, listing.price,
                                     listing.url, listing.availability, listing.image_url, listing.category))
                if cur.fetchone()[0]:
                    inserted += 1
                else:
                    updated += 1
    return inserted, updated


def start_run(conn: psycopg.Connection, store_id: UUID) -> int:
    with conn.transaction():
        with conn.cursor() as cur:
            cur.execute("INSERT INTO harvest_run (store_id) VALUES (%s) RETURNING id", (store_id,))
            run_id = cur.fetchone()[0]
    return run_id


def finish_run(conn: psycopg.Connection, run_id: int, ok: bool,
               seen: int, kept: int, error: str | None = None) -> None:
    with conn.transaction():
        with conn.cursor() as cur:
            cur.execute(
                "UPDATE harvest_run SET finished_at = now(), ok = %s,"
                " listings_seen = %s, listings_kept = %s, error = %s WHERE id = %s",
                (ok, seen, kept, error, run_id))


# --------------------------------------------------------------- clustering
# Canonical products. These are permanent identities; offers are temporary
# observations of them.

def load_variant_keys(conn: psycopg.Connection) -> dict[VariantKey, UUID]:
    """Every canonical variant that exists, as keys the matcher can compare.

    Clustering needs the whole set in memory because resolving a partial key
    means asking "how many existing variants is this compatible with?" -- and
    the answer must be exact. One is an attachment; two is a refusal.
    """
    with conn.cursor() as cur:
        # `category` is part of the key and MUST be read back. Omitting it made
        # every stored variant claim to be a smartphone, which is silent and
        # expensive: display_name took the phone branch and dereferenced a NULL
        # colour, is_wellformed applied the phone capacity guard to television
        # diagonals, and the `known` map in cluster_offers could never match a
        # freshly-built non-phone key -- so every TV, fridge and laptop was a
        # candidate for being created a second time on every cycle.
        # The write side of this was fixed earlier (upsert_variant omitted the
        # column); the read side was missed.
        cur.execute(
            "SELECT id, brand, model_tokens, ram_gb, storage_gb, color, sim_kind,"
            " coalesce(category, 'smartphones')"
            " FROM variant")
        return {
            VariantKey(brand=brand, model_tokens=frozenset(tokens), ram_gb=ram,
                       storage_gb=storage, color=color, sim_kind=sim,
                       category=category): vid
            for vid, brand, tokens, ram, storage, color, sim, category
            in cur.fetchall()
        }


def upsert_variant(conn: psycopg.Connection, key: VariantKey) -> UUID:
    """Find or create the canonical variant for this identity.

    The slug is a deterministic function of the key, so ON CONFLICT on it makes
    creation idempotent and race-free -- and makes duplicate canonical products
    structurally impossible, which is the defect this platform exists to fix.
    """
    with conn.transaction():
        with conn.cursor() as cur:
            cur.execute(
                            "INSERT INTO variant (brand, model_tokens, ram_gb, storage_gb,"
                            " color, sim_kind, category, display_name, slug)"
                            " VALUES (%s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s)"
                            " ON CONFLICT (slug) DO NOTHING RETURNING id",
                            (key.brand, sorted(key.model_tokens), key.ram_gb, key.storage_gb,
                             key.color, key.sim_kind, key.category, display_name(key), slug(key)))
            row = cur.fetchone()
            if row is not None:
                return row[0]
            # Already existed -- another offer in this same run created it.
            cur.execute("SELECT id FROM variant WHERE slug = %s", (slug(key),))
            return cur.fetchone()[0]


def attach_offer_to_variant(conn: psycopg.Connection, offer_id: UUID,
                            variant_id: UUID) -> None:
    """Attach, and clear any previous refusal.

    An offer parked as ambiguous in one cycle is often resolvable in a later
    one, once some other retailer's listing has created the variant it needed.
    Leaving the unresolved row behind would make that count a permanently
    rising number nobody can act on.
    """
    with conn.transaction():
        with conn.cursor() as cur:
            cur.execute("UPDATE offer SET variant_id = %s WHERE id = %s",
                        (variant_id, offer_id))
            cur.execute("DELETE FROM unresolved_offer WHERE offer_id = %s", (offer_id,))


def record_unresolved(conn: psycopg.Connection, offer_id: UUID, reason: str,
                      candidate_ids: list[UUID] | None = None) -> None:
    """Refuse to guess, and say why.

    unresolved_offer is a quality metric we watch, not a dumping ground: a
    rising rate means the matcher needs work, so the reason must be specific.
    """
    with conn.transaction():
        with conn.cursor() as cur:
            cur.execute("UPDATE offer SET variant_id = NULL WHERE id = %s", (offer_id,))
            cur.execute(
                "INSERT INTO unresolved_offer (offer_id, reason, candidate_ids)"
                " VALUES (%s, %s, %s)"
                " ON CONFLICT (offer_id) DO UPDATE SET"
                "   reason = EXCLUDED.reason,"
                "   candidate_ids = EXCLUDED.candidate_ids,"
                "   recorded_at = now()",
                (offer_id, reason, candidate_ids or []))


def delete_empty_malformed_variants(conn: psycopg.Connection, is_wellformed) -> int:
    """Remove variants that hold no offers and could never hold one.

    Deliberately narrow. A variant whose offers have merely gone out of stock
    is KEPT -- schema.sql makes min_price nullable precisely so we neither
    publish an unbuyable price nor lose the product's history. This deletes
    only variants the clustering guard would refuse outright, which can never
    be attached to again, and which would otherwise serve a product page with
    no price for a phone that does not exist.
    """
    removed = 0
    with conn.transaction():
        for key, variant_id in load_variant_keys(conn).items():
            if is_wellformed(key):
                continue
            with conn.cursor() as cur:
                cur.execute(
                    "DELETE FROM variant WHERE id = %s"
                    " AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM offer o WHERE o.variant_id = %s)",
                    (variant_id, variant_id))
                removed += cur.rowcount
    return removed


def delete_orphan_variants(conn: psycopg.Connection) -> int:
    """Remove variants that no offer references at all.

    NOT the same as a variant whose offers are out of stock. An offer row is
    never deleted -- the tables are append-and-upsert -- so a variant with zero
    offers can only be the residue of RE-KEYING: a parser or vocabulary change
    moved every offer to a differently-named variant and left the old row
    behind. schema.sql's decision to keep a real variant with a NULL price
    covers out-of-stock, which still has offers pointing at it; this covers a
    row that can never be reached again.

    Measured after the category-identity change: 928 of 1,263 laptop variants
    held no offers. They do not render -- every listing query inner-joins
    variant_price, which aggregate deletes alongside them -- but they make the
    variant count meaningless as a health signal, and the quality report's
    "no offers at all" line is meant to be a small number worth reading.

    Safe to run every cycle: upsert_variant recreates any of these on demand
    from its deterministic slug, so deleting one is at worst undone next run.
    """
    with conn.transaction():
        with conn.cursor() as cur:
            cur.execute(
                "DELETE FROM variant v"
                " WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM offer o WHERE o.variant_id = v.id)")
            return cur.rowcount


def refresh_display_names(conn: psycopg.Connection) -> int:
    """Regenerate every variant's display_name from its key. Returns rows changed.

    display_name is a pure function of the key, but it is STORED, and
    `upsert_variant` uses ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING -- so a variant's name is
    written once, at creation, and never revisited. A correction to the
    generator would then apply only to products first seen afterwards, leaving
    the existing catalogue on the old wording indefinitely. That is the same
    shape as the schema.sql header rule: a change that silently applies to
    fresh data and not to production.

    Adding the eSIM marker was exactly such a correction, and it had 52 live
    variants to repair.
    """
    changed = 0
    with conn.transaction():
        for key, variant_id in load_variant_keys(conn).items():
            with conn.cursor() as cur:
                cur.execute(
                    "UPDATE variant SET display_name = %s"
                    " WHERE id = %s AND display_name IS DISTINCT FROM %s",
                    (display_name(key), variant_id, display_name(key)))
                changed += cur.rowcount
    return changed


def load_attached_offers(conn: psycopg.Connection) -> list[dict]:
    """Offers currently attached to a variant, with their latest raw payload.

    The mirror of `load_unattached_offers`, and the input to the drift check:
    an attachment written in an earlier cycle is only trustworthy while the
    name it was derived from is unchanged. See `cluster.detach_drifted_offers`.
    """
    with conn.cursor() as cur:
        cur.execute("""
            SELECT o.id, o.name_raw, o.variant_id, o.category,
                   (SELECT r.payload FROM raw_offer r
                     WHERE r.store_id = o.store_id AND r.source_key = o.source_key
                     ORDER BY r.observed_at DESC LIMIT 1)
              FROM offer o
             WHERE o.variant_id IS NOT NULL
        """)
        return [{"id": oid, "name_raw": name, "variant_id": vid, "category": cat, "raw": payload}
                for oid, name, vid, cat, payload in cur.fetchall()]


def detach_offer(conn: psycopg.Connection, offer_id: UUID) -> None:
    """Return an offer to the unattached pool so clustering reconsiders it."""
    with conn.transaction():
        with conn.cursor() as cur:
            cur.execute("UPDATE offer SET variant_id = NULL WHERE id = %s", (offer_id,))


def load_unattached_offers(conn: psycopg.Connection) -> list[dict]:
    """Offers not currently attached to a variant, with their latest raw payload.

    Deliberately includes offers already sitting in unresolved_offer: they must
    be reconsidered every run, not parked forever.
    """
    with conn.cursor() as cur:
        cur.execute("""
            SELECT o.id, o.name_raw, o.category,
                   (SELECT r.payload FROM raw_offer r
                     WHERE r.store_id = o.store_id AND r.source_key = o.source_key
                     ORDER BY r.observed_at DESC LIMIT 1)
              FROM offer o
             WHERE o.variant_id IS NULL
        """)
        return [{"id": oid, "name_raw": name, "category": cat, "raw": payload}
                for oid, name, cat, payload in cur.fetchall()]



def try_advisory_lock(conn: psycopg.Connection, key: int) -> bool:
    """Take a session-scoped advisory lock, or return False immediately.

    Session-scoped rather than transaction-scoped on purpose: the lock must
    outlive every transaction a cycle runs, and must be released even if the
    process is killed. Postgres drops it when the connection closes, however
    that happens -- a lock TABLE would need explicit crash cleanup, and that
    cleanup is where this kind of guard usually fails.
    """
    with conn.cursor() as cur:
        cur.execute("SELECT pg_try_advisory_lock(%s)", (key,))
        return bool(cur.fetchone()[0])



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/db/schema.sql

-- Idempotent. Executed on every application start.
--
-- HOW TO CHANGE THIS FILE. Never edit an existing CREATE TABLE to add or alter a
-- column: CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS is a no-op on a database that already has the
-- table, so the change would apply on a fresh database and silently not apply in
-- production. Instead append an explicit migration at the bottom of the file --
-- ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS ... -- and bump the schema_version row.

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS schema_version (
    version     integer PRIMARY KEY,
    applied_at  timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
INSERT INTO schema_version (version) VALUES (1) ON CONFLICT (version) DO NOTHING;

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS store (
    id           uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    slug         text UNIQUE NOT NULL,
    name         text NOT NULL,
    website_url  text NOT NULL,
    is_active    boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true,
    created_at   timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);

-- Append-only observation log. Price history falls out of this for free.
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS raw_offer (
    id           bigserial PRIMARY KEY,
    store_id     uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES store(id),
    source_key   text NOT NULL,
    name_raw     text NOT NULL,
    price        bigint NOT NULL CHECK (price BETWEEN 100000 AND 100000000),
    url          text NOT NULL,
    availability text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'UNKNOWN'
        CHECK (availability IN ('IN_STOCK', 'OUT_OF_STOCK', 'UNKNOWN')),
    image_url    text,
        payload      jsonb,
        observed_at  timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
    );
    -- Plan 5: which category sweep produced this observation (defaults smartphones).
    ALTER TABLE raw_offer ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS category text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'smartphones';
    CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS raw_offer_store_key_time
        ON raw_offer (store_id, source_key, observed_at DESC);

-- Current state, one row per retailer listing. variant_id is filled in Plan 3.
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS offer (
    id           uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    store_id     uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES store(id),
    source_key   text NOT NULL,
    variant_id   uuid,
    name_raw     text NOT NULL,
    price        bigint NOT NULL CHECK (price BETWEEN 100000 AND 100000000),
    url          text NOT NULL,
    availability text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'UNKNOWN'
        CHECK (availability IN ('IN_STOCK', 'OUT_OF_STOCK', 'UNKNOWN')),
    image_url    text,
        first_seen   timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
        last_seen    timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
        UNIQUE (store_id, source_key)
    );
    -- Plan 5: which category sweep produced this offer (defaults smartphones).
    ALTER TABLE offer ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS category text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'smartphones';
    CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS offer_last_seen ON offer (last_seen DESC);
    CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS offer_variant ON offer (variant_id) WHERE variant_id IS NOT NULL;

-- One row per source per cycle. Makes a silently-dead source visible.
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS harvest_run (
    id            bigserial PRIMARY KEY,
    store_id      uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES store(id),
    started_at    timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    finished_at   timestamptz,
    ok            boolean,
    listings_seen integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    listings_kept integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    error         text
);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS harvest_run_store_started
    ON harvest_run (store_id, started_at DESC);

INSERT INTO store (slug, name, website_url) VALUES
    ('uzum',      'Uzum',      'https://uzum.uz'),
    ('alifshop',  'Alif Shop', 'https://alifshop.uz'),
    ('beemarket', 'BeeMarket', 'https://market.beeline.uz'),
    ('idea',      'Idea',      'https://idea.uz'),
    ('mediapark', 'Mediapark', 'https://mediapark.uz'),
    ('olcha',     'Olcha',     'https://olcha.uz'),
    ('texnomart', 'Texnomart', 'https://texnomart.uz')
ON CONFLICT (slug) DO NOTHING;

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Migration 2: canonical products. Appended, never edited into the tables
-- above -- see the header comment.
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS variant (
    id            uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    brand         text NOT NULL,
    model_tokens  text[] NOT NULL,
    ram_gb        integer,                 -- NULL is legitimate: Apple omits RAM
        storage_gb    integer NOT NULL,        -- NOT NULL for phones (see Plan 5 note)
        color         text NOT NULL,           -- hard identity field for PHONES
        sim_kind      text NOT NULL CHECK (sim_kind IN ('dual', 'esim_only')),
        display_name  text NOT NULL,           -- generated from the key, never copied
        slug          text UNIQUE NOT NULL,
        category      text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'smartphones',
        created_at    timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
    );
    CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS variant_identity
        ON variant (brand, ram_gb, storage_gb, sim_kind);
    CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS variant_siblings
        ON variant (brand, storage_gb, ram_gb, sim_kind);   -- colour-sibling lookup

    -- Plan 5 (categories, 2026-08-10): non-phone categories (televisions,
    -- laptops, ACs, fridges, washers, ovens) carry no colour/SIM identity, so
    -- both hard identity fields become nullable. Idempotent by construction:
    -- DROP NOT NULL on an already-nullable column is a no-op, and the sim_kind
    -- CHECK passes for NULL (UNKNOWN). Existing phone rows are untouched.
    ALTER TABLE variant ALTER COLUMN color DROP NOT NULL;
    ALTER TABLE variant ALTER COLUMN sim_kind DROP NOT NULL;
    -- storage_gb is also relaxed: a TV or fridge has no memory figure, and
    -- requiring one would make most appliances unkeyable.
    ALTER TABLE variant ALTER COLUMN storage_gb DROP NOT NULL;

-- min_price is NULLABLE on purpose. Only IN_STOCK offers may set a headline
-- price, and a variant can legitimately have offers where every one of them is
-- out of stock. NOT NULL would force us either to publish an out-of-stock price
-- as if it were buyable, or to delete the row and lose the variant's history.
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS variant_price (
    variant_id            uuid PRIMARY KEY REFERENCES variant(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    min_price             bigint,
    min_store_id          uuid REFERENCES store(id),
    min_offer_id          uuid REFERENCES offer(id),
    median_price          bigint,
    max_price             bigint,
    offer_count           integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    in_stock_offer_count  integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    store_count           integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    computed_at           timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    CHECK ((min_price IS NULL) = (in_stock_offer_count = 0))
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS unresolved_offer (
    offer_id       uuid PRIMARY KEY REFERENCES offer(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    reason         text NOT NULL CHECK (reason <> ''),
    candidate_ids  uuid[] NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}',
    recorded_at    timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS unresolved_reason ON unresolved_offer (reason);

DO $$ BEGIN
    ALTER TABLE offer ADD CONSTRAINT offer_variant_fk
        FOREIGN KEY (variant_id) REFERENCES variant(id) ON DELETE SET NULL;
EXCEPTION WHEN duplicate_object THEN NULL;
END $$;

INSERT INTO schema_version (version) VALUES (2) ON CONFLICT (version) DO NOTHING;

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Migration 3: asaxiy.uz, the eighth source.

-- Appended rather than added to the migration-1 seed list above, per the
-- header: that INSERT already ran in production, and `ON CONFLICT (slug) DO
-- NOTHING` makes re-running it a no-op for the seven rows that exist -- but
-- the row for an EIGHTH store would only ever be inserted on a database
-- fresh enough to be running migration 1 for the first time. On production
-- it would silently never appear, harvest_all would report "store not
-- seeded", and the source would look broken for a reason nothing points at.
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

INSERT INTO store (slug, name, website_url) VALUES
    ('asaxiy', 'Asaxiy', 'https://asaxiy.uz')
ON CONFLICT (slug) DO NOTHING;

INSERT INTO schema_version (version) VALUES (3) ON CONFLICT (version) DO NOTHING;

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

INSERT INTO store (slug, name, website_url) VALUES
    ('radius', 'Radius', 'https://radius.uz')
ON CONFLICT (slug) DO NOTHING;

INSERT INTO schema_version (version) VALUES (4) ON CONFLICT (version) DO NOTHING;

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

INSERT INTO store (slug, name, website_url) VALUES
    ('glotr', 'Glotr', 'https://glotr.uz')
ON CONFLICT (slug) DO NOTHING;

INSERT INTO schema_version (version) VALUES (5) ON CONFLICT (version) DO NOTHING;

INSERT INTO store (slug, name, website_url) VALUES
    ('openshop', 'OpenShop', 'https://openshop.uz')

"""Brand vocabulary and per-brand policy.

Single home for anything brand-specific, because three modules need it:
memory.py (who publishes RAM), features.py (alias resolution) and keys.py
(key completeness + display label).
"""

# TWO ROLES, WITH OPPOSITE FAILURE MODES -- read this before removing an entry.
#
# 1. RECOGNITION vocabulary for extract_features. A brand missing here degrades
#    ONE listing's identity.
# 2. CRAWL FRONTIER, via narxnoma.sources.brand_queries. idea, alifshop and
#    uzum have no browsable catalogue endpoint, so this list is their ENTIRE
#    reach. A brand missing here is not a degraded name -- it is ZERO OFFERS,
#    permanently, from three of seven sources, and nothing in the system
#    reports the absence of something never looked for.
#
# So this list is cheap to extend and expensive to trim. Adding a brand nobody
# sells costs one wasted query per cycle; omitting one costs its whole catalogue.

# Order matters: matched as substrings, so sub-brands come before parents.
BRANDS: list[str] = [
    "apple", "samsung", "honor", "xiaomi", "vivo", "oppo", "realme",
    "tecno", "infinix", "nothing", "google", "asus", "huawei",
    "motorola", "zte", "meizu",
    "itel", "nokia", "oneplus", "nubia", "tcl", "blackview", "doogee",
    "ulefone",
]

BRAND_ALIASES: dict[str, str] = {
    "iphone": "apple",
    "redmi": "xiaomi",
    "poco": "xiaomi",
    "macbook": "apple",
    # Avalon prefixes its model codes "AVL-": "AVALON AVL-RF512", and some
    # listings drop the brand word entirely ("Стиральная машина AVL-WM 7030").
    "avl": "avalon",
}

# Brands that appear ONLY outside smartphones: TV, appliance and laptop makers.
#
# MEASURED, not guessed. Every entry here was read off the live catalogue: the
# 2,076 non-phone offers were normalised, their category noun stripped, and the
# remaining head token counted. Each token below was then confirmed by eye
# against three real listings as a manufacturer rather than a descriptor --
# which is why "полуавтомат" (28 hits), "двухкамерный", "сплит-система" and
# "игровой" are NOT here: they describe the appliance, they do not make it.
#
# Kept SEPARATE from BRANDS on purpose. BRANDS doubles as the crawl frontier
# (see above), and querying uzum/alifshop/idea for "bosch" under a *phone*
# sweep would spend requests on a brand that sells no phones. These are
# recognition-only; the category sweeps reach them by category, not by brand.
APPLIANCE_BRANDS: list[str] = [
    # TV / large appliance
    "artel", "premier", "hisense", "shivaki", "ziffler", "wellstars",
    "toshiba", "panasonic", "hikvision", "alpicool", "texnowell",
    "avalon", "brando", "moonx", "mytech", "meiga", "immer", "sonor",
    "volto", "konig", "rosso", "rulls", "vesta", "frame", "litv",
    "haier", "midea", "bosch", "beko", "indesit", "whirlpool", "gorenje",
    "electrolux", "ariston", "siemens", "candy", "roison", "ferre",
    "sony", "philips", "sharp", "supra", "yasin", "goldstar",
    "7tech", "arg", "lg",
    # Second measured pass, after the category frontier tripled the non-phone
    # catalogue (2,076 -> 6,320 offers). Same method: unrecognised head tokens
    # counted across every non-phone title, each confirmed against three real
    # listings as a manufacturer.
    "hofmann", "gefest", "elite", "aux", "ideal", "loretto", "bench",
    "kleo", "sitronic", "volna", "itech", "goodwell", "helper", "aiwa",
    "sirius", "biryusa", "sencor", "vestel", "zanussi", "hansa",
    # Third measured pass, 2026-08-10, prompted by asaxiy: 63 of its 693
    # offers had no identity at all, and the same head words were failing for
    # uzum, olcha, idea and alifshop too -- 873 non-phone offers in total.
    # Same method again: count the unrecognised head token across every
    # unresolved title, confirm each against real listings as a maker.
    #
    # Counted before adding (hits / stores that publish them):
    #   gree 38/3   atlant 25/1   keetab 11/2   chuwi 10/4   konnen 10/3
    #   jpe 9/1     ario 9/1      qlt 8/1       magna 8/2    ssmart 8/2
    #   huamei 7/1  lunix 7/1     caderno 6/1   apetito 5/1  adreamer 5/1
    #   vitech 4/1  beston 4/1    chigo 4/1     microsoft 4/2  aufit 3/2
    #
    # NOT added, and why: "lrf" (7, idea: "LRF-456GS") and "gtw" (4, idea:
    # "GTW-734") are code prefixes whose manufacturer no listing anywhere in
    # the catalogue spells out -- unlike "prm" and "shiv" below, which are
    # confirmed. Guessing a brand is how two different products become one
    # page. "rivacase" (7, texnomart) is real, and its listings are laptop
    # BAGS ("Noutbuk sumkasi"); leaving it unrecognised is what keeps them
    # out of the laptop catalogue.
    "gree", "atlant", "keetab", "chuwi", "konnen", "jpe", "ario", "qlt",
    "magna", "ssmart", "huamei", "lunix", "caderno", "apetito", "adreamer",
    "vitech", "beston", "chigo", "microsoft", "aufit",
    # Two characters, which is why the head-token count above never saw it: a
    # 2-char token was skipped as too short to be a name. Found by counting
    # them separately. BQ's 55FU32B and 43FS36B are each carried by asaxiy,
    # uzum and alifshop -- two real three-store comparisons that were lost.
    "bq",
    # laptops
    "lenovo", "acer", "msi", "dell", "bikon", "gigabyte", "razer",
    "hp",
]

# Aliases that apply OUTSIDE smartphones only.
#
# Kept separate from BRAND_ALIASES because that dict has two other jobs: it is
# probed first in the phone path's plain SUBSTRING scan (pinned by 14,860
# golden rows), and it feeds BRAND_QUERIES, the phone crawl frontier -- so an
# appliance alias added there would spend a real query per cycle at three
# retailers asking for a phone brand that does not exist.
#
# Each entry confirmed against listings that spell the brand out in full:
APPLIANCE_BRAND_ALIASES: dict[str, str] = {
    # A BUG, not a gap: "biryusa" was added to the vocabulary in the Latin
    # spelling and its own display label is "Бирюса" -- but every listing
    # writes it in Cyrillic, and normalize() leaves an all-Cyrillic token
    # alone (correctly: there is no Latin in it to fold). 20 offers across
    # asaxiy and uzum were unkeyable because the two spellings never met.
    "бирюса": "biryusa",
    # Premier prefixes its codes "PRM-", and idea drops the brand word
    # entirely: "PRM-350TFNFID/DI". Confirmed by uzum's "Холодильник Premier
    # PRM-250TFDF/W", which carries both.
    "prm": "premier",
    # Same shape for Shivaki: uzum publishes both "Стиральная машина SHIVAKI

    # and alifshop's "Shivaki WM1106 S Sentaku" names the same line.
    "shiv": "shivaki",
    # A misspelling on uzum, not a second brand: "Газовые плиты Zifler Breeze
    # Series BGS-6031BE" is Ziffler's Breeze line, which uzum also lists
    # correctly spelled.
    "zifler": "ziffler",
    # idea and alifshop write "Konnen", the brand writes "KÖNNEN", and
    # normalize() keeps the umlaut.
    "können": "konnen",
}

#: Alias resolution for non-phone categories: the phone aliases still apply
#: (a MacBook is an Apple laptop) plus the appliance-only ones above.
NON_PHONE_ALIASES: dict[str, str] = {**BRAND_ALIASES, **APPLIANCE_BRAND_ALIASES}

#: Recognition vocabulary for non-phone categories: appliance makers plus the
#: phone brands that also sell TVs, laptops and white goods (Samsung, Apple,
#: Xiaomi, Honor, Huawei, ASUS, TCL all do).
NON_PHONE_BRANDS: list[str] = APPLIANCE_BRANDS + BRANDS

    "msi": "MSI", "dell": "Dell", "bikon": "BIKON", "gigabyte": "Gigabyte",

    "hofmann": "Hofmann", "gefest": "Gefest", "elite": "Elite",
    "aux": "AUX", "ideal": "Ideal", "loretto": "Loretto", "bench": "Bench",
    "kleo": "Kleo", "sitronic": "Sitronic", "volna": "Volna",
    "itech": "iTech", "goodwell": "Goodwell", "helper": "Helper",
    "aiwa": "AIWA", "sirius": "Sirius", "biryusa": "Бирюса",
    "sencor": "Sencor", "vestel": "Vestel", "zanussi": "Zanussi",
    "hansa": "Hansa",
    "gree": "Gree", "atlant": "ATLANT", "keetab": "Keetab", "chuwi": "CHUWI",
    "konnen": "Konnen", "jpe": "JPE", "ario": "ARIO", "qlt": "QLT",
    "magna": "Magna", "ssmart": "Ssmart", "huamei": "Huamei",
    "lunix": "Lunix", "caderno": "Caderno", "apetito": "Apetito",
    "adreamer": "Adreamer", "vitech": "Vitech", "beston": "Beston",
    "chigo": "Chigo", "microsoft": "Microsoft", "aufit": "Aufit",
    "bq": "BQ",
}

# Brands that never print RAM in consumer product names. For these, RAM is
# absent rather than unknown, so a key without RAM is still complete.
BRANDS_WITHOUT_PUBLISHED_RAM: frozenset[str] = frozenset({"apple"})

BRAND_LABELS: dict[str, str] = {
    "apple": "Apple iPhone",   # "iphone" is stripped as a NON_MODEL_TOKEN
    "samsung": "Samsung",
    "honor": "Honor",
    "xiaomi": "Xiaomi",
    "vivo": "Vivo",
    "oppo": "OPPO",
    "realme": "Realme",
    "tecno": "Tecno",
    "infinix": "Infinix",
    "nothing": "Nothing",
    "google": "Google",
    "asus": "ASUS",
    "huawei": "Huawei",
    "motorola": "Motorola",
    "zte": "ZTE",
    "meizu": "Meizu",
    "itel": "Itel",
    "nokia": "Nokia",
    "oneplus": "OnePlus",
    "nubia": "Nubia",
    "tcl": "TCL",
    "blackview": "Blackview",
    "doogee": "Doogee",
    "ulefone": "Ulefone",
}


def canonical_brand(token: str) -> str:
    """Collapse a sub-brand to its parent ('redmi' -> 'xiaomi')."""
    return BRAND_ALIASES.get(token, token)


def canonical_non_phone_brand(token: str) -> str:
    """Collapse an appliance sub-brand or code prefix ('prm' -> 'premier').

    Separate from canonical_brand rather than a category flag on it, so the
    phone path -- a plain substring scan pinned by 14,860 golden rows -- keeps
    resolving through BRAND_ALIASES and nothing else.
    """
    return NON_PHONE_ALIASES.get(token, token)


def brand_label(brand: str, category: str = "smartphones") -> str:
    """Display label for a brand, in the context of a category.

    Category matters for exactly one entry, and getting it wrong is visible to
    every user: BRAND_LABELS maps "apple" to "Apple iPhone", because for phones
    the word "iPhone" is stripped as a NON_MODEL_TOKEN and has to be put back.
    A MacBook is also brand "apple", so the phone label would render
    "Ноутбук Apple iPhone MacBook Air" on a laptop page.
    """
    if category != "smartphones":
        if brand in APPLIANCE_BRAND_LABELS:
            return APPLIANCE_BRAND_LABELS[brand]
        if brand == "apple":
            return "Apple"
        return BRAND_LABELS.get(brand, brand.capitalize())
    return BRAND_LABELS.get(brand, brand.capitalize())



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/matching/categories.py
