"""Non-phone identity vocabulary.

A phone's identity is a signature: brand + model + RAM + storage + colour + SIM.
A television's is not. A TV, fridge, oven or air conditioner is identified by
its **catalogue code** -- "UE55DU7100UXUZ", "RT47CG6442B1WT", "NV68A1145RS" --
optionally preceded by a model NAME ("Predator Helios Neo", "Serie 6", "Alba").
Everything else in the title is marketing.

That inverts two rules the phone matcher depends on, which is why this module
exists instead of a flag inside features.py:

1. **The SKU-code rule must not fire.** features.py discards mixed letter+digit
   tokens of five or more characters, because for a phone "sm-a175f" is a
   catalogue code and "a17" is the identity. For a TV there IS no friendlier
   name: the code *is* the product. Measured on the live catalogue, that single
   rule made 100% of ovens, and the great majority of TVs and fridges,
   permanently unkeyable.

2. **Alphabetic tokens must be kept.** features.py keeps a word only if it is a
   known MODEL_MODIFIER ("pro", "max", "note"), because for phones any other
   word is noise. Appliance model names are made of exactly such words --
   "Vostro", "ThinkPad", "Aspire", "Predator" -- and dropping them merges every
   Acer laptop of the same memory tier into one product.

Both relaxations WIDEN what counts as identity, so the risk they introduce is
splitting one product into several, never merging two into one. That is the
safe direction for this project: a duplicate page is a coverage bug, a merged
page is the exact defect the platform exists to prevent.

The instrument that keeps splitting under control is the stopword vocabulary
below. "Телевизор Artel UA43H3502 Android TV" and "Телевизор Artel UA43H3502"
are one product, so "android", "tv" and "smart" must never reach the key.
"""
import re

# Product-type nouns, Russian and Uzbek (Latin), across all six non-phone
# categories. These lead almost every title in the live catalogue and identify
# the CATEGORY, which is already an axis of the key -- so repeating them inside
# model_tokens adds nothing and splits "Смарт-телевизор X" from "Телевизор X".
CATEGORY_NOUNS: frozenset[str] = frozenset({
    # televisions
    "телевизор", "телевизоры", "телевизора", "televizor", "televizori",
    "telewizor", "тв", "tv",
    # laptops
    "ноутбук", "ноутбуки", "ноутбука", "noutbuk", "noutbuki", "laptop",
    "netbuk", "нетбук",
    # air conditioners
    "кондиционер", "кондиционеры", "кондиционера", "konditsioner",
    "konditsioneri", "kondisioner", "сплит", "система", "системы",
    # fridges
    "холодильник", "холодильники", "холодильника", "автохолодильник",
    "автохолодильники", "muzlatgich", "muzlatgichi", "sovutgich",
    "sovutgichi", "морозильник", "морозильники",
    # washers
    "стиральная", "стиральные", "стиральной", "машина", "машины", "машинка",
    "mashina", "mashinasi", "kir", "yuvish", "стирально", "сушильная",
    # ovens / hobs
    "духовой", "духовка", "духовки", "шкаф", "духовых", "духовые",
    "плита", "плиты", "плитка", "duxovka", "duxovkasi", "pech",
    "варочная", "панель", "вытяжка", "печь",
})

# Words that describe HOW the appliance works or is installed. Real words, real
# information -- and not identity: "Двухкамерный холодильник Artel HD455" and
# "Холодильник Artel HD455" are one fridge. Measured as head tokens on the live
# catalogue ("полуавтомат" 28, "автомат" 10, "бытовой" 5, "двухкамерный" 3).
DESCRIPTORS: frozenset[str] = frozenset({
    "полуавтомат", "полуавтоматическая", "автомат", "автоматическая",
    "двухкамерный", "двухкамерная", "однокамерный", "трехкамерный",
    "встраиваемый", "встраиваемая", "встраиваемые", "встроенный",
    "напольный", "настольная", "настенный", "колонный", "кассетный",
    "витринный", "бытовой", "бытовая", "промышленный",
    "газовая", "газовые", "газовый", "электрическая", "электрический",
    "комбинированная", "комбинированный", "индукционная", "индукционный",
    "безрамочный", "игровой", "игровая", "портативный", "переносной",
    "вертикальная", "горизонтальная", "фронтальная",
    "новый", "новая", "оригинал", "оригинальный",
})

# Marketing and platform words. These vary between listings of the SAME
# product, so any one of them reaching the key splits a real product in two.
# "Телевизор Artel UA43H3502 Android TV" vs "Телевизор Artel UA43H3502" is the
# measured case that motivates the list.
MARKETING: frozenset[str] = frozenset({
    "smart", "смарт", "android", "андроид", "google", "assistant", "webos",
    "tizen", "yandex", "алиса", "ai", "wifi", "wi", "fi", "bluetooth",
    "inverter", "invertor", "инвертор", "инверторный", "инверторная",
    # Inverter-type markers on air conditioners, stated by some listings of a
    # unit and not by others: "Artel Dc Full Shahrisabz 12" and "Artel
    # Shahrisabz 12" are one air conditioner, as are "Artel Marvarid Inv 18"
    # and "Artel Marvarid 18". "art" is Artel's own product-line prefix
    # ("Artel ART HDG Iceberg 12"), not a model.
    # Series letters ("12BE", "12HE", "HS") are deliberately NOT here: those
    # ride inside the figure token and may distinguish real units, and merging
    # two real products is the failure this module must not have.
    "inv", "dc", "full", "art",
    "led", "qled", "oled", "nanocell", "frameless",
    "uhd", "fhd", "hd", "ultrahd", "fullhd", "4k", "8k", "2k",
    "dyuym", "дюйм", "дюйма", "дюймов",
    "eco", "premium", "series", "serie", "серия", "gen",
    # NOTE: "pro", "air", "max", "plus", "ultra", "lite", "mini" and "neo" are
    # deliberately ABSENT. They read as marketing on a phone, but outside
    # phones they are the model name itself -- a MacBook Air is not a MacBook
    # Pro, and "Predator Helios Neo" is not "Predator Helios". Stripping them
    # would merge genuinely different products, which is the one failure this
    # module must not have. features.py keeps them for phones for the same
    # reason (MODEL_MODIFIERS).
    "new", "новинка", "хит", "акция", "скидка", "подарок", "гарантия",
    "доставка", "бесплатно", "рассрочка", "кредит",
    "official", "официальный", "оригинальный",
    "energy", "class", "класс",
})

# Measurement units and their figures' companions. A capacity in litres or
# kilograms is a SPEC, and the same product is listed with and without it.
UNITS: frozenset[str] = frozenset({
    "кг", "kg", "л", "лт", "литр", "литров", "l", "см", "cm", "мм", "mm",
    "вт", "w", "квт", "kw", "в", "v", "гц", "hz", "об", "мин", "rpm",
    "btu", "btuh", "м", "m", "м2", "m2", "м3",
    "гб", "gb", "тб", "tb", "мб", "mb", "ram", "ssd", "hdd", "emmc",
    "ddr", "ddr3", "ddr4", "ddr5", "lpddr4", "lpddr5",
    "ips", "va", "tn",
})

# Processors and graphics. A laptop's CPU is a specification, not its name --
# and it only reaches the tokens when the retailer omits the "/" delimiter that
# usually fences the spec off. Measured: "Ноутбук Acer Aspire Snapdragon X1
# 26100" became a different product from "Ноутбук Acer Aspire".
#
# Bare part numbers ("1334U", "26100", "N4405") are deliberately NOT listed:
# they are indistinguishable from real model codes by shape, and the vendor
# words below are enough to catch the titles that carry them.
# "apple" is a BRAND, and "m1".."m4" are the identity of a MacBook -- an Air M4
# is not an Air M2. Both are deliberately absent for the same reason "pro" and
# "air" are absent from MARKETING.
PROCESSORS: frozenset[str] = frozenset({
    "intel", "amd", "qualcomm", "mediatek", "nvidia",
    "snapdragon", "ryzen", "core", "celeron", "pentium", "athlon", "atom",
    "i3", "i5", "i7", "i9", "u5", "u7", "u9",
    "rtx", "gtx", "geforce", "radeon", "iris", "arc", "vega", "graphics",
    "processor", "процессор", "видеокарта", "graphic",
})

#: Everything a non-phone model token must not be.
NON_MODEL: frozenset[str] = (
    CATEGORY_NOUNS | DESCRIPTORS | MARKETING | UNITS | PROCESSORS
)

#: The same, for a category that has no processor. See CATEGORIES_WITH_CPU.
NON_MODEL_WITHOUT_CPU: frozenset[str] = (
    CATEGORY_NOUNS | DESCRIPTORS | MARKETING | UNITS
)

# Categories where a PROCESSORS word is a specification rather than a name.
# Only a computer has a CPU; on an air conditioner the same words are the
# model. Measured 2026-08-10 across all 5,132 non-laptop non-phone offers:
# exactly 8 (0.16%) contain a PROCESSORS word, and every one is an air
# conditioner whose LINE NAME collides with a chip --
#     "Кондиционер Rulls ARC 12 Inverter"     (Intel Arc)     6 offers, 2 stores
#     "Кондиционер Sitronic Vega Inverter 18" (Radeon Vega)   2 offers
# -- and all 8 were unresolved, so scoping this re-keys nothing that already
# clustered. cut_at_processor made it worse than a plain stopword would: it
# truncates positionally, so "Rulls ARC 12" lost its capacity as well as its
# name and could not form a key at all.
CATEGORIES_WITH_CPU: frozenset[str] = frozenset({"laptops"})

# Categories where a RAM/storage figure is a real specification. A laptop has
# 16 GB and a 512 GB SSD; a television, fridge, oven, washer or air conditioner
# has neither.
#
# This matters because the phone extractor reads a LONE plausible capacity as
# storage, and the plausible-storage set (32, 64, 128, 256, 512) overlaps
# exactly with television diagonals and fridge capacities. Measured: "Телевизор
# Premier 32PRM840FSV" was keyed with storage_gb = 32 and rendered as
# "Телевизор Premier 32PRM840FSV 32 ГБ" -- a spec the product does not have,
# printed on the page, and worse, a key that SPLITS from the same television
# listed without its diagonal.
CATEGORIES_WITH_MEMORY: frozenset[str] = frozenset({"laptops"})

# A laptop listing's identity ends where its specification begins. Retailers
# delimit that boundary explicitly and consistently:
#     "Ноутбук Acer Predator Helios Neo 16S AI / U9-275HX / DDR5 32GB / ..."
# Everything after the first "/" is CPU, GPU, memory and panel -- specification,
# not name. Keeping it would key the laptop on its graphics card.
#
# This is the same instrument uzum.md section 5a already applies to uzum card
# titles (truncate at the first comma); the delimiter set is wider here because
# these are seven retailers' free-text titles rather than one API's field.
#
# Applied to model TOKENS only. Memory and colour are still read from the WHOLE
# title, so "Acer Aspire 8/512" keeps storage 512 and stays distinct from the
# 8/256 -- truncating those away would merge two real products.
_SPEC_DELIMITERS = re.compile(r"[/|(\[]")


def head_for_model(name: str) -> str:
    """The part of a title that names the product, before its specification."""
    if not name:
        return ""
    return _SPEC_DELIMITERS.split(name, 1)[0]


def cut_at_processor(tokens: list[str], category: str = "laptops") -> list[str]:
    """Drop everything from the first processor word onward.

    The "/" fence catches most laptop specifications, but retailers that omit
    it run the spec straight on from the name:

        "Ноутбук Acer Aspire Snapdragon X1 26100"
        "Ноутбук ASUS Vivobook 17 X1704VA Core 5-120U 16GB 512GB"

    A vendor word is a reliable marker that the name has ended, whereas the
    part numbers after it ("x1", "26100", "5") are indistinguishable from real
    model codes by shape -- which is why PROCESSORS lists only the words and
    this function handles the rest positionally.

    Positional truncation is only right where the word really is a spec
    boundary. On a category with no processor it is a name, and cutting there
    discards the rest of the name too -- see CATEGORIES_WITH_CPU.
    """
    if category not in CATEGORIES_WITH_CPU:
        return tokens
    for i, t in enumerate(tokens):
        if t in PROCESSORS:
            return tokens[:i]
    return tokens


# Product-line names. Identity treats every model token alike, but a NAME has
# to read the way the product is sold: "Vivobook Go 15", not "15 Go Vivobook".
# model_tokens is a frozenset, so display order has to be reconstructed, and
# alphabetical order puts the screen size first on almost every laptop.
FAMILY_WORDS: frozenset[str] = frozenset({
    # laptops
    "vivobook", "zenbook", "expertbook", "chromebook", "macbook", "thinkpad",
    "thinkbook", "ideapad", "yoga", "legion", "loq", "aspire", "swift",
    "predator", "nitro", "travelmate", "extensa", "pavilion", "envy",
    "elitebook", "probook", "omen", "victus", "vostro", "inspiron",
    "latitude", "precision", "xps", "modern", "prestige", "katana",
    "cyborg", "bravo", "sword", "raider", "stealth", "tuf", "rog", "gaming",
    # televisions / appliances product lines
    "bravia", "crystal", "frame", "serif", "neoqled", "smarttv",
    "alba", "naomi", "marvarid", "milagro", "apetito", "shaxrisabz",
    "grand", "forever", "city", "tashkent",
})


def ordered_non_phone_tokens(tokens: frozenset[str] | set[str]) -> list[str]:
    """Model tokens in the order a person writes them.

    Family name first ("Vivobook"), then the remaining words alphabetically,
    then catalogue codes, then bare figures such as a diagonal or a BTU class.
    Ordering is DISPLAY ONLY -- identity is the unordered set, so two listings
    that name the same tokens in different orders remain one product.
    """
    families, words, codes, figures = [], [], [], []
    for t in sorted(tokens):
        if t in FAMILY_WORDS:
            families.append(t)
        elif t.isdigit():
            figures.append(t)
        elif any(c.isdigit() for c in t):
            codes.append(t)
        else:
            words.append(t)
    figures.sort(key=lambda x: (len(x), x))
    return families + words + codes + figures


# Things sold ALONGSIDE the product, in the product's own category feed. A
# laptop bag is returned by a laptop sweep, carries a brand and a code, and so
# forms a perfectly valid key -- "Rivacase 7531" became a laptop. Unlike the
# phone case, nothing downstream rejects it: the price fence passes it and the
# key system accepts it, so it reaches the catalogue as a product.
_ACCESSORY = re.compile(
    r"\b("
    r"sumka\w*|сумк\w*|chexol\w*|чехол\w*|"
    r"kronshteyn\w*|кронштейн\w*|подставк\w*|крепление|крепление\w*|"
    r"pult\w*|пульт\w*|kabel\w*|кабел\w*|переходник\w*|удлинител\w*|"
    r"filtr\w*|фильтр\w*|мешок|мешк\w*|шланг\w*|картридж\w*|"
    r"lampa\w*|лампочк\w*|щетк\w*|щётк\w*|насадк\w*|"
    r"аксессуар\w*|aksessuar\w*|салфетк\w*|чистящ\w*|"
    r"стабилизатор\w*|подключение|установка|монтаж"
    r")\b",
    re.I,
)

# The same shape the phone denylist uses (sources/not_a_phone.py): a title that
# LEADS with the product noun is the product, and an accessory word inside it
# describes what is in the box. "Телевизор ... с пультом" is a television.
_LEADS_WITH_CATEGORY_NOUN = re.compile(
    r"^\s*(" + "|".join(sorted(CATEGORY_NOUNS, key=len, reverse=True)) + r")\b",
    re.I,
)


def is_accessory(name: str | None, category: str = "smartphones") -> bool:
    """True when a title in a category feed is an accessory, not the product.

    Conservative in the direction this project always chooses: a title that
    names its own category first is never an accessory, so the failure mode is
    admitting a bag rather than losing a laptop.
    """
    if category == "smartphones" or not name:
        return False
    if _LEADS_WITH_CATEGORY_NOUN.match(name.strip()):
        return False
    return bool(_ACCESSORY.search(name))


def is_model_token(token: str, category: str = "laptops") -> bool:
    """True when a token may stand as part of a non-phone product's identity.

    Deliberately permissive about SHAPE and strict about VOCABULARY: any word
    or code survives unless it is a known category noun, descriptor, marketing
    word or unit. Shape cannot separate "wgk264zxme" (identity) from "ddr5"
    (specification) -- both are letters plus digits -- so the vocabulary does
    the work, and every entry in it was measured against live titles.

    `category` decides one thing: whether the PROCESSORS words are part of
    that vocabulary. They are a specification on a computer and a model name
    on everything else -- see CATEGORIES_WITH_CPU. The default keeps the
    stricter laptop vocabulary, so a caller that does not pass a category
    gets exactly the behaviour this function always had.
    """
    stoplist = NON_MODEL if category in CATEGORIES_WITH_CPU else NON_MODEL_WITHOUT_CPU
    if not token or token in stoplist:
        return False
    # A bare unit-suffixed figure is a spec: "43дюйма", "512gb", "1200об".
    if re.fullmatch(r"\d+(?:gb|гб|tb|тб|кг|kg|л|lt|см|cm|вт|w|hz|гц|об|rpm|дюйм\w*)", token):
        return False
    # A lone letter is never identity; it is a stray initial from a split code.
    return len(token) > 1



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/matching/colors.py
================================================
"""Colour vocabulary.

Only BASE colour words are mapped. Finish/qualifier words (velvet, тёмно,
меланж) must never map: "Velvet Grey" -> {black, gray} would match a Black
listing. The base colour word is always present anyway, so qualifiers add
nothing but false positives.

An unresolvable colour must REJECT, not pass. Treating it as a wildcard let
"Конфетный" and "Хром" match both Awesome Gray and Awesome Navy at once.
"""

COLOR_MAP: dict[str, str] = {
    # --- english base words ---
    "black": "black", "midnight": "black", "obsidian": "black",
    "white": "white", "moonlight": "white", "cloud": "white",
    "silver": "silver", "meteor": "silver", "titanium": "silver",
    "gray": "gray", "grey": "gray", "space": "gray", "charcoal": "gray", "graphite": "gray",
    "gold": "gold", "desert": "gold", "sunrise": "gold", "champagne": "gold",
    "sandy": "gold", "sand": "gold",
    "blue": "blue", "cyan": "blue", "ocean": "blue", "aqua": "blue",
    "navy": "blue", "sky": "blue", "glacier": "blue", "denim": "blue",
    "red": "red", "scarlet": "red",
    "brown": "brown", "reddish": "brown", "coffee": "brown",
    "green": "green", "sage": "green", "mint": "green", "emerald": "green",
    "jade": "green", "forest": "green", "marrs": "green", "agate": "green",
    "purple": "purple", "violet": "purple",
    "lavender": "purple", "lilac": "purple", "plum": "purple",
    "orange": "orange", "cosmic": "orange", "amber": "orange", "coral": "orange",
    "pink": "pink", "rose": "pink",
    "beige": "beige", "cream": "beige", "ivory": "beige",
    "burgundy": "burgundy", "wine": "burgundy",
    "yellow": "yellow", "lime": "yellow",
    # --- russian (uzum Цвет values; see parsers/uzum.md section 8) ---
    "черный": "black", "черное": "black", "черная": "black",
    "угольный": "gray", "серый": "gray",
    "серебряный": "silver", "серебристый": "silver", "титановый": "silver",
    "синий": "blue", "голубой": "blue", "ледяной": "blue", "бирюзовый": "blue",
    "фиолетовый": "purple", "лавандовый": "purple", "сиреневый": "purple",
    "зеленый": "green", "мятный": "green",
    "оранжевый": "orange", "манго": "orange",
    "золотой": "gold", "золотистый": "gold", "песочный": "gold",
    "бежевый": "beige", "кремовый": "beige",
    "белый": "white", "молочный": "white",
    "бордовый": "burgundy", "коричневый": "brown", "алый": "red",
    "красный": "red", "розовый": "pink", "желтый": "yellow",
    # --- uzbek (uzum card/model strings) ---
    "qora": "black", "oq": "white", "kumush": "silver", "kulrang": "gray",
    "tilla": "gold", "oltin": "gold", "sariq": "yellow", "yashil": "green",
    "kok": "blue", "moviy": "blue", "muz": "blue",
    "binafsha": "purple", "pushti": "pink", "qizil": "red",
    "jigarrang": "brown", "bej": "beige",
    # --- latin transliterations in uz-locale listings ---
    "cherniy": "black", "chernyi": "black", "beliy": "white", "seriy": "gray",
    "siniy": "blue", "goluboy": "blue", "zeleniy": "green",
    "fioletoviy": "purple", "rozoviy": "pink", "zolotoy": "gold",
    "serebristiy": "silver",
}

# Words that modify a colour but are NOT colours. Never map these.
QUALIFIERS: frozenset[str] = frozenset({
    "velvet", "light", "dark", "golden", "deep", "matte", "glossy", "cobalt",
    "темно", "светло", "небесно", "металлик", "меланж",
    "toq", "och",          # uzbek: dark / light
})


def colors_in_order(tokens: list[str]) -> list[str]:
    """Mapped colours, in the order their words appear."""
    return [COLOR_MAP[t] for t in tokens if t in COLOR_MAP]


def primary_color(tokens: list[str]) -> str | None:
    """The single canonical colour: the LAST mapped token.

    In both English and Russian colour names the base colour comes last
    ("Space Black", "Glacier Blue", "Тёмно-серый"), so the final mapped
    word is the identity and earlier ones are shading.
    """
    found = colors_in_order(tokens)
    return found[-1] if found else None



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/matching/features.py
================================================
"""Feature extraction: normalised name -> structured identity."""
import re
from dataclasses import dataclass

from narxnoma.matching.brands import (
    BRAND_ALIASES,
    BRANDS,
    NON_PHONE_ALIASES,
    NON_PHONE_BRANDS,
    canonical_brand,
    canonical_non_phone_brand,
)
from narxnoma.matching.categories import (
    CATEGORIES_WITH_MEMORY,
    cut_at_processor,
    head_for_model,
    is_model_token,
)
from narxnoma.matching.colors import COLOR_MAP, QUALIFIERS, colors_in_order, primary_color
from narxnoma.matching.memory import scan_memory
from narxnoma.matching.normalize import normalize

# "galaxy" is deliberately absent: mediapark lists the A07 as
# "SAMSUNG SM-A075F A07" with no "Galaxy". Identity comes from a07/a17/s26.
MODEL_MODIFIERS: frozenset[str] = frozenset({
    "pro", "ultra", "lite", "plus", "max", "mini", "note", "redmi",
    "air", "fe", "se",          # genuine designators, not marketing words
    # Family words for the Galaxy Z line. Without them both "Galaxy Z Fold 7"
    # and "Galaxy Z Flip 7" reduce to model_tokens={'7'} and collapse onto the
    # identical key samsung-7-12-256-black, merging two products whose prices
    # differ by roughly a factor of two.
    "fold", "flip",
})

NON_MODEL_TOKENS: frozenset[str] = frozenset({
    "gb", "гб", "tb", "тб", "smartfon", "смартфон", "iphone",
    "apple", "samsung", "honor", "xiaomi", "vivo", "oppo", "realme",
    "tecno", "infinix", "poco", "nothing", "google", "asus", "huawei",
    "motorola", "zte", "meizu",
    "esim", "nanosim", "sim", "nano", "point",
    "rang", "smartfoni", "telefon", "chidamli", "zarbaga",   # uz filler
})

# Sub-brands must be probed BEFORE their parents, so a "Redmi Note 15" is
# recognised by "redmi" rather than falling through to a bare "xiaomi".
_BRAND_PROBE_ORDER: list[str] = list(BRAND_ALIASES) + BRANDS

_SKU_MIN_LEN = 5
_PREFIX_NOISE = re.compile(r"\b(смартфон|smartfon\w*)\b")

# Probed with WORD BOUNDARIES, unlike the phone list below, which is a plain
# substring scan. The appliance vocabulary contains genuinely short names --
# "lg", "arg", "hp" -- and a substring scan finds "arg" inside "charger" and
# "large". A trailing digit still counts as a boundary so the glued "Hp15-fd00"
# that idea and olcha both publish is recognised.
#
# ...except for these, where the same allowance reads a brand out of somebody
# else's SKU. Adding "bq" for BQ's televisions relabelled two ASUS laptops:
#     "Ноутбук ASUS VivoBook X1502VA-BQ827 / i7-13620H / DDR4 16GB / ..."
#     "ASUS Vivobook 15 X1502VA-BQ982 i7-13620H/16/512GB Cool Silver"
# "-BQ827" is ASUS's own suffix, normalize() splits the hyphen to "bq827", and
# the trailing-digit boundary matched it -- so both machines were keyed as a BQ
# and merged with each other. Caught by reading every multi-store variant the
# change produced, which is the only thing that can catch a substitution: the
# result looked exactly like a healthy two-store match.
#
# A strict boundary still finds the televisions ("Телевизор BQ 43FS36B" has
# "bq" as its own token) and no longer finds the ASUS suffix. Two characters
# is the real problem -- there are simply more SKUs containing "bq" than
# containing "hofmann" -- but "hp" and "lg" are two characters as well and
# their digit-glued forms are measured and real, so this is an opt-out per
# brand rather than a rule about length.
_NO_DIGIT_GLUE: frozenset[str] = frozenset({"bq"})

_NON_PHONE_PROBE_ORDER: list[str] = list(NON_PHONE_ALIASES) + NON_PHONE_BRANDS
_NON_PHONE_BRAND_RE: dict[str, re.Pattern[str]] = {
    b: re.compile(rf"\b{re.escape(b)}\b" if b in _NO_DIGIT_GLUE
                  else rf"\b{re.escape(b)}(?=\b|\d)")
    for b in _NON_PHONE_PROBE_ORDER
}


def _detect_brand(n: str, category: str) -> str | None:
    """Brand from a normalised name.

    The phone path is left EXACTLY as it was -- a substring scan in the
    documented probe order -- because 14,860 golden rows pin its behaviour and
    this change is not the place to move it.
    """
    if category == "smartphones":
        for b in _BRAND_PROBE_ORDER:
            if b in n:
                return canonical_brand(b)
        return None
    for b in _NON_PHONE_PROBE_ORDER:
        if _NON_PHONE_BRAND_RE[b].search(n):
            return canonical_non_phone_brand(b)
    return None


def _non_phone_model_tokens(
    name: str | None, brand: str | None, reserved: set[str],
    category: str = "laptops",
) -> set[str]:
    """Identity tokens for a TV, laptop, fridge, AC, washer or oven.

    Reads the HEAD of the title only (see categories.head_for_model): a
    laptop's specification follows a delimiter and is not part of its name.
    Memory and colour were already taken from the whole title by the caller,
    so truncating here costs no identity field.

    `category` reaches the two processor rules and nothing else -- a CPU name
    is a specification on a computer and a model name on an air conditioner.
    See categories.CATEGORIES_WITH_CPU.
    """
    head = normalize(head_for_model(name or ""))
    tokens = set(cut_at_processor(head.split(), category))
    known_brands = set(_NON_PHONE_PROBE_ORDER)
    out: set[str] = set()
    for t in tokens:
        # The brand is an axis of the key already; repeating it inside
        # model_tokens would let "LG" and "LG LG" become two products.
        if t in known_brands or canonical_non_phone_brand(t) == brand:
            continue
        if t in NON_MODEL_TOKENS or t in COLOR_MAP or t in QUALIFIERS:
            continue
        if t in reserved:
            continue
        if _CYRILLIC_WORD.fullmatch(t):
            # An appliance's model name is written in Latin -- "Shaxrisabz",
            # "Naomi Pro", "Frost", "HD276FND". A Cyrillic WORD in the title is
            # descriptive: a colour or finish the palette does not know
            # ("Матовый", "Асфальт", "Мокрый"), or a Russian abbreviation for
            # the appliance type ("Кп" for комбинированная плита, "Гп" for
            # газовая плита).
            #
            # MEASURED: those three finish words alone split one fridge into
            # three products -- "Artel Frost HD276FND", "... Матовый ...", and
            # "... Асфальт Мокрый ..." -- at three different prices. Colour is
            # deliberately NOT identity outside phones, so a colour word that
            # merely escaped the palette must not become identity by accident.
            #
            # Enumerating every finish word cannot work: retailers invent them.
            # The script is the reliable signal, and the failure direction is
            # safe -- a product genuinely named in Cyrillic keeps its brand and
            # any Latin tokens, and becomes unkeyable only if it has none.
            continue
        if not is_model_token(t, category):
            continue
        out.add(t)
    return _collapse_to_catalogue_code(out)


_CYRILLIC_WORD = re.compile(r"[а-я]+")

# A manufacturer's catalogue code identifies exactly one product -- that is
# what it is FOR. Seven characters is the floor measured across the live
# catalogue: every real code observed is at least eight ("HD276FND",
# "B392SMBB", "RT62K7110SL", "UE55DU7100UXUZ", "ARTSID3AW12BE"), while the
# spec fragments that must not be mistaken for one are shorter ("i7", "16s",
# "1255u", "a315", "ddr5").
_CODE_MIN_LEN = 7


def _is_catalogue_code(token: str) -> bool:
    return (
        len(token) >= _CODE_MIN_LEN
        and any(c.isdigit() for c in token)
        and any(c.isalpha() for c in token)
    )


def _collapse_to_catalogue_code(tokens: set[str]) -> set[str]:
    """When a title states a catalogue code, the code IS the identity.

    Retailers decorate the same code differently, and every decoration became
    a separate product. All of these are ONE Samsung fridge:

        "Холодильник Samsung RB29FERNDSA"
        "Холодильник Samsung 290L RB29FERNDSA"     (capacity)
        "Холодильник Samsung Inox RT62K7110SL"     (finish, in Latin)
        "Холодильник LG Apzqcis Gn B392SMBB"       (an internal code fragment)
        "Кондиционер Artel Everest ARTSID3AW12BE"  (product line)

    Measured before this rule: 1,235 pairs where one variant's tokens were a
    strict subset of another's in the same brand and category. The code cannot
    collide -- two different products never share one -- so collapsing onto it
    can only merge listings that genuinely are the same product.

    A title with NO code keeps all its tokens: "Телевизор MoonX 32" and
    "Кондиционер Midea Naomi Pro 09" have nothing else to be identified by.
    """
    codes = {t for t in tokens if _is_catalogue_code(t)}
    return codes or tokens


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Features:
    brand: str | None
    ram_gb: int | None
    storage_gb: int | None
    colors: frozenset[str]
    primary_color: str | None
    model_tokens: frozenset[str]
    normalized: str


def extract_features(name: str | None, category: str = "smartphones") -> Features:
    """Normalised name -> structured identity.

    `category` selects the identity model. It defaults to smartphones so every
    existing caller, test and golden-corpus row keeps the exact behaviour it
    had; the non-phone branch is additive. See categories.py for why a
    television cannot be read with a phone's rules.
    """
    normalized = normalize(name)
    n = _PREFIX_NOISE.sub("", normalized).strip()
    n = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", n)

    brand = _detect_brand(n, category)

    ram, storage, stated_ram = scan_memory(name, n, brand)

    tokens = n.split()
    token_set = set(tokens)
    colors = frozenset(colors_in_order(tokens))
    primary = primary_color(tokens)

    # stated_ram is reserved as well as ram -- see the note on the phone path
    # below for why. Both branches share the set.
    reserved_figures = {
        str(storage or ""), str(ram or ""), str(stated_ram or ""),
        "5g", "4g", "0",
    }

    if category != "smartphones":
        if category not in CATEGORIES_WITH_MEMORY:
            # Not "unknown" -- ABSENT. A television has no RAM and no SSD, so a
            # figure that looks like one is a diagonal or a litre capacity that
            # happened to land in the plausible-storage set.
            ram = storage = None
            reserved_figures = {"5g", "4g", "0"}
        return Features(
            brand=brand,
            ram_gb=ram,
            storage_gb=storage,
            colors=colors,
            primary_color=primary,
            model_tokens=frozenset(
                _non_phone_model_tokens(name, brand, reserved_figures, category)
            ),
            normalized=n,
        )

    # stated_ram is reserved as well as ram. For Apple, ram is suppressed to
    # None (Apple publishes no RAM), but the figure the reseller wrote is
    # still a memory figure -- without reserving it, "iPhone 17 Pro 12/256GB"
    # keeps "12" as a MODEL token and keys as apple-12-17-pro-256-silver,
    # which splits the product exactly as the RAM did and additionally
    # hard-rejects the listing on the reverse model-token check.
    reserved = reserved_figures
    model_tokens: set[str] = set()
    for t in token_set:
        if t in NON_MODEL_TOKENS or t in COLOR_MAP or t in QUALIFIERS or t in reserved:
            continue
        if re.fullmatch(r"\d+(?:gb|гб|tb|тб)", t):
            continue
        # Skip SKU/catalogue codes (letters+digits, len >= 5): "a075f",
        # "sm-a175f", and spec-blurb fragments like "120hz", "200mp", "6500mah".
        #
        # 5 is measured, not guessed. Across all 14,860 golden rows, accept/reject
        # agreement is 99.993% at 5 and 99.987% at 6, 7, 8 and with the rule off
        # entirely -- raising it is strictly worse. At 6 the rule stops discarding
        # 5-character mixed tokens, so "120hz" and "200mp" survive as model tokens
        # and then reject a legitimate Redmi Note 15 Pro match: the marketing-blurb
        # failure uzum.md section 5a exists to prevent, recreated by loosening the
        # rule that prevents it. See test_sku_min_len_is_measured_not_guessed.
        #
        # KNOWN GAP: this also discards real designators of 5+ characters --
        # "reno15", "magic8", "fold4". Those products are currently unkeyable. The
        # fix is a targeted designator allowlist applied BEFORE this rule, not a
        # different threshold; length cannot separate the two populations, because
        # real designators and catalogue codes overlap in length.
        if len(t) >= _SKU_MIN_LEN and any(c.isalpha() for c in t) and any(c.isdigit() for c in t):
            continue
        if any(c.isdigit() for c in t):
            if t.isdigit() and int(t) in {
                (storage or -1), (ram or -1), (stated_ram or -1)
            }:
                continue
            model_tokens.add(t)
        elif t in MODEL_MODIFIERS:
            model_tokens.add(t)

    return Features(
        brand=brand,
        ram_gb=ram,
        storage_gb=storage,
        colors=colors,
        primary_color=primary,
        model_tokens=frozenset(model_tokens),
        normalized=n,
    )



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/matching/keys.py
================================================
"""Canonical variant identity.

Canonical products are permanent identities; offers are temporary
observations. A key is complete only when every identity field is known --
brand, model, RAM, storage, colour, SIM. Anything less is a PartialKey and
must be resolved unambiguously or parked.
"""
from dataclasses import dataclass

from narxnoma.matching.brands import (
    BRAND_LABELS,
    BRANDS_WITHOUT_PUBLISHED_RAM,
    brand_label,
)
from narxnoma.matching.categories import ordered_non_phone_tokens
from narxnoma.matching.features import Features

SIM_KINDS = ("dual", "esim_only", "unknown")

# Product categories the crawler can sweep. V1 was smartphones only; Plan 5
# (2026-08-10) extends to the other big electronics lines each source sells.
CATEGORIES = (
    "smartphones",
    "televisions",
    "laptops",
    "acs",
    "fridges",
    "washers",
    "ovens",
)

# RU display prefix per category, used by display_name() (crawler UI language
# is Russian; the web layer localises separately).
CATEGORY_PREFIX = {
    "smartphones": "Смартфон",
    "televisions": "Телевизор",
    "laptops": "Ноутбук",
    "acs": "Кондиционер",
    "fridges": "Холодильник",
    "washers": "Стиральная машина",
    "ovens": "Духовой шкаф",
}

# Categories whose identity is NOT the phone signature. Phones key on
# brand+model+RAM+storage+colour+sim; a TV, fridge or oven keys on
# brand+model alone (colour/capacity/usage are not identity).
NON_PHONE_CATEGORIES = frozenset(CATEGORIES) - {"smartphones"}

_COLOR_RU = {
    "black": "Черный", "white": "Белый", "silver": "Серебристый",
    "gray": "Серый", "gold": "Золотой", "blue": "Синий", "red": "Красный",
    "brown": "Коричневый", "green": "Зеленый", "purple": "Фиолетовый",
    "orange": "Оранжевый", "pink": "Розовый", "beige": "Бежевый",
    "burgundy": "Бордовый", "yellow": "Желтый",
}

# Model tokens must be ordered deliberately: model_tokens is a frozenset, so
# alphabetical sorting produces "17 Max Pro", "15 Note Redmi" and "Fe S25".
# Family words, read first. "fold"/"flip" join them for the same reason
# "note" is here: the real name is "Galaxy Z Fold 7", never "Galaxy Z 7 Fold".
_MODEL_PREFIX_ORDER = ["redmi", "note", "fold", "flip"]
_MODEL_SUFFIX_RANK = {
    "pro": 1, "plus": 2, "max": 3, "ultra": 4, "air": 5,
    "fe": 6, "se": 7, "lite": 8, "mini": 9,
}
_UPPERCASE_TOKENS = {"fe", "se"}                  # initialisms, not words


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VariantKey:
    brand: str
    model_tokens: frozenset[str]
    ram_gb: int | None        # None is legitimate for brands that omit RAM
    storage_gb: int | None    # None outside smartphones (a TV has no storage)
    color: str | None         # None outside smartphones (colour is not identity)
    sim_kind: str | None      # None outside smartphones (no SIM)
    category: str = "smartphones"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PartialKey:
    brand: str | None
    model_tokens: frozenset[str]
    ram_gb: int | None
    storage_gb: int | None
    color: str | None
    sim_kind: str
    category: str = "smartphones"


def _check_category(category: str) -> None:
    if category not in CATEGORIES:
        raise ValueError(f"category must be one of {CATEGORIES}, got {category!r}")


def variant_key(f: Features, sim_kind: str, category: str = "smartphones") -> VariantKey | None:
    """A complete canonical identity, or None if any identity field is unknown.

    RAM is required only for brands that publish it. Apple does not, and
    extract_memory deliberately refuses to infer it -- so requiring RAM here
    would make every iPhone permanently unkeyable.

    Non-phone categories (TVs, laptops, ACs, fridges, washers, ovens) key on
    brand + model alone; colour, SIM, RAM and storage are NOT identity for
    them (a TV's black bezel doesn't distinguish products, and requiring a
    memory figure would make most appliances unkeyable).
    """
    _check_category(category)
    if not (f.brand and f.model_tokens):
        return None
    if category in NON_PHONE_CATEGORIES:
        return VariantKey(
            brand=f.brand,
            model_tokens=f.model_tokens,
            ram_gb=None,
            storage_gb=f.storage_gb or None,
            color=None,
            sim_kind=None,
            category=category,
        )
    if not f.storage_gb or not f.primary_color:
        return None
    if f.brand not in BRANDS_WITHOUT_PUBLISHED_RAM and not f.ram_gb:
        return None
    if sim_kind not in SIM_KINDS:
        raise ValueError(f"sim_kind must be one of {SIM_KINDS}, got {sim_kind!r}")
    if sim_kind == "unknown":
        sim_kind = "dual"
    return VariantKey(
        brand=f.brand,
        model_tokens=f.model_tokens,
        ram_gb=f.ram_gb,
        storage_gb=f.storage_gb,
        color=f.primary_color,
        sim_kind=sim_kind,
        category=category,
    )


def partial_key(f: Features, sim_kind: str, category: str = "smartphones") -> PartialKey:
    _check_category(category)
    return PartialKey(
        brand=f.brand,
        model_tokens=f.model_tokens,
        ram_gb=f.ram_gb,
        storage_gb=f.storage_gb,
        color=f.primary_color,
        sim_kind=sim_kind,
        category=category,
    )


def _compatible(partial: PartialKey, full: VariantKey) -> bool:
    if partial.brand is not None and partial.brand != full.brand:
        return False
    if partial.model_tokens != full.model_tokens:
        return False
    if partial.ram_gb is not None and partial.ram_gb != full.ram_gb:
        return False
    if partial.storage_gb is not None and partial.storage_gb != full.storage_gb:
        return False
    if partial.color is not None and partial.color != full.color:
        return False
    if partial.sim_kind != "unknown" and partial.sim_kind != full.sim_kind:
        return False
    return True


def resolve_partial(partial: PartialKey, candidates: list[VariantKey]) -> VariantKey | None:
    """Attach only when EXACTLY ONE canonical variant is compatible.

    Zero matches or two-plus matches both return None. Never guess: an
    ambiguous offer belongs in unresolved_offer, not on a product page.
    """
    matches = [c for c in candidates if _compatible(partial, c)]
    return matches[0] if len(matches) == 1 else None


def ordered_model_tokens(tokens: frozenset[str]) -> list[str]:
    """Family prefix, then designators, then modifiers in canonical rank.

    Alphabetical order is wrong: "17 Pro Max" sorts to "17 Max Pro" and
    "Redmi Note 15" to "15 Note Redmi".
    """
    prefixes = [t for t in _MODEL_PREFIX_ORDER if t in tokens]
    suffixes = sorted(
        (t for t in tokens if t in _MODEL_SUFFIX_RANK),
        key=lambda t: _MODEL_SUFFIX_RANK[t],
    )
    used = set(prefixes) | set(suffixes)
    designators = sorted(t for t in tokens if t not in used)
    return prefixes + designators + suffixes


def _token_label(token: str) -> str:
    if token in _UPPERCASE_TOKENS or any(c.isdigit() for c in token):
        return token.upper()
    return token.capitalize()


def display_name(key: VariantKey) -> str:
    """Generated from the key -- never copied from a retailer.

    Copying retailer names is exactly how a competitor ended up with
    "Cosmic orange" and "Cosmic Orange" as two separate products.

    EVERY field of the key must reach the name, or the generator reintroduces
    the confusion it exists to prevent. sim_kind was omitted, so an eSIM-only
    phone and its dual-SIM sibling -- different products, priced differently --
    rendered byte-identical; 42 pairs of live variants were indistinguishable,
    including "Смартфон Apple iPhone 17 Pro 256 ГБ, Оранжевый" twice, at
    16,985,000 and 17,690,000. test_no_two_distinct_keys_share_a_display_name
    states the invariant directly.

    Non-phone categories: "Телевизор Samsung UE55 Неодим" -- colour/SIM are
    absent from the key, and storage only appears when the spec carries one
    (a laptop's 512GB SSD does; a fridge's capacity does not).
    """
    prefix = CATEGORY_PREFIX.get(key.category, key.category.capitalize())
    brand = brand_label(key.brand, key.category)
    order = (
        ordered_non_phone_tokens
        if key.category in NON_PHONE_CATEGORIES
        else ordered_model_tokens
    )
    model = " ".join(_token_label(t) for t in order(key.model_tokens))
    if key.category in NON_PHONE_CATEGORIES:
        parts = [prefix, brand, model]
        if key.storage_gb:
            parts.append(f"{key.storage_gb} ГБ")
        return " ".join(parts)
    color = _COLOR_RU.get(key.color, key.color.capitalize())
    memory = f"{key.ram_gb}/{key.storage_gb}" if key.ram_gb else str(key.storage_gb)
    sim = " (eSIM)" if key.sim_kind == "esim_only" else ""
    return f"{prefix} {brand} {model} {memory} ГБ, {color}{sim}"


def slug(key: VariantKey) -> str:
    order = (
        ordered_non_phone_tokens
        if key.category in NON_PHONE_CATEGORIES
        else ordered_model_tokens
    )
    parts = [key.brand, *order(key.model_tokens)]
    if key.ram_gb:
        parts.append(str(key.ram_gb))
    if key.storage_gb:
        parts.append(str(key.storage_gb))
    if key.color:
        parts.append(key.color)
    if key.sim_kind == "esim_only":
        parts.append("esim")
    # Non-phone slugs are category-prefixed so a TV and a laptop can never
    # claim the same URL. Phone slugs are left unchanged for DB compatibility
    # (the existing ~1,400 rows key on the current format).
    if key.category in NON_PHONE_CATEGORIES:
        parts.insert(0, key.category)
    return "-".join(parts)



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/matching/memory.py
================================================
"""RAM and storage extraction.

The lookbehind (?<![a-zа-я\\d]) before each figure is what stops a model
code being read as memory. See docs/parsers/_matching-engine.md.
"""
import re

from narxnoma.matching.brands import BRANDS_WITHOUT_PUBLISHED_RAM

_UNIT = re.compile(r"(?<![a-zа-я\d])(\d{1,4})\s*(gb|гб|tb|тб)\b")

# Zero-width: the two figures are captured inside a lookahead instead of
# being consumed, so re.finditer's matches can overlap. A plain consuming
# regex can only offer non-overlapping candidates, and a model number
# directly adjacent to RAM ("Redmi Note 15 12/256" normalises to
# "redmi note 15 12 256") makes the model+RAM pair ("15", "12") match
# first, consuming "12" and hiding the real memory pair ("12", "256") from
# ever being scanned. Matching zero-width lets the scan advance one
# character at a time and consider "12 256" too, so the plausibility check
# below can reject "15 12" and still go on to find the real pair.
_STORAGE_PAIR = re.compile(r"(?<![a-zа-я\d])(?=(\d{1,3})\s+(\d{2,4})(?!\d))")
_RAM_PAIR = re.compile(r"(?<![a-zа-я\d])(?=(\d{1,2})\s+(\d{2,4})(?!\d))")
_PAIR_SEPARATOR = re.compile(r"\d\s*[/\\+|]\s*\d")

_MAX_PLAUSIBLE_RAM = 24

# Plausible RAM/storage sizes for the bare-pair fallbacks ("12 256" with no
# unit and no separator). A model number like the "7" in "Galaxy Z Fold 7
# 512 ГБ" or the "5" in "Galaxy Watch 5 44mm" has the same shape as a real
# memory pair; restricting candidates to values retailers actually sell
# stops the model number from being absorbed as RAM or storage.
_PLAUSIBLE_RAM_GB = frozenset({1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 16, 18, 24})
_PLAUSIBLE_STORAGE_GB = frozenset({32, 64, 128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048})

# Last-resort: a storage figure standing entirely alone, with neither a unit
# nor a second adjacent figure. Every rule above needs one or the other, so
# "Apple iPhone 12 pro 128 Синий" read as no memory at all and the listing
# became unkeyable -- while "Apple iPhone 12 128 Алый" read 128 correctly,
# purely because the model number happened to sit next to the capacity.
#
# The shape is common rather than exotic: uzum states memory in a per-SKU
# characteristic whose value is often the bare figure (Память: "128"), and
# that value reaches the name with nothing beside it.
_LONE_FIGURE = re.compile(r"(?<![a-zа-я\d])(\d{2,4})(?!\d)")

# A lone figure is much weaker evidence than a pair, so it needs screening a
# pair does not: these units mark a figure as something else entirely, and
# 64 (МП), 128 (mp) and 512 are all plausible capacities sitting in blurbs.
_OTHER_UNIT = re.compile(
    r"^\s*(mp|мп|mah|мач|hz|гц|mm|мм|w|вт|nm|нм|dpi|ppi|fps|core|ядер)\b"
)


def scan_memory(
    raw: str | None, normalized: str, brand: str | None
) -> tuple[int | None, int | None, int | None]:
    """Return (ram_gb, storage_gb, stated_ram_gb).

    ``stated_ram_gb`` is the RAM figure a retailer actually wrote, BEFORE the
    Apple suppression below removes it from identity. Callers that build model
    tokens need it: a suppressed figure is still a memory figure, and must not
    be allowed to fall through and become a model token instead.
    """
    ram: int | None = None
    storage: int | None = None

    # Collect ALL unit-bearing figures. Sellers write "8ГБ/256ГБ", where a
    # single-match regex reads the RAM as the storage.
    units: list[int] = []
    for m in _UNIT.finditer(normalized):
        val = int(m.group(1))
        if m.group(2) in ("tb", "тб"):
            val *= 1024
        units.append(val)

    if len(units) >= 2:
        storage = max(units)
        if min(units) <= _MAX_PLAUSIBLE_RAM:
            ram = min(units)
    elif len(units) == 1:
        if units[0] >= 32:
            storage = units[0]
        else:
            ram = units[0]

    if storage is None:                      # bare "6 128" form
        for m in _STORAGE_PAIR.finditer(normalized):
            a, b = int(m.group(1)), int(m.group(2))
            if max(a, b) not in _PLAUSIBLE_STORAGE_GB:
                continue
            storage = max(a, b)
            break

    if storage is None:                      # lone "128" form -- see _LONE_FIGURE
        for m in _LONE_FIGURE.finditer(normalized):
            val = int(m.group(1))
            if val not in _PLAUSIBLE_STORAGE_GB:
                continue
            if _OTHER_UNIT.match(normalized[m.end():]):
                continue
            storage = val
            break

    # A bare "12 256" pair normally means RAM/storage -- but "iPhone 16 256 GB"
    # has the identical shape, and reading 16 as RAM deletes the model number
    # from model_tokens, making iPhone 16 / 17 / Air interchangeable.
    # normalize() has already erased the "/" that tells them apart, so test the
    # RAW string, and never infer RAM for Apple (which does not publish it).
    has_separator = bool(_PAIR_SEPARATOR.search(str(raw or "")))
    if ram is None and (has_separator or brand not in BRANDS_WITHOUT_PUBLISHED_RAM):
        for m in _RAM_PAIR.finditer(normalized):
            a, b = int(m.group(1)), int(m.group(2))
            if min(a, b) not in _PLAUSIBLE_RAM_GB or max(a, b) not in _PLAUSIBLE_STORAGE_GB:
                continue
            ram = min(a, b)
            break

    # Global Apple-RAM suppression, applied to EVERY path (unit-bearing,
    # bare-pair and separator alike) rather than only to the bare-pair
    # fallback. Apple does not publish RAM, so any RAM figure appearing in an
    # Apple listing came from a reseller's own wording and must never become
    # identity. Guarding only the bare-pair path let "iPhone 17 Pro 12/256GB"
    # yield ram=12 through the unit-bearing pass while "iPhone 17 Pro 256 ГБ"
    # yielded None, splitting one phone into two canonical products
    # (apple-17-pro-12-256-silver and apple-17-pro-256-silver) -- a split
    # offer pool and two different displayed prices.
    stated_ram = ram
    if brand in BRANDS_WITHOUT_PUBLISHED_RAM:
        ram = None

    return ram, storage, stated_ram


def extract_memory(
    raw: str | None, normalized: str, brand: str | None
) -> tuple[int | None, int | None]:
    """Return (ram_gb, storage_gb). Either may be None."""
    ram, storage, _ = scan_memory(raw, normalized, brand)
    return ram, storage



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/matching/normalize.py
================================================
"""String normalisation. See parsers/_matching-engine.md section 1."""
import re

_REPLACEMENTS = [
    ("–", "-"), ("—", "-"),   # en dash, em dash
    (",", " "), ("/", " "), ("|", " "),
    ("ё", "е"),               # ё -> е
]

# Latin/Cyrillic lookalikes. Retailers really do mix the two scripts inside one
# word, and the two spellings are visually identical, so nothing downstream can
# tell them apart -- they simply become two products.
#
# MEASURED on the live catalogue: "Tелевизор Artel A32PHB100" (Latin T),
# "Cтиральная машина Bosch" (Latin C), "Стиральная машинa" and "Газовые плитa"
# (Latin a), and "Автохолодильник Alpicool С20" (CYRILLIC С inside a model
# code, where every other listing writes a Latin C). The project ledger already
# recorded uzum shipping "Cмартфон" with a Latin C for the same reason.
#
# Keyed by the LOWERCASE form, because normalize() lowercases first: an
# uppercase Latin T has already become "t" by the time this runs.
_LAT_TO_CYR = str.maketrans("abcehkmoptxy", "авсенкмортху")
_CYR_TO_LAT = str.maketrans("авсенкмортху", "abcehkmoptxy")

_HAS_CYR = re.compile(r"[а-я]")
_HAS_LAT = re.compile(r"[a-z]")
_HAS_DIGIT = re.compile(r"\d")


def _fold_homoglyphs(token: str) -> str:
    """Force one script per token, so two spellings cannot become two products.

    Which script wins depends on what the token IS, because the two populations
    pull opposite ways:

    - a token carrying a DIGIT is a model code ("с20", "a32phch010"), and model
      codes are Latin. Folding Cyrillic -> Latin makes "С20" and "C20" one code.
    - a token of letters only is a Russian word ("машинa", "tелевизор"), so the
      stray Latin character folds INTO Cyrillic.

    A token already written in one script is left completely alone: the
    translation only fires when both scripts are present, which is the only
    situation where one of them must be a typo.
    """
    cyr = len(_HAS_CYR.findall(token))
    lat = len(_HAS_LAT.findall(token))
    if not cyr:
        return token
    # A token carrying a digit is a model code, and model codes are Latin --
    # even when EVERY letter in it was typed in Cyrillic ("Alpicool С20").
    if _HAS_DIGIT.search(token):
        return token.translate(_CYR_TO_LAT)
    if not lat:
        return token
    # Letters only, both scripts: the minority script is the typo. Measured
    # case that forces this to be a majority test rather than a fixed
    # direction -- "PRЕMIER" is a Latin brand containing one Cyrillic Е, so
    # folding Latin->Cyrillic produced the unmatchable "рrемiеr" and lost the
    # brand entirely, while "машинa" is a Cyrillic word with one Latin a.
    return token.translate(_CYR_TO_LAT if lat > cyr else _LAT_TO_CYR)


def normalize(s: str | None) -> str:
    """Lowercase, fold separators, strip punctuation, collapse whitespace."""
    if not s:
        return ""
    t = str(s).lower().strip()
    for a, b in _REPLACEMENTS:
        t = t.replace(a, b)
    # '+' becomes ' plus ' ONLY as a trailing model marker (e.g. "Pro+").
    # A '+' inside "SIM+eSIM" is followed by a letter, so it becomes a space.
    t = re.sub(r"\+(?=\s|$)", " plus ", t)
    t = t.replace("+", " ")
    t = re.sub(r"[^\w\s]", " ", t, flags=re.UNICODE)
    t = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", t).strip()
    # Per token, after splitting: a mixed-script token is a typo in one script
    # or the other, and the two spellings are indistinguishable to a reader.
    return " ".join(_fold_homoglyphs(tok) for tok in t.split())



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/matching/scoring.py
================================================
"""Target-vs-candidate scoring.

Hard reject beats accumulation: a wrong storage can never be out-scored by
matching brand + colour.
"""
import re

from narxnoma.matching.features import Features

ACCEPT_THRESHOLD = 30
HARD_REJECT = -100


def _model_tokens_missing(needed: frozenset[str], other: Features) -> str | None:
    other_tokens = set(other.normalized.split())
    for mt in needed:
        # The fallback must be word-boundary anchored, not a raw substring
        # test: "6" is a substring of "16", so an iPhone 16 satisfied an
        # iPhone 6 target's model token.
        if mt not in other_tokens and not re.search(
            rf"\b{re.escape(mt)}\b", other.normalized
        ):
            return mt
    return None


def score_match(target: Features, cand: Features) -> int:
    score = 0

    if target.brand and cand.brand:
        if target.brand != cand.brand:
            return HARD_REJECT
        score += 10

    if target.storage_gb and cand.storage_gb:
        if target.storage_gb != cand.storage_gb:
            return HARD_REJECT
        score += 20
    elif target.storage_gb and not cand.storage_gb:
        return HARD_REJECT          # kills accessories for free
    elif cand.storage_gb and not target.storage_gb:
        # Symmetric: a target with no storage would otherwise score exactly 30
        # (brand 10 + model 15 + colour 5) against a candidate of ANY capacity,
        # so "iPhone 17 Pro Black" accepted a 256 GB and a 1 TB listing alike
        # and pooled their prices. Storage is a hard identity field in both
        # directions -- unlike RAM, which stays deliberately asymmetric below.
        return HARD_REJECT

    # Asymmetric on RAM: a MISSING ram is "unknown, don't reject"; a
    # MISMATCHED ram is a hard reject. Many retailers omit RAM.
    if target.ram_gb and cand.ram_gb:
        if target.ram_gb != cand.ram_gb:
            return HARD_REJECT
        score += 10

    if _model_tokens_missing(target.model_tokens, cand) is not None:
        return HARD_REJECT
    # The reverse direction is what stops "17 Pro Max" matching "17 Pro".
    if _model_tokens_missing(cand.model_tokens, target) is not None:
        return HARD_REJECT
    if target.model_tokens or cand.model_tokens:
        score += 15

    if target.colors:
        if not cand.colors:
            return HARD_REJECT      # unresolvable colour must reject, not pass
        if not (target.colors & cand.colors):
            return HARD_REJECT
        score += 5

    return score


def why(target: Features, cand: Features) -> str:
    """Explain a rejection. Mirrors score_match exactly."""
    if target.brand and cand.brand and target.brand != cand.brand:
        return f"brand {cand.brand}!={target.brand}"
    if target.storage_gb and cand.storage_gb and target.storage_gb != cand.storage_gb:
        return f"storage {cand.storage_gb}!={target.storage_gb}"
    if target.storage_gb and not cand.storage_gb:
        return "cand has NO storage"
    if cand.storage_gb and not target.storage_gb:
        return "target has NO storage"
    if target.ram_gb and cand.ram_gb and target.ram_gb != cand.ram_gb:
        return f"ram {cand.ram_gb}!={target.ram_gb}"
    missing = _model_tokens_missing(target.model_tokens, cand)
    if missing is not None:
        return f"target token '{missing}' missing"
    extra = _model_tokens_missing(cand.model_tokens, target)
    if extra is not None:
        return f"cand token '{extra}' extra"
    if target.colors and not cand.colors:
        return "cand colour unresolvable"
    if target.colors and cand.colors and not (target.colors & cand.colors):
        return f"colour {sorted(cand.colors)} vs {sorted(target.colors)}"
    return "OK"



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/matching/sim.py
================================================
"""SIM configuration. See parsers/uzum.md section 6.

eSIM-only variants sit beside dual-SIM ones at materially lower prices, so
they are a different canonical product, not a colour.
"""
import re

# Pair separators, widened to include "/" and "+": the rendered dual-variant
# spec sheet reads "nano-SIM/eSIM" (a slash, not a plus), and the raw
# characteristic value is "SIM+eSIM". Stripping all of \s _ . - / + collapses
# every documented spelling onto one canonical form before matching, so a
# single pair of regexes covers "SIM+eSIM", "nanoSim+eSim" and
# "nano-SIM/eSIM" alike.
_SEPARATORS = re.compile(r"[\s_.\-/+]")
# A bare "dual" is an explicit dual marker in its own right (uzum.md §6 lists
# it alongside SIM+eSIM, nanoSIM+eSIM and 2 SIM). The bare alternative also
# subsumes "dualsim", which is why that spelling no longer needs its own branch.
_DUAL = re.compile(r"simesim|dual|2sim|двесим")
_ESIM = re.compile(r"esim")


def is_esim_only(text: str | None) -> bool:
    low = _SEPARATORS.sub("", str(text or "").lower())
    if _DUAL.search(low):        # explicit dual markers win, checked first
        return False
    return bool(_ESIM.search(low))


def sim_kind(text: str | None) -> str:
    """One of 'esim_only', 'dual', 'unknown'."""
    if is_esim_only(text):
        return "esim_only"
    low = _SEPARATORS.sub("", str(text or "").lower())
    if _DUAL.search(low):
        return "dual"
    # Any remaining mention of a SIM at this point (eSIM-only and dual have
    # both been ruled out) is a physical one. Separator-stripping merges
    # "SIM карта" into "simкарта", where \bsim\b no longer matches because
    # Cyrillic к is a \w character under Python's Unicode-aware \b — so a
    # plain substring test replaces the old _PHYSICAL regex entirely.
    if "sim" in low:
        return "dual"
    return "unknown"



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/matching/stats.py
================================================
"""Turning many seller prices into one number."""
import statistics

_MIN_SAMPLES_FOR_IQR = 4
_IQR_FENCE = 1.5


def robust_median(prices: list[int]) -> tuple[float | None, list[int]]:
    """Median after IQR outlier stripping (only strips when >= 4 samples).

    Marketplaces carry both mis-listed accessories priced like phones and
    phones priced like accessories.
    """
    ordered = sorted(prices)
    if not ordered:
        return None, []
    if len(ordered) < _MIN_SAMPLES_FOR_IQR:
        return statistics.median(ordered), ordered
    q1, _, q3 = statistics.quantiles(ordered, n=4, method="inclusive")
    iqr = q3 - q1
    lo, hi = q1 - _IQR_FENCE * iqr, q3 + _IQR_FENCE * iqr
    kept = [p for p in ordered if lo <= p <= hi] or ordered
    return statistics.median(kept), kept
