"""Crawl stages. harvest -> extract -> cluster -> aggregate -> publish."""



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/pipeline/aggregate.py
================================================
"""Stage 4: many offers for one product become the numbers a page shows.

Two rules here carry the product's promise:

**Only an in-stock offer may set the headline price.** An out-of-stock listing
is still recorded -- it is a real observation and price history needs it -- but
quoting it as the price would advertise something nobody can buy.

**The headline price remembers which listing it came from.** `min_store_id` and
`min_offer_id` are not bookkeeping; they are the feature. They are what lets a
product page print the retailer's own wording beside the number, so a shopper
can check the match themselves. A competitor cannot do that, which is precisely
how their colour-pooled prices go unnoticed.
"""
import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass

from narxnoma.matching import robust_median

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# uzum is a multi-seller marketplace: one variant can carry dozens of listings
# from different sellers at wildly different prices. Below this many we report
# the plain figures, because IQR stripping on two or three samples discards
# real data rather than outliers.
MIN_SELLERS_FOR_SPREAD = 2


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Spread:
    """What a multi-seller marketplace is actually charging for one variant."""

    min_price: int
    median_price: int
    max_price: int
    seller_count: int


def recompute_variant_prices(conn) -> int:
    """Rebuild every variant's price rollup from its current offers.

    Returns the number of variants written. Rebuilding wholesale rather than
    patching incrementally is deliberate: an offer that went out of stock, or
    was re-attached to a different variant by clustering, must not leave a
    stale headline price behind.
    """
    with conn.transaction():
        with conn.cursor() as cur:
            cur.execute("""
                WITH stats AS (
                    SELECT o.variant_id,
                           count(*) AS offer_count,
                           count(*) FILTER (WHERE o.availability = 'IN_STOCK')
                               AS in_stock_offer_count,
                           count(DISTINCT o.store_id) AS store_count,
                           min(o.price) FILTER (WHERE o.availability = 'IN_STOCK')
                               AS min_price,
                           max(o.price) FILTER (WHERE o.availability = 'IN_STOCK')
                               AS max_price,
                           percentile_disc(0.5) WITHIN GROUP (ORDER BY o.price)
                               FILTER (WHERE o.availability = 'IN_STOCK')
                               AS median_price
                      FROM offer o
                     WHERE o.variant_id IS NOT NULL
                     GROUP BY o.variant_id
                ),
                cheapest AS (
                    -- Which listing the headline price came from. DISTINCT ON
                    -- with a deterministic tiebreak so the provenance does not
                    -- flap between equal-priced offers on every run.
                    SELECT DISTINCT ON (o.variant_id)
                           o.variant_id, o.id AS offer_id, o.store_id
                      FROM offer o
                     WHERE o.variant_id IS NOT NULL
                       AND o.availability = 'IN_STOCK'
                     ORDER BY o.variant_id, o.price, o.id
                )
                INSERT INTO variant_price (
                    variant_id, min_price, min_store_id, min_offer_id,
                    median_price, max_price, offer_count, in_stock_offer_count,
                    store_count, computed_at)
                SELECT s.variant_id, s.min_price, c.store_id, c.offer_id,
                       s.median_price, s.max_price, s.offer_count,
                       s.in_stock_offer_count, s.store_count, now()
                  FROM stats s
                  LEFT JOIN cheapest c ON c.variant_id = s.variant_id
                ON CONFLICT (variant_id) DO UPDATE SET
                    min_price            = EXCLUDED.min_price,
                    min_store_id         = EXCLUDED.min_store_id,
                    min_offer_id         = EXCLUDED.min_offer_id,
                    median_price         = EXCLUDED.median_price,
                    max_price            = EXCLUDED.max_price,
                    offer_count          = EXCLUDED.offer_count,
                    in_stock_offer_count = EXCLUDED.in_stock_offer_count,
                    store_count          = EXCLUDED.store_count,
                    computed_at          = now()
            """)
            written = cur.rowcount

            # A variant whose offers all vanished must not keep a price.
            cur.execute("""
                DELETE FROM variant_price vp
                 WHERE NOT EXISTS (
                    SELECT 1 FROM offer o WHERE o.variant_id = vp.variant_id)
            """)

    logger.info("recomputed prices for %d variants", written)
    return written


def uzum_spread(conn, variant_id) -> Spread | None:
    """What uzum's sellers are charging for one variant.

    Returns None when uzum has too few listings for a spread to mean anything.
    The median is IQR-stripped because a marketplace carries both mis-listed
    accessories priced like phones and phones priced like accessories, and a
    single one of either would drag a plain median off the real market price.
    """
    with conn.cursor() as cur:
        cur.execute("""
            SELECT o.price FROM offer o
              JOIN store s ON s.id = o.store_id
             WHERE o.variant_id = %s AND s.slug = 'uzum'
               AND o.availability = 'IN_STOCK'
        """, (variant_id,))
        prices = [row[0] for row in cur.fetchall()]

    if len(prices) < MIN_SELLERS_FOR_SPREAD:
        return None

    median, kept = robust_median(prices)
    return Spread(
        min_price=min(prices),
        median_price=int(median),
        max_price=max(prices),
        seller_count=len(prices),
    )



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/pipeline/backfill.py
================================================
"""Pass B: reach the one source that cannot be swept.

mediapark exposes only an autocomplete endpoint — no catalogue browse, no
pagination — so `can_sweep` is False and `sweep()` raises. The only way to
reach it is to ask about products we already know exist, which is why this
runs after clustering rather than during harvest.

It reads the variant list produced by the **previous** cycle, deliberately.
Feeding it this cycle's clustering output would make the pipeline cyclic for
one source's benefit; the cost is that a brand-new phone gets its mediapark
offer one cycle late, which is four hours.
"""
import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass

from narxnoma.db import repository as default_repo

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# One cycle must stay bounded. The catalogue grows without limit, but a
# backfill that scales with it would eventually outlast the four-hour window
# and collide with the next run. Variants are taken oldest-queried first, so
# the whole catalogue is still covered over successive cycles.
DEFAULT_MAX_QUERIES = 200


@dataclass
class BackfillResult:
    queried: int = 0
    offers_written: int = 0
    variants_matched: int = 0
    misses: int = 0


def build_query(display_name: str) -> str:
    """What to ask mediapark for.

    Kept nearly verbatim, including the memory figure and the colour word.
    Unlike every other source here, mediapark *ranks* rather than filtering
    with strict AND, so stripping terms measurably worsens recall — see
    docs/parsers/mediapark.md. The only thing removed is our own generated
    prefix, which is ours and not something a retailer would index.
    """
    query = display_name.strip()
    for prefix in ("Смартфон ", "Smartfon "):
        if query.startswith(prefix):
            query = query[len(prefix):]
    return query.replace(" ГБ,", "").replace(" ГБ", "").strip()


def backfill_search_only_sources(conn, source, store_id, variants,
                                 repo=default_repo,
                                 max_queries: int = DEFAULT_MAX_QUERIES) -> BackfillResult:
    """Search one non-sweepable source for products we already know about.

    `variants` is a sequence of (variant_id, display_name) from a previous
    cycle. Listings that come back are written as ordinary offers; clustering
    will decide on the next run whether each one really is that variant. This
    stage does not attach anything itself, because a search hit is a
    suggestion, not evidence.
    """
    result = BackfillResult()

    for _variant_id, display_name in list(variants)[:max_queries]:
        query = build_query(display_name)
        result.queried += 1
        try:
            listings = list(source.search(query))
        except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - one bad query must not end the pass
            logger.warning("backfill query failed for %r: %s", query, exc)
            result.misses += 1
            continue

        if not listings:
            result.misses += 1
            continue

        repo.record_raw_offers(conn, store_id, listings)
        inserted, updated = repo.upsert_offers(conn, store_id, listings)
        result.offers_written += inserted + updated
        result.variants_matched += 1

    logger.info(
        "backfill %s: %d queried, %d matched, %d offers written, %d misses",
        getattr(source, "slug", "?"), result.queried, result.variants_matched,
        result.offers_written, result.misses)
    return result



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/pipeline/cluster.py
================================================
"""Stage 3: offers become canonical products — or are refused, with a reason.

This is the whole competitive thesis. Everything else in this project is
plumbing around the rule implemented here:

    an offer is attached to a canonical product only when exactly one product
    can possibly be the right one.

The incumbent pools colour variants together, so on 23% of its multi-store
pages the headline price belongs to a different colour than the page's own
title — worst measured case 9.3% understated. That is not a bug in their
matcher; it is what happens when an ambiguous offer gets attached anyway
because attaching produces a fuller-looking catalogue. We refuse instead, and
count the refusals, because a wrong price is worse than a missing one.
"""
import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

from narxnoma.db import repository as default_repo
from narxnoma.matching import resolve_partial
from narxnoma.matching.categories import is_accessory
from narxnoma.pipeline.extract import OfferIdentity, identify

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


@dataclass
class ClusterResult:
    attached: int = 0
    created_variants: int = 0
    unresolved: int = 0
    reasons: dict[str, int] = field(default_factory=dict)

    def _note(self, reason: str) -> None:
        self.reasons[reason] = self.reasons.get(reason, 0) + 1


# Reasons are deliberately coarse and stable, so their counts mean something
# when tracked over time. The specifics go in the row itself.
REASON_AMBIGUOUS = "ambiguous: partial key matches several variants"
REASON_NO_CANDIDATE = "no candidate: partial key matches no existing variant"
REASON_NO_IDENTITY = "no identity: name yields neither a full nor a usable partial key"
REASON_MEMORY_IN_MODEL = "bad identity: a memory figure reached the model tokens"
REASON_ACCESSORY = "not the product: an accessory listed in the product's category"

# No phone is named "256". A capacity among the model tokens is always a
# parsing failure, and the variant it would mint is a product that does not
# exist -- so genuinely different SKUs pool behind one headline price, which
# is the incumbent's defect reproduced in our own data.
#
# This is a BACKSTOP, not a repair. It was written for a uzum leak (a card's
# storage RANGE reaching identity, so a 17 Pro 256, a 17 Pro Max 256 and a
# 17 Pro 512 became one variant priced at 18,349,000 against a median of
# 31,000,000) which is now fixed in the client. It stays because the failure
# is silent by construction -- a wrong variant is indistinguishable from a
# right one without reading the offers behind it -- and any source can
# reintroduce the same shape.
_CAPACITIES_GB = frozenset({"32", "64", "128", "256", "512", "1024", "2048"})

# Categories where a bare capacity figure among the model tokens is evidence of
# a parsing failure. That inference holds only where the figure COULD be a
# memory size: a phone or a laptop. Outside those, the same digits are ordinary
# product facts -- 32 and 64 are television diagonals, and a "Телевизор MoonX
# 32" is a real product whose diagonal is the only thing distinguishing it.
# Applying the phone guard to televisions refused exactly those listings.
_CAPACITY_GUARDED_CATEGORIES = frozenset({"smartphones", "laptops"})


def is_wellformed(key) -> bool:
    """Could this key describe a real product at all?

    Applied at every point a key can reach the catalogue, not just at
    creation: an offer may also arrive at a malformed variant by PARTIAL
    resolution, and one already sitting on a malformed variant agrees with it
    perfectly, so the drift check alone would keep it there forever. Checking
    once, in three places, is what makes the refusal actually hold.
    """
    if getattr(key, "category", "smartphones") not in _CAPACITY_GUARDED_CATEGORIES:
        return bool(key.model_tokens)
    return not (_CAPACITIES_GB & set(key.model_tokens))


def _has_usable_partial(identity: OfferIdentity) -> bool:
    """Enough to be worth resolving against existing variants.

    Brand and storage are the floor. Without them a partial key is compatible
    with far too much, and "resolves to exactly one" would start being true by
    accident rather than by evidence.
    """
    p = identity.partial
    return bool(p.brand and p.storage_gb and p.model_tokens)


def detach_drifted_offers(conn, repo=default_repo) -> int:
    """Detach offers whose stored variant contradicts what their name now says.

    Clustering only ever loads offers with `variant_id IS NULL`, so an offer
    attached in an earlier cycle is never reconsidered. That is correct while
    `name_raw` is stable — and wrong the moment a source fix changes it, which
    is precisely when the catalogue is most likely to be wrong. The uzum
    card-range repair rewrote 217 stored names; without this the offers behind
    them would have sat on their old, incorrect variants indefinitely, and
    nothing in the system would have reported it.

    Detaching is all this does. The offer returns to the normal pass and is
    re-attached, or refused, by the same rules as any other — so a fix can only
    move an offer to the right product or park it, never invent a new outcome.

    Two cases are deliberately distinguished:

    - a **full** key that maps to a different variant is a plain contradiction;
    - a **partial** key is judged by compatibility instead, because an offer
      attached by partial resolution never had a full key to compare. Tearing
      those down unconditionally would churn every partial attachment through
      detach and re-attach on every single cycle.
    """
    known = repo.load_variant_keys(conn)
    by_id = {vid: key for key, vid in known.items()}

    drifted = []
    for row in repo.load_attached_offers(conn):
        identity = identify(row)
        stored_key = by_id.get(row["variant_id"])
        if stored_key is None:
            continue
        if not is_wellformed(stored_key):
            # Nothing can legitimately sit here -- see is_wellformed. Without
            # this, an offer whose name still produces the malformed key
            # AGREES with its variant and is kept indefinitely.
            drifted.append(identity.offer_id)
        elif identity.key is not None:
            if identity.key != stored_key:
                drifted.append(identity.offer_id)
        elif resolve_partial(identity.partial, [stored_key]) is None:
            drifted.append(identity.offer_id)

    for offer_id in drifted:
        repo.detach_offer(conn, offer_id)
    if drifted:
        logger.info("detached %d offers whose name no longer matches their variant",
                    len(drifted))

    # A malformed variant can never hold an offer again -- is_wellformed
    # refuses it at every entry point -- so once emptied it is pure residue,
    # and a catalogue that keeps it serves a product page with no price for a
    # phone that does not exist. Deleting only the EMPTY ones keeps this
    # distinct from the deliberate decision to retain a real variant whose
    # offers have all gone out of stock; those stay, with a NULL price.
    removed = repo.delete_empty_malformed_variants(conn, is_wellformed)
    if removed:
        logger.info("removed %d emptied malformed variants", removed)
    return len(drifted)


def cluster_offers(conn, identities, repo=default_repo) -> ClusterResult:
    """Attach offers to canonical variants, refusing anything ambiguous.

    Full keys are processed before partials, in the same pass, because a
    partial must be able to resolve against a variant this very run created —
    otherwise the first offer of a new phone would park itself as unresolvable
    against a catalogue that was about to contain it.
    """
    result = ClusterResult()
    identities = list(identities)

    known = repo.load_variant_keys(conn)
    before = len(known)

    # --- pass one: complete identities define the catalogue -----------------
    partials = []
    for identity in identities:
        if identity.key is None:
            partials.append(identity)
            continue
        if not is_wellformed(identity.key):
            # See REASON_MEMORY_IN_MODEL: refusing costs one offer, while
            # cataloguing it corrupts a product other offers then join.
            repo.record_unresolved(conn, identity.offer_id, REASON_MEMORY_IN_MODEL)
            result.unresolved += 1
            result._note(REASON_MEMORY_IN_MODEL)
            continue
        if is_accessory(identity.features.normalized, identity.key.category):
            # A laptop bag swept from the laptop feed keys as cleanly as a
            # laptop does, so nothing further down would ever reject it.
            repo.record_unresolved(conn, identity.offer_id, REASON_ACCESSORY)
            result.unresolved += 1
            result._note(REASON_ACCESSORY)
            continue
        variant_id = known.get(identity.key)
        if variant_id is None:
            variant_id = repo.upsert_variant(conn, identity.key)
            known[identity.key] = variant_id
        repo.attach_offer_to_variant(conn, identity.offer_id, variant_id)
        result.attached += 1

    result.created_variants = len(known) - before

    # --- pass two: incomplete identities resolve against it -----------------
    candidates = [k for k in known if is_wellformed(k)]
    for identity in partials:
        if not _has_usable_partial(identity):
            repo.record_unresolved(conn, identity.offer_id, REASON_NO_IDENTITY)
            result.unresolved += 1
            result._note(REASON_NO_IDENTITY)
            continue

        match = resolve_partial(identity.partial, candidates)
        if match is not None:
            repo.attach_offer_to_variant(conn, identity.offer_id, known[match])
            result.attached += 1
            continue

        # resolve_partial returns None for both zero and two-or-more. The
        # difference matters to whoever reads the quality report: zero is a
        # catalogue gap, several is a genuinely under-specified listing.
        compatible = [known[k] for k in candidates
                      if resolve_partial(identity.partial, [k]) is not None]
        reason = REASON_AMBIGUOUS if len(compatible) > 1 else REASON_NO_CANDIDATE
        repo.record_unresolved(conn, identity.offer_id, reason, compatible)
        result.unresolved += 1
        result._note(reason)

    logger.info(
        "clustered: %d attached, %d variants created, %d unresolved",
        result.attached, result.created_variants, result.unresolved)
    return result

FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/pipeline/harvest.py

"""Stage 1: retailer APIs -> raw_offer + offer.

Each source runs in isolation. A source that fails degrades coverage for
this cycle; it never aborts the others and never corrupts their data. That
isolation guarantee covers the bookkeeping calls (start_run/finish_run) as
much as the sweep itself -- a transient database hiccup on start_run must
not be any more fatal to the cycle than a transient upstream 503.
"""
import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass

from narxnoma.db import repository as default_repo

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# Flush accumulated listings through the repository every this-many items
# instead of buffering an entire sweep in memory and writing it all at the
# end. repository.py's own docstring justifies the per-call commit boundary
# by saying a source that dies half way through should keep what it already
# fetched -- that guarantee is worthless if nothing is written until the
# generator that feeds it is fully exhausted. Harmless at today's sizes
# (olcha ~448) but matters as uzum's and alifshop's brand-by-brand paging
# grows.
WRITE_BATCH_SIZE = 500


@dataclass
class HarvestResult:
    """Outcome of one source's harvest attempt.

    `seen` and `kept` are currently always equal, which makes `seen`
    misleading if read as "how many items the retailer's catalogue had."
    Every Listing that reaches `sweep()`'s caller already passed validation
    inside the client via `try_build_listing` (sources/base.py) -- items
    that fail that validation are logged and dropped *before* they are
    yielded, not counted here. So `seen` counts listings the client chose
    to yield, not raw catalogue items. Threading a genuine drop count out
    of all seven clients is a larger change than this one; the honest fix
    for now is not pretending seen/kept can diverge. Task 12's
    EXPECTED_MIN_OFFERS floors -- not a gap between these two fields -- are
    what actually catch a source silently shrinking.
    """

    slug: str
    ok: bool
    seen: int = 0
    kept: int = 0
    error: str | None = None
    #: True when the source was never attempted because can_sweep is False
    #: (or another skip condition below). Kept distinct from `error` being
    #: set so callers never have to infer "was this a failure or a skip"
    #: from whether error happens to be non-None -- see harvest_all and
    #: cli.py's render_status.
    skipped: bool = False


def _flush_batch(conn, repo, store_id, batch: list) -> int:
    """Write one batch of listings through both tables. Returns the count
    written, so the caller can keep a running total across batches."""
    repo.record_raw_offers(conn, store_id, batch)
    repo.upsert_offers(conn, store_id, batch)
    return len(batch)


def harvest_source(conn, source, store_id, repo=default_repo,
                   category: str = "smartphones") -> HarvestResult:
    """Sweep one source (for one category) and persist what it yields.

    The entire function body -- including start_run and both finish_run
    calls -- lives inside the try block. Nothing here may propagate out to
    harvest_all: a database error on start_run is a source-independent
    failure just like a database error on finish_run, and either one
    escaping would abort every source still queued behind this one, which
    is exactly the isolation guarantee this module exists to provide.
    """
    listings_seen = kept = 0
    run_id = None
    try:
        run_id = repo.start_run(conn, store_id)

        batch: list = []
        for listing in source.sweep(category=category):
            listings_seen += 1
            batch.append(listing)
            if len(batch) >= WRITE_BATCH_SIZE:
                kept += _flush_batch(conn, repo, store_id, batch)
                batch = []
        if batch:
            kept += _flush_batch(conn, repo, store_id, batch)

        # See HarvestResult's docstring: listings_seen == kept here isn't a
        # coincidence worth preserving as a health signal, it's structural.
        repo.finish_run(conn, run_id, ok=True, seen=listings_seen, kept=kept)
        return HarvestResult(source.slug, ok=True, seen=listings_seen, kept=kept)
    except Exception as exc:                     # noqa: BLE001 - isolation is the point
        logger.exception("harvest failed for %s", source.slug)
        if run_id is not None:
            try:
                repo.finish_run(conn, run_id, ok=False, seen=listings_seen,
                                kept=kept, error=str(exc))
            except Exception:                     # noqa: BLE001 - see above
                # Recording the failure itself failed (e.g. the same DB
                # hiccup that caused start_run/writes to fail in the first
                # place). Log it and still return a failed HarvestResult --
                # raising here would defeat the whole point of this
                # try/except by taking the rest of the cycle down with it.
                logger.exception(
                    "also failed to record the failure against run %s for %s; "
                    "its harvest_run row may be left unfinished",
                    run_id, source.slug)
        return HarvestResult(source.slug, ok=False, seen=listings_seen,
                             kept=kept, error=str(exc))


def harvest_all(conn, sources, repo=default_repo,
                 categories: list[str] | None = None) -> list[HarvestResult]:
    """Sweep each source across every category (defaults to all Plan 5
    categories). A search-only source is skipped; a store that is not seeded
    is reported as failed. Results are aggregated per source across the
    categories swept (a source is ok only if every category succeeded).
    """
    if categories is None:
        from narxnoma.matching.keys import CATEGORIES
        categories = list(CATEGORIES)
    ids = repo.store_ids(conn)
    results = []
    for source in sources:
        # A search-only source (mediapark) has no catalogue to browse. Skip it
        # explicitly rather than calling sweep() -- sweep() raises for those, and
        # honouring can_sweep is the caller's job. skipped=True (not just a
        # string stuffed into `error`) is what lets the CLI render this
        # distinctly from an ordinary successful sweep -- see base.py's
        # Source protocol docstring for why an indistinguishable-from-success
        # skip is precisely the failure mode this must not become.
        if not source.can_sweep:
            logger.info("skipping %s: search-only source, nothing to sweep", source.slug)
            results.append(HarvestResult(source.slug, ok=True, skipped=True,
                                         error="search-only, skipped"))
            continue
        store_id = ids.get(source.slug)
        if store_id is None:
            results.append(HarvestResult(source.slug, ok=False, error="store not seeded"))
            continue
        seen = kept = 0
        ok = True
        error = None
        for category in categories:
            r = harvest_source(conn, source, store_id, repo=repo, category=category)
            seen += r.seen
            kept += r.kept
            if not r.ok:
                ok = False
                error = r.error or error
        results.append(HarvestResult(source.slug, ok=ok, seen=seen, kept=kept,
                                     error=error))
    return results



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/pipeline/quality.py
================================================
"""Is the matcher working? The unresolved rate only means something if watched.

`unresolved_offer` is a quality signal, not a dumping ground. Its count rising
means the matcher is failing to identify things it should — and because we
deliberately refuse rather than guess, that failure is silent by construction.
Nothing else in the system would notice.
"""
import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# Above this, something is wrong with the matcher rather than with the data.
# Measured baseline on a real five-source catalogue is ~21%, dominated by
# retailer catalogue-parent rows with no memory stated -- see the ledger. This
# threshold is what the plan specifies; treat a breach as "go and read the
# unresolved rows", not as a number to tune away.
MAX_UNRESOLVED_RATE = 0.05

# A variant whose cheapest offer sits far below its median is the shape of the
# card-price-versus-SKU-price bug: one listing that is not really this product,
# dragging the headline down. Worth surfacing permanently.
MIN_DIVERGENCE = 0.12


@dataclass
class QualityReport:
    total_offers: int = 0
    attached: int = 0
    unresolved: int = 0
    variants: int = 0
    single_offer_variants: int = 0
    empty_variants: int = 0
    diverging_variants: int = 0
    reasons: list[tuple[str, int]] = field(default_factory=list)
    unresolved_by_store: list[tuple[str, int]] = field(default_factory=list)

    @property
    def unresolved_rate(self) -> float:
        return self.unresolved / self.total_offers if self.total_offers else 0.0

    @property
    def is_alerting(self) -> bool:
        return self.unresolved_rate > MAX_UNRESOLVED_RATE


def quality_report(conn) -> QualityReport:
    report = QualityReport()
    with conn.cursor() as cur:
        cur.execute("""
            SELECT count(*), count(*) FILTER (WHERE variant_id IS NOT NULL)
              FROM offer
        """)
        report.total_offers, report.attached = cur.fetchone()

        cur.execute("SELECT count(*) FROM unresolved_offer")
        report.unresolved = cur.fetchone()[0]

        cur.execute("SELECT count(*) FROM variant")
        report.variants = cur.fetchone()[0]

        cur.execute("""
            SELECT reason, count(*) FROM unresolved_offer
             GROUP BY reason ORDER BY count(*) DESC
        """)
        report.reasons = cur.fetchall()

        cur.execute("""
            SELECT s.slug, count(*) FROM unresolved_offer u
              JOIN offer o ON o.id = u.offer_id
              JOIN store s ON s.id = o.store_id
             GROUP BY s.slug ORDER BY count(*) DESC
        """)
        report.unresolved_by_store = cur.fetchall()

        # A variant nobody else stocks has nothing to compare, which is the
        # honest limit of a comparison site rather than a defect.
        cur.execute("SELECT count(*) FROM variant_price WHERE offer_count = 1")
        report.single_offer_variants = cur.fetchone()[0]

        # A canonical product no retailer currently lists. Legitimate when a
        # phone is discontinued, and also the shape residue takes when
        # clustering moves offers away -- so it is worth watching rather than
        # acting on blindly. A sudden rise means offers left products en masse.
        cur.execute("""
            SELECT count(*) FROM variant v
             WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM offer o WHERE o.variant_id = v.id)
        """)
        report.empty_variants = cur.fetchone()[0]

        cur.execute("""
            SELECT count(*) FROM variant_price
             WHERE min_price IS NOT NULL AND median_price IS NOT NULL
               AND median_price > 0
               AND (median_price - min_price)::numeric / median_price > %s
        """, (MIN_DIVERGENCE,))
        report.diverging_variants = cur.fetchone()[0]

    logger.info("quality: %d/%d offers attached, %.1f%% unresolved",
                report.attached, report.total_offers, report.unresolved_rate * 100)
    return report


def render(report: QualityReport) -> str:
    lines = [
        f"offers            {report.total_offers:>8,}",
        f"  attached        {report.attached:>8,}",
        f"  unresolved      {report.unresolved:>8,}   "
        f"({report.unresolved_rate * 100:.1f}%, alert above "
        f"{MAX_UNRESOLVED_RATE * 100:.0f}%)",
        f"canonical variants{report.variants:>8,}",
        f"  single-offer    {report.single_offer_variants:>8,}   "
        f"(nothing to compare -- an honest limit, not a defect)",
        f"  no offers at all{report.empty_variants:>8,}   "
        f"(no retailer lists it now: discontinued, or offers moved away)",
        f"  min far below median{report.diverging_variants:>5,}   "
        f"(>{MIN_DIVERGENCE * 100:.0f}%: the shape of a wrong match dragging a headline down)",
    ]
    if report.reasons:
        lines.append("\nunresolved by reason:")
        lines += [f"  {n:>7,}  {reason}" for reason, n in report.reasons]
    if report.unresolved_by_store:
        lines.append("\nunresolved by source:")
        lines += [f"  {n:>7,}  {slug}" for slug, n in report.unresolved_by_store]
    if report.is_alerting:
        lines.append(
            f"\n*** ALERT: {report.unresolved_rate * 100:.1f}% unresolved. "
            f"Read the rows before changing the threshold. ***")
    return "\n".join(lines)



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/pipeline/report.py
================================================
"""Coverage and freshness per source.

A source that quietly returns fewer rows is more dangerous than one that
raises, because nothing alerts. Every source built so far shares this blind
spot: all of them raise loudly when an endpoint 404s or returns non-JSON,
but none of them notice a retailer that starts answering HTTP 200 with an
empty item list. In that case `sweep()` exits cleanly after page one, the
harvest records `ok = True` with zero listings, and the catalogue quietly
empties while every check stays green.

`harvest.HarvestResult.seen`/`kept` cannot catch this -- they are
structurally identical (see that module's docstring). This report is what
catches it instead, with three independent rules: the offer count against a
measured floor, the AGE of the newest offer against MAX_OFFER_AGE_HOURS, and
the outcome of the last recorded run. Any one of them firing is enough.

The age rule is not a refinement of the count rule, it is the load-bearing
one after day one: `offer` is upsert-only, so a source that dies leaves its
rows behind and keeps clearing its floor indefinitely.
"""
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime

# How old the newest offer for a swept source may be before the source is
# treated as dead. Harvest runs on a four-hour cycle, so 9 hours is a little
# over two cycles: one missed cycle is tolerated, two are not.
#
# This threshold is what makes the report able to detect a source that STOPPED,
# as opposed to one that never started. `offer` is upsert-only -- nothing in
# this system ever deletes or ages a row -- so a retailer that renames a
# category and starts returning nothing leaves yesterday's rows sitting in the
# table, the count keeps clearing the floor, and `report` keeps exiting 0 while
# the site serves month-old prices as current. The count alone only ever
# catches a source that was dead on its FIRST run against a fresh database.
MAX_OFFER_AGE_HOURS = 9

# Suspicion reasons, kept as named constants so the CLI, the tests and the
# report agree on the wording. An operator reading the table has to be able to
# tell "never fetched" from "stopped updating" from "last run failed" -- three
# different call-outs with three different fixes.
BELOW_FLOOR = "below floor"
NEVER_FETCHED = "never fetched"
STALE = f"stale (>{MAX_OFFER_AGE_HOURS}h)"
LAST_RUN_FAILED = "last run failed"

# Measured live 2026-07-26/27. These are floors, not targets: a source returning
# far fewer offers than this has almost certainly had its endpoint rescoped, not
# had its catalogue shrink. Update deliberately, with a note, never to silence an alert.
EXPECTED_MIN_OFFERS = {
    "olcha": 300,        # ~460 phones observed across 23 pages
    "texnomart": 300,    # ~500 observed across 25 pages
    "beemarket": 250,    # 436 observed via the smartfony category endpoint
    "idea": 100,         # no pagination; reach is bounded by the brand-query list
    "alifshop": 200,     # per-brand sweep
    "uzum": 200,         # per-brand GraphQL sweep
    "mediapark": 0,      # search-only, never swept
    # Measured live 2026-08-10 by counting every leaf asaxiy sweeps: 233
    # phones + 57 TVs + 90 laptops + 113 ACs + 107 fridges + 132 washers + 18
    # cookers = 750. asaxiy is a broad-range shop (books, toys, groceries) with
    # a narrower electronics range than the specialists, so this floor is set
    # against its own measured size, not against olcha's.
    "asaxiy": 400,
    # Measured live 2026-08-11 from radius's own category tree: 84 phones + 22
        # fridges + 18 TVs + 17 ACs + 17 washers + 16 laptops + 13 cookers = 187.
        # The smallest source by a wide margin, and the floor says so -- a floor
        # copied from a bigger shop would never fire, which is the one thing a
        # floor must not do.
        "radius": 120,
        # Re-measured live 2026-08-11: 8,138 offers kept across the seven
        # categories. The previous figure (~1,900) was wrong, and instructively
        # so: it came from reading "5 pages" off the pager on page 1, which is
        # WINDOWED and only ever shows a few links around the current page.
        # glotr's ACs alone run to 58 pages. Set at half the measured size --
        # this is a classifieds board, so its volume moves with how many
        # sellers happen to be posting, and a tight floor here would cry wolf.
        "glotr": 4000,
        # Measured live 2026-10-22 through `cli fetch`, all seven categories:
        # 432 phones + 113 laptops + 59 ovens + 50 fridges + 45 TVs + 39
        # washers + 31 ACs = 769. Single-seller shop listing only in-stock
        # goods, so its size moves with stock; floor at half.
        "openshop": 380,
    }


@dataclass
class SourceCoverage:
    slug: str
    offers: int
    median_price: int | None
    oldest_seen: datetime | None
    newest_seen: datetime | None
    last_run_ok: bool | None
    expected_min: int
    #: Every rule that fired, in report order. `reasons` is the single source
    #: of truth; `is_suspicious` is derived from it so the two cannot drift.
    reasons: tuple[str, ...] = ()
    #: Hours since the newest offer row for this source, or None when the
    #: source has no offers at all. Carried so the CLI can show it.
    age_hours: float | None = None

    @property
    def is_suspicious(self) -> bool:
        return bool(self.reasons)


def suspicion_reasons(
    *,
    offers: int,
    expected_min: int,
    age_hours: float | None,
    last_run_ok: bool | None,
) -> tuple[str, ...]:
    """Every rule that says this source is not healthy.

    Pure and DB-free so each rule can be tested independently.

    A source whose floor is 0 is exempt from ALL of them. Today that is only
    mediapark: it is search-only, harvest_all skips it by contract, so it has
    no runs and no offers BY DESIGN. Zero is its healthy state, and any rule
    that fired on it would be a permanent false alarm -- the fastest way to
    train an operator to ignore this report.
    """
    if expected_min == 0:
        return ()
    reasons: list[str] = []
    if offers < expected_min:
        reasons.append(BELOW_FLOOR)
    if age_hours is None:
        # No offer rows at all. Distinct from STALE on purpose: nothing was
        # ever stored, rather than something was stored and stopped refreshing.
        reasons.append(NEVER_FETCHED)
    elif age_hours > MAX_OFFER_AGE_HOURS:
        # THE RULE THIS REPORT WAS MISSING. `offer` is upsert-only, so a dead
        # source keeps yesterday's row count forever and clears its floor
        # while serving stale prices.
        reasons.append(STALE)
    if last_run_ok is False:
        # Not `not last_run_ok`: None means "no run recorded yet", which the
        # other two rules already cover, and which is not itself a failure.
        reasons.append(LAST_RUN_FAILED)
    return tuple(reasons)


def coverage_report(conn) -> list[SourceCoverage]:
    with conn.cursor() as cur:
        cur.execute("""
            SELECT s.slug,
                   count(o.id),
                   percentile_disc(0.5) WITHIN GROUP (ORDER BY o.price),
                   min(o.last_seen),
                   max(o.last_seen),
                   (SELECT hr.ok FROM harvest_run hr
                     WHERE hr.store_id = s.id
                     ORDER BY hr.started_at DESC LIMIT 1),
                   -- Age is computed by Postgres, against the same clock and
                   -- time zone that wrote last_seen, rather than compared to a
                   -- Python datetime.now() that may not even be tz-aware.
                   EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (now() - max(o.last_seen))) / 3600.0
              FROM store s
              LEFT JOIN offer o ON o.store_id = s.id
             GROUP BY s.id, s.slug
             ORDER BY s.slug
        """)
        rows = []
        for (slug, offers, median_price, oldest_seen, newest_seen,
             last_run_ok, age_hours) in cur.fetchall():
            expected_min = EXPECTED_MIN_OFFERS.get(slug, 0)
            age_hours = float(age_hours) if age_hours is not None else None
            rows.append(SourceCoverage(
                slug=slug, offers=offers, median_price=median_price,
                oldest_seen=oldest_seen, newest_seen=newest_seen,
                last_run_ok=last_run_ok, expected_min=expected_min,
                age_hours=age_hours,
                reasons=suspicion_reasons(
                    offers=offers, expected_min=expected_min,
                    age_hours=age_hours, last_run_ok=last_run_ok,
                ),
            ))
        return rows

"""Market research: a list of products -> every market's offers -> an analysable workbook.

    targets.py    read the product list (csv / xlsx / txt)
    collect.py    sweep or search the markets, with an on-disk cache
    match.py      the matching engine applied to a target list, keeping every offer
    model.py      Target / Offer / Research and the JSON that lets a workbook be rebuilt
    workbook.py   the analysable .xlsx (Excel Tables, formulas, heat map, Settings)
    excel/        PivotTables: Excel COM driver, Data Model probe, raw-OOXML writer

Everything outside `workbook.py` and `excel/` needs only the standard library and
`requests`; the workbook layer needs `openpyxl` (extra: `pip install narxnoma-crawler[excel]`).
"""



================================================

import sys

from narxnoma.research.cli import main

sys.exit(main())



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/research/build.py
================================================
"""From a `Research` to the finished .xlsx: workbook, then pivots, then an Excel check.

    pivots="auto"    Excel COM if desktop Excel is here, else raw OOXML
    pivots="com"     Excel COM (flat pivots, slicers, chart, Data Model if present); needs Excel
    pivots="ooxml"   written into the package, Excel builds them on open; no slicers / chart / model
    pivots="none"    Tables, formulas, heat map only; Insert > PivotTable > tblOffers does the rest

Order matters and is not negotiable: openpyxl builds, THEN pivots are added, THEN nothing may
re-save the file through openpyxl (it drops slicers and the Data Model without a word).
"""

import logging
import tempfile
from pathlib import Path

from narxnoma.research.model import Research
from narxnoma.research.workbook import build_workbook

logger = logging.getLogger("narxnoma.research")


def make_workbook(
    r: Research, out: str | Path, pivots: str = "auto", model: str = "auto", verify: bool = True
) -> dict:
    from narxnoma.research.excel import ooxml_pivot, pivots as com

    out = Path(out)
    out.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    info: dict = {"out": str(out), "pivots": "none", "lines": [], "errors": None}
    use_com = pivots == "com" or (pivots == "auto" and com.excel_available())
    if pivots == "com" and not com.excel_available():
        raise com.ExcelUnavailable("pivots='com' needs Windows + desktop Excel")

    if use_com:
        tmp = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="narxnoma_")) / "built.xlsx"
        layout = build_workbook(r, str(tmp))
        res = com.add_pivots(tmp, out, model=model)
        info["lines"] += res.lines
        if not res.ok:
            raise RuntimeError("Excel pivot step failed:\n" + str(res))
        info["pivots"], info["model"] = "com", res.model
        tmp.unlink(missing_ok=True)
    elif pivots in ("auto", "ooxml"):
        tmp = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="narxnoma_")) / "built.xlsx"
        layout = build_workbook(r, str(tmp), pivot_placeholders=True)
        info["lines"] += [
            "PIVOT " + n
            for n in ooxml_pivot.inject_pivots(str(tmp), str(out), layout["offers_cols"])
        ]
        info["pivots"] = "ooxml"
        tmp.unlink(missing_ok=True)
    else:
        layout = build_workbook(r, str(out))

    info.update(layout=layout)
    if verify and com.excel_available():
        res = com.verify(out)
        info["lines"] += res.lines
        info["errors"] = com.error_count(res)
        if info["errors"]:
            logger.error("Excel reports %d formula error cell(s) in %s", info["errors"], out)
    return info



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/research/cli.py
================================================
"""Command line for market research. Database-free; needs `requests`, and `openpyxl` for workbooks.

    python -m narxnoma.research run --targets products.xlsx --out research.xlsx
    python -m narxnoma.research run --targets list.txt --category smartphones --sources uzum,olcha
    python -m narxnoma.research workbook --from research.json --out research.xlsx --pivots ooxml
    python -m narxnoma.research check research.xlsx

`run` sweeps (24 h cache), matches, saves research.json next to the workbook so the workbook can
be rebuilt without fetching, and builds the workbook. Also reachable as `python -m narxnoma.cli
research ...`.
"""

import argparse
import logging
import sys
from pathlib import Path

from narxnoma.matching.keys import CATEGORIES


def _add_build_args(p: argparse.ArgumentParser) -> None:
    p.add_argument(
        "--pivots",
        choices=("auto", "com", "ooxml", "none"),
        default="auto",
        help="PivotTables: auto = Excel COM if present else raw OOXML (default); "
        "com = Excel only (slicers, chart, Data Model); ooxml = no Excel needed; none",
    )
    p.add_argument(
        "--model",
        choices=("auto", "yes", "no"),
        default="auto",
        help="Power Pivot Data Model with the COM pivots: auto = probe, fall back to flat "
        "pivots (default); yes = fail if unavailable; no = skip",
    )
    p.add_argument(
        "--no-verify",
        action="store_true",
        help="skip the Excel recalculation check of the finished file",
    )


def build_parser() -> argparse.ArgumentParser:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        prog="narxnoma.research",
        description=__doc__,
        formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter,
    )
    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest="cmd", required=True)

    run = sub.add_parser("run", help="targets -> markets -> matched offers -> workbook")
    run.add_argument("--targets", required=True, help=".xlsx / .csv / .txt product list")
    run.add_argument(
        "--category", choices=sorted(CATEGORIES), help="category for products whose file gives none"
    )
    run.add_argument("--sources", help="comma-separated markets (default: all but glotr)")
    run.add_argument("--out", required=True, help="output .xlsx")
    run.add_argument("--json", help="where to save the research data (default: <out>.json)")
    run.add_argument("--title", default="Market research")
    run.add_argument("--cache-dir", default="database/research_cache")
    run.add_argument("--max-age-h", type=float, default=24)
    run.add_argument("--force", action="store_true", help="ignore the sweep cache")
    run.add_argument(
        "--usd-rate", type=float, help="UZS per USD for USD inputs (default: central bank, live)"
    )
    _add_build_args(run)

    wb = sub.add_parser("workbook", help="rebuild a workbook from saved research.json, no fetching")
    wb.add_argument("--from", dest="src", required=True, help="research.json from a previous run")
    wb.add_argument("--out", required=True)
    _add_build_args(wb)

    chk = sub.add_parser("check", help="open a workbook in Excel, recalculate, report errors")
    chk.add_argument("path")
    chk.add_argument("--save", action="store_true", help="also write the calculated values back")
    return parser


def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
    args = build_parser().parse_args(argv)
    logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO, format="%(asctime)s %(levelname)s %(message)s")
    if hasattr(sys.stdout, "reconfigure"):
        sys.stdout.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8")

    if args.cmd == "check":
        from narxnoma.research.excel import pivots as com

        res = com.verify(args.path, save=args.save)
        print(res)
        n = com.error_count(res)
        return 0 if res.ok and not n else 1

    from narxnoma.research.build import make_workbook
    from narxnoma.research.model import Research

    if args.cmd == "workbook":
        research = Research.from_json(args.src)
    else:
        from narxnoma.research.run import run_research
        from narxnoma.research.targets import load_targets

        targets = load_targets(args.targets, default_category=args.category)
        if not targets:
            logging.error("no products found in %s", args.targets)
            return 1
        markets = [m.strip() for m in args.sources.split(",")] if args.sources else None
        research = run_research(
            targets,
            markets,
            cache_dir=args.cache_dir,
            max_age_h=args.max_age_h,
            force=args.force,
            title=args.title,
            usd_rate=args.usd_rate,
        )
        js = Path(args.json) if args.json else Path(args.out).with_suffix(".json")
        js.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        research.to_json(js)
        logging.info("research data saved: %s", js)

    info = make_workbook(
        research, args.out, pivots=args.pivots, model=args.model, verify=not args.no_verify
    )
    print("\n".join(info["lines"]))
    print(
        f"wrote {info['out']}  pivots={info['pivots']}  "
        f"products={info['layout']['n_products']}  offers={info['layout']['n_offers']}"
    )
    return 1 if info["errors"] else 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/research/collect.py
================================================
"""Gather the markets' listings for a research run.

Sweep, don't search per product: sweeping each (market, category) once costs a few hundred
requests, gives every target the same candidate pool, and -- the part that matters -- lets a
price be RE-MATCHED without re-fetching, so a matcher fix is a seconds-long re-run. Sweeps are
cached on disk and reused for `max_age_h` hours; prices move daily, so the default is 24.

Search-only sources (`can_sweep` is False -- mediapark) are reached one query per target.
glotr is left out of the default set: its robots.txt declares `Crawl-delay: 10` and it is
crawled only by grant, in its own process with NARXNOMA_MIN_DELAY=10 NARXNOMA_MAX_DELAY=11.
"""

import dataclasses
import json
import logging
import os
import re
import time
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from pathlib import Path

from narxnoma.matching.keys import CATEGORY_PREFIX
from narxnoma.research.model import Target
from narxnoma.sources import SOURCES

logger = logging.getLogger("narxnoma.research")

#: Market column order, fixed so two runs can be diffed side by side. Removing a market that
#: matched nothing would silently change the shape of the file between runs.
MARKET_ORDER = (
    "uzum",
    "texnomart",
    "mediapark",
    "alifshop",
    "beemarket",
    "olcha",
    "idea",
    "asaxiy",
    "radius",
    "openshop",
    "glotr",
)
DEFAULT_EXCLUDE = frozenset({"glotr"})


def default_markets() -> list[str]:
    return [m for m in MARKET_ORDER if m in SOURCES and m not in DEFAULT_EXCLUDE]


@dataclass
class Collected:
    #: (market, category) -> listing dicts
    sweeps: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
    #: market -> target name -> listing dicts (search-only markets)
    searches: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
    #: market -> ISO time of its OLDEST catalogue -- a survey is only as fresh as its stalest column
    fetched_at: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
    #: market -> why it has no (or partial) data
    notes: dict = field(default_factory=dict)


def _iso(ts: float) -> str:
    return datetime.fromtimestamp(ts, timezone.utc).astimezone().isoformat(timespec="seconds")


def _listing_dict(listing) -> dict:
    d = dataclasses.asdict(listing)
    d.pop("raw", None)
    return d


def _sweep_cached(slug: str, category: str, cache_dir: Path, max_age_h: float, force: bool):
    """-> (listings, fetched_ts). Raises on a failed sweep: a half-swept catalogue must not
    be recorded as a success."""
    path = cache_dir / f"{slug}__{category}.json"
    if path.exists() and not force:
        age_h = (time.time() - path.stat().st_mtime) / 3600
        if age_h <= max_age_h:
            logger.info("%-10s %-12s cache hit (%.1f h old)", slug, category, age_h)
            return json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")), path.stat().st_mtime
    logger.info("%-10s %-12s sweeping...", slug, category)
    listings = [_listing_dict(x) for x in SOURCES[slug].sweep(category)]
    cache_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    tmp = path.with_suffix(".tmp")
    tmp.write_text(json.dumps(listings, ensure_ascii=False), encoding="utf-8")
    os.replace(tmp, path)  # never leave a truncated cache behind
    logger.info("%-10s %-12s %d listings", slug, category, len(listings))
    return listings, path.stat().st_mtime


def refresh_cache(markets: list[str], categories: list[str], cache_dir: str | Path) -> dict:
    """Re-sweep every (market, category) and overwrite its cache, ignoring age.

    -> {"ok": [(slug, cat, n)], "failed": [(slug, cat, why)]}. One failed sweep never stops
    the others, and a failed sweep leaves the previous cache file untouched (writes are atomic).
    Search-only markets have nothing to sweep and are skipped."""
    out: dict = {"ok": [], "failed": []}
    cache = Path(cache_dir)
    for slug in markets:
        if not SOURCES[slug].can_sweep:
            continue
        for cat in categories:
            try:
                listings, _ = _sweep_cached(slug, cat, cache, 0, True)
            except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 -- one source must not end the refresh
                logger.error("%s/%s failed: %s", slug, cat, exc)
                out["failed"].append((slug, cat, str(exc)))
                continue
            out["ok"].append((slug, cat, len(listings)))
    return out


def search_query(target: Target) -> str:
    """Keep the query close to verbatim: a ranking autocomplete (mediapark) needs the RAM/storage
    and colour words to rank the exact variant into its short list. Only the leading category
    noun is dropped."""
    nouns = "|".join(re.escape(p) for p in CATEGORY_PREFIX.values())
    return re.sub(rf"^\s*(?:{nouns}|Смартфон|Телевизор)\s+", "", target.name, flags=re.I).strip()


def collect(
    markets: list[str],
    targets: list[Target],
    cache_dir: str | Path,
    max_age_h: float = 24,
    force: bool = False,
) -> Collected:
    out = Collected()
    cache = Path(cache_dir)
    categories = sorted({t.category for t in targets if t.category})
    for slug in markets:
        src = SOURCES[slug]
        stamps = []
        if src.can_sweep:
            for cat in categories:
                try:
                    listings, ts = _sweep_cached(slug, cat, cache, max_age_h, force)
                except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 -- one source must not end the run
                    logger.error("%s/%s failed: %s", slug, cat, exc)
                    out.notes[slug] = (
                        out.notes.get(slug, "") + f"{cat}: sweep failed ({exc}). "
                    ).strip()
                    continue
                out.sweeps[(slug, cat)] = listings
                stamps.append(ts)
                if not listings:
                    out.notes[slug] = (out.notes.get(slug, "") + f"{cat}: no listings. ").strip()
        else:
            hits, failures = {}, 0
            todo = [t for t in targets if t.category]
            for i, t in enumerate(todo, 1):
                try:
                    hits[t.name] = [_listing_dict(x) for x in src.search(search_query(t))]
                except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
                    failures += 1
                    logger.warning("%s search %r failed: %s", slug, t.name, exc)
                    hits[t.name] = []
                if i % 25 == 0:
                    logger.info("%-10s search %d/%d", slug, i, len(todo))
            out.searches[slug] = hits
            stamps.append(time.time())
            if failures:
                out.notes[slug] = f"{failures} of {len(todo)} searches failed."
        if stamps:
            out.fetched_at[slug] = _iso(min(stamps))
        elif slug not in out.notes:
            out.notes[slug] = "no data collected."
    return out



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/research/match.py
================================================
"""Apply the matching engine to a target list and keep every accepted offer.

The matcher decides identity; this module only runs it at scale. Two choices carry over
from the surveys this was extracted from, because each one was a defect once:

* **Bucket by brand first.** `score_match` hard-rejects on a brand mismatch before anything
  else, so comparing a Samsung target against a store's 400 Apple listings is pure waste. A
  listing whose brand could not be read goes in the `None` bucket and is always considered --
  dropping it would lose every listing whose brand we merely failed to parse.
* **The accept threshold depends on the category.** A phone can score 50 (brand 10 + storage 20
  + RAM 10 + model 15 + colour 5) against ACCEPT_THRESHOLD 30. A television has no storage,
  RAM or identity-colour, so a *perfect* match -- brand plus a bidirectional model-code match
  -- scores exactly 25 and a threshold of 30 empties the category. 25 still demands the brand
  AND the model tokens in both directions, so it is the same identity, not a looser one.
"""

import re
from collections import defaultdict

from narxnoma.matching import ACCEPT_THRESHOLD, extract_features, score_match
from narxnoma.research.model import Offer, Target

NON_PHONE_ACCEPT = 25


def accept_threshold(category: str) -> int:
    return ACCEPT_THRESHOLD if category == "smartphones" else NON_PHONE_ACCEPT


# Words that differ between two listings of the SAME product and carry no identity: the
# category noun, marketing, units, packaging language. Used only for the Name-delta audit
# column, never for matching.
_NOISE = re.compile(
    r"^(?:кондиционер\w*|телевизор\w*|холодильник\w*|морозильник\w*|стиральн\w*|машин\w*|"
    r"смартфон\w*|ноутбук\w*|televizor\w*|konditsioner\w*|muzlatgich\w*|sovutgich\w*|kir|yuvish|"
    r"mashina\w*|автомат|inverter|invertor|инвертор\w*|inv|smart|смарт|android|андроид|tv|тв|"
    r"led|qled|oled|uhd|fhd|hd|4k|8k|btu|вт|kg|кг|л|litr|дюйм\w*|new|новый|воздуха|сплит|"
    r"система|белый|черный|серый|g|dc|full|art|ru|uz|с|и|в|на)$",
    re.I,
)


def name_delta(target: str, matched: str) -> str:
    """Significant words in one name but not the other.

    A non-empty value is NOT an error: the matcher deliberately collapses decorated variants of
    one catalogue code. But `ASW-H12A4/FADR` vs `ASW-H12A4/LR1DI` can be a different indoor unit
    at a different price, and the number alone cannot show it. These are the rows to read.
    """

    def toks(s):
        return {
            t
            for t in re.split(r"[^0-9a-zA-Zа-яА-ЯёЁ]+", (s or "").lower())
            if len(t) > 1 and not _NOISE.match(t)
        }

    a, b = toks(target), toks(matched)
    parts = []
    if a - b:
        parts.append("ours: " + " ".join(sorted(a - b)[:6]))
    if b - a:
        parts.append("theirs: " + " ".join(sorted(b - a)[:6]))
    return " | ".join(parts)


def index_by_brand(listings: list[dict], category: str) -> dict:
    buckets: dict = defaultdict(list)
    for listing in listings:
        feats = extract_features(listing["name_raw"], category)
        buckets[feats.brand].append((listing, feats))
    return buckets


def match_target(
    target: Target, market: str, buckets: dict | None, extra_listings: list[dict] | None = None
) -> list[Offer]:
    """Every accepted offer of one market for one target, cheapest first."""
    if not target.category:
        return []
    tfeat = extract_features(target.name, target.category)
    threshold = accept_threshold(target.category)
    candidates: list = []
    if buckets is not None:
        candidates = list(buckets.get(tfeat.brand, []))
        if tfeat.brand is not None:
            candidates += buckets.get(None, [])
    if extra_listings:
        candidates += [
            (x, extract_features(x["name_raw"], target.category)) for x in extra_listings
        ]

    offers, seen = [], set()
    for listing, cfeat in candidates:
        score = score_match(tfeat, cfeat)
        if score < threshold:
            continue
        ident = (listing.get("source_key"), listing["url"], listing["price"])
        if ident in seen:  # the None bucket can repeat a brand bucket's row
            continue
        seen.add(ident)
        offers.append(
            Offer(
                target=target.name,
                market=market,
                listing_name=listing["name_raw"],
                price=int(listing["price"]),
                url=listing["url"],
                availability=listing.get("availability", "UNKNOWN"),
                score=score,
                name_delta=name_delta(target.name, listing["name_raw"]),
                source_key=listing.get("source_key"),
            )
        )
    offers.sort(key=lambda o: (o.price, -o.score))
    return offers



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/research/model.py
================================================
"""What a research run produces, and the JSON that lets a workbook be rebuilt without fetching."""

import json
from dataclasses import asdict, dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path


@dataclass
class Target:
    """One product the user wants researched."""

    name: str
    category: str | None = None  # a narxnoma category slug, or None when unknown
    our_price: float | None = None  # in `currency`; None when the user has no price for it
    currency: str = "UZS"
    note: str = ""
    extra: dict = field(default_factory=dict)  # any other column of the input, kept as-is


@dataclass
class Offer:
    """One accepted listing for one target. Nothing is reduced here: the workbook keeps them all."""

    target: str
    market: str
    listing_name: str
    price: int
    url: str
    availability: str = "UNKNOWN"
    score: int = 0
    name_delta: str = ""
    source_key: str | None = None


@dataclass
class Research:
    targets: list[Target]
    offers: list[Offer]
    markets: list[str]  # fixed column order
    fetched_at: dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)  # market -> ISO time of its catalogue
    notes: dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)  # market -> why it has no data
    run_at: str = ""
    usd_rate: float | None = None
    usd_rate_date: str = ""
    title: str = "Market research"

    def to_json(self, path: str | Path) -> None:
        Path(path).write_text(
            json.dumps(asdict(self), ensure_ascii=False, indent=1), encoding="utf-8"
        )

    @classmethod
    def from_json(cls, path: str | Path) -> "Research":
        d = json.loads(Path(path).read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
        return cls(
            targets=[Target(**t) for t in d["targets"]],
            offers=[Offer(**o) for o in d["offers"]],
            markets=d["markets"],
            fetched_at=d.get("fetched_at", {}),
            notes=d.get("notes", {}),
            run_at=d.get("run_at", ""),
            usd_rate=d.get("usd_rate"),
            usd_rate_date=d.get("usd_rate_date", ""),
            title=d.get("title", "Market research"),
        )



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/research/run.py
================================================
"""One call from a product list to a `Research`: collect, then match."""

import logging
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path

from narxnoma.research.collect import MARKET_ORDER, collect, default_markets
from narxnoma.research.match import index_by_brand, match_target
from narxnoma.research.model import Research, Target
from narxnoma.sources import SOURCES
from narxnoma.sources.http import get_json, polite_session

logger = logging.getLogger("narxnoma.research")

CBU_URL = "https://cbu.uz/ru/arkhiv-kursov-valyut/json/USD/"


def cbu_usd_rate() -> tuple[float, str]:
    """Official USD rate in UZS and its date, from the central bank. Raises when unreachable:
    a silent default would put a wrong number in every converted price."""
    data = get_json(polite_session(), CBU_URL)
    row = data[0] if isinstance(data, list) else data
    return float(str(row["Rate"]).replace(",", ".")), str(row["Date"])


def run_research(
    targets: list[Target],
    markets: list[str] | None = None,
    cache_dir: str | Path = "database/research_cache",
    max_age_h: float = 24,
    force: bool = False,
    title: str = "Market research",
    usd_rate: float | None = None,
) -> Research:
    markets = [m for m in MARKET_ORDER if m in (markets or default_markets())]
    unknown = [m for m in (markets or []) if m not in SOURCES]
    if unknown:
        raise ValueError(f"unknown market(s): {unknown}")
    rate_date = ""
    if any(t.currency == "USD" and t.our_price for t in targets):
        if usd_rate is None:
            usd_rate, rate_date = cbu_usd_rate()
        else:
            rate_date = "given on the command line"

    got = collect(markets, targets, cache_dir, max_age_h=max_age_h, force=force)
    pools = {key: index_by_brand(listings, key[1]) for key, listings in got.sweeps.items()}

    offers = []
    for t in targets:
        if not t.category:
            continue
        for m in markets:
            if SOURCES[m].can_sweep:
                offers += match_target(t, m, pools.get((m, t.category)))
            else:
                offers += match_target(t, m, None, got.searches.get(m, {}).get(t.name))
    logger.info(
        "%d offers for %d of %d products",
        len(offers),
        len({o.target for o in offers}),
        len(targets),
    )
    notes = dict(got.notes)
    no_cat = [t.name for t in targets if not t.category]
    if no_cat:
        logger.warning(
            "%d products have no category and were not matched (pass --category)", len(no_cat)
        )
    return Research(
        targets=targets,
        offers=offers,
        markets=markets,
        fetched_at=got.fetched_at,
        notes=notes,
        run_at=datetime.now().isoformat(timespec="seconds"),
        usd_rate=usd_rate,
        usd_rate_date=rate_date,
        title=title,
    )



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/research/targets.py
================================================
"""Read the product list the user wants researched.

Accepted shapes, picked by file extension:

    .xlsx / .xlsm   first sheet, header row; columns found by name (see ALIASES)
    .csv            same, UTF-8 (with or without BOM), delimiter sniffed
    .txt            one product name per line; "name;price" or "name<TAB>price" carries a price

Only the name is required. A price is "our price" -- the baseline every delta is measured
against; with none, the workbook still compares the markets with each other. A category is a
narxnoma slug (see `CATEGORY_ALIASES` for the words accepted); with none, the run-wide
`default_category` applies.
"""

import csv
import re
from pathlib import Path

from narxnoma.matching.keys import CATEGORIES
from narxnoma.research.model import Target

ALIASES = {
    "name": (
        "name",
        "product",
        "product name",
        "title",
        "item",
        "товар",
        "наименование",
        "название",
        "mahsulot",
        "nomi",
    ),
    "price": ("our price", "price", "our price (sum)", "наша цена", "цена", "narx", "baho"),
    "category": ("category", "категория", "turkum", "kategoriya"),
    "currency": ("currency", "валюта", "valyuta"),
    "note": ("note", "notes", "comment", "примечание", "комментарий"),
}

CATEGORY_ALIASES = {
    "smartphones": (
        "smartphones",
        "smartphone",
        "phone",
        "phones",
        "смартфон",
        "смартфоны",
        "телефон",
        "телефоны",
        "смартфонлар",
        "smartfon",
        "telefon",
    ),
    "televisions": (
        "televisions",
        "television",
        "tv",
        "tvs",
        "тв",
        "телевизор",
        "телевизоры",
        "televizor",
    ),
    "laptops": ("laptops", "laptop", "ноутбук", "ноутбуки", "noutbuk"),
    "acs": (
        "acs",
        "ac",
        "air conditioner",
        "air conditioners",
        "кондиционер",
        "кондиционеры",
        "konditsioner",
    ),
    "fridges": ("fridges", "fridge", "refrigerator", "холодильник", "холодильники", "muzlatgich"),
    "washers": (
        "washers",
        "washer",
        "washing machine",
        "стиральная машина",
        "стиральные машины",
        "kir yuvish mashinasi",
    ),
    "ovens": ("ovens", "oven", "духовой шкаф", "духовка", "духовые шкафы", "duxovka"),
}
_CAT_LOOKUP = {a: slug for slug, names in CATEGORY_ALIASES.items() for a in names}


def category_slug(value) -> str | None:
    """A narxnoma slug for whatever the user wrote, or None."""
    if value is None:
        return None
    v = str(value).strip().lower()
    if v in CATEGORIES:
        return v
    return _CAT_LOOKUP.get(v)


def _norm_header(h) -> str:
    return re.sub(r"\s+", " ", str(h or "").strip().lower())


def _map_columns(headers: list) -> dict[str, int]:
    found: dict[str, int] = {}
    normed = [_norm_header(h) for h in headers]
    for key, names in ALIASES.items():
        for want in names:
            if want in normed and key not in found:
                found[key] = normed.index(want)
    return found


def _to_price(v) -> float | None:
    if v in (None, ""):
        return None
    if isinstance(v, (int, float)):
        return float(v) if v > 0 else None
    s = re.sub(r"[^\d.,]", "", str(v).replace("\xa0", "").replace(" ", ""))
    if not s:
        return None
    if "," in s and "." in s:
        s = s.replace(",", "")
    elif "," in s:
        s = s.replace(",", ".") if len(s.rsplit(",", 1)[1]) <= 2 else s.replace(",", "")
    try:
        p = float(s)
    except ValueError:
        return None
    return p if p > 0 else None


def _cell(row, cols, key):
    i = cols.get(key)
    return row[i] if i is not None and i < len(row) else None


def _rows_to_targets(headers: list, rows, default_category: str | None) -> list[Target]:
    cols = _map_columns(headers)
    if "name" not in cols:
        raise ValueError(
            f"no product-name column found in {list(headers)!r}; "
            f"name one of: {', '.join(ALIASES['name'])}"
        )
    known = set(cols.values())
    out, seen = [], set()
    for row in rows:
        name = str(_cell(row, cols, "name") or "").strip()
        if not name or name.lower() in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(name.lower())
        cur = str(_cell(row, cols, "currency") or "UZS").strip().upper()
        out.append(
            Target(
                name=name,
                category=category_slug(_cell(row, cols, "category")) or default_category,
                our_price=_to_price(_cell(row, cols, "price")),
                currency="USD" if cur in ("USD", "$", "ДОЛЛАР") else "UZS",
                note=str(_cell(row, cols, "note") or ""),
                extra={
                    str(headers[i]): row[i]
                    for i in range(min(len(headers), len(row)))
                    if i not in known and headers[i] and row[i] not in (None, "")
                },
            )
        )
    return out


def load_targets(path: str | Path, default_category: str | None = None) -> list[Target]:
    p = Path(path)
    ext = p.suffix.lower()
    if ext in (".xlsx", ".xlsm"):
        from openpyxl import load_workbook  # optional dependency, only for xlsx input

        ws = load_workbook(p, read_only=True, data_only=True).worksheets[0]
        it = ws.iter_rows(values_only=True)
        headers = list(next(it))
        return _rows_to_targets(headers, [list(r) for r in it], default_category)
    if ext == ".csv":
        text = p.read_text(encoding="utf-8-sig")
        dialect = csv.Sniffer().sniff(text[:4096], delimiters=",;\t")
        rows = list(csv.reader(text.splitlines(), dialect))
        return _rows_to_targets(rows[0], rows[1:], default_category)
    if ext == ".txt":
        out, seen = [], set()
        for line in p.read_text(encoding="utf-8-sig").splitlines():
            line = line.strip()
            if not line or line.startswith("#"):
                continue
            parts = re.split(r"\t|;", line, maxsplit=1)
            name = parts[0].strip()
            if name.lower() in seen:
                continue
            seen.add(name.lower())
            out.append(
                Target(
                    name=name,
                    category=default_category,
                    our_price=_to_price(parts[1]) if len(parts) > 1 else None,
                )
            )
        return out
    raise ValueError(f"unsupported target file type {ext!r}; use .xlsx, .csv or .txt")



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/research/workbook.py
================================================
"""The analysable workbook: a database you can pivot, and formulas that follow your edits.

A static survey file is a picture. This one is a small database plus a view of it:

    Offers    Excel Table `tblOffers`   one row per (product x market) offer, every one kept. The
                                        pivot source. Category / Baseline / Delta / Band are
                                        formulas, so a table row you add fills itself in.
    Products  Excel Table `tblProducts` one row per researched product. Our price is an INPUT
                                        (yellow): change it and every delta, colour, band follows.
    Markets   Excel Table `tblMarkets`  one row per market: coverage, delta, wins, freshness.
    Prices    product x market grid     the cheapest accepted offer per market, as formulas over
                                        `tblOffers`, plus min / median / delta columns. Heat map.
    Heatmap   product x market grid     each cell's difference from baseline, colour scale.
    Links     product x market grid     as-fetched snapshot, every price a hyperlink to its offer.
    Settings  thresholds + baseline     named cells (cfg_par, cfg_mid, cfg_sat, cfg_baseline) that
                                        the heat map, the bands and the pivots all read.
    Notes     provenance                every derived number defined, every market's freshness.

The heat map is CONDITIONAL FORMATTING, never a baked fill. A baked fill goes stale the moment
a price is edited, and a wrong colour is read faster than the number it contradicts. Each rule
compares a cell to ITS ROW's baseline -- which a colour scale cannot do, a formula rule can --
and takes its thresholds from Settings, so it is data-driven and survives a re-sort.

PivotTables are not made here: openpyxl cannot write them and would drop them on re-save. Run
`excel/` (COM, or the raw-OOXML writer) AFTER this module, and never re-save the result through
openpyxl: it silently removes slicers.
"""

from datetime import datetime

from openpyxl import Workbook
from openpyxl.chart import BarChart, Reference
from openpyxl.formatting.rule import ColorScaleRule, FormulaRule
from openpyxl.styles import Alignment, Border, Font, PatternFill, Side
from openpyxl.utils import get_column_letter as L
from openpyxl.workbook.defined_name import DefinedName
from openpyxl.workbook.properties import CalcProperties
from openpyxl.worksheet.datavalidation import DataValidation
from openpyxl.worksheet.hyperlink import Hyperlink
from openpyxl.worksheet.table import Table, TableStyleInfo

from narxnoma.research.model import Research

NAVY = "1F3864"
HEADER_FILL = PatternFill("solid", fgColor=NAVY)
HEADER_FONT = Font(bold=True, color="FFFFFF")
INPUT_FILL = PatternFill("solid", fgColor="FFF2CC")
LINK_FONT = Font(color="0563C1", underline="single")
MONEY = "#,##0"
PCT = "+0.0%;-0.0%;0.0%"

# heat colours, darkest = furthest from the baseline. Red: the market is CHEAPER than the
# baseline (we are exposed); green: the market is dearer (our price is competitive).
HEAT = {
    "cheap3": "F8696B",
    "cheap2": "FBA39C",
    "cheap1": "FDD9D6",
    "dear1": "DDF0E1",
    "dear2": "A9D8B2",
    "dear3": "63BE7B",
}

BANDS = (
    "1 Market much cheaper",
    "2 Market cheaper",
    "3 Market slightly cheaper",
    "4 About equal",
    "5 Market slightly dearer",
    "6 Market dearer",
    "7 Market much dearer",
)


def tr(table: str, col: str) -> str:
    """Structured reference to a column of the current row."""
    return f"{table}[[#This Row],[{col}]]"


def _header(ws, row, titles, widths=None):
    for i, t in enumerate(titles, 1):
        c = ws.cell(row=row, column=i, value=t)
        c.fill, c.font = HEADER_FILL, HEADER_FONT
        c.alignment = Alignment(wrap_text=True, vertical="center", horizontal="center")
        if widths:
            ws.column_dimensions[L(i)].width = widths[i - 1]
    ws.row_dimensions[row].height = 32


def _table(ws, name, ref, style="TableStyleMedium2"):
    t = Table(displayName=name, ref=ref)
    t.tableStyleInfo = TableStyleInfo(name=style, showRowStripes=True)
    ws.add_table(t)
    return t


def _calc_columns(ws, table: Table, formulas: dict[str, str]) -> None:
    """Mark columns as calculated so a row added to the table fills itself in.

    `_initialise_columns()` names every column "Column1..N"; the names must be put back from the
    header cells or Excel refuses to open the file (table column names must equal the header text).
    """
    from openpyxl.worksheet.table import TableFormula

    table._initialise_columns()
    first = table.ref.split(":")[0]
    row = int("".join(ch for ch in first if ch.isdigit()))
