================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/sources/alifshop.py
================================================
"""alifshop.uz client: multi-seller marketplace, brand-query sweep.

alifshop has no browsable catalogue -- like idea.uz, `sweep()` loops over a
fixed list of brand queries (see narxnoma.sources.brand_queries) and calls
the search endpoint once per brand per page. `page` is **0-based** here,
unlike beemarket/texnomart which are 1-based, and `size` is capped at 20
server-side regardless of what is requested. The smartphone category alone
runs to thousands of listings, too many to page through fully, so
`sweep()` stops at `n_pages` (from the response) or a practical cap of 3
pages (60 listings) per brand -- see docs/parsers/alifshop.md. `search(query)`
issues the same call with the caller's query text, page 0.

alifshop is a multi-seller marketplace: the same phone is listed by many
partners at different prices, and every one of those is a distinct,
legitimate offer -- `source_key` is the item's own `id` (a UUID assigned
per listing by alifshop itself), which distinguishes each partner's offer
even when the product name, brand, and even list price are identical. It
does not collapse multiple sellers of the same phone into one row, which
is exactly what this source must not do.

alifshop's price is in **tiyin** (1/100 SUM) -- see coerce_tiyin_price.

No per-item category signal exists. Unlike idea's `category_id` or
beemarket's `category.slug`, individual items in this response carry no
category field at all -- the only category information in the payload is
`aggregations.category`, a dataset-wide histogram over the *whole* result
set for the query (e.g. "Смартфоны Samsung": 690, "Микроволновые печи":
50). That histogram cannot label any single item, but the search endpoint
itself accepts that histogram's own bucket names back as a `category`
filter, and it genuinely narrows server-side (verified live: a "Samsung"
query returns 1,034 items; adding `"category": "Смартфоны Samsung"` drops
that to 690, all genuine phones; adding a nonsense category value returns
0, proving the server really filters rather than ignoring an unknown key).
The catch is the bucket name is brand-specific ("Смартфоны Samsung",
"Смартфоны Xiaomi", ...), so it must be discovered per brand query rather
than hardcoded -- see `phone_category_from_aggregations`. `sweep()` fetches
page 0 unfiltered once per brand to read the aggregation, then re-requests
with the discovered `category` value and paginates that filtered set. If
no phone-like bucket is found (a retailer rename), it logs a warning and
falls back to the old unfiltered sweep for that brand rather than yielding
nothing.
"""
import logging
from dataclasses import replace
from typing import Iterator

from narxnoma.sources.base import Listing, coerce_price, paginate, try_build_listing
from narxnoma.sources.brand_queries import queries_for
from narxnoma.sources.http import polite_session, post_json
from narxnoma.sources.not_a_phone import is_phone_listing

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

API_URL = "https://gw.alifshop.uz/web/client/search/full-text"
# Not .../product/<slug> -- that 404s ("Biz buni topa olmadik"). The slug
# from the API is correct; only this path prefix differs. Verified live by
# opening a generated link in a browser -- see docs/parsers/alifshop.md.
PRODUCT_URL = "https://alifshop.uz/moderated-offer/{slug}"

SIZE = 20  # server-side cap; asking for more is silently ignored
MAX_PAGES = 3  # practical cap per brand query -- 60 listings is enough for a stable median

_HEADERS = {
    "Accept": "application/json",
    "Accept-Language": "ru",
    "Content-Type": "application/json",
    # Fixed public string, not a credential.
    "service-token": "service-token-alifshop",
}


def _coerce_tiyin_price(raw_price) -> int | None:
    """Convert alifshop's tiyin price to SUM.

    The single most dangerous unit bug in this project: alifshop reports
    price in tiyin (1/100 SUM). There is exactly one price field and one
    code path here on purpose -- a *partial* divisor bug (dividing on one
    path but not another) produces prices 100x too large that still look
    plausible and would slip past PRICE_MIN/PRICE_MAX undetected. Always
    run the raw value through coerce_price first so non-numeric/None/zero
    values are rejected the same way every other source rejects them,
    *then* divide -- never divide an already-untrusted raw value.
    """
    tiyin = coerce_price(raw_price)
    if tiyin is None:
        return None
    return tiyin // 100


def _parse_item(item: dict) -> Listing | None:
    item_id = item.get("id")
    if not item_id:
        return None
    slug = item.get("slug")
    if not slug:
        return None
    product = item.get("product") or {}
    name = product.get("name")
    price = _coerce_tiyin_price(item.get("price"))

    quantity = item.get("quantity") or 0
    availability = "IN_STOCK" if quantity > 0 else "OUT_OF_STOCK"

    title_image = product.get("title_image") or {}

    return try_build_listing(
        store_slug="alifshop",
        source_key=str(item_id),
        name_raw=name,
        price=price,
        url=PRODUCT_URL.format(slug=slug),
        availability=availability,
        image_url=title_image.get("sm_path"),
        raw=item,
    )


def parse(payload: dict) -> list[Listing]:
    """Turn one raw search response into Listings. Pure function, no I/O.

    Unfiltered and faithful to the payload -- category narrowing happens at
    the request, via `category` in the search body (see module docstring
    and `phone_category_from_aggregations`), not here. parse() must stay a
    pure, unfiltered view of whatever payload it is given, filtered or not.
    """
    items = (payload or {}).get("items") or []
    listings = []
    for item in items:
        lst = _parse_item(item)
        if lst is not None:
            listings.append(lst)
    return listings


def phone_category_from_aggregations(payload: dict) -> str | None:
    """Pick the phones bucket out of a response's category facet.

    alifshop has no per-item category, but the search backend facets on
    category and accepts that facet's own display string as a filter. The
    bucket names are brand-specific ("Смартфоны Samsung"), so the value has
    to be discovered per query rather than hardcoded.

    Matching rule: `key.casefold().startswith("смартфон")` -- a *prefix*
    test, not a substring test. The observed bucket "Чехлы для смартфонов"
    (phone cases) *contains* "смартфон" and a naive `in` check would select
    it -- precisely the noise this filtering exists to remove. "Смарт-часы"
    (smartwatches) starts with "смарт" but not "смартфон", so the prefix
    rule correctly excludes it too.

    Reads defensively: a missing `aggregations` key, an empty `terms` list,
    or non-dict/non-string junk in a bucket all just yield no match. If
    more than one bucket matches (unexpected, but not impossible), the one
    with the largest doc_count wins and the rest are logged.
    """
    terms = (((payload or {}).get("aggregations") or {}).get("category") or {}).get("terms") or []
    matches = [
        term for term in terms
        if isinstance(term, dict)
        and isinstance(term.get("key"), str)
        and term["key"].casefold().startswith("смартфон")
    ]
    if not matches:
        return None
    best = max(matches, key=lambda term: term.get("doc_count") or 0)
    others = [term for term in matches if term is not best]
    if others:
        logger.info(
            "alifshop: multiple phone-like category buckets, picked %r (doc_count=%s); others=%s",
            best["key"],
            best.get("doc_count"),
            [(term["key"], term.get("doc_count")) for term in others],
        )
    return best["key"]


# Plan 5: category -> the string alifshop's category facet uses to name that
# bucket (verified live 2026-08-10). We match by the fragment shown, then
# pick the largest doc_count bucket among matches.
CATEGORY_BUCKET_KEYWORD = {
    "smartphones": "смартфон",
    "televisions": "телевизор",
    "laptops": "ноутбук",
    "acs": "кондиционер",
    "fridges": "холодильник",
    "washers": "стиральн",
    "ovens": "духов",
}


def category_from_aggregations(payload: dict, keyword: str) -> str | None:
    """Pick the matching bucket out of a response's category facet.

    Same discovery contract as phone_category_from_aggregations but for any
    category fragment: the bucket keys are display strings, so the value is
    discovered per query rather than hardcoded. The largest doc_count bucket
    whose key contains the (casefolded) fragment wins.
    """
    terms = (((payload or {}).get("aggregations") or {}).get("category") or {}).get("terms") or []
    matches = [
        term for term in terms
        if isinstance(term, dict)
        and isinstance(term.get("key"), str)
        and keyword.casefold() in term["key"].casefold()
    ]
    if not matches:
        return None
    best = max(matches, key=lambda term: term.get("doc_count") or 0)
    return best["key"]


class AlifshopSource:
    slug = "alifshop"
    can_sweep = True

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._session = None

    def _get_session(self):
        if self._session is None:
            self._session = polite_session(
                referer="https://alifshop.uz/",
                origin="https://alifshop.uz",
                extra_headers=_HEADERS,
            )
        return self._session

    def _fetch(self, query: str, page: int, category: str | None = None) -> dict:
        session = self._get_session()
        body = {"query": query, "page": page, "size": SIZE}
        if category:
            body["category"] = category
        return post_json(session, API_URL, json=body)

    def sweep(self, category: str = "smartphones") -> Iterator[Listing]:
        """Every item alifshop returns across a fixed list of brand queries,
        walking pagination up to n_pages or MAX_PAGES per brand.

        Per brand: fetch page 0 unfiltered (one request, doubles as both
        the first page of items and the aggregation used to find the
        category's bucket -- see category_from_aggregations), then re-request
        page 0 *with* the discovered `category` bucket and paginate that
        filtered result set. If no matching bucket is found, fall back to the
        unfiltered sweep. The phone denylist (is_phone_listing) applies ONLY
        to the smartphones sweep -- running it on a TV sweep would drop every
        "телевизор"/"ноутбук" title. Every listing is tagged with the sweep's
        category.
        """
        keyword = CATEGORY_BUCKET_KEYWORD.get(category)
        if keyword is None:
            raise ValueError(f"unknown category {category!r}")
        seen: set[str] = set()
        # Phones sweep by BRAND, everything else by the product NOUN: the
        # brands that sell white goods here (Artel, Premier, Shivaki, LG) sell
        # no phones, so the phone frontier never asked for them at all. The
        # bucket discovery below still runs per query and still refuses to fall
        # back to an unfiltered sweep for a non-phone category.
        for query in queries_for(category):
            first_payload = self._fetch(query, 0)
            bucket = category_from_aggregations(first_payload, keyword)
            if bucket:
                logger.info(
                    "alifshop: narrowing brand query %r (>=%s bucket) to %r",
                    query, category, bucket,
                )
                payload = self._fetch(query, 0, category=bucket)
            elif category == "smartphones":
                logger.warning(
                    "alifshop: no %s category bucket for %r -- sweeping unfiltered",
                    category, query,
                )
                payload = first_payload
            else:
                # Honesty over coverage: falling back to the unfiltered sweep
                # for a NON-phone category would tag phones (from an "apple"
                # brand query, say) as TVs/ACs. Skip the brand instead.
                logger.warning(
                    "alifshop: no %s category bucket for %r -- skipping brand (no unfiltered fallback for non-phone categories)",
                    category, query,
                )
                continue

            def fetch(page):
                return self._fetch(query, page, category=bucket)

            def parse_page(payload):
                for listing in parse(payload):
                    if listing.source_key in seen:
                        continue
                    if category == "smartphones" and not is_phone_listing(listing):
                        continue
                    seen.add(listing.source_key)
                    if listing.category != category:
                        listing = replace(listing, category=category)
                    yield listing

            def following(payload, page):
                n_pages = (payload or {}).get("n_pages") or 1
                nxt = page + 1
                return nxt if nxt < n_pages and nxt < MAX_PAGES else None

            yield from paginate(fetch, parse_page, following, first_page=0,
                                max_pages=MAX_PAGES)


    def search(self, query: str) -> Iterator[Listing]:
        payload = self._fetch(query, 0)
        yield from parse(payload)



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/sources/asaxiy.py
================================================
"""asaxiy.uz client: the one source that is genuinely server-rendered HTML.

docs/parsers/README.md opens with "None of these sites can be scraped from
HTML -- every one is a JavaScript SPA that renders an empty shell." asaxiy is
the exception, and the exception is load-bearing here: it has **no public JSON
API at all**. Probed live 2026-08-10, the only `/ajax/*` endpoints its own
frontend calls are `check-phone`, `login-validate`, `send-verification-sms`
and `new-filter-url` -- account and filter plumbing, no catalogue. Its category
pages return a complete, fully-populated product grid in the initial HTML
document, and its "show more" button re-requests that same URL. So the
durable path for THIS retailer is the inverse of the rule: parse the page.

Three things make that safe rather than fragile, and each is asserted by a
test against a recorded page:

1. **The identity is not scraped from prose.** Every card carries a hidden
   `<span id="product_cart_data_<id>">` whose data-attributes are the same
   values asaxiy's own cart uses: `data-id`, `data-name`, `data-name-ru`,
   `data-price`, `data-old-price`, `data-img`, `data-brand-name`. We read
   those, not the rendered price text ("6 719 000 so'm") -- no thousands
   separators, no currency word, no locale to get wrong.
2. **The product URL is tied to the product id.** The card's anchor is
   `<a href="/product/<slug>" onclick="sendSelectItemEvent(<id>, this)">`, and
   the parser requires that id to equal the cart block's id. A card boundary
   that ever shifted would therefore produce no listing rather than a listing
   with a neighbour's URL -- the failure mode that matters when splitting a
   document on a class name.
3. **Russian is requested by path, not by cookie.** See LANGUAGE below.

THE PAGINATION TRAP, measured live: a page number beyond the end does not
404 and does not return an empty grid -- it **301-redirects to page 1 and
serves 24 real products again**. `stiralnye-mashiny/page=9` (of 6 pages)
returned 24 cards. A client that stopped on "an empty page" would loop
forever re-ingesting page 1 and record a triumphant harvest of duplicates.
The end of the catalogue is `totalPages`, which every full page states in
its own inline script (`let totalPages = parseInt('6', 10) || 1;`), and
`sweep()` additionally stops the moment a page contributes no product id it
has not already seen. Two independent stops for one trap, because the
failure is silent.

LANGUAGE. The corpus is Russian; asaxiy defaults to Uzbek (`<html lang="uz">`).
Neither the `_language` cookie nor `Accept-Language` changes that -- both were
tried and both returned Uzbek titles. `?language=ru` works, but only because
it 301s to the canonical `/ru/product/...` path, and that redirect is dropped
when the request carries `X-Requested-With: XMLHttpRequest`. So this client
requests the `/ru/` path directly: stateless, no cookie jar to keep warm, no
redirect to survive. `parse()` still prefers `data-name-ru` over `data-name`,
which means it yields Russian names even from an Uzbek page -- belt and
braces, and testable against a fixture recorded either way.

WHY FULL PAGES AND NOT THE LIGHTER FRAGMENT. Sending `X-Requested-With:
XMLHttpRequest` gets the bare product grid (~310 KB instead of ~800 KB), which
is tempting. It also drops the `totalPages` script -- the only end-of-
catalogue signal there is, against a paginator that answers out-of-range
pages with page 1. Trading the stop condition for bandwidth is not a trade.
Our largest category is 10 pages.
"""
import html as html_module
import logging
import re
from dataclasses import replace
from typing import Iterator

from narxnoma.sources.base import Listing, coerce_price, paginate, try_build_listing
from narxnoma.sources.http import get_text, polite_session

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

BASE_URL = "https://asaxiy.uz"
# The Russian catalogue path. Page 1 has no suffix; page N >= 2 appends
# "/page=N" as a PATH segment, not a query parameter (asaxiy's own pagination
# links and its "show more" button both use this form).
CATEGORY_URL = BASE_URL + "/ru/product/{path}"

# category -> asaxiy leaf category path, verified live 2026-08-10 by walking
# the full category tree off /product/bytovaya-tehnika and counting each leaf:
#
#   smartphones   233 items / 10 pages
#   televisions    57 items /  3 pages
#   laptops        90 items /  4 pages
#   acs           113 items /  5 pages
#   fridges       107 items /  5 pages
#   washers       132 items /  6 pages
#   ovens          18 items /  1 page
#
# Leaves deliberately NOT swept, each checked rather than assumed:
#
#   .../kuhnya/mikrovolnovye-i-duhovye-pechi -- reads like the oven category
#   and is not one. All 120 of its listings were read: 91 microwaves, 29
#   mini-ovens, and ZERO built-in ovens. Meanwhile all 540 `ovens` offers the
#   other six sources have contributed contain no microwave at all. Sweeping
#   it would add a product type no other retailer files under `ovens`, so
#   nothing could ever price-compare, and every one would render under the
#   "Духовой шкаф" label. asaxiy simply does not sell built-in ovens.
#
#   .../kuhnya/nastolnye-plity -- 3 items, tabletop hotplates (Sonifer
#   SF-3050, BQ HP124SS). A countertop hob is not the freestanding cooker the
#   other sources list under `ovens`.
#
#   .../noutbuki (the parent) -- carries laptop BAGS, stands, docks and
#   chargers as siblings of the machines. `noutbuki-2` is the leaf that holds
#   only laptops.
CATEGORY_PATHS = {
    "smartphones": "telefony-i-gadzhety/telefony/smartfony",
    "televisions": "televizory-video-i-audio/televizory",
    "laptops":     "kompyutery-i-orgtehnika/noutbuki/noutbuki-2",
    "acs":         "klimaticheskaya-tehnika/kondicionery",
    "fridges":     "bytovaya-tehnika/krupnaya-tehnika-dlya-kuhni/holodilniki",
    "washers":     "bytovaya-tehnika/tehnika-dlya-doma-2/stiralnye-mashiny",
    "ovens":       "bytovaya-tehnika/krupnaya-tehnika-dlya-kuhni/gazovye-plity",
}

_HEADERS = {
    # A catalogue page, not JSON -- polite_session's default Accept would be
    # a lie about what this client wants.
    "Accept": "text/html,application/xhtml+xml,application/xml;q=0.9,*/*;q=0.8",
    "Accept-Language": "ru-RU,ru;q=0.9",
}

# One product card starts here. Splitting a document on a class name is only
# safe because nothing downstream trusts the split alone: a card that does not
# also contain a cart block AND an anchor whose sendSelectItemEvent id matches
# that block yields no listing at all.
_CARD_BOUNDARY = re.compile(r'(?=<div class="product__item )')

# The hidden block asaxiy's own add-to-cart reads. Machine-generated, one per
# card, and the source of every field except the URL.
_CART_DATA = re.compile(r'<span id="product_cart_data_(?P<id>\d+)"(?P<attrs>.*?)hidden>', re.S)
_ATTR = re.compile(r'data-([a-z\-]+)="(?P<value>[^"]*)"')

# The product link, and the assertion that it belongs to this card. The `/ru`
# segment is optional because asaxiy localises its own hrefs: the Uzbek page
# links to /product/<slug>, the Russian one to /ru/product/<slug>. Whichever
# it gives us is what we store, so a stored link opens the page in the
# language the sweep read.
_PRODUCT_LINK = re.compile(
    r'<a\s+href="(?P<path>(?:/ru)?/product/[^"?#]+)"\s+'
    r'onclick="sendSelectItemEvent\((?P<id>\d+)')

# Structural, language-independent stock signal: an in-stock card renders the
# buy control, an out-of-stock one renders "● Нет в наличии" instead.
# Measured on the whole washers category (144 cards): 132 carried the control
# and 12 did not, and the 12 were exactly the 12 carrying the out-of-stock
# text -- no card had both and none had neither.
#
# The CLASS, not the button's href, and not the text. The href is localised
# (`/cart/simple?slug=` on an Uzbek page, `/ru/cart/simple?slug=` on a Russian
# one) -- reading it cost a first attempt that scored every Russian card
# out-of-stock while looking perfectly reasonable. The text is localised too.
# `js-buy-guest` is the hook asaxiy's own JavaScript binds to, identical on
# both language paths.
_ADD_TO_CART = "js-buy-guest"

# `let totalPages = parseInt('6', 10) || 1;` -- see the module docstring. This
# is the ONLY end-of-catalogue signal; an out-of-range page serves page 1.
_TOTAL_PAGES = re.compile(r"totalPages\s*=\s*parseInt\('(\d+)'")

# Things asaxiy files inside a product category that are not that product.
# Its leaves are broader than the other six retailers': `stiralnye-mashiny`
# holds tumble dryers, drying racks, stacking kits and laundry gel alongside
# the washing machines, and `televizory` holds wall-mounting SERVICES.
#
# This matters more than the usual "search returned an accessory", because
# these DO form keys. "Сушильная машина Bosch WTN86211ME" has a brand and a
# catalogue code, so it clusters perfectly -- into a variant the site then
# renders as "Стиральная машина Bosch WTN86211ME". A tumble dryer labelled a
# washing machine is not a coverage gap, it is a wrong page.
#
# A DENYLIST, not an allowlist, for the reason not_a_phone.py gives: asaxiy's
# titles are template-generated and noun-led today ("Смартфон Xiaomi ...",
# 233 of 233), and an allowlist would silently drop the whole category the
# day that template changes. Earned by reading all 769 live titles across the
# seven swept leaves on 2026-08-10: it drops 76 (9.9%), every one of them
# genuinely not the category's product, and drops nothing real.
#
# The ^ anchor is load-bearing on one entry. "Сушильная машина" at the START
# is a tumble dryer; "Стирально-сушильная машина" and "Встраиваемая стирально-
# сушильная машина" are washer-dryer COMBOS -- washing machines, which olcha
# and uzum already list under `washers`. Anchoring keeps the combos.
_WRONG_PRODUCT = re.compile(
    r"^\s*("
    r"услуг\w*|установк\w*|монтаж\w*|настенный\s+монтаж|"
    r"лицензи\w*|официальн\w+\s+лицензионн\w*|"
    r"кронштейн\w*|мобильн\w+\s+кронштейн\w*|"
    r"подставк\w*|соединительн\w*|антивибрацион\w*|"
    r"гель|универсальн\w+\s+гель|порош\w*|universal\s+liquid|"
    r"сушилк\w*|сушильн\w+\s+машин\w*"
    r")",
    re.I,
)


def is_wanted(listing: Listing) -> bool:
    """False for the non-products asaxiy mixes into a category leaf.

    Applied in `sweep()` only -- `parse()` stays a faithful pure function of
    one document, as in every other client here.
    """
    return not _WRONG_PRODUCT.match(listing.name_raw or "")


def total_pages(page_html: str) -> int:
    """How many pages this category has, per the page's own script.

    Returns 1 when the marker is absent. That is the safe direction: a
    missing signal ends the sweep after one page (visible immediately as a
    coverage shortfall) rather than walking a paginator whose end we cannot
    see, against a site that answers out-of-range pages with page 1.
    """
    match = _TOTAL_PAGES.search(page_html or "")
    if match is None:
        logger.warning("asaxiy: no totalPages marker on this page; treating it as the last")
        return 1
    return max(1, int(match.group(1)))


def _card_listing(card: str) -> Listing | None:
    """One card -> a Listing, or None if the card is not a complete product.

    Every field comes from the cart data-attributes except the URL, which
    must independently name the same product id -- see the module docstring.
    """
    data = _CART_DATA.search(card)
    if data is None:
        return None
    product_id = data.group("id")
    attrs = {key: value for key, value in _ATTR.findall(data.group("attrs"))}

    link = _PRODUCT_LINK.search(card)
    if link is None or link.group("id") != product_id:
        # Either no product anchor in this chunk, or the chunk straddles a
        # card boundary and the anchor belongs to a different product. Both
        # are "we do not know this product's URL", and a price we cannot
        # attribute to a specific listing is worthless.
        logger.warning("asaxiy: no anchor matching product %s; dropping the card", product_id)
        return None

    # data-name-ru first: it is present even on an Uzbek page, so the parser
    # yields Russian names whichever language the request happened to get.
    name = attrs.get("name-ru") or attrs.get("name") or ""

    return try_build_listing(
        store_slug="asaxiy",
        source_key=product_id,
        # The attribute values are HTML-escaped in the document
        # ("bug&lsquo;", "&#039;"); unescaping is what makes them the same
        # strings the site renders, which is what the matcher expects.
        name_raw=html_module.unescape(name).strip(),
        price=coerce_price(attrs.get("price")),
        url=BASE_URL + link.group("path"),
        availability="IN_STOCK" if _ADD_TO_CART in card else "OUT_OF_STOCK",
        image_url=attrs.get("img") or None,
        raw={"id": product_id, **attrs},
    )


def parse(page_html: str) -> list[Listing]:
    """Turn one raw category page into Listings. Pure function, no I/O.

    Unfiltered and faithful to the document, like every other client's
    `parse()`. Category scoping is crawl policy and lives in the
    CATEGORY_PATHS leaves that `sweep()` requests.
    """
    listings = []
    for card in _CARD_BOUNDARY.split(page_html or "")[1:]:
        listing = _card_listing(card)
        if listing is not None:
            listings.append(listing)
    return listings


class AsaxiySource:
    slug = "asaxiy"
    can_sweep = True

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._session = None

    def _get_session(self):
        if self._session is None:
            self._session = polite_session(
                referer=f"{BASE_URL}/",
                origin=BASE_URL,
                extra_headers=_HEADERS,
            )
        return self._session

    def _url(self, path: str, page: int) -> str:
        url = CATEGORY_URL.format(path=path)
        return url if page <= 1 else f"{url}/page={page}"

    def sweep(self, category: str = "smartphones") -> Iterator[Listing]:
        """Every product in one asaxiy category, walking pagination to the end."""
        path = CATEGORY_PATHS.get(category)
        if path is None:
            raise ValueError(f"unknown category {category!r}")
        session = self._get_session()

        def fetch(page):
            return get_text(session, self._url(path, page))

        # The second stop. `totalPages` is the primary one, but an out-of-range
        # page here serves page 1 rather than nothing, so a wrong or missing
        # totalPages would walk to MAX_PAGES re-ingesting page 1 and record a
        # triumphant harvest of duplicates. Ids already seen cannot be new
        # products, so a page contributing none is the end of the catalogue
        # however we got there.
        #
        # `state` is what makes the two stops one mechanism rather than two
        # half-mechanisms: `paginate` yields a whole page's listings to the
        # loop below before it calls `following`, so by the time `following`
        # runs, `state["new"]` is exactly how many products THIS page added.
        state = {"new": 0}

        def following(page_html, page):
            if state["new"] == 0:
                logger.info("asaxiy %s: page %d added no product not already "
                            "seen; treating it as the end of the catalogue",
                            category, page)
                return None
            state["new"] = 0
            return page + 1 if page < total_pages(page_html) else None

        seen: set[str] = set()
        refused = 0
        for listing in paginate(fetch, parse, following):
            if listing.source_key in seen:
                continue
            seen.add(listing.source_key)
            state["new"] += 1
            if not is_wanted(listing):
                refused += 1
                continue
            yield replace(listing, category=category) if listing.category != category else listing
        # Counted and logged rather than dropped in silence: this number moving
        # is how a change to asaxiy's title template becomes visible, and the
        # denylist's own failure mode is refusing more than it should.
        if refused:
            logger.info("asaxiy %s: refused %d listings that are not %s",
                        category, refused, category)

    def search(self, query: str) -> Iterator[Listing]:
        """asaxiy's search is `/product?key=<term>`, the same grid markup.

        Present so the source satisfies the protocol; the sweep is what the
        harvest uses, and Pass B only queries sources that cannot be swept.
        """
        session = self._get_session()
        page_html = get_text(session, f"{BASE_URL}/ru/product", params={"key": query})
        yield from parse(page_html)

"""Shared vocabulary for every retailer client."""
import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any, Iterator, Protocol, runtime_checkable

from narxnoma.sources.http import pause

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# A price outside this range is a unit bug, not a bargain. alifshop reports
# tiyin (1/100 SUM); forgetting the divisor lands 100x high.
PRICE_MIN = 100_000
PRICE_MAX = 100_000_000

AVAILABILITY = ("IN_STOCK", "OUT_OF_STOCK", "UNKNOWN")


def is_plausible_price(price: int | None) -> bool:
    return price is not None and PRICE_MIN <= price <= PRICE_MAX


def coerce_price(value: Any) -> int | None:
    """Coerce a retailer's raw price field to an int, or None if unusable.

    Retailer price fields are untyped in practice: olcha returns
    `discount_price` as an int but `total_price` as a string; mediapark
    returns `price` as a string; alifshop returns tiyin. Assuming any of
    these is already an int is how a whole source silently yields nothing --
    every client must run its price fields through this before building a
    Listing.

    `None`, `""`, `0`, `"0"`, and any non-numeric string all return `None`.
    A negative value also returns `None`: a negative price is meaningless
    and the plausibility fences in `Listing` would reject it anyway.
    """
    if value in (None, "", 0, "0"):
        return None
    try:
        price = int(float(value))
    except (TypeError, ValueError):
        return None
    if price < 0:
        return None
    return price


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Listing:
    """One offer as the retailer stated it. Nothing is inferred here."""

    store_slug: str
    source_key: str
    name_raw: str
    price: int
    url: str
    availability: str = "UNKNOWN"
    image_url: str | None = None
    #: Which category sweep produced this listing (Plan 5). Defaults to
    #: smartphones so none of the existing V1 clients need to change.
    category: str = "smartphones"
    raw: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict, compare=False, hash=False)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        for name in ("store_slug", "source_key", "name_raw", "url"):
            if not getattr(self, name):
                raise ValueError(f"Listing.{name} must not be empty")
        if not is_plausible_price(self.price):
            raise ValueError(f"implausible price {self.price!r} for {self.source_key!r}")
        if self.availability not in AVAILABILITY:
            raise ValueError(f"unknown availability {self.availability!r}")


def try_build_listing(**kwargs) -> Listing | None:
    """Build a Listing, or return None and log why.

    One malformed item must never kill a sweep of several hundred, and all
    seven clients must drop them the same way -- so the handling lives here
    rather than being re-implemented per client.
    """
    source_key = kwargs.get("source_key") or "<no source_key>"
    try:
        return Listing(**kwargs)
    except ValueError as exc:
        logger.warning("dropping malformed listing %r: %s", source_key, exc)
        return None
    except TypeError as exc:
        # Wrong or missing keyword arguments is a bug in the calling client,
        # not bad retailer data -- log it loudly so it doesn't hide next to
        # routine drop-and-count warnings, but still don't kill the sweep.
        logger.error("dropping listing %r built with bad arguments: %s", source_key, exc)
        return None


@runtime_checkable
class Source(Protocol):
    """One retailer.

    CALLER CONTRACT: check `can_sweep` before calling `sweep()`. A source that
    cannot browse a catalogue raises from `sweep()` rather than yielding
    nothing -- returning an empty iterator would let a caller that forgot this
    check record a perfectly successful harvest of zero listings, which is
    indistinguishable from a retailer whose catalogue really is empty. Failing
    loudly is the point.
    """

    slug: str

    #: False when the retailer exposes no catalogue browse (mediapark, whose
    #: only endpoint is a no-pagination autocomplete). Such a source is reached
    #: through `search()` alone.
    can_sweep: bool

    def sweep(self, category: str = "smartphones") -> Iterator[Listing]:
        """Every phone the retailer will show us, without a target list.

        Raises NotImplementedError when `can_sweep` is False.
        """

    def search(self, query: str) -> Iterator[Listing]:
        """Listings matching a query. Used for sources that cannot be swept."""


# A runaway paginator -- a retailer whose reported last_page grows with every
# request, or a next_page that never returns None -- would loop until the cron
# killed it. Well above the largest real catalogue observed (beemarket, 28
# pages; uzum, ~14 per brand).
MAX_PAGES = 200

# Per-PAGE retries. The unit matters: retrying the whole sweep would re-fetch
# every page already yielded and already written to the database in
# WRITE_BATCH_SIZE chunks by harvest_source.
PAGE_RETRIES = 2


def paginate(fetch_page, parse_page, next_page, first_page: int = 1,
             max_pages: int = MAX_PAGES,
             retries: int = PAGE_RETRIES) -> Iterator[Listing]:
    """Walk a paginated endpoint, surviving a transient failure on one page.

    Six clients hand-rolled this loop and not one of them retried, so a single
    bad response lost that source's entire cycle -- a beemarket 502 really did
    cost a whole source in a live run, and a retry minutes later succeeded.

    `fetch_page(page) -> payload`, `parse_page(payload) -> list[Listing]`,
    `next_page(payload, page) -> int | None`. **next_page is the only thing
    that ends the sweep.** A page that parses to nothing does NOT: its items
    may simply have been dropped by the price fence or a category filter, and
    stopping there would lose every following page. A client whose end signal
    really is an empty payload (uzum, paginating by offset with no last_page)
    expresses that in its own next_page.

    A page that fails all its attempts RAISES. That is deliberate: swallowing it
    would let a half-swept catalogue be recorded as a success, which is the one
    failure mode `EXPECTED_MIN_OFFERS` and `harvest_run.ok` exist to catch.
    Everything yielded before the failure is already durable, so the caller
    keeps it.
    """
    page = first_page
    for _ in range(max_pages):
        payload = None
        for attempt in range(retries + 1):
            try:
                payload = fetch_page(page)
                break
            except Exception:
                if attempt == retries:
                    raise
                logger.warning("page %d failed (attempt %d/%d), retrying",
                               page, attempt + 1, retries + 1)
                # Back off before trying again -- a retry loop must not hit a
                # struggling API at full speed.
                pause()

        # NOT `if not listings: return`. A page whose items were all dropped
        # by the price fence or a category filter would end the sweep and lose
        # every following page. next_page decides; see the docstring.
        yield from parse_page(payload)

        following = next_page(payload, page)
        if following is None:
            return
        page = following

FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/sources/beemarket.py

"""market.beeline.uz (BeeMarket) client: category-scoped sweep, generic search.

BeeMarket is fully browsable, but the task brief's documented endpoint
(`GET /api/web/search?q=&is_bshop=1`) is a sweep of BeeMarket's *entire*
electronics catalogue -- phones, headphones, air fryers, keyboards, network
gear -- 4,306 items across 270 pages as of 2026-07-31, to find a few hundred
phones. A live probe found a cheaper, genuinely server-side-narrowed
alternative: `GET /api/web/categories/smartfony/products`, the same request
BeeMarket's own https://market.beeline.uz/smartfony category page issues.
It returned 436 items across 28 pages, every one of them category
`smartfony` with `is_smartphone: true` -- so `sweep()` uses it instead of
the generic search.

Probes tried and their results (see docs/parsers/beemarket.md for the
retailer's documented shape):
  - `category=smartfony` / `category_id=1` / `category_ids[]=1` /
    `category_slug=smartfony` / `is_smartphone=1` as extra params on the
    generic `/api/web/search` endpoint: all silently ignored -- total and
    last_page stayed at 4,306 / 270 regardless.
  - `q=smartfon` (the texnomart-style broad category-name query) on
    `/api/web/search`: BeeMarket's search does literal AND text matching
    against product names, which don't contain the word "smartfon" --
    returned 1 irrelevant accessory, not the phone catalogue.
  - `/api/web/categories/smartfony/products`: genuinely narrows server-side
    (436 items / 28 pages vs 4,306 / 270). Found by watching the network
    requests BeeMarket's own frontend makes when browsing to
    https://market.beeline.uz/smartfony. `q=` and `is_bshop=` have no
    observed effect on this endpoint (page contents were identical with and
    without them), so it always returns BeeMarket's full smartfony catalogue
    regardless of those params -- sweep() still passes `is_bshop=1` per the
    brief's documented contract in case that changes.

`search(query)` has no equivalent category-scoped shortcut (the category
endpoint ignores `q`), so it uses the documented generic search endpoint,
which does honour `q` (confirmed live: `q=Honor X6c` returned 74 items, all
Honor-branded, vs 4,306 unfiltered).

`parse()` is unfiltered and works unchanged against either endpoint's
response -- both return the same doubly-nested `data.data` list / envelope
shape. Category filtering is crawl policy, not parsing, so it lives in
`sweep()`'s `_filter_phone_listings` instead (see PHONE_CATEGORY_SLUGS).
"""
import logging
from collections import Counter
from dataclasses import replace
from typing import Iterator

from narxnoma.sources.base import Listing, coerce_price, paginate, try_build_listing
from narxnoma.sources.http import get_json, polite_session

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# The documented generic search endpoint (task brief / docs/parsers/beemarket.md).
# Used by search(), which needs `q` to actually filter -- see module docstring.
API_URL = "https://market.beeline.uz/api/web/search"

# The category-scoped endpoint sweep() uses instead of API_URL -- see module
# docstring for why. This is the same request BeeMarket's own category page
# issues; it is undocumented by the retailer (not in docs/parsers/beemarket.md)
# but was confirmed live on 2026-07-31 to return only the smartfony category.
SWEEP_CATEGORY_SLUG = "smartfony"
SWEEP_API_URL = "https://market.beeline.uz/api/web/categories/{slug}/products"

PRODUCT_URL = "https://market.beeline.uz/{category_slug}/{slug}"
DEFAULT_CATEGORY_SLUG = "smartfony"

# Plan 5: category -> beemarket category.slug, verified live 2026-08-10 from
# /api/web/categories/list (smartfony id=1 top-level; the rest are leaves under
# Elektronika / Крупная бытовая техника).
CATEGORY_SLUGS = {
    "smartphones": "smartfony",
    "televisions": "televisions",           # id 68
    "laptops": "laptops",                   # id 113
    "acs": "air-conditioners",              # id 86
    "fridges": "refrigerators",             # id 52
    "washers": "washing-machines",          # id 50
    "ovens": "built-in-ovens",              # id 136
}

# category.slug is BeeMarket's only human-readable category signal (unlike
# idea's undocumented numeric category_id). Observed live on 2026-07-31 by
# sweeping the generic, unscoped /api/web/search?q=&is_bshop=1 page 1 and
# inspecting every item's category.slug:
#   smartfony            = smartphones (e.g. Honor X9d, Huawei Nova Y63)
#   headphones            = headphones / charging cables
#   aerogrills-fryers      = air fryers
#   keyboards              = keyboards
#   network-equipment      = Wi-Fi access points
# and confirmed against /api/web/categories/list: "smartfony" (id 1) is a
# top-level category with no active_children, i.e. the sole phone category
# -- there is no separate feature-phone or tablet category to also include.
# Only smartfony is a phone category. The phone slug list still drives the
# smartphones filter; non-phone sweeps trust the category-scoped endpoint.
PHONE_CATEGORY_SLUGS = frozenset({"smartfony"})

_HEADERS = {
    "Accept": "*/*",
    "Accept-Language": "ru",
}


def _parse_item(item: dict) -> Listing | None:
    item_id = item.get("id")
    if not item_id:
        return None
    slug = item.get("slug")
    if not slug:
        return None
    name = item.get("name")

    # Fallback chain verified against the live API and used in practice by
    # the seed implementation: actual_price (current, discount applied) ->
    # discount_price -> price (struck-through pre-discount list price, do
    # not use unless nothing else is available).
    price = coerce_price(item.get("actual_price"))
    if price is None:
        price = coerce_price(item.get("discount_price"))
    if price is None:
        price = coerce_price(item.get("price"))

    category_slug = ((item.get("category") or {}).get("slug")) or DEFAULT_CATEGORY_SLUG
    url = PRODUCT_URL.format(category_slug=category_slug, slug=slug)

    return try_build_listing(
        store_slug="beemarket",
        source_key=str(item_id),
        name_raw=name,
        price=price,
        url=url,
        image_url=item.get("image_url") or item.get("image"),
        raw=item,
    )


def parse(payload: dict) -> list[Listing]:
    """Turn one raw response into Listings. Pure function, no I/O.

    Unfiltered and faithful to the payload -- category filtering is crawl
    policy, not parsing, and belongs in sweep() instead (see
    _filter_phone_listings and PHONE_CATEGORY_SLUGS). Works identically
    whichever endpoint the payload came from: both API_URL and
    SWEEP_API_URL share the same doubly-nested `data.data` item list /
    `data.last_page` envelope.
    """
    items = ((payload or {}).get("data") or {}).get("data") or []
    listings = []
    for item in items:
        lst = _parse_item(item)
        if lst is not None:
            listings.append(lst)
    return listings


def _filter_phone_listings(listings: list[Listing]) -> list[Listing]:
    """Keep only listings in PHONE_CATEGORY_SLUGS; log the category mix seen.

    Category filtering is crawl policy (V1 is smartphones only), so it
    lives here rather than in parse(), which stays a pure, unfiltered view
    of the payload. Extracted as a standalone function so it is testable
    directly against a recorded fixture, without a live sweep. sweep() only
    calls the category-scoped SWEEP_API_URL, which should already return
    nothing but smartfony -- this filter is defense in depth for the day
    that stops being true (a retailer rename, a scoping regression, etc.).
    """
    def category_slug(listing: Listing) -> str | None:
        return (listing.raw.get("category") or {}).get("slug")

    counts = Counter(category_slug(listing) for listing in listings)
    logger.info("beemarket category distribution: %s", dict(counts))
    filtered_out = {slug: n for slug, n in counts.items() if slug not in PHONE_CATEGORY_SLUGS}
    if filtered_out:
        logger.info("beemarket filtered out non-phone categories: %s", filtered_out)
    return [listing for listing in listings if category_slug(listing) in PHONE_CATEGORY_SLUGS]


class BeemarketSource:
    slug = "beemarket"
    can_sweep = True

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._session = None

    def _get_session(self):
        if self._session is None:
            self._session = polite_session(
                referer="https://market.beeline.uz/",
                extra_headers=_HEADERS,
            )
        return self._session

    def sweep(self, category: str = "smartphones") -> Iterator[Listing]:
        """Every item in one category, walking pagination to the end.

        Phones hit the smartfony category endpoint and are additionally run
        through the phone filter (defense in depth). Non-phone categories
        trust their category-scoped endpoint (the slugs in CATEGORY_SLUGS are
        verified live leaves, so no phone filter applies). Every listing is
        tagged with the sweep's category.
        """
        slug = CATEGORY_SLUGS.get(category)
        if slug is None:
            raise ValueError(f"unknown category {category!r}")
        url = SWEEP_API_URL.format(slug=slug)
        post_filter = _filter_phone_listings if category == "smartphones" else None
        for listing in self._walk(url, {"is_bshop": 1}, post_filter):
            if listing.category != category:
                listing = replace(listing, category=category)
            yield listing

    def search(self, query: str) -> Iterator[Listing]:
        yield from self._walk(API_URL, {"q": query, "is_bshop": 1})

    def _walk(self, url, params, post_filter=None) -> Iterator[Listing]:
        session = self._get_session()

        def fetch(page):
            return get_json(session, url, params={**params, "page": page})

        def parse_page(payload):
            listings = parse(payload)
            return post_filter(listings) if post_filter else listings

        def following(payload, page):
            data = (payload or {}).get("data") or {}
            return page + 1 if page < data.get("last_page", page) else None

        yield from paginate(fetch, parse_page, following)



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/sources/brand_queries.py
================================================
"""Canonical brand-query vocabulary: the CRAWL FRONTIER for every source with
no browsable catalogue.

idea.uz, alifshop.uz and uzum.uz have no full-catalogue browse endpoint, so
they sweep by issuing one search per brand instead of walking a category
endpoint -- and all three need the exact same list, so it lives here once
rather than being copy-pasted per client. It is the project's canonical
brand vocabulary (narxnoma.matching.brands.BRANDS) plus alias keys
(narxnoma.matching.brands.BRAND_ALIASES) such as "redmi", "poco",
"iphone": a retailer lists "Redmi Note 15" with no "Xiaomi" anywhere in
the name, so querying only the parent brand would miss it.

This list is a crawl frontier, not just a recognition vocabulary, and the
two roles have opposite failure modes:

1. RECOGNITION (narxnoma.matching.brands / extract_features): a brand
   missing there degrades ONE listing's identity.
2. CRAWL FRONTIER (this module): a brand missing HERE is ZERO OFFERS,
   permanently, from three of seven sources -- and nothing in the system
   reports the absence of something never looked for.

So the vocabulary is cheap to extend and expensive to trim: adding a brand
nobody sells costs one wasted query per cycle; omitting one costs its whole
catalogue. For the same reason this must never be hand-maintained separately
from narxnoma.matching.brands; deriving it here means a brand added there
becomes visible to every sweep that uses this module automatically. Sorted
for determinism.
"""
from narxnoma.matching.brands import BRAND_ALIASES, BRANDS

BRAND_QUERIES: tuple[str, ...] = tuple(sorted(set(BRANDS) | set(BRAND_ALIASES)))

# --- category frontier -------------------------------------------------------
#
# BRAND_QUERIES is a PHONE frontier, and using it for a category sweep is why
# uzum held 2,381 phones and ZERO air conditioners, fridges, washers or ovens:
# the sweep queried "apple", "samsung", "xiaomi" ... and then filtered the
# results down to category id 82. Two things defeat that.
#
# 1. The brands that sell white goods here -- Artel, Premier, Shivaki, LG,
#    Hisense, Bosch -- sell no phones, so they are not in BRAND_QUERIES at all
#    and were never queried.
# 2. Even for Samsung, which sells both, relevance ranks phones first and
#    pagination caps at offset 1000, so a fridge never surfaces.
#
# A category sweep therefore needs its own frontier: the product noun the
# retailer's own customers type, in Russian and Uzbek (Latin), plus the brands
# that actually sell in that category. The noun does the work -- one query
# reaches the whole category -- and the brand queries are there for products
# whose titles omit the noun entirely, which the live catalogue does contain
# ("Artel UA43H3502 Android TV", "SAMSUNG RB29FERNDSA 290L Sovutgichi").
#
# Deliberately NOT the full appliance vocabulary: 50 brands x 6 categories is
# 300 queries a cycle for a handful of extra listings. These are the brands
# measured as actually present in each category in the live catalogue.
_WHITE_GOODS = ("artel", "premier", "shivaki", "samsung", "lg", "bosch")

CATEGORY_QUERIES: dict[str, tuple[str, ...]] = {
    "televisions": (
        "televizor", "телевизор",
        "artel", "premier", "samsung", "lg", "hisense", "shivaki", "vesta",
    ),
    "laptops": (
        "noutbuk", "ноутбук", "laptop",
        "asus", "acer", "hp", "lenovo", "apple", "msi", "dell",
    ),
    "acs": (
        "konditsioner", "кондиционер", "split",
        "artel", "premier", "shivaki", "lg", "hisense", "midea",
    ),
    # "muzlatgich" is LAST on purpose: on uzum it resolves to freezers, not
    # refrigerators (verified live). "холодильник" and "sovutgich" are the
    # words that reach the refrigerator category, while other retailers do use
    # "Muzlatgichi" for a fridge, so it stays in the frontier.
    "fridges": (
        "холодильник", "sovutgich", "muzlatgich",
        *_WHITE_GOODS,
    ),
    "washers": (
        "kir yuvish mashinasi", "стиральная машина",
        *_WHITE_GOODS,
    ),
    "ovens": (
        "duxovka", "духовой шкаф", "газовая плита", "pech",
        *_WHITE_GOODS,
    ),
}


def queries_for(category: str) -> tuple[str, ...]:
    """The crawl frontier for one category.

    Phones keep the brand frontier they have always used; every other category
    gets the noun-led frontier above. A source that filters its results by a
    category id still needs this, because filtering can only remove what the
    query already returned.
    """
    if category == "smartphones":
        return BRAND_QUERIES
    try:
        return CATEGORY_QUERIES[category]
    except KeyError:
        raise ValueError(f"no crawl frontier for category {category!r}") from None



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/sources/glotr.py
================================================
"""glotr.uz client: another genuinely server-rendered Yii catalogue.

Like asaxiy, glotr has no public JSON API -- its pages are fully rendered
server-side (Yii framework: yii.js, yii.activeForm.js, /assets/...). A
category page (e.g. /noutbuki/) returns a complete product grid in the
initial HTML document, and pagination is a plain ``?page=N`` query
parameter. So the durable path is to parse the page.

One card, in source order (verified live 2026-08-11):

  <div class='product-card '>
    <div class='product-card__header'>
      <a href="/vstraivaemiy-...-p-881208/" class='product-card__swiper ...'>
        <div class='swiper-slide'>
          <img class='lazyload-img' src="data:image/png;base64,..."
               data-src='https://files.glotr.uz/...webp'
               alt='Встраиваемый духовой шкаф Artel Art-Classico I6701'>
        ... one more slide per extra photo ...
    <div class='product-card__content'>
      <a href='/vstraivaemiy-...-p-881208/' class='product-card__link ...'>Встраиваемый ...</a>
      <span class='text-stock text-success'>В наличии</span>
      <div class="price-retail proposal-price"> 2 610 800 <span>сум</span></div>

THE PHOTOS COME BEFORE THE NAME. That single fact is what this parser got
wrong for its whole first life: it cut each card from its *name* anchor to
the next card's name anchor, so the region it searched for an image began
after its own photos and ended inside the next card's. Every listing was
published with its NEIGHBOUR's picture -- 55 of 55 on page one of
/duxovie-shkafi/, i.e. not one image on the site was of the product it sat
under, while every count and every health check stayed green because a
present-but-wrong URL is indistinguishable from a right one in aggregate.

So a card is cut from ``<div class='product-card '``, which encloses BOTH
halves, and the product id is then read TWICE -- from the photo anchor and
from the name anchor. They must agree. When they do not, the card is dropped
rather than published: a markup shift must cost us a listing, never mint one
that pairs a real name with someone else's photograph and price.

PAGINATION -- and the second thing this parser got wrong. Past its last real
page glotr does not return an empty grid: it serves 21 unrelated products
(fridges, water coolers, sewing machines, a face-recognition controller)
under the heading "<category> - Страница N", and a DIFFERENT set every time.
The old stop was "the first page that adds no product id not already seen",
which such a page never triggers, so the sweep walked filler until MAX_PAGES
and filed all of it under whichever category it had been asked for. That is
where 2,739 "ovens" came from in a category holding 240.

The honest end-of-catalogue signal is glotr's own pager: the last real page
carries ``<li class="next disabled"><span>...</span></li>``, and filler pages
carry no ``<ul class="pagination-list">`` at all. We follow the next link and
stop when there is not one, keeping the no-new-ids rule underneath as a
backstop for the day the pager markup changes.

CATEGORIES. The phone catalogue lives under /telefoni-i-plansheti/ (phones
AND tablets) rather than a phones-only path, so the smartphones sweep applies
the shared is_phone_listing denylist to keep tablets out. The other six
categories are phones-clean category pages and need no post-filter.

ACCESS. glotr's robots.txt disallows the browse surface to every user agent.
We sweep it under an explicit grant from glotr; that permission is what makes
this client legitimate, and it is specific to this operator. Do not copy this
module's approach to another site whose robots.txt says the same thing.
"""
import logging
import re
from dataclasses import replace
from typing import Iterator

from narxnoma.sources.base import Listing, coerce_price, paginate, try_build_listing
from narxnoma.sources.http import get_text, polite_session
from narxnoma.sources.not_a_phone import is_phone_listing

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

BASE_URL = "https://glotr.uz"

# category -> glotr category path (verified live 2026-08-10).
CATEGORY_PATHS = {
    "smartphones": "telefoni-i-plansheti",
    "televisions": "televizori",
    "laptops": "noutbuki",
    "acs": "konditsioneri",
    "fridges": "xolodilniki",
    "washers": "stiralnie-mashini",
    "ovens": "duxovie-shkafi",
}

_HEADERS = {
    "Accept": "text/html,application/xhtml+xml,application/xml;q=0.9,*/*;q=0.8",
    "Accept-Language": "ru-RU,ru;q=0.9",
}

# The card container. It opens BEFORE the photos and closes after the price,
# so a region cut here holds every field of exactly one product -- which the
# name anchor, sitting between the two, cannot do.
_RE_CARD_START = re.compile(r"<div class='product-card '")
# The photo anchor and the name anchor. Both spell the product id in their
# href as the trailing -p-<id>; parse() requires the two to agree.
_RE_PHOTO_ANCHOR = re.compile(
    r"""<a\s+href=["'](?:/[^"']*?)-p-(\d+)/["']\s+class='product-card__swiper""")
_RE_LINK = re.compile(
    r"<a\s+href='(/[^']*?-p-(\d+)/)'\s+class='product-card__link[^']*'>(.*?)</a>",
    re.S,
)
_RE_AVAIL_BLOCK = re.compile(r"<span\s+class='text-stock\s+([^']+)'>")

# glotr's entire stock vocabulary, counted live 2026-08-11 across six category
# pages: text-success "В наличии" (51), text-warning "Под заказ" (4),
# text-secondary "Уточнить наличие" (1). There is no fourth badge and no
# text-danger -- an earlier test asserted one, against a hand-written fixture
# that invented it.
#
# NOTHING HERE MAPS TO OUT_OF_STOCK, and that is the finding rather than an
# omission: glotr is a board of sellers' listings, and a seller who runs out
# delists rather than marking the card. "Под заказ" means made to order and
# "Уточнить наличие" means ask -- neither asserts the item is gone. Both were
# previously read as OUT_OF_STOCK, which hid the offer from the product page
# on the strength of a claim glotr never made. UNKNOWN keeps the offer
# visible while still barring it from setting a headline price, which only
# IN_STOCK may do.
_AVAILABILITY = {
    "text-success": "IN_STOCK",
    "text-warning": "UNKNOWN",
    "text-secondary": "UNKNOWN",
}
_RE_PRICE = re.compile(r'class="price-retail[^"]*">\s*([\d\s]+)\s*<span>сум</span>')
_RE_IMG = re.compile(r"<img class='lazyload-img'[^>]*data-src='([^']+)'")

# glotr's own pager, and the only trustworthy end-of-catalogue signal --
# see the module docstring on what lies past the last page.
_RE_PAGER = re.compile(r'<ul class="pagination-list">(.*?)</ul>', re.S)
_RE_NEXT_LINK = re.compile(r'<li class="next">\s*<a\s+href="([^"]*)"')
_RE_PAGE_NO = re.compile(r"[?&]page=(\d+)")


def next_page_number(page_html: str) -> int | None:
    """The page glotr itself links to as "next", or None at the catalogue's end.

    Three shapes, all seen live:
      * ``<li class="next"><a href="/x/?page=5" ...>`` -> 5, a real next page;
      * ``<li class="next disabled"><span>`` -> None, this is the last page
        (note the class is "next disabled", so matching bare "next" is not
        enough -- it has to be the one carrying an anchor);
      * no ``<ul class="pagination-list">`` at all -> None, and this page is
        not part of the category: past its end glotr serves unrelated goods
        under the category's heading.
    """
    pager = _RE_PAGER.search(page_html)
    if pager is None:
        return None
    link = _RE_NEXT_LINK.search(pager.group(1))
    if link is None:
        return None
    number = _RE_PAGE_NO.search(link.group(1))
    return int(number.group(1)) if number else None

# glotr's category pages are NOISY: alongside the category's own products they
# mix in unrelated listings (a парфюм/стол/микроскоп on the laptop page, a
# радиотелефон on the TV page) and accessories (a "Беспроводная мышь Lenovo"
# on the laptop page). A denylist can never catch every intruder, so this is a
# POSITIVE filter: a listing is kept only if its name carries the category's
# product-type word. Verified live 2026-08-10 against the laptop and TV pages
# (43/50 laptop names start "Ноутбук ", real TVs all carry "Телевизор"/"ТВ").
_KEEP_RE = {
    "televisions": re.compile(r"телевизор|smart\s*tv|led\s*tv|\bтв\b|\btv\b"),
    "acs":         re.compile(r"кондиционер|сплит[- ]систем"),
    "fridges":     re.compile(r"холодильник|морозильн|винный шкаф"),
    "washers":     re.compile(r"стиральн"),
    "ovens":       re.compile(r"духов|газовая плита|электроплита|вытяжк|варочн"),
}

# Laptops are the messiest glotr category: names are inconsistent ("Ноутбук
# Acer…" vs bare "ASUS ROG Strix G16" vs "HP EliteBook — бизнес-ноутбук"),
# and non-laptops leak in (monitors, headphones, AIOs, printers, network
# gear, mice, keyboards). A startswith check is too strict (drops real
# brand-first laptops); a bare contains-check keeps "Клавиатура для ноутбука".
# So: keep a laptop iff (it carries the word "ноутбук" OR starts with a known
# laptop brand) AND its name does not signal a NON-laptop product/accessory.
_LAPTOP_BRAND_PREFIXES = (
    "asus", "lenovo", "hp ", "acer", "dell", "msi", "apple", "samsung",
    "huawei", "xiaomi", "toshiba", "gigabyte", "razer", "honor", "vivo ",
    "realme", "infinity", "irbis", "depo", "prestigio", "thunderobot",
)
_LAPTOP_DROP = re.compile(
    r"монитор|моноблок|наушник|гарнитур|принтер|сканер|мфу|свитч|коммутатор|роутер|маршрутизатор|"
    r"планшет|приставк|консоль|проектор|мыш|клавиатур|сумк|чехол|подставк|аккумулятор|блок питания|"
    r"зарядн|зарядк|кабель|адаптер|переходник|охлажд|коврик|флеш|док-станц|док станц|веб-камер|колонк|"
    r"микрофон|динамик|аккустик|павербанк|повербанк|разветвит|хаб|держател|экран для|запчаст|ремонт|"
    r"обслуживан|корпус|термопаст|вентилятор|кулер для|наручные|ссд|backpack|рюкзак|ink[- ]tank|printer"
)


def _is_laptop(name: str) -> bool:
    low = name.casefold().strip()
    has_laptop_word = "ноутбук" in low or "нетбук" in low or "ультрабук" in low or "макбук" in low
    brand_prefix = low.startswith(_LAPTOP_BRAND_PREFIXES)
    return (has_laptop_word or brand_prefix) and not _LAPTOP_DROP.search(low)

# A positive keep isn't enough: an accessory is often named "<thing> для
# ноутбука" and so carries the product-type word. This is the OTHER half --
# reject names that are accessories/spares/consumables for the category's
# device. Combined test: keep iff _KEEP_RE matches AND _DROP_RE does not.
# (laptops is startswith-based in sweep(), so it has no regex here.)
_DROP_RE = {
    "televisions": re.compile(
        r"антенн|кронштейн|пульт|подставк|кабель|тюнер|ресивер|креплени|держател|стенд|шкив|крючок|запчаст|ремонт"),
    "acs": re.compile(
        r"кронштейн|монтаж|установ|заправ|фреон|чистк|обслужив|пульт|дренаж|провод|запчаст|ремонт"),
    "fridges": re.compile(
        r"термометр|уплотнитель|полк|набор|органайзер|контейнер|кулер для воды|чистк|средство|запчаст|ремонт"),
    "washers": re.compile(
        r"порошок|средство|капсул|гель для|сетк|мешок|кондиционер для белья|смягчитель|чистк|запчаст|ремонт"),
    "ovens": re.compile(
        r"противень|форма|перчатк|рукавиц|щетк|средство|решетк|сковород|чистк|набор|запчаст|ремонт"),
}

# Phones were the one glotr category still judged by the shared is_phone_listing
# DENYLIST, on the reasoning that its page mixes in tablets and the phone
# detector already drops those. Counted against all 442 names glotr serves
# under /telefoni-i-plansheti/, that denylist kept 245 -- among them a SanDisk
# microSD card, an HP stylus, a thermal-imaging monocular, a pyrometer, an
# Epson MFP, a GNSS receiver, prayer-bead rings, a hand warmer, a patch panel,
# a kidney supplement, a toy kitchen, bicycle racks and made-to-order shower
# cabins. A denylist enumerates what to reject, and on a classifieds board the
# things to reject are not enumerable. The other six categories learned this
# already; phones had not.
#
# So phones get the same shape as the rest: a positive keep, then the
# accessory drop, then is_phone_listing on top to take out the tablets and
# watches that DO announce themselves as phones. Measured over the same 442:
# 124 kept, and the two rules were checked separately --
#   * the noun rule keeps 120, and the 4 it drops on the accessory rule are a
#     20W charger, a Borofone holder, a MagSafe charging station and a power
#     bank, all of which name a phone without being one;
#   * the brand-first rule adds exactly 4 more, every one a real handset
#     ("Xiaomi 13 Lite 8GB+256GB Black", two Redmi Note 12 Pro Plus, a Redmi
#     12C), and adds no false positive -- it is narrow because it demands a
#     memory pair as well as a leading phone brand, which is what separates
#     "Xiaomi 13 Lite 8/256" from "Xiaomi Pad 5 6/128Gb" only in combination
#     with is_phone_listing.
_PHONE_KEEP = re.compile(r"смартфон|\biphone\b|мобильн\w{0,3}\s+телефон|\bайфон\b")
_PHONE_DROP = re.compile(
    r"держател|чехол|стекл|плёнк|пленк|зарядк|зарядн|аккумулятор|power\s*bank|пауэрбанк|"
    r"повербанк|кабель|адаптер|переходник|карта\s+памяти|стилус|наушник|гарнитур|"
    r"увеличител|лупа|подставк|кольц|ремонт|обслужив|запчаст|дисплей|экран\s+для|"
    r"сумк|рюкзак|бампер|попсокет|селфи|штатив|трипод|док-станц|автодержател|"
    r"защитн|футляр|скотч|наклейк|шлейф|батаре|корпус для|модуль|разъем|разъём|"
    # Tablets, which the brand-first rule would otherwise take: a tablet is
    # named exactly like a handset here -- brand, model, memory pair, colour.
    # Auditing the first fixed sweep, 2 of 828 kept phones were tablets
    # ("Redmi Pad 2 Pro 8/256 Grey", "Samsung X216 8/128GB Gray (Tab A9+)");
    # is_phone_listing misses both because neither says "планшет".
    r"\bpad\b|\btab\b|планшет")
# Brands whose bare "<brand> <model> <memory>" names are handsets on this page.
_PHONE_BRAND_PREFIXES = (
    "xiaomi", "samsung", "apple", "honor", "huawei", "tecno", "infinix",
    "realme", "oppo", "vivo", "redmi", "poco", "google", "oneplus",
    "nokia", "itel", "zte", "motorola", "asus",
)
# "8/128", "8GB+256GB", "3+64" -- the memory pair a handset listing carries.
_PHONE_MEMORY = re.compile(r"\b\d{1,3}\s*(?:gb|гб|г\b)?\s*[+/]\s*\d{2,4}\s*(?:gb|гб)?\b")


def _is_phone(listing: Listing) -> bool:
    """glotr's phones page announces a handset, or we do not take it."""
    low = listing.name_raw.casefold().strip()
    announced = bool(_PHONE_KEEP.search(low))
    brand_first = low.lstrip('"').startswith(_PHONE_BRAND_PREFIXES) and bool(
        _PHONE_MEMORY.search(low))
    if not (announced or brand_first):
        return False
    if _PHONE_DROP.search(low):
        return False
    return is_phone_listing(listing)


def parse(page_html: str) -> list[Listing]:
    """Turn one category page into Listings. Pure function, no I/O.

    Each card is cut at its own ``<div class='product-card '``, which opens
    before the photos and closes after the price, so every field taken from
    the region belongs to one product. The product id is then read from both
    the photo anchor and the name anchor; a card whose two ids disagree is
    dropped, because that mismatch is the exact shape of the failure this
    parser used to publish silently -- one product's name over another's
    picture.
    """
    listings: list[Listing] = []
    starts = [m.start() for m in _RE_CARD_START.finditer(page_html)]
    for idx, start in enumerate(starts):
        end = starts[idx + 1] if idx + 1 < len(starts) else len(page_html)
        region = page_html[start:end]

        names = _RE_LINK.findall(region)
        if len(names) != 1:
            # Zero means the template moved the name out of the card; more
            # than one means the boundary no longer separates cards. Either
            # way we no longer know which name belongs to this region.
            logger.warning("glotr: card region %d holds %d name anchors, skipping",
                           idx, len(names))
            continue
        path, pid, raw_name = names[0]
        name = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", raw_name).strip()
        if not name:
            continue

        # The id, read a second time from the photo anchor. Agreement is what
        # licenses us to take the image from this region.
        photo = _RE_PHOTO_ANCHOR.search(region)
        if photo is not None and photo.group(1) != pid:
            logger.warning("glotr: card %r carries photos for %r -- dropping the card",
                           pid, photo.group(1))
            continue

        # Price: the price-retail block inside this card's region.
        price_match = _RE_PRICE.search(region)
        price = coerce_price(price_match.group(1).replace(" ", "")) if price_match else None
        if price is None:
            # Every card has a price; a missing one is a changed template.
            logger.warning("glotr: card %r has no parseable price", pid)
            continue

        # Availability: the text-stock badge inside this card's region.
        am = _RE_AVAIL_BLOCK.search(region)
        avail = _AVAILABILITY.get(am.group(1), "UNKNOWN") if am else "UNKNOWN"

        # Image: the first slide of this card's own swiper. A card with no
        # photo anchor gets no image rather than a borrowed one.
        image_url = None
        if photo is not None:
            im = _RE_IMG.search(region)
            if im:
                image_url = im.group(1)

        listing = try_build_listing(
            store_slug="glotr",
            source_key=pid,
            name_raw=name,
            price=price,
            url=f"{BASE_URL}{path}",
            availability=avail,
            image_url=image_url,
        )
        if listing is not None:
            listings.append(listing)
    return listings


class GlotrSource:
    slug = "glotr"
    can_sweep = True

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._session = None

    def _get_session(self):
        if self._session is None:
            self._session = polite_session(
                referer=f"{BASE_URL}/",
                origin=BASE_URL,
                extra_headers=_HEADERS,
            )
        return self._session

    def _url(self, path: str, page: int) -> str:
        url = f"{BASE_URL}/{path}/"
        return url if page <= 1 else f"{url}?page={page}"

    def sweep(self, category: str = "smartphones") -> Iterator[Listing]:
        """Every item in one glotr category, walking ?page=N to the end."""
        path = CATEGORY_PATHS.get(category)
        if path is None:
            raise ValueError(f"unknown category {category!r}")
        session = self._get_session()

        def fetch(page):
            return get_text(session, self._url(path, page))

        # TWO independent stops, because glotr does not answer an out-of-range
        # page with an empty grid -- it answers with 21 unrelated products
        # (see the module docstring). The primary stop is glotr's own pager;
        # the no-new-ids rule stays underneath for the day the pager markup
        # changes, so a lost end signal costs one wasted page, not a catalogue
        # of someone else's goods.
        state = {"new": 0}

        def following(page_html, page):
            if state["new"] == 0:
                logger.info("glotr %s: page %d added no new product; end of catalogue",
                            category, page)
                return None
            state["new"] = 0
            nxt = next_page_number(page_html)
            if nxt is None:
                logger.info("glotr %s: page %d is the last page its pager offers",
                            category, page)
            return nxt

        seen: set[str] = set()
        refused = 0
        for listing in paginate(fetch, parse, following):
            if listing.source_key in seen:
                continue
            seen.add(listing.source_key)
            state["new"] += 1
            # Every category is judged the same way -- a positive keep, then
            # an accessory drop. glotr is a classifieds board, so what appears
            # under a category heading is whatever a seller filed there.
            if category == "smartphones":
                keep = _is_phone(listing)
            elif category == "laptops":
                # glotr names laptops inconsistently ("Ноутбук Acer…" vs bare
                # "ASUS ROG Strix G16"); _is_laptop keeps real laptops and
                # drops the mice/keyboards/monitors that leak into the page.
                keep = _is_laptop(listing.name_raw)
            else:
                name = listing.name_raw.casefold()
                keep_re = _KEEP_RE.get(category)
                drop_re = _DROP_RE.get(category)
                keep = (keep_re is not None and bool(keep_re.search(name))
                        and not (drop_re is not None and drop_re.search(name)))
            if not keep:
                refused += 1
                continue
            if listing.category != category:
                listing = replace(listing, category=category)
            yield listing
        if refused:
            logger.info("glotr %s: refused %d listings that are not %s",
                        category, refused, category)

    def search(self, query: str) -> Iterator[Listing]:
        """glotr's search is a ?text=<term> category-less page; reuse the grid parser."""
        session = self._get_session()
        page_html = get_text(session, f"{BASE_URL}/search/", params={"text": query})
        yield from parse(page_html)

FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/sources/http.py
================================================
"""Polite HTTP. Every retailer call goes through here.

We look exactly like the retailer's own SPA: their User-Agent, their Origin
and Referer, one request at a time, with a delay between them.
"""
import random
import time

import requests

from narxnoma.config import Settings, load_settings

# Loaded lazily so tests can monkeypatch settings without an import-time
# side effect baking a delay in before the test gets a chance to change it.
_settings: Settings | None = None


class NonJsonResponse(ValueError):
    """Raised when a 200-status response body is not valid JSON.

    Retailers under load hand back WAF challenge pages, rate-limit HTML, or
    expired-session redirects with an HTTP 200 -- response.json() would
    otherwise raise a bare "expecting value: line 1 column 1" that tells you
    nothing about which retailer or endpoint misbehaved. Carrying the URL,
    status, and a body snippet here is what makes the cause diagnosable from
    a single log line instead of requiring a live repro. Subclasses
    ValueError so existing `except ValueError` callers keep working
    unchanged.
    """

    def __init__(self, url: str, status_code: int, body_snippet: str) -> None:
        self.url = url
        self.status_code = status_code
        self.body_snippet = body_snippet
        super().__init__(
            f"non-JSON response from {url} (status {status_code}): {body_snippet!r}"
        )


def _get_settings() -> Settings:
    global _settings
    if _settings is None:
        _settings = load_settings()
    return _settings


def polite_session(referer: str, origin: str | None = None,
                   extra_headers: dict[str, str] | None = None) -> requests.Session:
    settings = _get_settings()
    session = requests.Session()
    session.headers.update({
        "User-Agent": settings.user_agent,
        "Accept": "application/json, text/plain, */*",
        "Referer": referer,
    })
    if origin:
        session.headers["Origin"] = origin
    if extra_headers:
        session.headers.update(extra_headers)
    return session


def pause() -> None:
    settings = _get_settings()
    time.sleep(random.uniform(settings.min_delay, settings.max_delay))


def get_json(session: requests.Session, url: str, **kwargs) -> dict:
    settings = _get_settings()
    timeout = (settings.connect_timeout, settings.read_timeout)
    # The delay runs whether the request succeeds or raises: a failing
    # retailer is exactly when backing off matters most, and a retry loop
    # must not hit a struggling API at full speed.
    try:
        response = session.get(url, timeout=timeout, **kwargs)
        response.raise_for_status()
    finally:
        pause()
    try:
        return response.json() or {}
    except ValueError as exc:
        raise NonJsonResponse(url, response.status_code, response.text[:200]) from exc


def get_text(session: requests.Session, url: str, **kwargs) -> str:
    """Fetch a document body as text. For asaxiy, the one HTML source.

    Deliberately separate from get_json rather than a flag on it: the two
    have different failure modes. A JSON endpoint answering with HTML is a
    WAF challenge and must raise (NonJsonResponse); an HTML endpoint
    answering with HTML is Tuesday. Sharing the timeout and the
    always-runs-even-on-failure pause is the part worth sharing.

    `response.text` decodes using the charset the server declares, which
    asaxiy does state (`text/html; charset=UTF-8`). Without that header
    requests would fall back to latin-1 for text/* per RFC 2616 and quietly
    mojibake every Cyrillic name -- so a missing charset is worth failing on
    rather than guessing through.
    """
    settings = _get_settings()
    timeout = (settings.connect_timeout, settings.read_timeout)
    # See get_json: the delay must not be skipped on failure.
    try:
        response = session.get(url, timeout=timeout, **kwargs)
        response.raise_for_status()
    finally:
        pause()
    if response.encoding is None:
        raise ValueError(f"no charset declared for {url}; refusing to guess the encoding")
    return response.text


def post_json(session: requests.Session, url: str, **kwargs) -> dict:
    settings = _get_settings()
    timeout = (settings.connect_timeout, settings.read_timeout)
    # See get_json: the delay must not be skipped on failure.
    try:
        response = session.post(url, timeout=timeout, **kwargs)
        response.raise_for_status()
    finally:
        pause()
    try:
        return response.json() or {}
    except ValueError as exc:
        raise NonJsonResponse(url, response.status_code, response.text[:200]) from exc



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/sources/idea.py
================================================
"""idea.uz client: brand-query sweep, no browsable catalogue and no pagination.

idea has no full-catalogue browse endpoint, so `sweep()` loops over a fixed
list of brand queries and calls the search endpoint once per brand with
`quantity=100` (the API honours this, ~74-94 items per call depending on the
brand -- see docs/parsers/idea.md); results are merged and deduplicated by
`source_key` since the same product can surface under more than one brand
query. `search(query)` issues the same call with the caller's query text.

idea also mixes non-phone categories (appliances) into every response with
