no reliable textual signal to tell them apart, so `sweep()` filters on the
undocumented `category_id` field before yielding -- see PHONE_CATEGORY_IDS
and docs/parsers/idea.md.
"""
import logging
from collections import Counter
from dataclasses import replace
from typing import Iterator

import requests

from narxnoma.sources.base import Listing, coerce_price, try_build_listing
from narxnoma.sources.brand_queries import BRAND_QUERIES, queries_for  # noqa: F401  (BRAND_QUERIES re-exported: it is idea's phone frontier and tests pin it here)
from narxnoma.sources.http import get_json, polite_session

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

API_URL = "https://api.idea.uz/api/v2/search"
QUANTITY = 100

# idea has no full-catalogue browse endpoint, so sweep() is a fixed list of
# brand queries merged together (see module docstring) -- this list *is*
# the sweep's entire reach into idea's catalogue. Any brand missing here is
# permanently invisible to us. alifshop.py needs the identical list for the
# same reason, so it is shared rather than duplicated -- see
# narxnoma.sources.brand_queries (imported above) for the derivation and
# rationale.

# idea's `category_id` is the only usable category signal -- the textual
# `category`/`category_name`/`category_slug` fields are null or empty on
# every observed item. It is completely undocumented by the retailer;
# these mappings were derived by observation from the recorded fixture
# (tests/fixtures/sources/idea.json) and a live "Samsung" sweep on
# 2026-07-31:
#   55 = smartphones (e.g. Galaxy S25 Ultra, Galaxy A36)
#   26 = vacuum cleaners
#   24 = fridges
#    9 = TVs
# Only 55 is a phone category; V1 is smartphones only. Because these ids
# are undocumented and could be renumbered or a second phone category
# added without notice, sweep() logs the full category distribution every
# run (see _filter_phone_listings) so drift is visible instead of silently
# emptying or polluting the catalogue.
PHONE_CATEGORY_IDS = frozenset({55})

# Plan 5: category -> idea category_id set, verified live 2026-08-10 by
# searching each product type and reading the returned categories histogram.
CATEGORY_IDS = {
    "smartphones": PHONE_CATEGORY_IDS,
    "televisions": {9},
    "laptops": {73},
    "acs": {15},
    "fridges": {24},
    "washers": {131, 132, 23},
    "ovens": {179, 107, 17},
}

_HEADERS = {
    "Accept": "application/json",
    "Accept-Language": "uz",
}


def _parse_item(item: dict) -> Listing | None:
    product_id = item.get("id")
    if not product_id:
        return None
    # `name` is BRANDLESS: idea stores it as the model half only
    # ('VCC 5252 Black', 'Galaxy S25 Ultra 12/256 Titanium Blue'), while
    # `title_name` carries the same text with the brand in front
    # ('Samsung VCC 5252 Black changyutgichi'). Brand is a hard identity
    # field downstream -- variant_key() returns None without it -- so a
    # brandless name_raw is not a slightly worse name, it is an offer that
    # can never be clustered and silently drops out of the catalogue.
    # Measured on the recorded fixture: with `name`, 0 of 20 listings were
    # keyable; with `title_name`, 6 of 20 (and 14 of 20 recover a brand).
    # `title_name` is occasionally absent or empty, hence the fallback --
    # a brandless name still beats no listing at all.
    name = (item.get("title_name") or "").strip() or item.get("name")
    price = coerce_price(item.get("current_price"))

    return try_build_listing(
        store_slug="idea",
        source_key=str(product_id),
        name_raw=name,
        price=price,
        # idea is the only source that hands back a complete, ready-to-use
        # product URL -- take it verbatim, never reconstruct one from slug.
        url=item.get("url"),
        image_url=item.get("img"),
        raw=item,
    )


def parse(payload: dict) -> list[Listing]:
    """Turn one raw search response into Listings. Pure function, no I/O.

    Unfiltered and faithful to the payload -- category filtering is crawl
    policy, not parsing, and belongs in sweep() instead (see
    _filter_phone_listings and PHONE_CATEGORY_IDS).
    """
    products = (payload or {}).get("products") or []
    listings = []
    for item in products:
        lst = _parse_item(item)
        if lst is not None:
            listings.append(lst)
    return listings


def _filter_phone_listings(listings: list[Listing]) -> list[Listing]:
    """Keep only listings in PHONE_CATEGORY_IDS; log the category mix seen.

    Category filtering is crawl policy (V1 is smartphones only), so it
    lives here rather than in parse(), which stays a pure, unfiltered view
    of the payload. Extracted as a standalone function so it is testable
    directly against a recorded fixture, without a live sweep.
    """
    counts = Counter(listing.raw.get("category_id") for listing in listings)
    logger.info("idea category distribution: %s", dict(counts))
    filtered_out = {cid: n for cid, n in counts.items() if cid not in PHONE_CATEGORY_IDS}
    if filtered_out:
        logger.info("idea filtered out non-phone categories: %s", filtered_out)
    return [
        listing for listing in listings
        if listing.raw.get("category_id") in PHONE_CATEGORY_IDS
    ]


def _filter_by_category(listings: list[Listing], category_ids: set[int]) -> list[Listing]:
    """Keep only listings whose category_id is in the target set (Plan 5)."""
    keep = frozenset(category_ids)
    return [listing for listing in listings
            if listing.raw.get("category_id") in keep]


class IdeaSource:
    slug = "idea"
    can_sweep = True

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._session = None

    def _get_session(self):
        if self._session is None:
            self._session = polite_session(
                referer="https://idea.uz/",
                origin="https://idea.uz",
                extra_headers=_HEADERS,
            )
        return self._session

    def _fetch(self, query: str) -> list[Listing]:
        session = self._get_session()
        payload = get_json(
            session,
            API_URL,
            # type[] is a repeated query parameter, not a comma list --
            # requests expands a list value into repeated type[]= pairs.
            params={"search": query, "quantity": QUANTITY, "type[]": ["shops", "products"]},
        )
        return parse(payload)

    def sweep(self, category: str = "smartphones") -> Iterator[Listing]:
        """Every item idea returns across this category's query frontier.

        There is no pagination and no full-catalogue endpoint, so this
        merges one search call per query and deduplicates by source_key --
        the same product can appear under more than one query.
        Only listings whose category_id is in the target category's set are
        kept, and each is tagged with the sweep's category.

        Phones sweep by BRAND, everything else by the product NOUN. With no
        pagination at all, the frontier IS this source's entire reach, so a
        phone-brand frontier left idea holding 238 phones against 18 non-phone
        listings in total. See sources/brand_queries.py.
        """
        ids = CATEGORY_IDS.get(category)
        if ids is None:
            raise ValueError(f"unknown category {category!r}")
        seen: set[str] = set()
        # No pagination: idea returns one full response per query, so
        # there is no page loop to hoist onto base.paginate().
        for query in queries_for(category):
            try:
                found = self._fetch(query)
            except requests.HTTPError as exc:
                # idea answers a search with NO RESULTS with 404, not with an
                # empty list -- observed live on "lg", which sells no phones
                # here. One barren query must not cost the whole category: the
                # frontier deliberately contains terms that only some
                # categories match.
                #
                # Narrow on purpose. Only 404 is tolerated, and only per query;
                # a 500 or a connection error still raises and still fails the
                # run loudly, because "every query returned nothing" and "the
                # source is down" must not look the same. A source that ends up
                # yielding nothing is still caught by EXPECTED_MIN_OFFERS.
                if exc.response is not None and exc.response.status_code == 404:
                    logger.info("idea: no results for %r (404), continuing", query)
                    continue
                raise
            for listing in _filter_by_category(found, ids):
                if listing.source_key in seen:
                    continue
                seen.add(listing.source_key)
                if listing.category != category:
                    listing = replace(listing, category=category)
                yield listing

    def search(self, query: str) -> Iterator[Listing]:
        yield from self._fetch(query)



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/sources/mediapark.py
================================================
"""mediapark.uz client: autocomplete-only, no sweep.

mediapark exposes exactly one endpoint: a Diginetica autocomplete search
(`GET .../v1/diginetica/autocomplete?st=<text>&lang=ru`). There is no
full-catalogue browse endpoint and no pagination -- a query returns only
the top ~5-10 ranked hits. That makes a sweep structurally impossible, not
merely unimplemented: `can_sweep = False` and `sweep()` **raises**
`NotImplementedError` rather than yielding an empty iterator. An empty
iterator would let a caller that forgot to check `can_sweep` record a
perfectly successful harvest of zero listings -- silently, and
indistinguishable from a source whose catalogue really is empty. Raising
is what tells a careless caller, loudly, that it skipped the guard. Task
11's harvest stage must check `can_sweep` and skip this source rather than
call `sweep()` and catch the error.

Driving mediapark per-variant across a harvest cycle needs a variant list
that does not exist yet (Plan 3) -- out of scope here. Only `search(query)`
is implemented; `search()` forwards the caller's query text verbatim as
`st`, unlike beemarket/texnomart's `search()` which also just forwards the
caller's text. What is different about mediapark is the *quality* of the
query the caller should send: because this is a ranking autocomplete
engine rather than a strict-AND filter, dropping the RAM/storage half of a
query (the aggressive stripping used to build texnomart/beemarket queries)
measurably worsens recall here -- the exact variant needs to rank into a
short list of ~10, so callers should keep the query close to verbatim,
including the "6/128" form and the colour word (see docs/parsers/mediapark.md
section 4). That query-shaping is the caller's responsibility (task 11 /
matching), not something this module does to the string it's given.

No per-item category signal exists. Each product in the recorded response
carries only id/name/price/imageUrl/imageUrls/linkUrl/available/score/
oldPrice -- no category id or slug. The top-level `categories` key is a
taxonomy of *search-suggestion* links (e.g. "Смартфоны", "Samsung Galaxy
A15 | A16 | A17") with no back-reference to any item in `products`, so it
cannot be used to filter individual listings; unlike idea's category_id or
beemarket's category.slug, it doesn't label any specific product. A live
"Samsung" query (the recorded fixture) returned 10 items, all phones --
no off-category noise was observed in this recording, but since there is
no reusable per-item signal either way, `parse()` stays unfiltered and no
category filter is implemented. Off-category noise also matters less here
than for the other clients: there is no sweep to pollute, only individual
search() calls a caller inspects one at a time.
"""
import logging
from typing import Iterator

from narxnoma.sources.base import Listing, coerce_price, try_build_listing
from narxnoma.sources.http import get_json, polite_session

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

API_URL = "https://api.v2.mediapark.uz/v1/diginetica/autocomplete"

# The linkUrl mediapark hands back is relative; this is the only prefix
# ever applied to it. The slug it contains can lie about specs (see
# docs/parsers/mediapark.md section 3) -- it must never be parsed or
# reconstructed, only prefixed verbatim.
LINK_PREFIX = "https://mediapark.uz"

# x-device-id / x-session-id must be *present* but are not credentials --
# any value works (confirmed in docs/parsers/mediapark.md). Fixed strings,
# not secrets.
_HEADERS = {
    "Accept": "*/*",
    "Content-Type": "application/json",
    "Accept-Language": "ru",
    "x-device-id": "Ywh1FNq17HzyCBJ15BDZl",
    "x-session-id": "vzNu2usamNvHs6plQvNO4",
}


def _parse_item(item: dict) -> Listing | None:
    item_id = item.get("id")
    if not item_id:
        return None
    link = item.get("linkUrl")
    if not link:
        return None
    name = item.get("name")
    # price arrives as a string ("2699000") -- coerce_price handles the cast.
    price = coerce_price(item.get("price"))

    return try_build_listing(
        store_slug="mediapark",
        source_key=str(item_id),
        name_raw=name,
        price=price,
        # linkUrl verbatim, only ever prefixed with the host -- see module
        # docstring and docs/parsers/mediapark.md section 3 for why it must
        # never be parsed or reconstructed.
        url=LINK_PREFIX + link,
        image_url=item.get("imageUrl"),
        raw=item,
    )


def parse(payload: dict) -> list[Listing]:
    """Turn one raw autocomplete response into Listings. Pure function, no I/O.

    Unfiltered and faithful to the payload -- see module docstring for why
    no category filter exists (no usable per-item signal). Only the
    `products` key is read; `suggestions` and `categories` are query-
    refinement data, not listings.
    """
    products = (payload or {}).get("products") or []
    listings = []
    for item in products:
        lst = _parse_item(item)
        if lst is not None:
            listings.append(lst)
    return listings


class MediaparkSource:
    slug = "mediapark"
    can_sweep = False

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._session = None

    def _get_session(self):
        if self._session is None:
            self._session = polite_session(
                referer="https://mediapark.uz/",
                origin="https://mediapark.uz",
                extra_headers=_HEADERS,
            )
        return self._session

    def sweep(self, category: str = "smartphones") -> Iterator[Listing]:
        """Always raises -- mediapark has no full-catalogue endpoint.

        Deliberately not an empty generator: see module docstring for why
        an empty iterator here would be a silent failure mode this project
        exists to prevent. Callers must check `can_sweep` before calling
        this; Task 11's harvest stage does exactly that.
        """
        raise NotImplementedError(
            "mediapark is search-only: it exposes only an autocomplete "
            "endpoint with no full-catalogue browse and no pagination, so "
            "there is no way to sweep it. Check can_sweep before calling "
            "sweep()."
        )

    def search(self, query: str) -> Iterator[Listing]:
        session = self._get_session()
        payload = get_json(session, API_URL, params={"st": query, "lang": "ru"})
        yield from parse(payload)



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/sources/not_a_phone.py
================================================
"""Denylist of things that are not phones.

texnomart items carry NO category field -- verified against the recorded
fixture, unlike idea (category_id) and beemarket (a category endpoint). The
only usable signal is the product-type noun the retailer appends to the
name, which is weaker evidence, so this is a DENYLIST of things that are
not phones rather than an allowlist of things that are: an allowlist would
silently drop any phone whose name lacks the expected word, and losing a
real phone is worse than admitting an accessory the price fence and the
matcher still get a chance to reject.

Measured on 467 texnomart live offers: 142 (30.4%) are not phones, and 15
of them had already become canonical PRODUCTS -- a tablet carries a brand,
a memory pair and a colour, so it forms a full key exactly as a phone
does. alifshop's unfiltered fallback (brands without a "Смартфоны" bucket,
e.g. ASUS) is the same shape: 38 laptop offers became products.
"""
import re

_NOT_A_PHONE = re.compile(
    r"\b("
    r"plansh\w*|планшет\w*|noutbuk\w*|ноутбук\w*|"
    r"vivobook|zenbook|matebook|chromebook|macbook|ideapad|thinkpad|"
    r"zaryadk\w*|зарядн\w*|"
    r"plyonka\w*|плёнк\w*|пленк\w*|"
    r"quloqchin\w*|наушник\w*|kabel\w*|кабел\w*|powerbank|"
    r"televizor\w*|телевизор\w*|soat\w*|watch|часы|"
    r"matepad|ipad|galaxy tab"
    r")\b",
    re.I,
)

# NOTE: "adapter/адаптер" is deliberately NOT in the denylist. Measured on
# the live catalogue: "Смартфон Samsung Galaxy A37 5G: ... USB-адаптер
# 8/128+ 25 Вт ..." is a REAL PHONE whose title advertises the bundled
# charger, and 6 such offers were already canonical products. A standalone
# "USB-адаптер 25 Вт Samsung" carries no memory pair, so it can never form
# a variant key -- it is unkeyable and lands in unresolved_offer, never in
# the catalogue. Admitting noise the key-system already rejects is cheaper
# than losing a phone, which is this filter's one unforgivable failure
# mode. "зарядн\w*" stays: "Зарядное устройство ..." is the title shape of
# genuine charger listings.

# "чехол" is a case -- and also a GIFT. "Смартфон Samsung Galaxy S25 ultra в
# подарок чехол 12/256 ГБ" is a real phone sold with a free case; a bare
# `чехол\w*` dropped it from the live catalogue measurement. Only the
# accessory sense counts (a case listing, no gift mention nearby).
_CASE = re.compile(r"\b(chexol\w*|чехол\w*)\b", re.I)
_GIFT = re.compile(r"подарок", re.I)

# A title that STARTS with a phone word is a phone listing; accessory nouns
# in it are bundled features, not the product. Measured: "Смартфон Samsung
# Galaxy A37 5G: мощные технологии USB-адаптер 8/128+ 25 Вт" (real phone
# with the charger in the box) vs "Samsung EP-T6010NBEGRU 60W Black
# adapteri" (a standalone charger). The adapter itself stays a drop signal
# for titles that do not start with a phone word.
_PHONE_START = re.compile(
    r"^\s*(смартфон\w*|smartfon\w*|smartphone\w*|телефон\w*|iphone\w*)",
    re.I,
)

# "стекло" is a screen protector -- and ALSO a colour. "Смартфон OPPO A3
# (6/128) Черное стекло" is a real phone in black glass, and a bare
# `стекл\w*` dropped three of them. Only the accessory sense counts, so the
# word must be qualified. When in doubt about a material word, leave it out:
# this filter's failure mode must be admitting noise, never losing a phone.
_PROTECTOR = re.compile(r"(защитн\w*\s+стекл\w*|стекл\w*\s+защитн\w*|"
                        r"himoya\w*\s+shisha\w*)", re.I)


def is_phone_listing(listing) -> bool:
    """False for the accessories and non-phone devices these sources mix in.

    Shared by texnomart (no category field at all) and alifshop (whose
    category facet is absent for non-phone brands, so its sweep falls back to
    unfiltered). Applied in sweep() only -- parse() stays a faithful pure
    function of one response.
    """
    name = listing.name_raw or ""
    if _PHONE_START.match(name):
        # A phone's marketing title: accessory words ("USB-адаптер",
        # "в подарок чехол") describe what is IN THE BOX, not the product.
        return True
    if _NOT_A_PHONE.search(name) or _PROTECTOR.search(name):
        return False
    case = _CASE.search(name)
    if case:
        # Only the accessory sense counts: a case listing, not a phone sold
        # with a free case. The gift mention sits within ~16 chars on either
        # side of the word in every observed shape ("в подарок чехол",
        # "чехол в подарок").
        if not _GIFT.search(name[max(0, case.start() - 16): case.end() + 16]):
            return False
    return True


================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/sources/olcha.py
================================================
"""olcha.uz client: full-catalogue pull, not per-query search.

olcha's search endpoint caps results per query, so a full sweep of the
smartphone category is fewer calls and better coverage than one search per
phone (see docs/parsers/olcha.md). `search()` therefore filters the swept
catalogue client-side rather than hitting a different endpoint.
"""
from dataclasses import replace
from typing import Iterator

from narxnoma.sources.base import Listing, coerce_price, paginate, try_build_listing
from narxnoma.sources.http import get_json, polite_session

API_URL = "https://mobile.olcha.uz/api/v2/products"
PRODUCT_URL = "https://olcha.uz/ru/product/view/{alias}"
CATEGORY = "telefony"

# Plan 5: category -> olcha category alias, verified live 2026-08-10 from
# /api/v2/categories (leaf alias). "ovens" maps to olcha's only close
# category, built-in-and-freestanding stoves (olcha sells no built-in ovens).
CATEGORY_ALIASES = {
    "smartphones": "telefony",                       # id 10
    "televisions": "televizory",                     # id 2
    "laptops": "noutbuki",                           # id 6
    "acs": "konditsionery",                          # id 98
    "fridges": "kholodilniki-i-morozilnye-kamery-1", # id 41
    "washers": "stiralnye-mashiny",                  # id 37
    "ovens": "plity-gazovye-i-elektricheskie",       # id 42 (stoves)
}
PER_PAGE = 20  # server caps per_page at 20 regardless of what is requested

_HEADERS = {
    "Accept": "*/*",
    "Accept-Language": "ru",
    "clientmodel": "web",
}


def _parse_item(item: dict) -> Listing | None:
    alias = item.get("alias")
    name = item.get("name_ru")
    # discount_price arrives as int, total_price as string -- coerce_price
    # handles both so this fallback doesn't silently break on type alone.
    price = coerce_price(item.get("discount_price"))
    if price is None:
        price = coerce_price(item.get("total_price"))
    if not alias:
        return None

    quantity = item.get("quantity") or 0
    availability = "IN_STOCK" if quantity else "OUT_OF_STOCK"

    return try_build_listing(
        store_slug="olcha",
        source_key=alias,
        name_raw=name,
        price=price,
        url=PRODUCT_URL.format(alias=alias),
        availability=availability,
        image_url=item.get("main_image"),
        raw=item,
    )


def parse(payload: dict) -> list[Listing]:
    """Turn one raw catalogue-page response into Listings. Pure function, no I/O."""
    products = ((payload or {}).get("data") or {}).get("products") or []
    listings = []
    for item in products:
        lst = _parse_item(item)
        if lst is not None:
            listings.append(lst)
    return listings


class OlchaSource:
    slug = "olcha"
    can_sweep = True

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._session = None
        self._catalogue: list[Listing] | None = None

    def _get_session(self):
        if self._session is None:
            self._session = polite_session(
                referer="https://olcha.uz/",
                origin="https://olcha.uz",
                extra_headers=_HEADERS,
            )
        return self._session

    def sweep(self, category: str = "smartphones") -> Iterator[Listing]:
        """Every item in one olcha category, walking pagination to the end."""
        alias = CATEGORY_ALIASES.get(category)
        if alias is None:
            raise ValueError(f"unknown category {category!r}")
        session = self._get_session()

        def fetch(page):
            return get_json(
                session,
                API_URL,
                params={"page": page, "per_page": PER_PAGE, "category": alias},
            )

        def following(payload, page):
            paginator = ((payload or {}).get("data") or {}).get("paginator") or {}
            return page + 1 if page < paginator.get("last_page", page) else None

        for listing in paginate(fetch, parse, following):
            if listing.category != category:
                listing = replace(listing, category=category)
            yield listing

    def search(self, query: str) -> Iterator[Listing]:
        """olcha has no search endpoint; filter the already-swept catalogue by name."""
        if self._catalogue is None:
            self._catalogue = list(self.sweep())
        needle = query.casefold()
        for listing in self._catalogue:
            if needle in listing.name_raw.casefold():
                yield listing



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/sources/openshop.py
================================================
"""openshop.uz client: single-seller shop, full-catalogue sweep in ~120 requests.

openshop.uz is a Next.js front end over a Laravel JSON API at
`https://web.openshop.uz/api/v1`. Every call is a **POST**, filters travel in
the **query string** (not the body), and `language: ru` / `Accept-Language: ru`
select Russian names. See docs/parsers/openshop.md for the full write-up.

Three endpoints matter:

- `POST /shop/product/get_products?category_id=..&page=..&per_page=48` -- the
  catalogue browse the shop's own category pages call. `per_page` is capped at
  **48** server-side (asking for 100 or 500 silently returns 48), and deep
  paging is capped too: an unfiltered walk stops yielding new products after
  ~2,400 (page 50), while `meta.total` says 5,731. Walking each of the 11
  top-level categories separately (largest: 1,577) stays under the cap and
  returns every product -- verified 2026-10-14, 5,723 unique ids.
- `POST /shop/product/search_product?q=..` -- the header search box. Fuzzy over
  the SEO `tags` field and capped at 60 results, so a precise query still
  returns the whole brand. Useful for a human, useless for identity.
- `POST /shop/product/get_product?slug=..` with body `{"with_variants": true}`
  -- one product plus its variant group (`variants.axes` = RAM / colour /
  storage chips, `variants.matrix` = every sibling product id with its own
  price). This is how openshop TAGS goods: each colour x memory combination is
  its own product with its own id, slug and price, tied together by `group.id`.

Price: `price.total` is what the customer pays now (after any discount);
`price.old_price` is the struck-through figure. Both are integer SUM -- no
tiyin trap here. The public product page's `<title>` repeats `price.total`
("... | Цена: 6 466 000 сум"), which gives verification an independent read.

Product URL: `https://openshop.uz/shop/product/<slug>?lang=ru`. The `link` field in
the payload points at `web.openshop.uz`, which is the API host, not the shop.
"""
import logging
import re
import time
from typing import Iterator

import requests

from narxnoma.sources.base import Listing, coerce_price, paginate, try_build_listing
from narxnoma.sources.http import get_text, polite_session, post_json

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

API = "https://web.openshop.uz/api/v1"
PRODUCTS_URL = f"{API}/shop/product/get_products"
PRODUCT_URL_API = f"{API}/shop/product/get_product"
TREE_URL = f"{API}/shop/category/categories_tree"
# `?lang=ru` matters: without it the page renders in Uzbek ("Narx: ... so'm").
PRODUCT_URL = "https://openshop.uz/shop/product/{slug}?lang=ru"

PER_PAGE = 48          # server-side cap; larger values are silently clamped
DEEP_PAGE_CAP = 2400   # an unfiltered walk stops yielding new ids past this
# The API advertises `x-ratelimit-limit: 120` per minute. narxnoma's default
# pause (0.3-0.6 s) can average under 0.5 s on a fast connection, so this
# client enforces its own floor rather than relying on NARXNOMA_MIN_DELAY.
MIN_INTERVAL = 0.55

_HEADERS = {
    "Accept-Language": "ru",
    # openshop reads the locale from a custom `language` header, not only from
    # Accept-Language -- without it, names come back in Uzbek.
    "language": "ru",
}

# narxnoma category -> openshop SUBcategory ids (second tree level), verified
# against categories_tree on 2026-10-14.
CATEGORY_SUBCATEGORY_IDS = {
    "smartphones": {4},
    "televisions": {8},     # "Для дома" -- TVs are subsubcategory 30 inside it
    "laptops": {154},       # 1225 laptops, 1226 gaming laptops, 1522 MacBook
    "acs": {163},
    "fridges": {163},
    "washers": {163},
    "ovens": {163},
}
# The subsubcategory (third level = product TYPE) that actually holds each
# category inside the parents above. A category absent here keeps its whole
# subcategory. `ovens` is stoves, built-in ovens and hobs -- not microwaves,
# which no other source files under `ovens`.
CATEGORY_SUBSUBCATEGORY_IDS = {
    "televisions": {30},
    "acs": {31},
    "fridges": {33, 34},
    "washers": {44, 1411},
    "ovens": {32, 1215, 1403},
}
# openshop files countertop mini-ovens under "Духовые шкафы" (1215): 19 of the
# 78 `ovens` listings on 2026-10-22 ("Мини-печь", "Мини печь", "Mini oven").
# No other source files them under `ovens` (see asaxiy.py), so nothing could
# ever price-compare against them. Dropped by title, the only signal there is.
CATEGORY_EXCLUDE = {
    "ovens": re.compile(r"^\s*(?:мини[\s-]*печ|mini\s*oven)", re.I),
}

# Russian page: "... | Цена: 6 466 000 сум"; Uzbek page: "... | Narx: 6 466 000 so'm".
_TITLE_PRICE = re.compile(r"(?:Цена|Narx):\s*([\d\s ]+)\s*(?:сум|so)")


def page_url(slug: str) -> str:
    return PRODUCT_URL.format(slug=slug)


def title_price(title: str) -> int | None:
    """The price a product page's <title> states, or None."""
    m = _TITLE_PRICE.search(title or "")
    return int(re.sub(r"\D", "", m.group(1))) if m else None


def _parse_item(item: dict, category: str = "smartphones") -> Listing | None:
    slug = item.get("slug")
    # One price field, one code path: `total` is the discounted figure the
    # customer pays; `old_price` is display-only.
    price = coerce_price((item.get("price") or {}).get("total"))
    return try_build_listing(
        store_slug="openshop",
        source_key=str(item.get("id") or ""),
        name_raw=item.get("name") or "",
        price=price,
        # Never item["link"]: that is the API host (web.openshop.uz), not the shop.
        url=page_url(slug) if slug else "",
        availability="IN_STOCK" if item.get("is_stock") else "OUT_OF_STOCK",
        image_url=item.get("thumbnail_img"),
        category=category,
        raw=item,
    )


def parse(payload: dict, category: str = "smartphones") -> list[Listing]:
    """Turn one get_products response into Listings. Pure function, no I/O.

    Unfiltered: category narrowing is crawl policy and lives in sweep().
    """
    listings = []
    for item in (payload or {}).get("data") or []:
        listing = _parse_item(item, category)
        if listing is not None:
            listings.append(listing)
    return listings


def next_page(payload: dict, page: int) -> int | None:
    """The pager decides; an empty or fully-filtered page never ends a sweep."""
    meta = (payload or {}).get("meta") or {}
    last = meta.get("last_page") or page
    return page + 1 if page < last else None


class OpenshopSource:
    slug = "openshop"
    can_sweep = True

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._session = None
        self._last_request = 0.0

    def _throttle(self) -> None:
        wait = MIN_INTERVAL - (time.monotonic() - self._last_request)
        if wait > 0:
            time.sleep(wait)
        self._last_request = time.monotonic()

    def _get_session(self) -> requests.Session:
        if self._session is None:
            self._session = polite_session(
                referer="https://openshop.uz/",
                origin="https://openshop.uz",
                extra_headers=_HEADERS,
            )
        return self._session

    # -- raw access (no price fence) ------------------------------------------

    def category_tree(self) -> list[dict]:
        self._throttle()
        return post_json(self._get_session(), TREE_URL, json={}).get("data") or []

    def _page(self, page: int, **filters) -> dict:
        params = {"page": page, "per_page": PER_PAGE, "sort_by": "latest", **filters}
        self._throttle()
        return post_json(self._get_session(), PRODUCTS_URL, params=params)

    def iter_raw(self, **filters) -> Iterator[dict]:
        """Every raw product dict matching `filters` (category_id, search, ...).

        Raw on purpose: Listing's price fence (>= 100,000 SUM) would drop the
        cheap goods -- cables, kitchen tools, mugs -- that a whole-assortment
        comparison needs to see.
        """
        def fetch(page):
            return self._page(page, **filters)

        def raw_items(payload):
            return (payload or {}).get("data") or []

        def following(payload, page):
            total = ((payload or {}).get("meta") or {}).get("total") or 0
            if total > DEEP_PAGE_CAP and page == 1:
                # Not fatal -- the caller still gets the first ~2,400 -- but a
                # silently truncated sweep is exactly what must never pass as
                # complete, so say so.
                logger.warning("openshop: %s matches %s products, above the ~%s deep-paging cap; "
                               "split the query by category", filters, total, DEEP_PAGE_CAP)
            return next_page(payload, page)

        yield from paginate(fetch, raw_items, following, first_page=1)

    def iter_catalogue(self) -> Iterator[dict]:
        """The whole catalogue, walked one top-level category at a time."""
        seen: set[int] = set()
        for top in self.category_tree():
            got = 0
            for item in self.iter_raw(category_id=top["id"]):
                if item.get("id") in seen:
                    continue
                seen.add(item["id"])
                got += 1
                yield item
            logger.info("openshop: category %s %r -> %d products (declared %s)",
                        top["id"], top.get("name"), got, top.get("products_count"))

    def product_detail(self, slug: str) -> dict:
        """get_product: {product, variants, navbar, seo}."""
        self._throttle()
        return post_json(self._get_session(), PRODUCT_URL_API,
                         params={"slug": slug}, json={"with_variants": True}).get("data") or {}

    def page_title(self, slug: str) -> tuple[str, int | None]:
        """The public product page's <title> and the price it states.

        The page is server-rendered, so this is a read of what a customer sees,
        independent of the JSON listing the price was first taken from.
        """
        self._throttle()
        html = get_text(self._get_session(), page_url(slug), headers={"Accept": "text/html"})
        m = re.search(r"<title>(.*?)</title>", html, re.S)
        title = m.group(1).strip() if m else ""
        return title, title_price(title)

    # -- Source protocol -----------------------------------------------------

    def sweep(self, category: str = "smartphones") -> Iterator[Listing]:
        subs = CATEGORY_SUBCATEGORY_IDS.get(category)
        if subs is None:
            raise ValueError(f"unknown category {category!r}")
        keep = CATEGORY_SUBSUBCATEGORY_IDS.get(category)
        exclude = CATEGORY_EXCLUDE.get(category)
        for sub in sorted(subs):
            for item in self.iter_raw(subcategory_id=sub):
                if keep and item.get("subsubcategory_id") not in keep:
                    continue
                if exclude and exclude.search(item.get("name") or ""):
                    continue
                listing = _parse_item(item, category)
                if listing is not None:
                    yield listing

    def search(self, query: str) -> Iterator[Listing]:
        """get_products?search= -- paginated but fuzzy (the full name of one
        phone matches ~500 products). Raw listings; pick the variant by name."""
        for item in self.iter_raw(search=query):
            listing = _parse_item(item)
            if listing is not None:
                yield listing



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/sources/radius.py
================================================
"""radius.uz client: a Saleor storefront, queried through its public GraphQL.

The cleanest source of the nine. radius.uz runs Saleor, and its own front end
talks to `https://new.api.radius.uz/graphql/` anonymously -- no token, no
signing, no per-brand search. One query per category walks the whole thing
with cursor pagination, and every field we need is a typed column rather than
a string to be parsed: `name`, `brand.name`, `pricing.priceRange.start.amount`
(plain UZS), `isAvailable`, `variants[].sku`.

It is also the SMALLEST source: ~180 products across our seven categories,
against asaxiy's 693 and uzum's 5,461. That is not a defect and not a reason
to skip it. radius is a real Tashkent retailer with its own pricing, and a
source's value to a price comparison is the number of products it puts a
SECOND price on, not the number of rows it adds. Measured after the first
harvest, and recorded in docs/parsers/radius.md.

TWO TRAPS, both measured live 2026-08-11:

1. **The product URL is built from the DEFAULT VARIANT id, not the product
   id.** The site links to `/ru/product/<N>-<product-slug>`, and `N` is
   `defaultVariant.id`. Building it from `Product.id` yields a clean 404 on
   every product -- checked on six, all 404. This is exactly the trap
   docs/parsers/README.md rule 1 exists for ("never trust a slug you built
   yourself"), and it is invisible from the payload alone: both ids are
   plausible integers sitting next to the slug.

2. **Not every category hangs where its name suggests.** `noutbuki` holds 9
   laptops and none of them are Apple: the 7 MacBooks live under a top-level
   "Apple" category, as do the iPhones, which are absent from `smartfony`.
   Sweeping the obvious slug alone silently loses every Apple product --
   including the iPhones, which are the most-compared phones in the
   catalogue. Hence the slug TUPLES below.

Saleor exposes a `Category.products` connection that already includes
descendant categories (verified: `konditsionery` totalCount 17 = the sum of
its five brand children), so the sweep asks the parent and gets the brands
for free.
"""
import base64
import logging
import re
from dataclasses import replace
from typing import Iterator

from narxnoma.sources.base import Listing, coerce_price, paginate, try_build_listing
from narxnoma.sources.http import polite_session, post_json

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

API_URL = "https://new.api.radius.uz/graphql/"
SITE_URL = "https://radius.uz"
PRODUCT_URL = SITE_URL + "/ru/product/{variant_id}-{slug}"

# category -> radius category slugs, verified live 2026-08-11 by walking the
# whole tree through `categories(level: 0)` and reading every totalCount.
#
#   smartphones  smartfony 77 + the two Apple buckets 7   = 84
#   fridges      kholodilniki                             = 22
#   televisions  televizory                               = 18
#   acs          konditsionery                            = 17
#   washers      stiralnye-mashiny                        = 17
#   laptops      noutbuki 9 + noutbuki-apple 7            = 16
#   ovens        gazovye-plity 12 + dukhovoi-shkaf 1      = 13
#
# The Apple entries are the fragile ones: they are MARKETING buckets
# ("🆕 iPhone 17 | 17 Pro | 17 Pro Max"), not a stable "Смартфоны Apple"
# node, and radius renames them as lines launch. A missing slug here is
# silent -- it looks exactly like a retailer that stopped selling iPhones --
# so test_radius_categories.py asserts this map against the live tree and
# fails when a category with products is not covered by any entry.
#
# Deliberately NOT swept: mikrovolnovye-pechi (9) and mini-pechi (8). Same
# decision as asaxiy, for the same measured reason -- all 540 `ovens` offers
# the other sources contribute contain no microwave, so these could never
# price-compare and would render under the "Духовой шкаф" label.
CATEGORY_SLUGS: dict[str, tuple[str, ...]] = {
    "smartphones": ("smartfony", "iphone-17-17-pro-17-pro-max-air", "iphone-air-3"),
    "televisions": ("televizory",),
    "laptops": ("noutbuki", "noutbuki-apple"),
    "acs": ("konditsionery",),
    "fridges": ("kholodilniki",),
    "washers": ("stiralnye-mashiny",),
    "ovens": ("gazovye-plity", "dukhovoi-shkaf"),
}

PAGE_SIZE = 100

# THE VARIANT IS THE OFFER, not the product. This is the single most important
# decision in this client, and it was measured rather than assumed: the first
# version emitted one listing per Product and 1 of 80 phones attached to a
# canonical variant (1.3%). radius keeps the whole identity on the variant --
#
#   Product.name  "Samsung Galaxy A26"
#   Variant.name  "Смартфон Samsung Galaxy A26 5G 8/256GB Black (Черный)"
#
# -- so the product name has no memory pair and no colour, and a phone key
# needs both. Non-phones were attaching at 100% either way, which is exactly
# why this had to be read per category instead of trusted as an average.
#
# Each variant also has its own price (`priceAmount`), its own stock, and its
# own working URL. Verified live: /ru/product/<non-default variant id>-<slug>
# returns 200 and the page renders THAT colour.
_PRODUCT_FIELDS = """
        id
        name
        slug
        isAvailable
        brand { name }
        category { slug }
        defaultVariant { id }
        variants {
          id
          name
          sku
          priceAmount
          currency
          isAvailable
          quantityAvailable
          thumbnail(size: 512) { url }
        }
        pricing { priceRange { start { amount currency } } }
        thumbnail(size: 512) { url }
"""

CATEGORY_QUERY = """
query CategoryProducts($slug: String!, $first: Int!, $after: String) {
  category(slug: $slug) {
    slug
    products(first: $first, after: $after) {
      totalCount
      pageInfo { hasNextPage endCursor }
      edges { node {%s} }
    }
  }
}
""" % _PRODUCT_FIELDS

SEARCH_QUERY = """
query SearchProducts($search: String!, $first: Int!) {
  products(search: $search, first: $first) {
    edges { node {%s} }
  }
}
""" % _PRODUCT_FIELDS

_HEADERS = {
    "Accept": "application/json",
    "Content-Type": "application/json",
    "Accept-Language": "ru",
}


def numeric_id(global_id: str | None) -> str | None:
    """Saleor global ids are base64 "Product:5195" / "ProductVariant:8046".

    Returns the numeric part, or None if the value is not a global id we
    recognise. Returning None rather than raising matters: one malformed id
    must cost one listing, not the whole sweep.
    """
    if not global_id:
        return None
    try:
        decoded = base64.b64decode(global_id).decode("utf-8")
    except Exception:                       # noqa: BLE001 - any decode failure
        return None
    kind, _, number = decoded.partition(":")
    if not number.isdigit() or not kind:
        return None
    return number


def _availability(flag) -> str:
    """Saleor's published-and-in-stock flag -> our vocabulary.

    Absent is UNKNOWN rather than OUT_OF_STOCK: an unset field is not evidence
    of a sold-out product.
    """
    return {True: "IN_STOCK", False: "OUT_OF_STOCK"}.get(flag, "UNKNOWN")


# Saleor hands back a thumbnail URL for every image, but radius's thumbnailer
# does not actually RENDER every source format: an .avif original yields a
# /thumbnails/200/... URL that 404s. Sampling all 274 radius offers, 272
# thumbnails load and the 2 avif ones do not -- a well-formed URL for a file
# that was never generated, which no status check upstream can see because the
# field is present and non-null.
#
# The original under /media/ does exist and returns image/avif, so that is
# what we publish for those. Only for avif: /thumbnails/ is the right thing
# for a grid everywhere it works, and swapping wholesale would hotlink
# full-size originals for 272 products to fix 2.
_UNTHUMBNAILED = (".avif",)


def _image(thumbnail: dict | None) -> str | None:
    url = (thumbnail or {}).get("url")
    if not isinstance(url, str) or not url:
        return None
    if url.lower().endswith(_UNTHUMBNAILED) and "/thumbnails/" in url:
        return re.sub(r"/thumbnails/\d+/", "/media/", url, count=1)
    return url


def _parse_node(node: dict) -> list[Listing]:
    """One Saleor Product -> one Listing per VARIANT. See _PRODUCT_FIELDS."""
    product_id = numeric_id(node.get("id"))
    slug = node.get("slug")
    if not product_id or not slug:
        return []

    variants = node.get("variants") or []
    if not variants:
        # No variant means no price, no stock and no URL we can trust, and a
        # price we cannot attribute to a specific retailer listing is
        # worthless. Logged rather than dropped in silence: a whole catalogue
        # arriving variant-less is a schema change, not a quiet retailer.
        logger.warning("radius: product %s has no variants; dropping it", product_id)
        return []

    # Product-level fallbacks, for the fields a variant may leave unset.
    product_price = coerce_price(
        (((node.get("pricing") or {}).get("priceRange") or {}).get("start") or {})
        .get("amount"))
    product_image = _image(node.get("thumbnail"))

    listings = []
    for variant in variants:
        variant_id = numeric_id(variant.get("id"))
        if not variant_id:
            logger.warning("radius: product %s has a variant with no usable id",
                           product_id)
            continue
        price = coerce_price(variant.get("priceAmount"))
        if price is None:
            price = product_price
        listing = try_build_listing(
            store_slug="radius",
            # The VARIANT id, not the product id: one row per priced,
            # separately-linkable SKU, and source_key is the upsert key.
            source_key=variant_id,
            # The variant name carries the memory pair and the colour; the
            # product name carries neither.
            name_raw=variant.get("name") or node.get("name"),
            price=price,
            url=PRODUCT_URL.format(variant_id=variant_id, slug=slug),
            availability=_availability(
                variant.get("isAvailable")
                if variant.get("isAvailable") is not None
                else node.get("isAvailable")),
            image_url=_image(variant.get("thumbnail")) or product_image,
            raw={"product": {k: v for k, v in node.items() if k != "variants"},
                 "variant": variant},
        )
        if listing is not None:
            listings.append(listing)
    return listings


def parse(payload: dict) -> list[Listing]:
    """Turn one GraphQL response into Listings. Pure function, no I/O.

    Works unchanged against either query: the category connection and the
    search connection share the same `edges { node { ... } }` shape, so this
    reads whichever of the two the payload contains.
    """
    data = (payload or {}).get("data") or {}
    connection = data.get("category")
    connection = (connection or {}).get("products") if connection else data.get("products")
    listings = []
    for edge in (connection or {}).get("edges") or []:
        node = (edge or {}).get("node")
        if node:
            listings.extend(_parse_node(node))
    return listings


def next_cursor(payload: dict) -> str | None:
    page_info = ((((payload or {}).get("data") or {}).get("category") or {})
                 .get("products") or {}).get("pageInfo") or {}
    return page_info.get("endCursor") if page_info.get("hasNextPage") else None


class RadiusSource:
    slug = "radius"
    can_sweep = True

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._session = None

    def _get_session(self):
        if self._session is None:
            self._session = polite_session(
                referer=f"{SITE_URL}/",
                origin=SITE_URL,
                extra_headers=_HEADERS,
            )
        return self._session

    def _sweep_slug(self, slug: str) -> Iterator[Listing]:
        """One radius category slug, walked with Saleor cursor pagination."""
        session = self._get_session()
        # paginate() drives an integer page counter; Saleor pages by opaque
        # cursor. The cursor is carried here and the counter is used only for
        # paginate's own retry and MAX_PAGES bookkeeping.
        cursor: dict[str, str | None] = {"after": None}

        def fetch(_page):
            return post_json(session, API_URL, json={
                "query": CATEGORY_QUERY,
                "operationName": "CategoryProducts",
                "variables": {"slug": slug, "first": PAGE_SIZE,
                              "after": cursor["after"]},
            })

        def parse_page(payload):
            errors = (payload or {}).get("errors")
            if errors:
                # GraphQL reports failure with HTTP 200 and an `errors` key --
                # exactly the uzum trap documented in docs/parsers/uzum.md.
                # Raising here is what stops a half-swept category being
                # recorded as a successful harvest of nothing.
                raise ValueError(f"radius GraphQL errors for {slug!r}: {errors}")
            if ((payload.get("data") or {}).get("category")) is None:
                raise ValueError(f"radius: no such category {slug!r}")
            return parse(payload)

        def following(payload, page):
            after = next_cursor(payload)
            if after is None:
                return None
            cursor["after"] = after
            return page + 1

        yield from paginate(fetch, parse_page, following)

    def sweep(self, category: str = "smartphones") -> Iterator[Listing]:
        """Every product in one of our categories, across radius's slugs for it."""
        slugs = CATEGORY_SLUGS.get(category)
        if slugs is None:
            raise ValueError(f"unknown category {category!r}")

        # A product can legitimately sit under two of the slugs we sweep for
        # one category (Saleor's Category.products includes descendants, and
        # the Apple buckets overlap), and source_key is the upsert key -- so
        # the same product arriving twice would be counted twice in
        # HarvestResult while writing one row.
        seen: set[str] = set()
        for slug in slugs:
            for listing in self._sweep_slug(slug):
                if listing.source_key in seen:
                    continue
                seen.add(listing.source_key)
                yield (replace(listing, category=category)
                       if listing.category != category else listing)

    def search(self, query: str) -> Iterator[Listing]:
        """Saleor's `products(search:)`. Present for the Source protocol; the
        harvest uses sweep(), and Pass B only queries sources that cannot be
        swept."""
        session = self._get_session()
        payload = post_json(session, API_URL, json={
            "query": SEARCH_QUERY,
            "operationName": "SearchProducts",
            "variables": {"search": query, "first": PAGE_SIZE},
        })
        yield from parse(payload)



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/sources/texnomart.py
================================================
"""texnomart.uz client: generic-query sweep, not a browsable catalogue.

texnomart's search endpoint accepts a broad query (`q=smartfon`) and returns
a paginated result set spanning the whole phone assortment, so `sweep()`
walks that pagination instead of hitting a per-category catalogue endpoint
(see docs/parsers/texnomart.md). `search()` reuses the same endpoint with
the caller's query text.
"""
from typing import Iterator

import logging
from dataclasses import replace
from narxnoma.sources.base import Listing, coerce_price, paginate, try_build_listing
from narxnoma.sources.http import get_json, polite_session
from narxnoma.sources.not_a_phone import is_phone_listing

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

API_URL = "https://gw.texnomart.uz/api/common/v1/search/result"
PRODUCT_URL = "https://texnomart.uz/product/detail/{id}/"
SWEEP_QUERY = "smartfon"

# Plan 5: category -> texnomart search query word + the product-type noun a
# kept listing must carry. texnomart has no item category field, so a
# non-phone sweep queries the category word and then ALLOW-lists (keeps only
# titles containing that word) instead of running the phone denylist -- the
# denylist would drop every "Телевизор"/"Ноутбук" title.
CATEGORY_QUERY = {
    "smartphones": "smartfon",
    "televisions": "televizor",
    "laptops": "noutbuk",
    "acs": "konditsioner",
    "fridges": "xolodilnik",
    "washers": "stiraln",
    "ovens": "duxovka",
}
CATEGORY_KEEP_WORD = {
    "smartphones": None,  # phones use is_phone_listing instead
    "televisions": "televizor",
    "laptops": "noutbuk",
    "acs": "konditsioner",
    "fridges": "muzlatgich",
    "washers": "kir yuvish",
    "ovens": "duxovka",
}
LIMIT = 20

_HEADERS = {
    "accept-device": "web",
    "Accept-Language": "uz",
    "accept-region": "2",
}


def _parse_item(item: dict) -> Listing | None:
    product_id = item.get("id")
    if not product_id:
        return None
    name = item.get("name")
    # old_price is frequently 0 and must never be used as a price fallback --
    # sale_price is the only field texnomart guarantees to be current.
    price = coerce_price(item.get("sale_price"))

    return try_build_listing(
        store_slug="texnomart",
        source_key=str(product_id),
        name_raw=name,
        price=price,
        url=PRODUCT_URL.format(id=product_id),
        image_url=item.get("image"),
        raw=item,
    )


def parse(payload: dict) -> list[Listing]:
    """Turn one raw search-result response into Listings. Pure function, no I/O."""
    products = ((payload or {}).get("data") or {}).get("products") or []
    listings = []
    for item in products:
        lst = _parse_item(item)
        if lst is not None:
            listings.append(lst)
    return listings


class TexnomartSource:
    slug = "texnomart"
    can_sweep = True

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._session = None

    def _get_session(self):
        if self._session is None:
            self._session = polite_session(
                referer="https://texnomart.uz/",
                origin="https://texnomart.uz",
                extra_headers=_HEADERS,
            )
        return self._session

    def _paginate(self, query: str) -> Iterator[Listing]:
        session = self._get_session()

        def fetch(page):
            return get_json(
                session,
                API_URL,
                params={"q": query, "page": page, "limit": LIMIT},
            )

        def following(payload, page):
            pagination = ((payload or {}).get("data") or {}).get("pagination") or {}
            return page + 1 if page < pagination.get("total_page", page) else None

        yield from paginate(fetch, parse, following)

    def sweep(self, category: str = "smartphones") -> Iterator[Listing]:
        """Every item texnomart returns for the category's query word.

        The filter is crawl POLICY and lives here, not in parse(): parse must
        stay a faithful pure function of one response. Phones use the denylist
        (is_phone_listing); non-phone categories allow-list -- keep only names
        carrying the category's product-type noun -- so a TV sweep never
        admits a phone and never drops a TV.
        """
        query = CATEGORY_QUERY.get(category)
        if query is None:
            raise ValueError(f"unknown category {category!r}")
        keep_word = CATEGORY_KEEP_WORD.get(category)
        kept = dropped = 0
        for listing in self._paginate(query):
            if category == "smartphones":
                keep = is_phone_listing(listing)
            else:
                keep = keep_word and keep_word.casefold() in listing.name_raw.casefold()
            if keep:
                if listing.category != category:
                    listing = replace(listing, category=category)
                kept += 1
                yield listing
            else:
                dropped += 1
        logger.info("texnomart sweep (%s): %d kept, %d dropped",
                    category, kept, dropped)


    def search(self, query: str) -> Iterator[Listing]:
        yield from self._paginate(query)



================================================
FILE: narxnoma-crawler/narxnoma/sources/uzum.py
================================================
"""uzum.uz client: anonymous-token GraphQL, per-brand sweep.

uzum is the largest marketplace in Uzbekistan and the only source here with
auth, per-variant (SKU) pricing, and a failure mode that looks like success.
Read docs/parsers/uzum.md in full before touching this file -- every trap
below has a section there with the live evidence behind it.

Auth (docs/parsers/uzum.md section 1): `graphql.uzum.uz` returns a bare 401
without a Bearer token. The token is minted anonymously via
`POST id.uzum.uz/api/auth/token` with an empty JSON body -- no account, no
captcha. The response is **204 No Content**: the token is not in the body,
it arrives as a `Set-Cookie: access_token=<JWT>` header, which is why
`_mint_token` talks to `requests` directly instead of `get_json`/`post_json`
(those call `response.json()` unconditionally and would raise
`NonJsonResponse` on the empty 204 body). Exactly two headers are required
to mint -- `Accept-Language` (any value) and `Referer` (must be on the
uzum.uz origin); `Origin` is NOT required, the opposite of what you would
guess. TTL is 6 hours (`exp` in the JWT payload); `_get_token` caches the
token at module (process) level and refreshes ~5 minutes before `exp`.

The GraphQL endpoint itself (docs/parsers/uzum.md section 2) needs four
headers -- `Authorization`, `Content-Type`, `apollographql-client-name:
web-customers`, `x-iid: <any UUID>` -- or it returns a bare 401. Drop only
`apollographql-client-name` or `x-iid` and it instead returns **200 OK**
with `data: null` and an `errors` array: checking `response.status_code`
alone reports success on a request that returned nothing.
`UzumSource._fetch_payload` inspects `payload.get("errors")` explicitly and
raises `UzumGraphQLError` when present, so a broken sweep call is loud
rather than silently looking like "no more items for this brand." `parse()`
itself never raises for this -- it logs and returns `[]`, so a caller handed
one already-fetched payload gets an empty result rather than a crash.
`_fetch_payload` also survives a **server-side token revocation**: the cache
refreshes only when the locally decoded `exp` passes, so a token revoked
early arrives as a bare 401 the cache cannot predict. That 401 re-mints once
and retries; a second 401 is a real auth failure and raises.

**Card price is not product price** (section 5): a card bundles several
models and every storage tier under one `minSellPrice`. Every listing here
is built from a `skuList[]` entry using `sellPrice` -- never `minSellPrice`,
never `fullPrice` (a struck-through pre-discount figure, often fantasy).

**Off-category noise** (section 3): relevance search mixes in cases,
chargers and vacuum cleaners. `parse()` filters client-side on
`catalogCard.category.id` in `PHONE_CATEGORY_IDS` (15271 Android, 15272
iPhone) -- folded into `parse()` itself, not left to a separate crawl-policy
step, because `search()` hits the exact same noisy relevance endpoint as
`sweep()` and both need it.

**Pagination caps at offset ~= 1000** (section 3): beyond that `items` comes
back empty regardless of `limit`, so `sweep()` goes brand by brand (see
`narxnoma.sources.brand_queries.BRAND_QUERIES`) rather than attempting one
unbounded query -- the Android smartphone category alone runs to thousands
of multi-seller listings.

**name_raw** (sections 5a/5b/7): uzum hands back no single product-name
field. `name_raw` is the *only* handle the clustering stage gets on a
product's identity -- a name that loses the brand or the storage figure
cannot be keyed at all, and the offer drops out of the catalogue silently.
`_build_name_raw` therefore has three jobs, and each of them has already
been got wrong once:

- take the card title only up to its first comma (marketing blurb after
  that), and let the SKU's own `Модель` characteristic override it;
- when the head is reduced to a prefix in front of a `Модель` value, keep
  the whole brand-bearing head rather than its first word, after dropping
  a leading `Smartfon`/`Смартфон`/`Telefon`/`Телефон` product-type noun.
  Keeping only the first word turned
  `("Smartfon Samsung Galaxy A16, ...", "A16 8/256")` into
  `"Smartfon A16 Qora"` -- brand, RAM and storage all unrecoverable -- and
  10 of this recording's 20 cards lead with that noun, so it was the common
  case rather than an edge;
- when the SKU states its own memory, strip memory figures out of the
  *card-derived* text before combining, so the card's overall storage
  *range* never masquerades as this SKU's specific tier (the exact bug that
  once reported 2,672,010 for a phone whose true price was 3,068,010; see
  `test_known_product_name_raw_strips_the_cards_other_memory_tiers`).
  Memory stripping is **never** applied to the `Модель` value itself --
  that value is the trustworthy one, and stripping it deleted the very
  figure section 5b exists to preserve.

When neither the SKU's characteristics nor the truncated head yields any
memory at all, the *full* card title is searched for a memory pattern and
the match appended: cards that state memory after the first comma
("Smartfon Samsung Galaxy A16, 4/128 GB, 2 SIM, ...") otherwise produced a
storage-less, unkeyable name from information we were already holding in
`raw["card_title"]`.

Characteristic keys are a typo minefield (`Память`, `памяять`, `Памяьт:`,
`Xotirasi`, `RAM/ROM`, and -- observed live in this recording -- `xotra`, a
typo the doc's own `startswith("xotira")` sample does not catch;
`_char_kind` here classifies by the shorter `startswith("xot")` prefix
instead, and treats a bare `ram`/`rom` token as memory because `normalize`
folds `RAM/ROM` to `ram rom`), so keys are classified loosely, never matched
exactly. `_parse_sku` also puts the untouched card title and the SKU's full
`characteristicValues` list into `Listing.raw`, so a later clustering stage
can redo this name-building better without re-crawling.

**Two deliberate inversions of docs/parsers/uzum.md** (project decision, not
a doc bug -- see the task-10 brief): that document was written for a
target-driven matcher hunting one specific phone, where an eSIM-only
variant or a zero-stock SKU is noise to screen out. This client is an
*aggregator* cataloguing every real offer, so both instructions invert:

- eSIM-only variants (section 6) are **kept**, not dropped -- they are a
  legitimate, separate product at a genuinely different price. Whatever SIM
  characteristic exists survives into `raw["characteristic_values"]`
  verbatim for a later clustering stage to classify.
- Zero-stock SKUs (`availableAmount <= 0`, section 4) are **kept**, not
  dropped -- recorded with `availability="OUT_OF_STOCK"` rather than
  discarded. Hiding an out-of-stock offer is a presentation decision that
  belongs downstream, not a reason to lose the observation.
"""
import base64
import json
import logging
import re
import time
import uuid
from dataclasses import replace
from typing import Iterator

import requests

from narxnoma.config import load_settings
from narxnoma.matching.normalize import normalize
from narxnoma.sources.base import Listing, coerce_price, paginate, try_build_listing
from narxnoma.sources.brand_queries import queries_for
from narxnoma.sources.http import pause, polite_session, post_json

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

AUTH_URL = "https://id.uzum.uz/api/auth/token"
GRAPHQL_URL = "https://graphql.uzum.uz/"
# Canonical product URL WITHOUT skuId. uzum's own 302 meta-refresh builds
# id.uzum.uz/api/auth/redirect?redirectUrl=<product>?skuId=<id> with the
# nested query raw (never encoded), and the auth endpoint answers that
# malformed parameter with a conflict JSON -- the broken-link bug reported
# against the site. The clean URL produces a clean redirect + access_token
# cookie and lands on the product page (all SKUs listed there). SKU identity
# lives in source_key, not in the human-facing URL.
PRODUCT_URL = "https://uzum.uz/ru/product/{product_id}"

# 15271 = Смартфоны Android, 15272 = Смартфоны Apple iPhone (iOS) -- see
# docs/parsers/uzum.md section 3. Relevance search mixes in cases, chargers
# and (genuinely) vacuum cleaners; this is the only usable signal to drop them.
PHONE_CATEGORY_IDS = frozenset({15271, 15272})

# Plan 5: category -> uzum category.id set, verified live 2026-08-10 by
# querying makeSearch per product type and reading catalogCard.category.id.
#
# Re-verified live 2026-08-10 while fixing the category frontier. Two
# corrections, both found by reading catalogCard.category off a real response:
#   - ovens held only 11 (Духовые шкафы). "газовая плита" is category 13
#     (Газовые плиты) and is a different id, so every gas cooker uzum sells was
#     filtered out of a sweep that had gone and fetched it. The other sources
#     already harvest gas cookers into `ovens`.
#   - fridges gains 83 (Морозильники). The Uzbek "muzlatgich" resolves to
#     FREEZERS on uzum, not refrigerators -- "холодильник"/"sovutgich" are the
#     words that reach id 82 -- and freezers belong with fridges here for the
#     same reason cookers belong with ovens.
CATEGORY_IDS = {
    "smartphones": PHONE_CATEGORY_IDS,
    "televisions": {12601},
    "laptops": {12520},
    "acs": {14373},
    "fridges": {82, 83},
    "washers": {15291, 15296},
    "ovens": {11, 13},
}

PAGE_LIMIT = 72
# Pagination caps at offset ~= 1000 -- beyond that `items` is always empty
# regardless of limit (docs/parsers/uzum.md section 3). sweep() stops each
# brand here even if the response claims a larger `total`.
OFFSET_CAP = 1000

# Refresh the cached token 5 minutes before its 6-hour JWT `exp`, per
# docs/parsers/uzum.md section 1.
_TOKEN_REFRESH_MARGIN_SECONDS = 300

# Assumed TTL when the access_token cookie is not a decodable JWT. uzum can
# change the cookie format at any time; a crash inside _decode_jwt_exp would
# kill the entire sweep over a value that is only a refresh optimisation, so
# an undecodable token is treated as short-lived instead.
_TOKEN_FALLBACK_TTL_SECONDS = 3600

# Cached at module (process) level -- "mint a token once (cached for the
# process, refreshed ~5 minutes before its 6-hour exp)" per the task brief.
# Every UzumSource instance in this process shares one token.
_token_cache: dict = {"token": None, "iid": None, "exp": 0}

SEARCH_QUERY = """
query getMakeSearch($q: MakeSearchQueryInput!) {
  makeSearch(query: $q) {
    total
    items {
      catalogCard {
        __typename
        productId
        title
        category { id title }
        photos { link(trans: PRODUCT_540) { high } }
        ... on SkuGroupCard {
          skuList {
            id
            sellPrice
            fullPrice
            availableAmount
            characteristicValues { title characteristic { title } }
          }
        }
      }
    }
  }
}
"""


class UzumGraphQLError(RuntimeError):
    """Raised by the live fetch path (never by parse()) when a 200 OK
    GraphQL response carries an `errors` key -- docs/parsers/uzum.md
    section 2's headline trap: this looks like success by status code alone.
    """


def _decode_jwt_exp(token: str) -> int:
    """The `exp` claim of the access_token cookie, or a conservative
    fallback expiry if the cookie is not a decodable JWT.

    The expiry is only used to decide *when to refresh*, so it must never be
    able to raise: an opaque or reformatted cookie would otherwise crash
    _get_token and take the whole sweep with it, even though the token
    itself may be perfectly usable.
    """
    try:
        payload_b64 = token.split(".")[1]
        payload_b64 += "=" * (-len(payload_b64) % 4)
        claims = json.loads(base64.urlsafe_b64decode(payload_b64))
        return int(claims["exp"])
    except (AttributeError, IndexError, KeyError, TypeError, ValueError):
        logger.warning(
            "uzum: access_token is not a decodable JWT; assuming a %ds TTL",
            _TOKEN_FALLBACK_TTL_SECONDS,
        )
        return int(time.time()) + _TOKEN_FALLBACK_TTL_SECONDS


def _mint_token() -> tuple[str, str]:
    """POST id.uzum.uz/api/auth/token and pull the bearer token out of the
    Set-Cookie header (the 204 response body is empty -- see module
    docstring). Bypasses get_json/post_json on purpose: both call
    response.json() unconditionally and would raise NonJsonResponse on this
    endpoint's empty body.
    """
    settings = load_settings()
    iid = str(uuid.uuid4())
    session = requests.Session()
    try:
        response = session.post(
            AUTH_URL,
            headers={
                "Accept": "*/*",
                "Accept-Language": "ru-RU",       # required
                "Content-Type": "application/json",
                "Referer": "https://uzum.uz/",    # required, must be uzum.uz origin
                "User-Agent": settings.user_agent,
                "x-iid": iid,
            },
            json={},
            timeout=(settings.connect_timeout, settings.read_timeout),
        )
        response.raise_for_status()
    finally:
        pause()  # a real retailer call either way -- see http.get_json/post_json
    token = next((c.value for c in session.cookies if c.name == "access_token"), None)
    if not token:
        raise RuntimeError("uzum auth: no access_token cookie in the mint response")
    return token, iid


def _get_token() -> tuple[str, str]:
    """The process-cached bearer token and install id, minting or refreshing
    as needed. See module docstring for the caching contract.
    """
    if _token_cache["token"] and time.time() < _token_cache["exp"] - _TOKEN_REFRESH_MARGIN_SECONDS:
        return _token_cache["token"], _token_cache["iid"]
    token, iid = _mint_token()
    exp = _decode_jwt_exp(token)
    _token_cache.update(token=token, iid=iid, exp=exp)
    return token, iid


def _invalidate_token() -> None:
    """Force the next _get_token() to mint a fresh token.

    The cache refreshes on the locally decoded `exp`, which cannot see a
    *server-side* revocation: uzum can invalidate a token before its stated
    expiry, and the only signal is a 401 on the next graphql call. See
    UzumSource._fetch_payload.
    """
    _token_cache.update(token=None, iid=None, exp=0)


# `RAM/ROM` is in docs/parsers/uzum.md section 7's own observed-key list, but
# `normalize` folds the slash to a space and hands back `ram rom`, which no
# prefix test in this function recognised -- so the key classified as "other"
# and its memory value was dropped without a trace. Same class of bug as the
# `xotra` typo below. Matched on tokens, not as a substring: `rang` (colour)
# must not be caught by a bare `"ram" in k`.
_MEMORY_KEY_TOKENS = frozenset({"ram", "rom"})


def _char_kind(key: str) -> str:
    """Classify a characteristic key loosely -- never match exactly.
    docs/parsers/uzum.md section 7 catalogues the typo minefield
    (`Память`, `память`, `ПАМЯТЬ`, `Памят:`, `Память:`, `Памяьт:`,
    `памяять`, `Объем памяти`, `Xotirasi`, `RAM/ROM`); this recording
    additionally turned up `xotra` (missing the final `i`) and `Xotira:`
    (trailing colon, stripped by `normalize` anyway) -- neither matches the
    doc's own `startswith("xotira")` sample, which is why this uses the
    shorter `startswith("xot")` instead. Every key in section 7's list is
    exercised by test_char_kind_classifies_every_observed_key.
    """
    k = normalize(key)
    if (
        k.startswith("памя")
        or k.startswith("xot")
        or "объем памяти" in k
        or _MEMORY_KEY_TOKENS & set(k.split())
    ):
        return "memory"
    if k.startswith("модел") or k.startswith("model"):
        return "model"
    if "sim" in k or "сим" in k:
        return "sim"
    if k.startswith("цвет") or k.startswith("rang"):
        return "color"
    return "other"


def _characteristics(sku: dict) -> dict[str, str]:
    """First model/memory/color value seen for this SKU, classified via
    _char_kind. SIM values are not extracted here -- name_raw never carries
    them (see module docstring); they survive into Listing.raw verbatim as
    part of the full characteristicValues list instead.
    """
    found = {"model": "", "memory": "", "color": ""}
    for cv in sku.get("characteristicValues") or []:
        if not isinstance(cv, dict):
            continue
        key = ((cv.get("characteristic") or {}).get("title")) or ""
        kind = _char_kind(key)
        if kind in found and not found[kind]:
            value = cv.get("title")
            if value:
                found[kind] = str(value)
    return found


# docs/parsers/uzum.md section 5b, verbatim: when a SKU states its own
# memory, strip memory figures out of the card-derived spec before
# combining, or the card's overall storage range masquerades as this SKU's
# specific tier.
# The trailing `+` makes the chain greedy across MORE than two figures. A
# card offering three tiers ("Galaxy S25 Edge 12/256/512") otherwise had
# exactly two of them consumed, leaving "512" standing in the head -- where
# the bare-pair scan then read the card's 512 as the capacity and demoted
# the SKU's own 256 to a model token. That is the leak inverted, not fixed.
#
# `-` is a separator too, because uzum sellers write the RAM-ROM pair with a
# hyphen ("POCO C81 Pro (4-64/4-128)", "4GB-128GB") -- but ONLY when it is
# TIGHT, with no space on either side. A SPACED hyphen is prose, and treating
# it as a separator ate the model number: "REALME NOTE 70 - 2 дня работы"
# matched "70 - 2", so the strip deleted the 70 and left the battery figure
# standing in for the model ("realme note 6300 /128"). The capacity guard
# cannot catch that -- 6300 is not a capacity, so the variant looks perfectly
# well-formed -- and another retailer's Realme Note 70 keys correctly, so the
# two never merge and the offer pool splits. Slash and plus keep their
# optional spaces; only the hyphen is restricted, because only the hyphen
# appears in ordinary prose.
#
# The leading `(?<![a-zа-я])` still protects catalogue codes: the "0" of
# "SM-A075F" is preceded by a letter, so no chain can start inside one.
_MEMORY_PAIR_RE = re.compile(
    r"(?<![a-zа-я])\d{1,4}\s*(?:gb|гб|tb|тб)?"
    r"(?:(?:\s*[/\\+|]\s*|-)\d{1,4}\s*(?:gb|гб|tb|тб)?)+",
    re.I,
)
_MEMORY_SINGLE_RE = re.compile(
    r"(?<![a-zа-я\d])\d{1,4}\s*(?:gb|гб|tb|тб)\b",
    re.I,
)


# Residue left behind by the two substitutions above. Stripping "(8+8)/128
# GB" out of "Smartfon Oppo A5i Pro (8+8)/128 GB" leaves "( )/" hanging off
# the end -- SKU 6931382 in the recorded fixture parsed to
# "Smartfon Oppo A5i Pro ( )/ 8/128 Binafsha".
_EMPTY_BRACKETS_RE = re.compile(r"[(\[{]\s*[)\]}]")
# A separator with no word character immediately to its left is orphaned.
# Deliberately one-sided: a separator that IS preceded by a word character
# may be a real model marker ("Note 14 Pro+"), and must survive.
_ORPHAN_SEPARATOR_RE = re.compile(r"(?<!\w)[/\\|+]+")

# Anything smaller is a RAM figure or a screen size, not a storage tier --
# used to reject "4/6" out of "4/6/128Gb" and "2 SIM" when recovering memory
# from a full card title.
_MIN_PLAUSIBLE_STORAGE_GB = 32

# RAM sizes retailers actually sell. Used to tell a RAM/storage SPEC from a
# storage RANGE: "4/128" and "12/256" pair a RAM figure with a capacity and
# identify one product, while "128/256" and "256/512" pair two capacities
# and are the card's range across the SKUs it bundles -- they identify
# nothing, and recovering one puts a coin-toss capacity on a real product.
_PLAUSIBLE_RAM_GB = frozenset({1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 16, 18, 24})


def _is_storage_range(figures: list[int]) -> bool:
    """True when every figure is a capacity, so this states a range."""
    return len(figures) > 2 or (
        len(figures) == 2 and min(figures) not in _PLAUSIBLE_RAM_GB
    )


def _collapse(text: str) -> str:
    return re.sub(r"\s+", " ", text).strip(" ,;:-/\\|")


def _strip_memory(text: str) -> str:
    """Remove memory figures from CARD-DERIVED text only.

    Never call this on a SKU's own `Модель` or memory value:
    docs/parsers/uzum.md section 5b strips memory from the *card title*
    precisely because the SKU's own value is the trustworthy one. Applying
    it to `Модель` deleted the figure the whole section exists to preserve
    ("A16 8/256" -> "A16").
    """
    text = _MEMORY_PAIR_RE.sub(" ", text)
    text = _MEMORY_SINGLE_RE.sub(" ", text)
    text = _EMPTY_BRACKETS_RE.sub(" ", text)
    text = _ORPHAN_SEPARATOR_RE.sub(" ", text)
    return _collapse(text)


def _has_memory(text: str) -> bool:
    return bool(_MEMORY_PAIR_RE.search(text) or _MEMORY_SINGLE_RE.search(text))


# A bare capacity, with neither a unit nor a partner figure: `Память: "128"`,
# `Модель: "17 Pro 256"`. Restricted to real capacities so a model number
# ("17", "Note 14") is never mistaken for one.
_BARE_MEMORY_RE = re.compile(r"(?<![a-zа-я\d])(?:32|64|128|256|512|1024|2048)(?!\d)", re.I)


def _states_memory(text: str) -> bool:
    """Does this text state a memory figure IN ANY SHAPE?

    Distinct from `_has_memory`, which only recognises a separated pair or a
    unit-bearing figure. Both of this module's section-5b decisions ask
    "did the SKU tell us its own memory?", and for a bare figure `_has_memory`
    answered no -- so the card's overall RANGE was substituted for a figure
    the SKU had stated perfectly clearly:

    - the recovery gate appended the range beside the SKU's own capacity,
      turning `Память: "128"` on a "64/128/256 ГБ" card into
      `... 12 128 64/128 Алый`, where 64 becomes a MODEL token;
    - section 5b's strip gate never fired, so `Модель: "17 Pro 256"` on an
      "IPhone 17 Pro / Max 256/512 GB" card kept the card's range and keyed
      as 512 GB, merging three different SKUs onto one variant.

    A failure to RECOGNISE a stated figure is not a licence to substitute
    the card's range for it.
    """
    return bool(_has_memory(text) or _BARE_MEMORY_RE.search(text))


def _memory_from_title(title: str) -> str:
    """First plausible memory figure anywhere in the FULL card title.

    Last resort for cards that state memory after the first comma
    ("Smartfon Samsung Galaxy A16, 4/128 GB, 2 SIM, ...") while the SKU has
    no memory characteristic of its own. Without it those SKUs produce a
    name with neither RAM nor storage, which the clustering stage cannot
    key -- and the figure was sitting in raw["card_title"] all along.
    """
    saw_range = False
    for match in _MEMORY_PAIR_RE.finditer(title):
        figures = [int(f) for f in re.findall(r"\d{1,4}", match.group(0))]
        if not figures or max(figures) < _MIN_PLAUSIBLE_STORAGE_GB:
            continue
        # A range identifies nothing -- see _is_storage_range. Taking one
        # would put a coin-toss capacity on a real product, which is the
        # single defect this project exists to avoid; a name with no
        # capacity is refused by the clustering stage instead.
